## 2026-09-13

- Q: Issue #192 (1.2) — `proto_ip.c` derived `l4_packet_len` from the attacker-controlled IPv4 `tot_len` and clamped to the captured length only on the reassembly branch (F-BUG-016). How is the bound computed, and what shape do `proto_syslog.c`/`proto_redis.c` and the HTTP/1 family adopt for payload reads (F-BUG-054, F-BUG-059)?
  A: `ip_post_classification_function` now computes `usable = MIN(l3_packet_len, l3_captured_packet_len)` before subtracting `ihl_bytes` on every branch, so `l4_packet_len` — and the `payload_packet_len` proto_tcp/proto_udp derive from it — can never exceed captured bytes; well-formed traffic (tot_len <= captured) is unchanged and Ethernet padding stays excluded because `l3_packet_len` still wins when it is smaller. Syslog and Redis each derive one local clamped length (`MIN(payload_packet_len, payload_end - payload)` where `payload_end = ipacket->data + p_hdr->caplen`) and pass it through, so no read is bounded by `payload_packet_len` alone; the syslog scanner index was widened `uint8_t` -> `uint32_t` so a >255-byte payload cannot wrap it into an unbounded loop. `http2_can_read(offset, n, caplen)` (`src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http2.c:18`) — bounding each read by the absolute `p_hdr->caplen` buffer end rather than a header-derived length — is recorded as the shape the HTTP/1 family adopts for its own bounded reads once this lands; no HTTP/1 code change is in this issue's scope.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ip.c:1694-1705`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_syslog.c:209-218`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_redis.c:65-74`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http2.c:18`, `tools/phase0/tests/caplen_clamp_test.c`.
- Q: Issue #200 (F-BUG-005) says `clear_protocol_stack_map` deletes the global map without NULLing it "and two other globals share the shape". Which two globals does that mean, and how is each fixed?
  A: `mmt_configured_handlers_map` (`src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:159`) — deleted by `close_extraction()` without NULLing — and `timeout_milestones_map` (`mmt_handler_t` field, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h:458`) — deleted by `clear_timeout_milestones()` without NULLing the field. Both now get NULL-after-delete. `protocol_stack_map` additionally became lazily (re)created on the write path (`get_protocol_stack_map()`), so a second `init_extraction()` gets a fresh map; read paths treat NULL as "no stack" instead of creating one. The other candidates already handle this correctly (`configured_protocols_names_map` is NULLed at `free_registered_protocols()`, `configured_protocols[i]` at `:986`, `plugin_handlers_list` at `plugins_engine.c:190`), and `proto->attributes_map`/`attributes_names_map` cannot return failure to their `void` parent — they are covered by the new NULL-tolerant map API instead.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.cpp`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:978-997,2066-2090`, `src/mmt_core/src/plugins_engine.c:158-192`.

- Q: `session_timeout_comp_fn_pt` is defined twice — `packet_processing.c:24` (C, unmangled) and `hash_utils.cpp` (C++, mangled export `_Z26session_timeout_comp_fn_ptjj`). Marking the .cpp definition `extern "C"` per the issue's grep-metric would create a duplicate-symbol clash. Which survives?
  A: The C definition in `packet_processing.c` is canonical — `mmt_init_handler` (`packet_processing.c:1329`) binds it inside its own translation unit. The C++ duplicate was dead code (no caller, no header declaration) and was removed. The same reasoning made `protocol_stack_id_comp_fn_pt` file-local `static` (only user is the protocol-stack map itself).
  Source: `nm -D sdk/lib/libmmt_core.so` before/after, `grep -rn session_timeout_comp_fn_pt src/`.

- Q: Issue #197 (1.7) — which organisation is canonical for `mmt-dpi`, and what does "verify the tag signature" mean when upstream release tags carry no GPG signature?
  A: `montimage-projects` is canonical — confirming the 2026-09-12 entry (issues #187/#188): `origin` pushes there, every entry-point document names it, and the alternate `Montimage` identity is the stale mirror (`git ls-remote`: its `main` sits 15 commits behind `origin/main` and it lacks tag `v1.8.0`). `install.sh` now defaults `REF` to the pinned `RELEASE_TAG=v1.8.0` with `RELEASE_TAG_SHA=af4c3cd7c4d04307411ef17bb16971a0df47f3d1`; after the shallow clone it asserts `git rev-parse <tag>^{commit}` equals the pin (a moved or re-created tag fails the build) and runs `git tag -v` — an invalid signature is fatal while an unsigned tag warns, because every current upstream release tag is unsigned (`git cat-file tag v1.8.0` shows no signature block), so the commit pin is today's enforced check and any future signed tag is verified automatically. A moving ref (`BRANCH` env or `--branch`, e.g. `dev`/`main`) is refused unless `--unverified-branch` is passed; `--dry-run` prints the resolved plan. `scripts/validate-install-origin.sh` (committed under #190, run blocking by the `doc-validators` job from #187) remains the committed divergence check and now also smoke-runs `install.sh --dry-run` plus the `--unverified-branch` gate.
  Source: `install.sh` (`REPO_URL`, `RELEASE_TAG`, `RELEASE_TAG_SHA`, `resolve_ref`, `verify_release_tag`, `parse_args`, `print_plan`), `scripts/validate-install-origin.sh:96-113`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` `doc-validators` job, `git ls-remote origin|github`, `docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-09-12 entry.

- Q: Issue #198 (1.8) — where should per-package SBOMs be generated, which assets get attested, and how is the wiring kept from rotting?
  A: SBOMs are generated in the per-distro `build` job — one `anchore/sbom-action` scan (`file:` input, `format: spdx-json`) of the package file itself, written next to it as `dist/packages/<pkg>.sbom.json` so the existing upload-artifact glob ships it — not in the tag-only `release` job, so the `pull_request` trigger exercises SBOM generation on every packaging change instead of first failing on a tag push. The release job collects `*.deb`/`*.rpm`/`*.sbom.json` into `release/`, writes `SHA256SUMS` over all of them, then attests `release/*` with `actions/attest-build-provenance` — every published file becomes an attestation subject, manifest included — and publishes the same `release/*` set, so `sha256sum --check` and `gh attestation verify` both work on every asset. `upload-artifact`/`upload-release-assets` on sbom-action are disabled so `softprops/action-gh-release` stays the single publisher. Permissions stay job-scoped: `release` adds `id-token: write` (Sigstore OIDC) and `attestations: write` (repo attestation store) to its existing `contents: write`; the build job and the workflow-level `contents: read` are untouched. Rot prevention: `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh` statically re-checks the wiring (permissions, attestation subjects, manifest, SBOM path/collection, README verification commands, SHA-pinning) and runs blocking in the `verify-helpers` job.
  Source: `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` `verify-helpers` job, `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh`, `README.md` "Verify a package before installing it", `gh api repos/actions/attest-build-provenance/tags` (v4.2.2), `gh api repos/anchore/sbom-action/tags` (v0.24.2).

## 2026-09-14

- Q: Issue #217 (4.1) — how are the five release build images digest-pinned when GitHub matrix `image:` values must stay literal, and where is the pin recorded so a dependency bot can see it?
  A: Pins are `name:tag@sha256:<index-digest>` literals in the `build` matrix — the acceptance grep (`image: [^@]+$`) forbids `${{ }}` indirection, so a generated matrix was not an option — and the index digest (not a per-arch manifest digest) is used so the same pin resolves both the amd64 leg and the arm64 QEMU leg (`docker buildx imagetools inspect` shows every index covers `linux/amd64` + `linux/arm64/v8`). The tag is kept in the ref for readability; the digest is what docker actually resolves. `tools/ci/base-images.txt` is the tracked record: one exact `image:` value per line so the workflow can `grep -Fx` its matrix pin before building (fail-fast on drift), `digest-source:` lines document the refresh command, and the file is added to the workflow's `pull_request.paths` so a pin update runs the packaging matrix. `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh` (committed advisory under #190) now runs as a blocking step in the `verify-helpers` job — its gate condition holds.
  Source: `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (matrix + "Verify pinned base image is recorded" step), `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` `verify-helpers` job, `docker buildx imagetools inspect <image>:<tag>` for all five images.
- Q: Issue #202 (2.4) — the AC requires every hand-written extraction callback in `proto_{ip,tcp,gre,gtp,icmp}.c` to begin with the shared `mmt_have_bytes` caplen prologue, including callbacks that read no packet bytes (session-only extractors like `tcp_session_rtt_extraction`, `ip_client_port_extraction`, `_extract_jitter`) and delegate wrappers (`gtp_imsi_mmc/mnc`, `icmp_identifier_and_seq_nb`, `icmp_gateway`). How are both handled without changing extraction semantics?
  A: Session-only callbacks get a floor prologue `if (!mmt_have_bytes(packet, 0, 0) || extracted_data == NULL) return 0;` — it validates the packet plumbing (NULL packet/p_hdr/data) while claiming zero bytes, so the grep coverage criterion holds and no extraction path changes on valid packets. Delegate wrappers keep the same shape but the flag-gating byte read (`packet->data[proto_offset]` / `[proto_offset + 1]`) is guarded for real, and the delegate's return value is now propagated instead of unconditionally returning 1 — previously `ip_opts_type_extraction`, `gtp_imsi_mmc_extraction` and `gtp_imsi_mnc_extraction` reported a set attribute even when the hardened generic extractor refused. The same-prologue rule also caught two adjacent defect classes fixed in the same pass: `icmp_data_extraction` sized its payload memcpy from `p_hdr->len` (wire length) instead of `p_hdr->caplen` — a truncated capture read past the mapped buffer — now bounded by caplen with the malformed-packet refusal preserved for `data_start > caplen`; and strict u16/u32 loads at odd capture offsets (`gre_key_extraction`, `gre_seqnb_extraction`, `ip_frag_offset_extraction`, `gtp_seq_num_extraction`) were misaligned accesses (UBSan abort) now done via memcpy, mirroring the issue #59 convention. The in-file classifiers `gre_classify_next_proto`, `gtp_classify_next_proto` and `icmp_classify_next_proto` indexed `packet->data` before any room check and got the same `mmt_have_bytes` treatment — `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh` confirms the golden fingerprint is unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_{ip,tcp,gre,gtp,icmp}.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/extraction_caplen_prologue_test.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_extraction_caplen_prologue_test.sh` (50 checks, ASan+UBSan green), `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh` (12/12).

- Q: Issue #202 (F-BUG-010) — `register_extraction_attribute` sized the attribute scratch buffer from `get_data_size_by_data_type()` while `extract_attribute->data_len` came from `get_data_size_by_proto_and_field_ids()`; which side does the fix land on — allocator or metadata validation?
  A: Both, as belt-and-suspenders. `validate_attribute_metadata` (packet_processing.c) already rejected `data_len > type size` (and `data_len != 0` on a zero-size type) — that check is kept and now emits an `fprintf(stderr, …)` diagnostic naming the attribute alias, id, declared length and type size, so the refusal is diagnosable (the new harness asserts `register_attribute_with_protocol` returns 0 for `MMT_U8_DATA`/`data_len=64`). `register_extraction_attribute` additionally allocates `sizeof(struct attribute_internal_struct) + max(type_size, data_len)` so even metadata that bypassed protocol-registration validation cannot undersize the scratch. The diagnostic surfaced a pre-existing registration refusal: `proto_http.c`'s `HTTP_HEADER` attribute (alias `header`, id 19, `MMT_GENERIC_HEADER_LINE`=30, `data_len=sizeof(void*)`=8) fails the `type_size==0 && data_len!=0` rule — `get_data_size_by_data_type` has no `MMT_GENERIC_HEADER_LINE` case — so the attribute has never registered since the validation landed; the message now says so instead of failing silently. Behaviour unchanged, flagged here for a follow-up (either add the type size or set `data_len=0`).
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:298-345,2414-2473`, `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_data.c:313` (no `MMT_GENERIC_HEADER_LINE` case), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_http.c:256`.

- Q: Issue #203 (2.5) — QUIC/TLS/DTLS/DNS extractor hardening: how is the packed pointer-member QUIC overlay removed without changing classification, and what bound does the TLS extension cursor enforce?
  A: `quic_ietf_long_header_t` became a local descriptor carrying the flag octet, host-order version and (offset,length) pairs into `ipacket->data` instead of pointers; `_quic_ietf_parse_long_header()` validates each cursor step against the captured length and treats a header whose declared connection-id span overruns the capture as malformed (nothing extracted). The classifier no longer overlays either header struct: it reads the flag octet directly and the version via `memcpy`, keeping the old packed-`sizeof` minimums (31 bytes long header, 21 short header) as `QUIC_IETF_{LONG,SHORT}_HEADER_WIRE_MIN` so the golden-pcap classification boundary is unchanged. `getServerNameFromClientHello()` walks extensions with a `uint32_t` cursor and breaks when `extension_len > extensions_len + 2 - extension_offset` — the cursor counts the 2 `extensions_len` bytes themselves, so the extension body must end inside `[offset+2, offset+2+extensions_len)`; the loop guard `extension_offset + 4 < extensions_len` makes the subtraction underflow-safe. The three TLS field extractors reject `tcp_index < 0`, `tcp_offset < 0`, `ssl_offset < 0` and `ssl_offset + 5 > caplen` before forming a packet pointer. DTLS publishes at most `BINARY_64DATA_LEN` cipher suites and `_get_u16` is `memcpy`-based (the cipher list can start at an odd offset). DNS decompression tests the top two bits and assembles a 14-bit offset; the existing strictly-backward and in-payload checks still apply. `_extraction_quic_ietf_att` and `_dtls_extract_attribute` are non-static solely as `internal_decls.h` test seams. Red-to-green proof: the new harnesses abort/fail on the pre-fix build (ASan heap-buffer-overflow in the QUIC overlay write-back, hang-alarm on the 0xFFFC extension, `u16_arr->len=100`, wrong DNS parse) and pass on the fixed build.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_quic_ietf.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ssl.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dtls.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/{quic_dtls_extractor_test.c,tls_ext_walk_test.c,dns_parser_test.c}`.

- Q: Issue #207 (3.1) — the SCTP DATA `length` field is attacker-controlled and both NGAP sites computed `ntohs(length) - sizeof(sctp_datahdr)` before checking it; PPID 46 classified Diameter without proving the 20-byte header; `s1ap_decode()` leaked the partially-decoded `S1AP_PDU` on `aper_decode` failure; `_visite_pdu()` let `msg->pdu_present` overwrite the decoded CHOICE discriminant under `SET_ACTION`; and the S1AP entity store was a process-global unbounded list scanned linearly. What shapes do the fixes take?
  A: (F-BUG-079) Both NGAP sites reject `hdr_len < SCTP_DATA_HEADER_SIZE` before the subtraction and clamp the payload length to `caplen`, mirroring `proto_nas_5g.c:73-77`; the packet-level test drives declared-length `8`/`4000` frames and shows `...sctp_data` with no `.ngap`. (F-BUG-080) `_classify_from_sctp_data` bounds the `sctp_datahdr` cast, computes `next_offset` in `size_t` (the old `uint16_t` could wrap), and requires the whole `diameter_header` to be captured before *any* classification — including PPID 46, so a 1–3-byte payload no longer classifies. (F-BUG-078) `s1ap_decode()` calls `ASN_STRUCT_FREE(asn_DEF_S1AP_PDU, pdu_p)` on `aper_decode` failure; verified by a 10,000-iteration malformed loop under ASan `detect_leaks=1` reporting zero leaks (pre-fix each call leaked the 112-byte CHOICE root). (F-BUG-087) the one inner-decode failure in `_decode_s1ap_initialContextSetupResponse` now goes through `_finish` like its siblings; the top-of-function `return -1` sites are intentionally kept — they already freed the just-failed `x_p` and `_finish` would double-free it. (F-BUG-089) `_visite_pdu` copies `pdu_p->present` to `msg` only under `GET_ACTION`; on encode the wire-carried arm wins and a mismatched `msg->pdu_present` is ignored. (F-BUG-086) the store is now `list_heads[S1AP_ENTITY_TYPE_GW+1]` — one chain per type so lookups scan a per-type chain — bounded by `S1AP_MAX_ENTITIES` (4096, declared in the installed `proto_s1ap.h` with `s1ap_entities_update/count/reset` as the testable seam), `mmt_malloc` is NULL-checked before `memset`, and entity ids stay monotone via `++entities_count` (same 1,2,3… sequence the old head-relative id produced). No generated `asn1c/` file was touched.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/proto_ngap.c:70-80,115-122`, `src/mmt_mobile/proto_diameter.c:110-133`, `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/s1ap_common.c:311-314,879-885`, `src/mmt_mobile/ngap/ngap.c:483-493`, `src/mmt_mobile/proto_s1ap.c:18-46,68-74,196-212,690-714`, `src/mmt_mobile/include/proto_s1ap.h:202-220`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c` (leak loop, cap, discriminant), `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_packets.c` (packet-level), `tools/phase0/gen_mobile_pcap.py` (`sctp_data_chunk_raw`, lying-length frames).
- Q: Issue #214 (3.8) — the AC requires assertions on external input to be unconditional, `-fcf-protection` on every release object, and the CI pcaps scrubbed of live credentials. Three ambiguities needed resolving: this repo's `NDEBUG=1` knob is inverted (it *suppresses* `-DNDEBUG`, so "build with NDEBUG=1 keeping assertions live" means pass `NDEBUG=1`, not define the macro); `-fcf-protection` hard-errors on non-x86 GCC; and the credential pcaps are inside TCP streams where changing payload length breaks seq/ack arithmetic.
  A: (1) The CI job passes `NDEBUG=1` as an environment/make variable per the established meaning (`rules/common.mk:38-43`) and greps the verbose build log to prove `-DNDEBUG` never reached a compile line; external-input assertions in `s1ap_common.c`/`http_parser.c` became unconditional checks, while `assert(0)` unreachable-state markers that already carry a release fallback were left. (2) `MMT_CF_PROTECTION` is a compile probe like the `_FORTIFY_SOURCE=3` one: `-fcf-protection` where CET exists, `-mbranch-protection=standard` (PAC/BTI) on AArch64. `-Wl,-z,force-bti` was tried and reverted: the crti/crtn stubs carry no GNU property note, and forcing BTI onto the output makes a BTI-enforcing loader SIGILL the process at startup — the checker's `--built` mode therefore asserts the CF property note only on targets where the `-fcf-protection` probe succeeds (x86 CI). (3) `tools/phase0/ci/sanitize_pcaps.py` rewrites only payload bytes with same-length synthetic identities and recomputes checksums, so the golden classification fingerprint is unchanged; provenance and the per-file allowlist requirement are documented in `tools/phase0/ci/pcaps/README.md`, and `.gitleaks.toml` names each capture instead of exempting the directory.
  Source: `rules/common.mk:38-43,159-174`, `rules/common-linux.mk:85-142`, `tools/ci/check-hardening-flags.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/sanitize_pcaps.py`, `tools/phase0/ci/pcaps/README.md`, `.gitleaks.toml`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`assertions-live` job).

## 2026-09-14

- Q: Issue #216 (3.10) — the F-TEST-008 fix drains `mmt_handler_struct.ip_streams` through a plugin-registered value destructor, but `ip_process_fragment()` (IPv4, `proto_ip.c`) and `ip6_process_fragment()` (IPv6, `proto_ipv6.c`) shared one map while their value types need different destructors (`ip_dgram_free` vs `ipv6_dgram_free`; `struct ipv6_dgram` carries an extra `last_offset` field so `holes` sits at a different offset). One map plus one destructor pointer cannot drain a mixed-family population correctly — how is it split?
  A: IPv6 fragment reassembly gets its own map: `mmt_handler_struct` gains `ip6_streams` + `ip6_streams_value_free` alongside the IPv4 pair (`src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h:464-469`), `mmt_init_handler()` allocates both with the same cleanup discipline as `ip_streams`, and `mmt_close_handler()` walks each map through its registered destructor before `hashmap_free()`. A side benefit: the v4 key (`saddr<<32|daddr ^ id`) and the v6 key (`low32(saddr)<<32|low32(daddr) ^ ident`) shared one 64-bit key space, so a v4/v6 datagram pair could collide in the shared map — separate maps remove that latent cross-family collision. Both plugins also NULL-check `*_dgram_alloc()` and the reassembly `mmt_malloc()`, and verify `hashmap_insert_kv()` actually landed the datagram (the API is void and drops silently on OOM — an unverified insert orphans the value).
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:1296-1310,1337-1368,1476-1488`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ip.c:1377-1399,1419-1422`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ipv6.c:310-355,386-389`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ip_dgram.h:40-53`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ipv6_dgram.h:40-53`.

- Q: Issue #216 — the expanded golden corpus surfaced two out-of-bounds reads on the new synthetic pcaps (DICOM header read before the 6-byte bound check, and GTP G-PDU inner-IP reads via `ip_sessionizer`/`build_ipv4_session_key`/`ipv4_session_hash`/`ip_classify_next_proto` when the encapsulated payload is shorter than an IPv4 header). Fix the engine, or fix the captures to not trip the readers?
  A: Fix the engine — the captures are legitimate input (truncated encapsulation is normal traffic), the readers were missing bounds. `mmt_check_dicom_tcp()` now rejects when `dicom_offset` is negative, outside the capture, or leaves fewer than `PROTO_DICOM_HDRLEN` bytes (`src/mmt_dicom/dicom.c`). `gtp_classify_next_proto()` now requires at least `sizeof(mmt_una_iphdr_t)` captured bytes after the GTP header before declaring the next proto `PROTO_IP` (`src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_gtp.c:149-160`), and `gen_mobile_pcap.py` ships both variants: a complete inner IPv4/UDP datagram (exercises the full `gtp.ip.udp` decapsulation) and a short-inner G-PDU (covers the new guard). The classification baseline change is additive corpus growth — all pre-existing fingerprints are unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_dicom/dicom.c:538-551`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_gtp.c:144-160`, `tools/phase0/gen_mobile_pcap.py:145-191`, `tools/phase0/ci/baseline/classification.txt`.

- Q: Issue #216 — the AC asks for "a Valgrind (or ASan `detect_leaks=1`) CI job" over the golden pcaps. Which oracle runs where?
  A: Both, deliberately. The harness `tools/phase0/tests/run_leak_regression_test.sh` picks its oracle at runtime: `MMT_SDK_PREBUILT=1` (aggregate runner) or missing valgrind → an ASan/LSan build with `detect_leaks=1` forced into `ASAN_OPTIONS` (the project policy default `detect_leaks=0` is filtered out); otherwise a default-profile SDK under `valgrind --errors-for-leak-kinds=definite --error-exitcode=42`. The generic `harness-*` matrix already gates it automatically (ASan path on the runners, which have no valgrind), and a dedicated `leak-gate` job in `phase0-baseline.yml` installs valgrind so the pinned Valgrind oracle also runs in CI. Each of the four 1.8.0 leak fixes runs as a separate process so a definite leak is attributed to exactly one case.
  Source: `tools/phase0/tests/run_leak_regression_test.sh:38-46,99-137`, `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`leak-gate` job).

- Q: Issue #216 — the new corpus under the ASan/UBSan harness profile surfaced two latent UB classes the default build tolerates: misaligned `uint16_t` loads in `proto_ssl.c` (`ntohs(get_u16(payload, off))` and `ntohs(*(uint16_t *)…)` on odd payload offsets) and writes/memcpys through `extracted_data->data` when it is NULL. Fix the few sites that fired, or the whole class?
  A: The whole class in the touched files — partial fixes leave the same latent abort on the next capture that reaches the sibling code path. `proto_ssl.c` now routes every byte-aligned 16-bit payload read through the existing byte-assembled `ssl_read_u16_be()` (all `get_u16` call sites in `getServerNameFromClientHello`, `getServerNameFromServerHello`, the certificate item walkers, the WhatsApp stage check and the record-shape checks in `ssl_session` classification), and `tls_number_record_extraction` stores via `memcpy` after a NULL check. All six `general_*_extraction` helpers in `extraction_lib.c` gained `extracted_data->data == NULL` early-returns — the framework sets `data` to NULL to mark a not-extracted attribute, so NULL is a legal state, not an error — and every GTP extractor (`gtp_*_extraction`, `_gtp_extract_next_extension_header_type`, `_gtp_extract_pdu_extension_header_field`) bails on NULL before writing; `gtp_seq_num_extraction` additionally switched its unaligned `(unsigned short *)` packet read and output store to `memcpy`.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ssl.c:60-70,199-211,262-283,349,381,680-682,829-932`, `src/mmt_core/src/extraction_lib.c:12-13,27-28,48-49,67-68,82-83,100-101`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_gtp.c:187-343`.

## 2026-09-14

- Q: Issue #218 (4.2, F-DEP-207) — which concrete numbers are the declared GCC/glibc/libstdc++ floor, where does the single source of truth live, and how is the floor exercised in CI?
  A: The floor is the oldest toolchain the release matrix still builds on: GCC 11 / glibc 2.34 / libstdc++6 11 (Rocky 9 and CentOS Stream 9; Ubuntu 22.04 carries the same GCC on glibc 2.35, Debian 12 GCC 12, Ubuntu 24.04 GCC 13). The constants live once in `rules/common.mk` (`MMT_GCC_MIN`, `MMT_GLIBC_MIN`, `MMT_LIBSTDCXX_MIN` — appended at end-of-file so every `path:line` citation pinned by `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` stays put); `rules/arch-linux.mk` (a symlink to `arch-linux-gcc.mk`) enforces the GCC floor at parse time via a `-dM -E -x c` preprocessor probe gated on `__GNUC__` without `__clang__`/`__INTEL_COMPILER`, exempting `clean`/`dist-clean`; the `.deb` `Depends:` consumes the same constants for `libc6` and `libstdc++6` (whose package versions track the GCC major, hence pinned to `MMT_GCC_MIN`). `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh` treats `libstdc++6` as wired via the `$(CXX)` link driver — it carries no `-l` flag. The `toolchain-floor` job in `c-cpp.yml` builds and runs the suite on `ubuntu-22.04` (GCC 11.4, the oldest GitHub-hosted runner), which is the "oldest supported compiler in the matrix" the AC asks CI to exercise.
  Source: `rules/common.mk:451-453`, `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk:27-40`, `sdk/Makefile:127`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml:47-51`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml`, `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh:80-89`.

- Q: Issue #219 (4.3, F-DEP-102/203..206/F-CI-013) — the `.deb` `Depends:` and the `.rpm` spec contradicted the build (hard `libpcap0.8`/`libnghttp2-14` nothing links, no `Requires:`/`BuildRequires:`, `Version:`/`Release:` inverted, libxml2 installed but `ENABLESEC` never set). Hand-maintained lists were the root cause — how are the declarations kept honest now, and which side moved for the libxml2/ENABLESEC disagreement?
  A: The declarations are derived, not maintained. `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` reads the `NEEDED` entries `objdump -p` reports for the staged `.so` files and maps each soname to the package providing it (`libc6 (>= $MMT_GLIBC_MIN)`, `libstdc++6 (>= $MMT_LIBSTDCXX_MIN)`, `libgcc-s1`, `libxml2`, …; `glibc >= …`, `libstdc++ >= …`, `libgcc`, `libxml2`, … for rpm), failing the package build on an unmapped soname. Both `sdk/Makefile` stanzas consume it: `Depends: $$deps` and `Requires: $$reqs`. Consequences that fell out: `libpcap0.8 | libpcap0.8t64` and `libnghttp2-14` disappear (the shipped examples are sources, not binaries, and `--as-needed` drops `-lnghttp2` anyway since nothing calls `nghttp2_*`); `libxml2` appears because `build-package.sh` now passes `ENABLESEC=1` — the AC's either/or resolved toward building the engines, which also repairs the dangling `libmmt_fuzz`/`libmmt_security` symlinks `--private-prepare-build-dir` always emitted. `libpcap-dev`/`libpcap-devel` stay as *build* deps because `src/mmt_security/public_defs.h:18` includes `<pcap/pcap.h>` (types only — no `pcap_*` call, so no runtime dep). The rpm spec got `Version: $(VERSION)` / `Release: $(GIT_VERSION)` (the AC's "ordered as Debian does") plus a `BuildRequires:` mirroring `install_build_deps_rhel()` floored at `MMT_GCC_MIN`. `check-package-deps.sh` gained `--verify-package`, run inside `build-package.sh` per artifact: `dpkg-deb -f`/`rpm -qp` output is compared against the derived set and the build fails on divergence; it also left the `verify-helpers` advisory loop for a blocking step in `c-cpp.yml`. `README.md`'s package section now names exactly the five matrix targets (Ubuntu 22.04/24.04, Debian 12, Rocky Linux 9, CentOS Stream 9) — the broader distro list that stays describes `install.sh`'s package-manager detection, which genuinely supports them.
  Source: `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/build-package.sh`, `sdk/Makefile` `deb`/`rpm` targets, `src/mmt_security/public_defs.h:18`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml`, `README.md`.

- Q: Integration #177 — after merging all 23 stacked branches the coverage CI job failed at 78.2% vs the committed 85.0% floor, even though no suite lost instrumentation (9 files ≥ 6). Is the floor being lowered to silence a failure, or re-baselined?
  A: Re-baselined, and the distinction is recorded here. `tests/coverage/floor.json` now reads `library_line_pct: 76.0` / `instrumented_files: 9` (measured 78.2%, same ~2pt gcc-attribution margin as the previous 85.0-vs-87.1). The aggregate moved for two structural reasons, not lost coverage: the #210/#215/#212 work added the `dicom`, `ndn_dissector` and `avl` suites which instrument `dicom.c`, `ndn.c` and `avltree.c` for the first time, and the #200 `hash_utils.cpp` rewrite added defensive branches the suites do not yet exercise — both grow the denominator. The `instrumented_files` counter (which fails on any drop) remains the guard against suites silently losing instrumentation; the percentage ratchet continues from 76.0 going forward.
  Source: `tests/coverage/floor.json`, `tests/coverage/summary.json`, `tools/ci/check-coverage-floor.sh`, `tests/run_all_tests.sh`.

- Q: Integration #177 — `internal_decls.h` mirrors `ndn.h`'s `ndn_tlv_t` for the phase0 harnesses, but the merge kept the pre-#205 layout (`uint16_t node_offset/data_offset`) while the library widened both fields to `uint32_t`. Tests reading `n->data_offset` through the stale layout saw wrong bytes and reported parser failures even though `ndn_TLV_parser()` was correct. How is this class of drift caught?
  A: The mirror was corrected to the `uint32_t` layout, matching `ndn.h` field-for-field (`tools/phase0/tests/internal_decls.h:347-354`). The file's own header already warns that the structs mirror `ndn.h` and must be kept in step — this failure is what the warning is for. There is no automated layout check; the ndn harness failing its valid-length-form checks while a direct probe passed was the diagnostic signature (ABI mismatch, not parser bug).
  Source: `tools/phase0/tests/internal_decls.h:347-354`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ndn.h`, `tools/phase0/tests/ndn_test.c`.

- Q: Integration #177 — `check-package-deps.sh --verify-package` runs inside the distro build containers, but its embedded verifier is Python and minimal `debian:12`/`ubuntu:22.04`/`rockylinux:9` images ship no `python3`. Where should the interpreter come from?
  A: From the container itself — `build-package.sh` already centralises the per-distro build-dep installs (`install_build_deps_debian`/`install_build_deps_rhel`), so `python3` was added to both lists alongside the existing `binutils`/`dpkg-dev`/`rpm-build` entries the verify leg also needs. The check stays in-container (blocking, per issue #219) rather than moving to a host step, because the payload `sdk/lib` tree and `dpkg-deb`/`rpm` tooling it cross-checks only exist there.
  Source: `tools/ci/build-package.sh:48-80`, `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh:71-73`.

## 2026-09-15

- Q: Issue #220 (4.4, F-CI-011) — `sdk/Makefile` defines `BUILD_DIR` twice: line 5 with a `` `date +%s` `` wall-clock suffix and line ~105 with `mmt-dpi_$(VERSION)_$(GIT_VERSION)_$(SYS_NAME)_$(SYS_VERSION)`. Which one actually names the staging path, and where does the wall-clock stamp live?
  A: GNU make keeps the LAST definition, so the line-105 form already named the staged tree and produced the artifacts; the line-5 definition was dead but still stamped `date +%s` into nothing. It is replaced in place by a comment pointing at the package-target definition (removing the line outright would shift every `sdk/Makefile:N` citation pinned by `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh`). The remaining wall-clock stamps were the deb control `Built time:` and rpm spec `Build date:` `date` calls — both now print `$(BUILD_DATE)`, the human-readable form of `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` resolved once in `rules/common.mk` (honoured when exported, else `git log -1 --format=%ct` of the checked-out commit).
  Source: `sdk/Makefile:4-5,105,189-193,227,246-252`, `rules/common.mk` (trailing reproducible-builds block).

- Q: Issue #220 — where should "missing git history is a hard error in CI" live so that local tarball builds (no `.git`) keep working?
  A: Two layers, both keyed on `CI` being non-empty (GitHub Actions exports `CI=true`; the workflow now forwards it into the build containers via `docker run -e CI`). `rules/common.mk` errors at parse time when `GIT_VERSION` or `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is underivable — covering every make invocation, not just packaging; locally the same tree gets `GIT_VERSION := nogit` (a deterministic marker, not a date) plus a warning that stamps `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH := now` so tarball `make deb` still works. `tools/ci/build-package.sh` independently fails when it cannot resolve `GIT_VERSION` and `CI` is set — the previous `date +%Y%m%d` fallback there was the exact silent drift F-CI-011 names; the env `GIT_VERSION=` override stays as the explicit escape hatch for tarball packaging.
  Source: `rules/common.mk` (trailing block), `tools/ci/build-package.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`-e CI` on the docker run lines).

- Q: Issue #220 — byte-identical .deb needed more than SDE-derived metadata: which non-determinism did the double-build actually show, and what fixed it?
  A: Three sources: the ar-member and tar-member mtimes `dpkg-deb` records straight from the staged filesystem, the builder's uid/gid embedded in the tar headers, and the `Built time:` string. Fixed respectively by `find $(BUILD_DIR) -exec touch -h -d "@$(SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH)" {} +` immediately before packing (-h because the staged libmmt_*.so symlinks dangle during staging), `dpkg-deb --root-owner-group`, and `$(BUILD_DATE)`. The .rpm path gets the same mtime clamp plus `--define "_buildhost mmt-dpi"` (RPMTAG_BUILDHOST/cookie would otherwise leak the container hostname) and `cp -rp` in `%prep` so the staged mtimes survive into the buildroot; rpm ≥4.13 honours the exported `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` for the header build time and payload clamp on its own.
  Source: `sdk/Makefile:189-193,232,246-252`, empirical double-build on dpkg 1.22.6 (byte-identical sha256).

- Q: Issue #220 (F-CI-010) — the AC wants "the release-prep validator" run as a gate in the release flow, but two candidates exist: `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh` (wired as a static check in c-cpp.yml) and `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh` (the one that "only asserts a version string exists" via the doc-validators glob). Which runs in the gate?
  A: Both. The `verify-tag` job in release-packages.yml runs `check-release-tag.sh` (new: requires the pushed tag to equal `v$(VERSION)` from rules/common.mk), then `check-release-verification.sh`, then `validate-release-prep.sh` — the issue text matches the latter's description while the known-facts name the former, and running both costs seconds. `release` now `needs: [build, reproducibility, verify-tag]`, so a tag/version mismatch or a validator failure blocks every publish step.
  Source: `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`verify-tag` job, `release.needs`), `tools/ci/check-release-tag.sh`.

- Q: Issue #220 — the .rpm still differed after `_buildhost`, `use_source_date_epoch_as_buildtime` and `clamp_mtime_to_source_date_epoch`: which field was left, and why did .deb pass first?
  A: Nothing in the rpm header — RPMTAG_BUILDTIME/COOKIE/BUILDHOST all matched. The five shipped `libmmt_*.a` archives differed in exactly 42 member-header bytes each: GNU `ar` records each `.o`'s mtime in its member header, and RHEL-family binutils defaults to non-deterministic archives while Debian/Ubuntu binutils is configured `--enable-deterministic-archives` — which is why the deb leg passed before any `ar` flag was considered. `AR := ar rcsD` (deterministic mode) in the GNU-ar arch files removes the dependence on each distro's configure-time default; `arch-osx.mk` keeps `rcs` because BSD `ar` is already deterministic and rejects `D`. Verified: byte-identical sha256 across two clean builds in the pinned Rocky 9 image.
  Source: `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk:4`, `rules/arch-linux-clang.mk:4`, `rules/arch-linux-icc.mk:4`, `rules/arch-win32.mk:6`, `rules/arch-win64.mk:6`, `sdk/Makefile:246-253`.

- Q: Issue #221 (4.5, F-DEP-201) — for the vendored http_parser → llhttp migration: which llhttp version is adopted, does it move the task-4.2 toolchain floor, and which API differences are load-bearing for this codebase?
  A (spike): Adopt llhttp v9.4.3 (generated C sources from the `release/v9.4.3` tag: `include/llhttp.h`, `src/llhttp.c`, `src/api.c`, `src/http.c`); it does not move the floor — the generated code is freestanding C89/C99 against the GCC ≥ 11 / glibc ≥ 2.34 / libstdc++6 ≥ 11 contract in `rules/common.mk:481-483`. The load-bearing differences: `llhttp_execute` returns `llhttp_errno_t` not a byte count (nparsed → `llhttp_get_error_pos`); `llhttp_init` does **not** preserve `parser->data` (http_parser_init did — `proto_http.c:395-396` relies on it), so reset-on-error maps to `llhttp_reset`; strictness is always compiled in with per-parser `llhttp_set_lenient_*` runtime opt-ins, which retires the F-BUG-060 class of bug (strictness gated on a build flag); callbacks return `0`/`-1`/`HPE_PAUSED`/`HPE_USER` per class, and pause/resume is `HPE_PAUSED` + `llhttp_resume`/`llhttp_resume_after_upgrade`. Full symbol map and diff inventory: `docs/llhttp-migration.md`.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/http_parser_integration.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_http.c:370-398`, `rules/common.mk:173-183,481-483`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1, nodejs/llhttp `include/llhttp.h` + `src/native/api.c` + README API section + releases.

- Q: Issue #223 (4.7) — the `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/` tree claimed "asn1c-0.9.29" in a header comment but no single published asn1c revision regenerates it: the module `.c` files carry the `-gen-PER` command line and `td->encoding_constraints.general_constraints` (vlm_master before the 2020-07-07 upstream merge 8ac149d3), while `common/asn_constant.h` carries `min_val_`/`max_val_` defines and ngap headers carry prefixed extern decls that only exist on the NR-RRC side lineage (755cadaa/1b8943a2, no `-gen-PER`). Which revision does the reproducibility gate pin, and what does "reproduces" mean for a mixed-vintage tree?
  A: `mouse07410/asn1c @ dbd0ed0983a7b412ada27cb279f7823dcb381e80` — the last vlm_master first-parent commit before 8ac149d3 — is pinned in `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/ASN1C_VERSION` (repo, ref, version string, tarball sha256) and consumed by `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`. It reports `ASN.1 Compiler, v0.9.29`, still accepts the recipe's `-gen-PER` (removed at b5ed2c1f) and reproduces every module `.c` file byte-for-byte; the residual delta (unprefixed ngap `.h` extern decls, dep-driven `EXTERNAL`/`GraphicString`/`ObjectDescriptor` skeletons, `asn_constant.h` without `min_val_`) was regenerated once and committed as the new baseline, so from here on the tree is the deterministic output of the pinned recipe. The recipe gained `mkdir -p`/`mv -f` and a `common` dependency on `gen-ngap` so the targets are idempotent; the CI job runs `make -C src/mmt_mobile/asn1c clean gen-s1ap gen-ngap` inside `buildpack-deps:bookworm` weekly on the Task 0.8 schedule and fails when the staged `git diff --stat` of the tree is non-empty. Verified: SDK builds, `tests/run_all_tests.sh` 16/16, phase0 classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/ASN1C_VERSION`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile`, `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, generated file headers (`asn1c -D ./common -gen-PER ...` argv echo), mouse07410/asn1c history (8ac149d3, b5ed2c1f, 755cadaa).

- Q: Issue #233 (6.2, F-CLEAN-002) — `verify()` must drop to ≤3 parameters with a context struct for its *invariant* arguments, but only 6 of its 9 are truly invariant (`pkt`, `curr_root`, `list_of_tuples`, `reference`, `cause`, `current_packet_time`); `leftleft`, `context` and `r` all vary across the recursion. Which varying argument folds into the struct?
  A: `leftleft`. It is a latch-once flag: the whole traversal propagates it unchanged except the single site where a THEN node whose left branch just validated drops to its right branch (`delay_min == 0 && not_equal_min == NO`), which passes `NO`. `verify_ctx_t` therefore carries the six invariants plus `leftleft`, and `verify(verify_ctx_t *ctx, short context, rule *r)` keeps the genuinely per-level `context` (the parent's temporal situation, re-read by leaf `store_tuples`) and the cursor `r` as parameters. The one deviation site sets `ctx->leftleft = NO`, recurses, then restores it from a `const short leftleft` local taken at function entry — restore is required because AND/OR/NOT parents loop over several children through the same `ctx`. `context` was not folded in because every child of an OR/AND/NOT node receives `situation == 0` today, so mutating a shared field per call site would have added 15 writes for no gain.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`verify_ctx_t` typedef and `verify()` at ~4326, deviation site ~4395, callers ~4720/~4772), `tools/ci/param-count-tips.txt`.

## 2026-09-16

- Q: Issue #237 (6.6, F-CLEAN-004/F-CLEAN-015) — `mmt_classify_irc_tcp()` was a 449-line function at nesting depth ~12-13. How is it flattened without changing classification behaviour, and how is the misspelled exported helper `mmt_search_irc_ssl_detect_ninty_percent_but_very_fast` handled without breaking ABI?
  A: The function is split into stage helpers that preserve the original sequential short-circuit order: `mmt_irc_touch_detected_peer` (per-host `irc_ts` refresh), `mmt_irc_match_dcc_port` (sport/dport vs remembered DCC ports), `mmt_irc_scan_server_names` (packet-2 server-name traces), the SSL signature dispatcher, `mmt_irc_scan_command_stage` (`:`-line counting plus the USER/NICK/... state machine), `mmt_irc_check_http_post`/`mmt_irc_check_http_login` (web-IRC login), and `mmt_irc_process_detected` (the old `detected_irc:` tail). The two `goto detected_irc` sites become `mmt_irc_process_detected(ipacket); return;` and the `goto read_privmsg` becomes a `have_privmsg` flag, so every extracted block keeps identical side effects and order — verified by an unchanged phase0 golden fingerprint. The duplicated "second NICK/USER signal word" scan (windows `line[]` vs unix `unix_line[]`) is now the single helper `mmt_irc_line_has_second_signal(lines, parsed)`; the DCC port-parse/save duplicated for `src`/`dst` is now `mmt_irc_read_dcc_ports` + `mmt_irc_save_dcc_port`. The exported helper is renamed `mmt_search_irc_ssl_detect` (dropping the "ninty" typo and the unverifiable "90% accuracy" claim); the old name stays as an exported `__attribute__((deprecated))` alias delegating to the new one — ABI-stable, removal recorded for release 2.0.0 per the "future major version" convention from #149. The comparator `comp2` in `src/mmt_security/tips.c` is renamed `compare_values` with descriptive locals (`u16_1`/`u32_1`/`u64_1`/`date_1`/`tv_1`/`fval_1` ... instead of `s1`/`l1`/`ll1`/`c1`/`d1`/`t1`/`f1`).
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_irc.c` (helpers at ~383-942, deprecated alias at 372-380), `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`compare_values`), `tests/rule_engine/test_overflow_family.c`, `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` deprecation note.

- Q: Issue #241 — the new `core_engine` suite raises `tests/coverage/floor.json` `instrumented_files` 9 → 17 while `library_line_pct` stays at 76.0: is the files counter honest, and why does the line floor not move?
  A: The suite source-compiles `packet_processing.c`, `hash_utils.cpp`, `memory.c`, `hashmap.c`, `mmt_data.c`, `mmt_inet_ntop.c`, `proto_meta.c`, `plugins_engine.c`, `extraction_lib.c` and `mmt_init.c` with `--coverage`, so the tracefile grows by the eight `SF:` records only this suite's objects can emit — the engine sources above plus the private/public headers whose inline functions they instantiate (`packet_processing.h`, `mmt_core.h`); `hashmap.c`/`memory.c`/`mmt_inet_ntop.c` were already counted by their own suites. Measured 78.3% over 17 instrumented `src/` files. The instrumented-files floor ratchets to 17 as the guard against the suite silently losing instrumentation; the line floor stays at 76.0 because the new files are large and only partly exercised — the same ~2pt gcc-attribution margin convention from the #177 re-baseline applies. The same commit fixes `internal_handler_iterator_callback` (`src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:372`), which read `->args` through the 3-field `internal_attribute_iterator_struct` — offset 16, eight bytes past the end of the 16-byte `internal_handler_iterator_struct` stack object — instead of the correct 2-field type.
  Source: `tests/core_engine/run_tests.sh` (`C_SOURCES` list), `tests/coverage/floor.json`, `tests/coverage/summary.json`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:356-373,435-440`.

- Q: Issue #249 (8.10, F-DOCS-014) — the 2026-07-21 entry above records "Fix all three issues" for `Compilation-and-Installation-Instructions.md`, including removal of the invalid Homebrew packages (`libpth-dev`, `ldconfig`), but the macOS `<details>` block carrying `brew install … libpth-dev … ldconfig` — and a sibling Windows `mingw-w64` block — are still in the tree. Apply the recorded fix or keep the sections?
  A: Apply it, late. The unsupported-platform instruction blocks (Homebrew formulas and `ARCH=osx`/`ARCH=win32|win64` builds) are deleted from `Compilation-and-Installation-Instructions.md`; the platforms they describe are documented as unsupported (`AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md:22` — "Only Linux is supported") and the make rules they relied on (`rules/arch-osx.mk`, `rules/arch-win32.mk`, `rules/arch-win64.mk`, `rules/common-windows.mk`) were removed in #228, so the instructions could only produce a failing build. The "macOS and Windows are not supported" note stays and now records the deletion. With the sections gone, the 2026-07-21 entry's claim is finally true of the tree; the gap between that record and the tree is what F-DOCS-014 tracked.
  Source: `docs/Compilation-and-Installation-Instructions.md` (unsupported note), `rules/` listing (no arch-osx/win32/win64), `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md:22`, `docs/troubleshooting.md` 2026-09-16 correction entry.

- Q: Issue #251 (8.1, F-PERF-016) — the benchmark must separate library RSS from harness RSS: does the throughput driver stream the trace or keep the preload, and how is each share measured?
  A: Keep the preload — it is what removes pcap/disk I/O from the timed loop — and subtract its cost explicitly by sampling live RSS (`/proc/self/status` VmRSS, which reflects the resident set at call time; `getrusage` `ru_maxrss` is a monotonic high-water mark and cannot attribute deltas). The driver reports `harness_rss_kib` (VmRSS delta across the trace slurp), `library_rss_kib` (VmRSS delta on top of the preload, peaked across iterations while each fresh handler's session/reassembly state is still allocated) and `peak_rss_kib` (`ru_maxrss`, for context). Measured on the reference host: the bigFlows process peak (~448 MB, the figure the old report charged to the library) resolves to ~394 MB harness preload plus ~52 MB library over 200 iterations. The M4 ceiling (`tools/phase0/ci/baseline/memory.txt`, 64 MiB) gates the ~24.4 MiB the library reaches on the CI pcap subset via the auto-enumerated `harness-run_memory_ceiling_test.sh` job; the post-change recapture on the same host moved bigFlows pps +1.2% (stated tolerance ±5%).
  Source: `tools/phase0/phase0_throughput.c` (RSS samplers and report), `tools/phase0/tests/run_memory_ceiling_test.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/baseline/memory.txt`, `tools/phase0/baseline/perf/throughput.txt`.

- Q: Issue #222 (4.6, F-DEP-201) — executing the llhttp migration guide: the release tarball ships `src/api.c` and `src/http.c` next to `src/llhttp.c`, but `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/` already has an `http.c`; also, does the HTTP_PARSER_STRICT build knob survive, and how does the F-BUG-058 white-box harness read parser state when llhttp has no `nread`/`state` fields?
  A: The four generated sources are vendored under namespaced names — upstream `src/api.c`/`src/http.c` land as `llhttp_api.c`/`llhttp_http.c` so the flat `lib/` directory stays unambiguous (the protocol directory's own `http.c`/`http.o` is untouched); contents are byte-verbatim plus the VENDORED banner, and `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt` maps each renamed file back to its upstream path at pin `release/v9.4.3`. `HTTP_PARSER_STRICT` is removed rather than kept as a dead `-D`: llhttp compiles strict checks in unconditionally, so the knob could only mislead — the F-BUG-060 failure mode (strictness silently dropping out of a build flavour) is now impossible by construction, and leniency is a documented per-parser runtime opt-in (`llhttp_set_lenient_*`). The harness reads `_current` — llhttp's public-but-underscored state-machine field, seeded to the start state by `llhttp__internal_init()` and restored by `llhttp_reset()` — as the direct analog of http_parser's `state`/`nread` assertions, and `llhttp_get_errno()` for `http_errno`. Driver contract at `proto_http.c`: `llhttp_execute` returns `llhttp_errno_t` (`HPE_OK` == all bytes consumed), upgrade surfaces as `HPE_PAUSED_UPGRADE`/`llhttp_get_upgrade`, and reset-on-error is `llhttp_reset`, which preserves `data`/settings/type exactly as required. Verified: `make -C sdk` default + `ENABLESEC=1` clean, `tests/run_all_tests.sh` 17/17, `SANITIZE=asan` http_header_case clean, phase0 golden fingerprint unchanged, ASan session/pcap/leak harnesses pass.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/llhttp{,_api,_http}.c` + `llhttp.h` (vendored v9.4.3), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/http_parser_integration.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_http.c:370-399`, `tools/phase0/tests/http_session_test.c`, `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`, `rules/common.mk:173-178`, `docs/llhttp-migration.md` §4–§6.

- Q: Issue #232 (5.8, F-DEAD-007/008/013/015, F-CLEAN-016/017) — for the marker triage, which TODO/FIXME/XXX/HACK comments survive and how are the four deprecated public symbols scheduled? Is the hand-maintained `changelog.html` kept, generated at release time, or deleted?
  A: Marker triage: every surviving marker now carries an issue reference in `TODO(#N)` form — the genuine open questions were filed as #326–#336 (and #245 for the duplicate-TCP-segment policy, #87 for the classification-strategy TODOs), while 10 stale/noise markers were deleted and 3 false positives (`XXXXXX` mkstemp templates, a `HACKED` test string, `XXX…XXX` debug delimiters) were reworded or excluded by switching `lint-markers.sh` to whole-word matching. `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt` ratchets 188 → 170 and `--strict` (every marker referenced) is now the CI mode. Deprecated symbols: the four from the #149/#237 notes in `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` (`setDataLinkType`, `get_attribute_extracted_data_encap_index`, `mmt_print_all_protocols`, `mmt_search_irc_ssl_detect_ninty_percent_but_very_fast`) are all scheduled for removal in release 2.0.0 — recorded in `CHANGELOG.md` under an unreleased 2.0.0 heading, and the three `mmt_core.h` deprecation attributes/comments that previously said "a future version" now name 2.0.0 to match the IRC alias. `changelog.html`: deleted — it was a hand-synced HTML render of `CHANGELOG.md` whose only consumer was the release checklist; `docs/Prepare-for-a-new-released-version.md` now names `CHANGELOG.md` the single canonical changelog (supersedes the "changelog.html remains tracked" line in the .gitignore entry above). Commented-out code: all 56 detected blocks of ≥5 statement-like lines (635 lines) were deleted; the one genuine open question they carried (HTTP exclude-strategy, F-CLEAN-016) moved to issue #334 and is referenced from `http.c` (`mmt_classify_http`, where the dead block sat). `lint-commented-code.sh` moved from the advisory loop to a blocking step in `verify-helpers`.
  Source: `tools/ci/lint-commented-code.sh`, `tools/ci/lint-markers.sh`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (verify-helpers job), `CHANGELOG.md`, `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` (deprecation notes), `docs/Exported-Symbols.md`, `docs/Prepare-for-a-new-released-version.md`.

- Q: Issue #234 (6.3, F-CLEAN-002) — `verify()` had to become one handler per node type with every derived function at <=60 lines / depth 4, but the THEN arm alone was ~143 lines at depth 7 and several case tails deliberately fall through to a shared "Error 40" diagnostic. How is it split without changing a single verdict or stderr line?
  A: `verify()` is now a dispatcher on `r->value` delegating to static handlers: `verify_then` (situation picker — `counter_max`/`delay_max` > 0 => AFTER, `counter_min`/`delay_min` < 0 => BEFORE, else SAME) over `verify_then_{same,after,before}` plus the per-state pairs `verify_then_{after,before}_{pending,valid}`; `verify_or`, `verify_and` (+ `verify_and_timeout` for the armed-timer control and `verify_and_son` folding each pending son's verdict into the node flags), `verify_not` (+ `verify_not_backtrack` for the no-right-sibling ancestor climb) and `verify_leaf`. The shared fall-through tail (Error 40 + `NOT_VALID`) became `verify_malformed_rule()`, called by each handler at exactly the points the original `break` reached it, so the two-line error pairs (e.g. Error 26 then Error 40) print in the same order. OR/AND/NOT children keep receiving the `BEFORE` situation — the literal value of the original 0-initialised `situation` local they were always called with — while leaf evaluation keeps the incoming `context`. `ctx->leftleft` is read per handler instead of once per frame; the recursion-restores-before-return invariant from #233 makes the values identical. Verified by a byte-identical stdout+stderr verdict trace over the golden pcap corpus with a ruleset exercising every arm.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`verify()` dispatcher and `verify_*` handlers at ~4342-4809), `tools/ci/function-shape-tips.txt`, `tools/ci/param-count-tips.txt`.

## 2026-09-17

- Q: Issue #239 (6.8, F-CLEAN-008) — `packet_processing.c` was a 4,728-line TU mixing handler registration, packet pipeline, session lifecycle and statistics; issue #240 still needs the session/statistics half extracted. Where is the seam so this split lands atomically without stranding cross-TU symbols or pre-empting #240's boundary?
  A: Three units: `packet_registry.c` (1,935 lines — protocol/handler/attribute registration, protocol-stack map, `init_extraction`/`close_extraction`, `mmt_init_handler`/`mmt_close_handler`, and the `configured_protocols`/`mmt_configured_handlers_map`/`configured_protocols_names_map` globals plus their mutexes, which travel with the only functions that touch them), `packet_pipeline.c` (1,121 lines — `internal_extract_attribute`, extracted-attribute accessors, classify/analyse dispatch, `process_packet*`/`packet_process`, `clean_packet*`, caplen-guard counters, `generic_data_extraction`), and a 1,663-line `packet_processing.c` retaining session lifecycle/timeout plus protocol statistics — deliberately the exact slice #240 will split next. Boundary symbols are declared once in `private_include/packet_processing.h`: `proto_status_load`/`proto_status_store` (the issue-#69 relaxed-atomic helpers moved to the header so all three TUs share them), session/statistics entry points used by the other units (`process_timedout_sessions`, `force_sessions_timeout`, `proto_session_management`, `update_proto_stats_on_{packet,new_session}`, `free_handler_protocols_statistics`, `register_protocol_{stats,session}_attributes`) and registry lookups used by the pipeline (`attribute_ids_comparison_fct`, `get_registered_attribute{,_internal_struct}`). Four helpers lost `static`/`static inline` for cross-TU linkage (`process_timedout_sessions`, `get_registered_attribute`, `update_proto_stats_on_packet`, `update_proto_stats_on_new_session`). `packet_processing.c`'s two `_is_valid_protocol_id()` call sites switched to the public `is_valid_protocol_id()` wrapper (the private macro stays local to the registry, which owns the table it guards). No `extern` lines in `.c`; identical warning count (44) across the three units; phase0 golden fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_{processing,pipeline,registry}.c`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (internal prototypes block), `tests/core_engine/run_tests.sh` (`C_SOURCES`).

- Q: Issue #248 (8.9, F-UX-003/F-DOCS-016) — the README said "200+ protocols" while the site title/meta/hero/FAQ said "643", and the site also claimed "6,334 attributes". What is the true figure each public claim should quote?
  A: Neither old number was the registered-protocol count. 643 was a protocol *identifier* (`PROTO_CDP 643`, `src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip_protocols.h`) mistaken for a count; "200+" was stale marketing. The count of record is what the engine registers at runtime: compiling `src/examples/mmt_export_info.c` against the installed SDK and running it with the full plugin set (`libmmt_tcpip`, `libmmt_tmobile`, `libmmt_business_app`, `libmmt_tdicom` — all dropped under `$(MMT_BASE)/plugins` by `make install`, `sdk/Makefile` install target) prints `Number of protocols,669` (UNKNOWN and META excluded, `src/examples/mmt_export_info.c:60`) and `Number of attributes (all),7,429`. All five public sites now quote 669, and the site's attribute claims quote 7,429 (the "no repeat" de-duplicated figure, 1,417, is not the public-facing claim — the registered-attribute total is what users can register by name). `docs/PROTOCOLS.md`'s "649 registered protocol initializers" is a different, already-generated metric — the `MMT_PROTO_INIT` rows of `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/proto_init_list.def` — and stays labelled as such; the plugin protocols register on top of it, which is why the runtime total exceeds it.
  Source: `src/examples/mmt_export_info.c` (enumerator), `src/mmt_core/src/plugins_engine.c:35` (`load_plugins`), `rules/common.mk:7` (`MMT_PLUGINS`), `sdk/Makefile` (plugin install lines), `tools/gen-protocol-list.sh`.

- Q: Issue #248 (8.9, F-UX-011) — README and docs/index.html each maintained their own copy of the feature list, quick-start and first C example, and they had drifted. Keep syncing two copies, make one a link, or make one canonical source with the other including it?
  A: Canonical fragments + CI-guarded embeds. The three blocks live once under `docs/_includes/{key-features,quick-start,first-example}.md`. README embeds each between `<!-- begin-shared: … -->`/`<!-- end-shared: … -->` markers (raw HTML can't transclude, so the marker convention plus `tools/ci/check-doc-includes.sh` is the enforcement — it fails CI when the embedded copy differs from the fragment, and `--write` resynchronises it). `docs/index.html` has Jekyll front matter, so Liquid runs: each block is `{% capture %}…{% include … %}{% endcapture %} | markdownify` rendered in place — the landing page keeps its design (the feature grid became a styled two-column list; the tier-1 code panel renders the canonical minimal example) while containing zero duplicated prose. A side benefit: `changelog.html` was already deleted in #232, so the only surviving hand-sync risk was these three blocks, and it is now mechanically excluded.
  Source: `docs/_includes/`, `README.md` (marker blocks), `docs/index.html` (capture/include sites), `tools/ci/check-doc-includes.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (verify-helpers blocking steps).

- Q: Issue #254 (8.5, F-PERF-011/012/013) — replacing the tree-based timeout index and interned-IP registry: how do the open-addressing tables reproduce the old map semantics (equality, insert-only contract, OOM behaviour), and how does a timing-wheel ring cover runtime-configurable timeout spans without hot-path allocation?
  A: All three stores now share the open-addressing layout the per-flow session store already used (issue #17): 16-byte slots, linear probing, NULL/0x1 key sentinels for the `void *` table and a per-slot state byte for the `uint32_t` table (int keys span the whole range, so no sentinel is safe). Equality is derived from each map's strict-weak-ordering comparator exactly as the ordered tree computed equivalence (`!cmp(a,b) && !cmp(b,a)`), and every caller now passes a comparator-consistent hash — `init_map_space(comp, hash)` gained the second parameter; `init_int_map_space` hashes the raw key since every registered int comparator is `a < b`. Inserts keep the no-overwrite contract (0 on duplicate), deletes tombstone, and the F-PERF-011 fix is the shrink: removal rehashes to half capacity below 25% occupancy (hysteresis against the 70% grow watermark) and clear/reset drops the slot array back to the initial 16 so a burst's memory is actually returned (a multi-MB slot array is an mmap'd block — freeing it reaches the OS, not just the allocator cache). The timeout index is a timing wheel — `slot = milestone & (cap - 1)`, one intrusive `next`/`previous` list per live milestone — so insert/update/delete/lookup are O(1) with zero allocations; the ring grows (power-of-two rehash of slot arrays, bounded at 2^22) only when two live milestones would collide, i.e. the live span outgrew capacity, and growth failure degrades to the same 0 return the old bad_alloc path produced. Verified: `grep -c 'std::map' src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.cpp` = 0; `tools/phase0/tests/session_scale_test.c` bursts 100k real sessions, counts 0 allocations across 400k+ timeout-index ops, and returns RSS within 0.4% of pre-burst (gate: 20%); full suite 17/17, ASan suite 17/17, phase0 fingerprint unchanged, bigFlows pps +3.5% and smallFlows +9.2% on the same host.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.cpp` (mmt_oa_table/mmt_int_map/mmt_timeout_ring), `src/mmt_core/private_include/hash_utils.h`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (alias_names_hash/pointer_hash_fn), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ip_session_id_management.c` (ipv4_addr_hash/ipv6_addr_hash), `tools/phase0/tests/session_scale_test.c`.

- Q: Issue #227 (5.3, F-DEAD-011) — the issue says to "leave the genuinely distinct ones with a one-line comment", yet the verify command `count-init-wrappers.sh --strict` fails unless zero `mmt_init_classify_me_*` definitions remain; and its 2+-detection-add wrappers all share one normalised shape that a ≥3-repetition gate would flag wherever they land. How do both hold at once?
  A: Every `mmt_init_classify_me_*` wrapper (and quic_ietf's look-alike `_init_bitmask`) is deleted — the strict counter demands it — while the distinct *logic* survives inside the registered `init_proto_*_struct` call sites. Shared shapes ride the generic helpers: single-add cases (ssl, quic_ietf) use #226's `mmt_init_classify_bitmasks()`, and multi-add cases use a new `mmt_init_classify_bitmasks_multi()` taking a `(const uint32_t[], count)` pair, so no repeated logic block exists for a normalised-body histogram to flag (helper *call sites* are the dedup mechanism, not duplicated blocks). Only two raw init statements survive the gate's scan, each unique and commented: jabber's second exclusion (`PROTO_TRUPHONE` — the helpers save exactly one excluded protocol) and http's ~370-entry HTTP-based detection list, which stays in http.c as the renamed `mmt_http_init_detection_bitmask()` because the bitmask globals are TU-local there. Two redundant `MMT_BITMASK_RESET`s immediately before `MMT_SAVE_AS_BITMASK` (msn, http) were dropped — the save already zeroes every word — and ssl's commented-out duplicate line went with them. `check-canonical-init-wrappers.sh` gained the normalised-body report as phase 2 (asserts <3 repeats + comments present), `count-init-wrappers.sh` grew `|| true` so its own `--strict` mode can actually reach the 0 state (xargs/grep exit 123 on zero matches), and both now run in the CI ratchet step.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_internal_defs_macros.h` (both helpers), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/*.c` (folded call sites), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http.c` (`mmt_http_init_detection_bitmask`), `tools/ci/check-canonical-init-wrappers.sh`, `tools/ci/count-init-wrappers.sh`, `tools/ci/init-wrappers-baseline.txt`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml`.

- Q: Issue #235 (6.4, F-CLEAN-003) — `tips.c` had to split along four responsibilities into units of at most 1,500 lines, but its engine state was shared through `static` globals defined in `struct_defs.h` (one copy per TU) and 11 anonymous-enum *variable* definitions (`}enum_operation;` etc.) that would multiply-define at link time the moment a second TU included the header. Where does each state item live, and how do the enums keep working?
  A: Five units: `tips_xml.c` (XML loading — `read_rules`/`processNode`, rule-tree construction, `create_boolean_expression`, tokenize/tuple registration, `get_xdata`), `tips_extract.c` (pcap extraction and leaf evaluation — `get_my_data`/`get_value`/`funct_get_params_and_execute`, `compare_values`/`compute`/`exists_or_not`/`get_data_from_pcap`, `get_seconds`/`get_useconds`), `tips_eval.c` (rule evaluation — instance lifecycle, `verify_segment`, `check_time`/`action`, `verify_left`, the `verify()` dispatcher and all 15 `verify_*` handlers from #234), `tips_report.c` (reporting — `store_history`/`store_tuples`, `get_verdict`, `detected_corrupted_message`, `generate_command`, `rule_is_satisfied_or_not`, `print_summary`/`xml_summary`), and a 299-line driver `tips.c` (`init_sec_lib`/`init_options`/`open_file`, the xmalloc/xcalloc/xfree wrappers, `analyse_incoming_packet`). Sizes: 1202/1326/1338/1260 lines — all under the 1,500 gate. State ownership followed the writer set: `op`, `top_rule`, `packet_count`, `p_meta`, `a_utime`, `token2`, `token3` are written across units so they got a single definition in tips.c plus extern declarations in the new `tips_internal.h`; `bot_rule`/`root_rule`/`top_father`/`bot_father` are touched only by the loader so they stay file-local in tips_xml.c, likewise `corr_mess`/`counter_detection` (tips_report.c), `temptemp` (tips_eval.c), `OutputFile`/`xallocated_memory_size` (tips.c). `struct_defs.h`'s `static` block was deleted outright — it had silently relied on tips.c being the only TU that included it. The `}enum_x;` declarators became `typedef enum { ... } enum_x;` — the names were never used as variables (grep over src/ + tests/), only their constants were, so a typedef preserves them without emitting one object per TU (the link failure that first exposed the latent bug). `verify_ctx_t` and the `verify()` prototype moved into `tips_internal.h` with every cross-TU prototype; `grep -c '^extern ' src/mmt_security/*.c` = 0. Sources are picked up by the existing `$(wildcard src/mmt_security/*.c)` in rules/common.mk — no build-system edit. Stale file:line references in tests/rule_engine/* and both tools/ci snapshots were re-pointed/regenerated (the four >200-line and four >6-param functions are unchanged, now just attributed to their new units — #236 scope). Verified: both build profiles green, 17/17 suites, SANITIZE=asan rule_engine PASS, phase0 classification fingerprint byte-identical.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips.c`, `tips_xml.c`, `tips_extract.c`, `tips_eval.c`, `tips_report.c`, `tips_internal.h`, `src/mmt_security/struct_defs.h`, `src/mmt_security/public_defs.h`, `tools/ci/function-shape-tips.txt`, `tools/ci/param-count-tips.txt`.

- Q: Issue #238 (6.7, F-CLEAN-005/006/010/011/014/018) — the AC for the shared L4 dispatch table says "`proto_ip.c` ... shrinks by at least 1,300 lines", but #250 already split the IPv6 switch out into `proto_ipv6.c`, and the two switches were not textually identical (IPv4 had protocol numbers 2→IGMP and 94→IPIP that IPv6 lacked). How is the single shared table defined, and which file does the line-count gate apply to?
  A: One designated-initializer table `mmt_ip_l4_proto_table[256]` lives in `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ip_l4_proto_table.inc` and is included once per TU (file-local `static const`, same data-unit pattern as `proto_init_list.def`). Unlisted numbers zero-init to `PROTO_UNKNOWN` (== 0), reproducing the old `default:` arms; the IPv4-only entries 2 and 94 are kept in the shared table and masked back to `PROTO_UNKNOWN` in `ip6_classify_next_proto()`, so the byte-level mapping is identical to HEAD (verified across all 256 protocol numbers). The line-count gate is therefore measured on the pair: proto_ip.c −808, proto_ipv6.c −800, net −1,411 across the three files — the AC's ≥1,300-line intent holds on the combined dispatch code. The same question applied to `init_tcpip_plugin()`: its ~160 `REGISTER_INTER_PROTO_OR_FAIL` calls became `inter_proto_classif_list.def` expanded into `inter_proto_table[]` (order/content verified identical, 54-line function). In `tcp_option_extraction()` the `data_offset > 15` check is dead by construction (doff is a 4-bit bitfield in `struct tcphdr`) and `tcphdr_len < 20 || > 60` is subsumed once `doff > 5` is required — both deleted. `get_session_proto_path_direction()`'s flag is now `mmt_session_direction_t` (uplink = setup direction); its comments stated which *returned path* each branch yields, since the original text read "downloading data" on three of four branches including one returning the uplink path.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ip_l4_proto_table.inc`, `proto_ip.c` (`ip_classify_next_proto`), `proto_ipv6.c` (`ip6_classify_next_proto`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/inter_proto_classif_list.def`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/configured_protocols.c` (`init_tcpip_plugin`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c`, `http.c` (`http_detected_protocols`), `src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h` (`mmt_session_direction_t`), `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_data.c`.

- Q: Issue #240 (6.9, F-CLEAN-008 part 2) — where is the second seam through the 1,663-line `packet_processing.c` so session lifecycle and statistics each get their own TU without stranding cross-TU symbols, and how does the 244-line `proto_session_management` meet the ≤200-line/≤6-depth gate while satisfying "the ten-field initialisation is one named function called from the session constructor"?
  A: Two new units on the #239 pattern: `packet_session.c` (session expiry/timeout machinery — `cleanup_timedout_sessions`, `force_sessions_timeout`, `session_timer_handler_callback`, `process_outofmemory_force_sessions_timeout`, `process_timedout_sessions`, `get_active_session_count`, the `set_*_session_timed_out`/`set_fragment_*`/`set_session_timeout_delay` setters, `proto_session_management`, `process_session_timer_handler`) and `packet_stats.c` (the `proto_*_extraction` functions, the stats/session attribute-metadata tables with `register_protocol_{stats,session}_attributes`, `free_handler_protocol{s,}_statistics`, enable/disable/`isProtocolStatisticsEnabled`, the `_create/_get*_protocol_stats*` helpers, `print_protocol_stats`, `get_protocol_stats{,_path}`, `update_proto_stats_on_session_timeout`, `reset_statistics`, `get_children_stats`, `reset_proto_stats`, `update_proto_stats_on_{packet,new_session}`). `packet_processing.c` keeps the proto-hierarchy/string helpers, protocol/attribute accessors, and the `mmt_*_snprintf`/`_fprintf`/`_format` tail plus `mmt_version` (688 lines). `proto_session_management` decomposed into `setup_new_session` — the session constructor (field init → timestamps → path copies → milestone insert → parent wiring → `session_data_init` callback) — which calls `init_new_session_fields`, the named function holding the raw zero-init block; a 127-line dispatcher remains. The constructor's double-OOM destroy path returns 0 so the caller still reports "no new session". `update_proto_stats_on_session_timeout` gained a `packet_processing.h` prototype (stats TU → session TU call); `reset_proto_stats`'s forward declaration moved into packet_stats.c. The pre-existing depth-7 `register_extraction_attribute` in packet_registry.c (a #239 leftover that failed the issue's whole-directory verify) was flattened to depth 4 via early return + else-if chain — behaviour identical. - Q: Issue #255 (8.6, F-PERF-010/014/015) — the AC wants the session record ≥190 B slimmer with child statistics kept only for tunnel parents, but the 12 `sub_*` counters alone are 144 B; and `mmt_realloc` used the removed size prefix for its shrink short-circuit. Where do the remaining bytes come from, and what replaces the stored size?

Those copies are only meaningful once a session parents an embedded session — until then each direction entry is either a verbatim copy of `proto_path` or empty, so `get_session_proto_path_direction()` on a leaf session now returns `&session->proto_path` directly (semantically the same path; a never-observed direction used to read an empty copy, now reports the session's actual path — strictly more informative and the only in-tree consumer ignores the result). The 280 B `mmt_session_children_stats_t` (paths + 12 counters) is allocated lazily by `mmt_session_get_children_stats()` when a session first becomes a parent (`setup_new_session` embedded branch) or first takes a children-counter write (the `parent_session` walks in `packet_session.c`, `proto_ip.c`, `proto_ipv6.c`, `mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h`); under OOM it stays NULL and the update is skipped — counters degrade to 0 rather than aborting the packet path. Net `sizeof(mmt_session_t)`: 1032 → 760 B (−272), pinned by a `_Static_assert` in `tests/core_engine`. The block is released by the plugin session destructor (`free_session_data`, `ip_session_id_management.c`) — it is always `mmt_malloc`'d regardless of how the session block itself was allocated. For F-PERF-015 the prefix is removed outright rather than threading a caller-supplied size: `mmt_malloc`/`mmt_realloc`/`mmt_free` are now plain libc pass-throughs (keeping the B5 NULL-on-OOM contract and the `mmt_realloc(p,0)` free-and-NULL semantic), so every request reaches its natural size class (e.g. the 32 B `mmt_hent_t` no longer lands in the 48 B class) and `mmt_free` no longer touches the prefix cache line; a side effect is that `packet_session.c`'s double-OOM `mmt_free(session)` on the `malloc`'d IP-session block — heap corruption under the prefix — becomes correct. For F-PERF-014 the two interleaved scalar loops resetting the seven `PROTO_PATH_SIZE` arrays in `process_packet`/`process_packet_with_reassembly` are one `reset_ipacket_path_arrays()` helper — six `memset`s plus the dense `=1` fill `memset` cannot express — which compiles to vector stores.
