## 2026-09-17

- Q: Issue #245 (8.3, F-PERF-003/004/005/006 + F-BUG-038) — bounding TCP reassembly while keeping the extracted image identical: how do segment ordering, duplicates, overlaps and sequence wrap behave once a consumed prefix can no longer be re-emitted, and how does the flattened buffer stay under a per-flow ceiling?
  A: The pending store is a seq-sorted doubly-linked list carved from a reclaimable bump-block chain (`mmt_segblk_t`, tcp_segment.h) — each block counts its live carved bytes so a fully consumed block is freed mid-flow instead of pinning memory to teardown (the old per-flow arena could only be released wholesale). In-order arrivals append through a per-direction tail pointer and strictly-reversed arrivals prepend at head — both O(1), so `tcp_seg_insert()`'s walk only runs for genuinely interior reorders and is bounded by the ceiling-limited pending window (instrumented via `mmt_tcp_reasm_insert_visits()`). Ordering is wrap-aware (`tcp_seq_before` — signed 32-bit subtraction) so a stream crossing 2^32 keeps appending instead of re-sorting. The per-direction flattened image (`mmt_tcp_reasm_t.image[]`) is a persistent buffer grown geometrically and returned as-is: extraction drains only newly pending segments, so each payload byte is memcpy'd exactly once (`mmt_tcp_reasm_bytes_moved()`) and unchanged bytes are never recopied (the old path reallocated + recopied the whole list per read). `mmt_session_t.tcp_reasm` is a lazily-allocated extension — sessions without TCP payload keep one NULL pointer. The bound is `mmt_handler->tcp_reassembly_limit` (default `MMT_TCP_REASSEMBLY_LIMIT_DEFAULT` = 4 MiB, `set_tcp_reassembly_limit()`, 0 restores): `live` = pending carves + image bytes never exceeds it — segments are refused when the carve would overflow and counted via `mmt_tcp_reasm_bytes_dropped()`. Two deliberate semantic resolutions: (1) once bytes are emitted into the image the consumed prefix is gone — a segment whose whole range lies below the emitted frontier (a late retransmission) is dropped rather than spliced mid-image, while a *partially* overlapping segment is still appended in full, preserving the pre-#245 sorted-concat output for overlapping ranges byte-for-byte; (2) `ipacket->internal_packet` in reassembly mode now aliases the shared per-protocol-context packet exactly like the non-reassembly path (the ~6.7 KB per-packet malloc+memset is gone — F-PERF-003), so a packet held past its callback sees scratch state overwritten by later packets — the same contract the non-reassembly `current_ipacket` always had; the pooled `mmt_ipacket_slot_t` still gives a held packet its own ipacket + copy buffer until `mmt_drop_packet()`. Per-packet protocol-offset snapshots moved from a per-layer heap `proto_hierarchy_t` into the ipacket's embedded `internal_proto_headers_offset` (F-BUG-002's owned flag is never set on a pooled packet; the free guard stays as defence). Verified: both build profiles green, 17/17 suites (default + ASan; the rule_engine TSan failure reproduces on a clean tree — pre-existing), phase0 golden fingerprint byte-identical, valgrind clean, harness: 2048-packet in-order stream 9,446 → 617 ns/pkt, 2 packet allocations total, 0 list visits, bytes moved == stream bytes.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c` (offer/drain/image helpers + extraction rewrite), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/tcp_segment.c` + `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/tcp_segment.h` (mmt_segblk store, wrap helpers, instrumented insert), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_{ip,ipv6}.c` (shared context packet), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (ipacket pool + counters), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_session.c` (embedded offsets, setter, lazy init), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (handler init + pool drain), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (mmt_tcp_reasm_t, mmt_ipacket_slot_t, stat decls), `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` (set_tcp_reassembly_limit, limit macro), `tools/phase0/tests/{tcp_reassembly_perf_test.c,run_tcp_reassembly_perf_test.sh}`.

Typed flags: `enum_yes` (leftleft, reg_tuple, first_time, skip_refs, `my_strstr`/`get_time_value` reverse+direct), `enum_found` (`found` out-param), `enum_operation` (operator/action/ope), `enum_operation_type` (context/situation), `enum_print` (state, print_option, verdict selectors), `enum_type_rule_node`, `enum_clean` (`create_father`'s clean). Remaining `short` params are genuine numbers (`event_id`, `size`, `depth`, `jump`/`ref` out-pointers); `short` *return* types carrying verdict codes (`event_found`, `action`, `check_time`, `init_time`) were left as-is — the AC gates parameter flags, not result codes. `get_data_from_pcap`'s local `short found = 0` became `enum_found found = NOT_FOUND` — equivalent because `funct_get_params_and_execute` writes `*found` on every path that returns non-NULL, and the single no-write path (`xmalloc` failure) returns NULL, which forces the caller's error branch regardless of the initial value. ## 2026-09-21

- Q: Issue #252 (8.2, F-PERF-002/017) — the AC demands "0 classifier checkers per packet on an already-classified TCP flow" *and* an unchanged golden fingerprint, but the checker chain (`mmt_check_ssl`, `mmt_check_ftp`, ...) is also the flow's per-packet protocol engine (stage machines, SNI/hostname service detection via `mmt_int_*_add_connection`). How can the walk be skipped without losing mid-flow service classification?
  A: It cannot — a first implementation that skipped the whole walk on a converged layer collapsed every `http.*`/`ssl.*` service line in the fingerprint (e.g. `ssl.google` 23,917 → 0). The resolution keeps the winning checker but reaches it in O(1): `mmt_session_t.proto_checkers[PROTO_PATH_SIZE]` records the checker node that owns each layer, and `proto_packet_classify_next` dispatches straight to it when `proto_path[index+1]` is already converged — the ~98 sibling probes that were no-ops on a committed flow never run. The counter semantics follow: `mmt_classify_checker_calls` counts *chain probes* (0 on converged layers — the AC), while `mmt_classify_direct_calls` separately counts the dispatches so the per-packet engine calls stay visible. Owner attribution cannot rely on the MATCHED verdict alone — FTP-class checkers classify via `add_connection` while returning a non-matched status — so two plugin funnels stamp `ipacket->mmt_classifier_claim` from `ipacket->mmt_current_classifier` (set by the pipeline around every `classify_me` call): `mmt_change_internal_flow_packet_protocol` (the deepest tcpip stack-mutation funnel, covering `mmt_internal_add_connection` and the four direct `mmt_change_internal_flow_packet_protocol` callers) and `set_classified_proto` (plugins that write the path inside `classify_me`, e.g. mmt_mobile). The slot is only (re)written when `proto_path[index+1]` actually changes — first detection and reclassification — so a converged layer's owner cannot be displaced by a later checker's correlated-protocol claim; a path change to a different protocol clears/refreshes the owner, and a layer converged with no identifiable owner (port/IP fallback, external `set_classified_proto`) keeps the full walk. Sessions are initialised with the slots NULLed in `init_new_session_fields`; checker nodes outlive sessions (handler lifetime), so no dangling. Verified: phase0 golden fingerprint byte-identical (20-pcap CI gate PASS), valgrind clean, harness `classified_flow_fastpath` PASS — 459 classified-flow packets, 0 IP/TCP/UDP chain probes, 925 direct dispatches; same-machine throughput bigFlows 954,524 → 1,059,993–1,108,923 pps (+11–16%), smallFlows 1,486,122 → 1,928,127–2,821,570 pps (+30–90%) — the host's absolute pps drifts ~2×, so BEFORE/AFTER were captured minutes apart on the same tree state.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`proto_packet_classify_next` dispatch + claim bookkeeping, `set_classified_proto` claim hook, counters), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`mmt_session_t.proto_checkers`, frozen attribute/handler arrays), `src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h` (`ipacket` claim channel), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_session.c` (slot init), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h` (funnel claim), `tools/phase0/tests/classified_flow_fastpath_test.c`.

  A: F-PERF-007: the dense `leaves[256]` node (2,056 B x ~9,091 nodes, built in `_constructor`) became a sparse node of 24 B plus a sorted `(char -> child)` edge array (16 B/edge) built lazily by `_ensure_tree()` on the first `get_proto_id_by_hostname()` call — the per-packet read is one acquire load, the one-time build is serialised on a `PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER` mutex (the packet_registry pattern), the root is published with a release store, and a failed build is latched (`_host_name_trie_init_failed`) so OOM keeps the old permanent-disable-with-one-log behaviour instead of retrying per packet. Lookup and the '.child' fallback walk the sorted edges with early-out; `_free_tree_node` frees edges+nodes under the same lock from the destructor. `mmt_hostname_trie_resident_bytes()` exports accounted node+edge bytes (always exported — it moves only at build/free time): measured 0 before first lookup, 488,552 B after (vs the 4 MiB bound), VmRSS delta 676 KiB, valgrind 0 bytes in use at exit. The FNV-1a key hashes (13/37 serially-dependent multiplies) became independent per-word multiplies plus a one-step finalizer (v4: 4 multiply ops, 2-deep chain; v6: 6 ops, 2-deep) — source-counted by the harness — and the table's fmix64 mixer is documented static-inline at the call site. Stats tripwires `mmt_oa_equal_call_count`/`mmt_oa_comp_call_count` (assert/sanitizer builds, relaxed atomics, always-exported weak-linkable accessors — the packet_pipeline counter contract) let the harness prove zero comparator calls on the equal path and identical answers on the fallback. `init_session_map_space` grew a third parameter; all in-tree callers updated (packet_registry, test_fi_engine, test_session_table, the new harness); `init_map_space` passes NULL and keeps the two-call path. ## 2026-09-21

- Q: Issue #87 — "configurable DPI profiles with per-level protocol toggles" bundles four detection levers (inspection depth, application-protocol/hostname detection, port-based detection, IP-range detection), but three of the four already exist as per-handler flags and the fourth has no natural single gate. Where does the profile model live so a depth cap is honoured by every classifier without editing each protocol file, and what do the presets pin?
  A: The levers map onto the existing handler fields — `hostname_classify` (application/hostname), `port_classify` (+ `port_classify_payload_confirm` untouched), `ip_address_classify` — plus one new field `classification_max_depth` (default `PROTO_PATH_SIZE - 1` = unlimited) on `mmt_handler_t`. Depth is enforced centrally in `packet_pipeline.c`, not per protocol: `proto_packet_classify_next()` skips the checker walk and post-classification when the next `proto_path` index would exceed the cap (that skip is the CPU saving), and `set_classified_proto()` refuses new appends beyond the cap while still allowing updates/reclassification of in-bound slots — so out-of-band writers honour the same bound and the `TODO(#87)` markers in `proto_tcp.c`/`proto_udp.c` are resolved. `pre_classify` header parsing still runs, so transport sessions and attribute extraction keep working under a shallow cap (index 0 = META, 1 = link, 2 = network, 3 = transport, 4 = first app layer). `mmt_dpi_profile_t` is an opt-in struct applied by `mmt_apply_dpi_profile`/`mmt_apply_dpi_profile_by_name` to individual handlers; four presets pin the stock configuration (`default`, also the implicit base), the L2–L4 structural-only configuration (`minimal`, depth 3, all heuristics off), depth 5 with heuristics but no port hint (`balanced`), and everything on including the port hint (`full`). Operator profiles come from `mmt_load_dpi_profiles_file()` or `MMT_DPI_PROFILES_FILE` — `<name> <depth> <app> <port> <ip-range>` lines, `#` comments, case-insensitive names, collisions with predefined/loaded names and out-of-range toggles rejected, up to 32 customs — and `MMT_DPI_PROFILE` selects a name at `mmt_init_handler()` time (unknown name logs and keeps defaults). Zero behaviour change without opt-in: the golden fingerprint is byte-identical. New suite `dpi_profiles` (suite 20) covers presets, custom-file parsing/rejection paths, env selection, and both depth gates.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/dpi_profiles.c` (registry/parser/apply), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (depth gates in `proto_packet_classify_next` + `set_classified_proto`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (handler field init + env hookup), `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` (public API + presets), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`classification_max_depth`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/{proto_tcp,proto_udp}.c` (resolved TODO(#87)), `tests/dpi_profiles/`, `docs/DPI-Profiles.md`.

- Q: Issue #327 (F-DEAD-008) — `proto_packet_analyze` overwrote `retval` with every analyser's return, so a later `MMT_CONTINUE` erased an earlier `MMT_DROP`/`MMT_SKIP` verdict and the post-analysis return was discarded entirely. Classification-side markers were verified-correct rather than changed: checker verdicts are already tested via `MMT_CLASSIFY_MATCHED_MASK` (winner stops the walk), `proto_packet_classify_next`'s exit code is the `post_classify` verdict (falsy mapped to `MMT_SKIP` by the caller), and the classification-depth threshold the marker asked for is `classification_max_depth` (issue #87). `init_protocol_struct` now initialises every `protocol_t` field explicitly (belt-and-suspenders — the struct is memset at `init_extraction`, and `mmt_init_handler` enables `data_analyser.status`/`classify_next.status` per registered protocol, so the 0-inits match the previous effective state). ## 2026-09-22

- Q: Issue #371 — what does "exact-release-SHA correctness" concretely require, and which evidence can the publish job trust?
  A: Two evidence classes, asserted by the new `release-gates` job before `release` runs. Same-run evidence arrives through the `needs` context (results of `build` — the package matrix carrying the installed-consumer check — `reproducibility` and `verify-tag`); commit evidence is queried from the check runs recorded on the candidate SHA (`unit-tests`, `sanitizer-tests`, `ASan/UBSan profile compiles`, `Golden classification fingerprint unchanged`/`classification-gate`). Verdicts are fail-closed: missing, queued/in-progress-only, failed, skipped, cancelled, neutral or wrong-SHA evidence is ineligible, and per check-run NAME the newest completed run wins — required because a tag push re-triggers `c-cpp.yml` (legs still in flight when the gate runs) and rebase-merges leave same-SHA check runs from both the PR and the main push (observed: two `build` runs at `45ae46e3`, failure then success). Check runs are matched by name with a `name (` matrix-suffix rule because GitHub names unnamed matrix legs `<job> (<values>)`; the classification job is matched by display name with the job id as fallback. `phase0-baseline.yml` does not trigger on tag pushes, so its evidence must come from an earlier run at the SHA — a tag on a commit that never ran the gates is correctly ineligible.
  Source: `tools/ci/check-release-gates.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`release-gates` job, `release.needs`), `tools/ci/tests/test-release-gates.py`, `gh api repos/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/commits/45ae46e3/check-runs` (duplicate `build` runs, matrix-suffixed names).

## 2026-09-22

- Q: Issue #375 (F-BUG-002) — how should `udp_pre_classification_function` reconcile the UDP `len` field with the enclosing IP payload bound, and what does `udp->len == 0` mean per family?
  A: `payload_packet_len` is now `max(0, MIN(udp_len, l4_packet_len) - 8)` — the UDP header's declared datagram length bounds the payload inside the enclosing IP payload (IPv4 `tot_len` via `l4_packet_len`, IPv6 `payload_len` net of extension headers — newly applied to `l4_packet_len` for UDP, so over-captured tails past `payload_len` are not L4 data), and the pre-existing caplen clamp (`F-BUG-107`/#195) still bounds reads to captured bytes last. Three resolved edge cases: `udp_len == 0` over IPv4 keeps the enclosing bound (kernel semantics — "rest of the datagram"); `udp_len == 0` over IPv6 is legal only in a jumbogram (`payload_len == 0`, RFC 2675) and keeps the captured bound, while a non-jumbo IPv6 packet declaring it is malformed and exposes zero payload; the `udp->len` field itself is only read when `l4_offset + 8 <= caplen`, so a corrupt offset past the capture can never be dereferenced (the earlier code never dereferenced `packet->udp` at all). Session `data_byte_volume`/`data_packet_count` (`mmt_connection_tracking`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h:463-489`) consequently count only declared UDP payload, which is the intended behaviour change behind "trailing captured bytes cannot affect extraction/detection". Verified: new `parser_boundaries` suite (unit reproducer + `packet_process` API path) green default and ASan/UBSan, full suite run, golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_udp.c:22-45,65-97`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h:463-489`, `tests/parser_boundaries/` (`run_tests.sh`, `test_udp_bounds_unit.c`, `test_udp_payload_bounds.c`), `tools/phase0/tests/caplen_clamp_test.c` (API-path pattern reused).

## 2026-09-22

- Q: Issue #376 (F-BUG-001 follow-up) — how does the central caplen guard separate an extractor's wire extent from the attribute's output capacity (`data_len`)?
  A: `data_len` is the capacity of the attribute's output scratch (`register_extraction_attribute()` sizes the buffer `max(type_size, data_len)`, `packet_registry.c:1613-1640`), not the number of wire bytes the extractor reads — conflating the two made `internal_extract_attribute()` refuse `DTLS_CLIENT_HELLO_CIPHER_SUITE` (132-byte `mmt_u16_array_t` result) on a valid 67-byte record and `ICMP_DATA` (1028-byte buffer) on ordinary packets. The guard (`src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c:190-222`) now checks the *wire extent*: `general_byte_to_byte_extraction()` is the one stock extractor whose contract memcpy()s exactly `data_len` wire bytes at `position_in_packet`, so for it the full `proto_offset + position_in_packet + data_len <= caplen` extent stays required; every other extractor self-bounds (the `mmt_have_bytes()` caplen prologues), so the central floor is the declared start inside the capture — `proto_offset + max(position_in_packet,0) + 1 <= caplen`, which also keeps POSITION_NOT_KNOWN (#193/F-BUG-032) on the same floor. Alternative considered and rejected: a `wire_len` field on `attribute_metadata_t` — touches the public plugin ABI and every positional initializer while adding no information the extraction-function dispatch does not already carry. The #193 harness's two "declared extent past caplen is refused" cases encoded the conflation and were re-purposed to pin the separation (output capacity past caplen now *reaches* the self-bounding extractor), plus byte-copier controls on both sides of the floor; `tests/parser_boundaries` gained the `dtls` fixture (unit: `internal_extract_attribute` + real `_dtls_extract_attribute` over crafted records; API: `mmt_init_handler`/`packet_process` with a registered attribute handler — valid 67-byte ClientHello extracts cipher 0x1301, truncated fields reject, 100-suite list clamps to the 64-entry array, direct and normal paths agree). Verified: `run_tests.sh dtls` default and ASan/UBSan, `run_position_unknown_guard_test.sh` and `run_extraction_caplen_prologue_test.sh` (corpus `unvalidated=0`), golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c:183-236`, `src/mmt_core/src/extraction_lib.c:9-22` (the byte-copy contract), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c:1605-1640`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dtls.c:105-233`, `tools/phase0/tests/position_unknown_guard_test.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/{test_dtls_wire_extent_unit.c,test_dtls_wire_extent_api.c,run_tests.sh}`.

## 2026-09-22

- Q: Issue #377 (F-BUG-003) — how should the SOA answer parser advance past the two rdata names, and why did `real_length`/`is_ref` misplace the mailbox and the five 32-bit fields?
  A: By consumed wire length, measured independently of the decoded value: the new `dns_name_wire_length()` walks the label encoding at the rdata position only — each literal label counts its length byte plus content, the root terminator counts 1, a compression pointer counts exactly 2 — never following pointer targets (they live earlier in the message, outside the record). The old code advanced by `real_length`, which is one byte short for terminator-ended names (the terminator node returns NULL and is never counted) and, via an `is_ref` shortcut that is dead code anyway — `dns_extract_name_value()` never sets `is_ref` on the value struct — counted a pure pointer as 1 byte, not 2; a literal primary name therefore started the mailbox walk on its own terminator and a compressed one inside the pointer's low byte, shifting the serial/refresh/retry/expire/minTTL block. Names and fields are now bounded by the declared rdata extent (`data_length` clamped to the captured remainder), so a truncated name or a short RDLENGTH rejects the record (`data` NULL) or withholds the integer block instead of reading the next record's bytes; a pointer whose target fails to decode still counts its 2 wire bytes, keeping the record aligned with an empty decoded name — the two functions deliberately differ there. The same `real_length + 1` convention in `dns_extract_queries()`/`dns_extract_answers()` is exact for terminator-ended and pure-pointer names but one byte long for mixed names (literal labels + pointer); left unchanged here — recorded as residual risk for a follow-up since the issue scopes to SOA and those paths feed no test fixture in this suite. Verified: new `dns-soa` fixture in `tests/parser_boundaries` (7 packet/API fixtures: literal+pointer headline `a`/`b`/42, mixed `x.b`, compressed primary, self-pointer, out-of-range target, short RDLENGTH with trailing capture, truncated name) — 25/25 checks pass default and ASan/UBSan; golden classification fingerprint unchanged (SOA runs in attribute extraction, post-classification).
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c:27-59` (`dns_name_wire_length`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c:572-637` (SOA case), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c:341-411` (real_length accumulation), `tests/parser_boundaries/{test_dns_soa_consumed_bytes.c,run_tests.sh}`.

- Q: Issue #378 (F-BUG-004) — how should the TXT answer parser treat its rdata, and what should `dns_answer_t->data` hold for a multi-string record?
  A: A TXT rdata is a sequence of `<character-string>` fields — a length byte plus that many content bytes — that must tile the declared RDLENGTH; the old code read only the first field and bounded it by `payload_end`, so a string-length byte near the rdata end absorbed the following record's captured bytes (RDLENGTH=1 + length 3 read the next record's `abc`). The `case 16` walk now bounds every field by `rdata_end = data_anwser_payload + min(data_length, captured remainder)` — the same clamp pattern as the SOA fix — and rejects the record (`data` NULL) when any field's declared length runs past it; `a_length` still advances by RDLENGTH, so the next record stays aligned. `data` concatenates all field contents into one NUL-terminated string — the usual multi-string TXT reading (RFC 7208 §3.3) — instead of silently returning only the first field; single-string records are unaffected. Alternative considered and rejected: keep first-field-only extraction — it fixes the confinement bug but discards valid rdata content and leaves later fields unvalidated, contradicting the issue's "sequential TXT fields" wording. TXT rdata carries no domain names, so no message-wide bound for compression is needed there — `dns_extract_name_value()` keeps `payload_end` for the PTR/NS/CNAME/MX/SOA paths that legitimately follow pointers to earlier message bytes. Verified: new `dns-txt` fixture in `tests/parser_boundaries` (10 packet/API fixtures: RDLENGTH-short headline with trailing `abc`, malformed-first + valid-second, single/empty/multi/multi+empty strings, truncated mid-sequence field with trailing capture, declared-length-past-RDLENGTH inside capture, consecutive records, RDLENGTH=0) — green default and ASan/UBSan; golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c:527-566` (TXT case), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c:541-543` (rdata clamp), `tests/parser_boundaries/{test_dns_txt_rdata_bounds.c,run_tests.sh}`.

## 2026-09-22

- Q: Issue #379 (F-PERF-001) — how should the interned-IPv6-address store hash all 16 address bytes, be keyed per handler, and bound collision work?
  A: Three coordinated changes. (1) `ipv6_addr_hash()` folded `w[0] ^ (w[1] << 1)` — the shift dropped `w[1]`'s top bit so 1 of the 128 address bits never reached the bucket index, and the XOR let correlated differences cancel into the same bucket; both 64-bit words now feed independent multiplies plus a one-step finalizer (same shape as `ipv6_session_hash()`, still consistent with `ipv6_addr_comp`'s memcmp equivalence). (2) `mmt_oa_table` gained a `seed` field mixed into `hash_of()` (`mmt_mix64(hash(k) ^ seed)`), drawn per table from a process-local counter decorrelated through fmix64 — two maps are keyed independently (a collision set computed for one handler's table does not transfer) while staying deterministic across runs because table creation order is fixed, which the golden classification fingerprint and reproducible tests rely on. (3) `mmt_oa_insert`'s probe loop is now explicitly bounded by `cap` (matching find/update/remove) and fails when a full scan finds no EMPTY terminator — unreachable under the `used < cap` invariant, so it is defense-in-depth, not a semantic change. Per-operation work is therefore at most O(cap) probes = 2·cap comparator calls even under forced collisions; the seed is the mitigation, the probe ceiling is the guarantee. Alternatives rejected: changing `generic_hash_fct` to take a seed (touches every registered hash across the plugin ABI for no benefit — the table is the only consumer that can hold per-handler state); seeding `mmt_int_map` too (its keys are internal ids, not attacker traffic — out of scope); a per-probe comparator-count budget enforced inside the table (the load-factor/resize contract already keeps clusters short — a hard cap on probe length would silently drop live entries). Verified: new `resource_bounds` suite (`ipv6-hash` fixture, 10,532 checks — 128/128 bits change the hash, old-fold pair separates, two tables carry different seeds, 2,048 distinct addresses preserved on both, real `get_ip6_id`/`findID6` path, 4,797/84,607 comparator calls at 1,024/16,384 vs the 751,360/200,273,920 budgets, 17.6x growth vs the 24x limit, forced-collision ops each ≤ 2·cap, saturated-table insert fails bounded) — green default and ASan/UBSan; `DEFAULT_SUITES` grew 25 → 26 with AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md and the CITATIONS registry reconciled; golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ip_session_id_management.c:42-63`, `src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.cpp:57-65,85-103,194-218,256-279`, `tests/resource_bounds/{run_tests.sh,ipv6_addr_fns.c,test_ipv6_hash_bounds.cpp}`, `tests/run_all_tests.sh:155-182`.

## 2026-09-23

- Q: Issue #380 (F-PERF-002) — how does TCP reassembly keep duplicate offers allocation-free, and what does the per-flow `tcp_reassembly_limit` bound?
  A: Two coordinated changes. (1) `tcp_reasm_offer()` now finds the insert position — tail append, tail/head equality, head prepend, or an interior `tcp_seg_locate()` walk (the `tcp_seg_insert()` walk without a node, one visit per node examined, so the #245 visit accounting is unchanged) — BEFORE carving; a duplicate sequence number is dropped and counted once without touching the block chain. Previously the carve came first and the release of a duplicate only decremented the block's live count, so every duplicate burned head-block bump space: 1,024 retained one-byte segments each followed by 169 duplicates allocated 1,024 blocks (16,809,984 B, 1,025 allocator calls) while `live` stayed ~98 KB; now 7 blocks (114,912 B, 8 calls — the same as without duplicates). This also settles the #245 duplicate-policy TODO: the first segment wins. (2) The limit now bounds reserved storage, not content: `mmt_tcp_reasm_t.reserved` = Σ(block header + cap) + both image capacities, maintained by `mmt_segblk_carve/release/free_all` and the image realloc; `live` stays the content gauge (`content <= reserved <= limit`). Admission requires `reserved (+ any new block) + owed[0] + owed[1] <= limit`, where `owed[d] = max(0, image_len + pending_len - image_cap)` is the image growth still needed to flatten the direction's pending bytes; a new block is right-sized to the remaining room (never below the aligned carve), and an emptied head block that the carve does not fit is freed first and credited to that room (otherwise it stayed behind the new head, reserved until teardown), and image growth (x4 geometric) is clamped to `cap_max = limit - (reserved - image_cap[dir]) - owed[!dir]`. Review follow-up: the speculative part of a growth step — beyond the direction's `image_len + pending_len` — takes at most half of what `cap_max` leaves free, and an offer the invariant would refuse first trims both images' idle capacity (beyond `image_len + pending_len`, 16-B rounded) with a shrinking realloc and credits `reserved` (a failed shrink keeps the buffer and accounting), then recomputes `owed` — the trimmed cap excludes the arriving segment — before re-checking admission. Without this, one direction passing 1 MiB reserved nearly the whole budget as empty capacity and starved the other (a keep-alive request after a 1.46 MB response lost 137 of 200 segments; a peer that sent 1.08 MB and stopped capped the other side at 1 MiB). Trimming happens only at packet arrival, the same pointer contract the growth realloc already relies on (readers hold an image pointer only within the current packet callback). Under that invariant a drain can always absorb what was admitted, so images stay gap-free; if the limit is lowered mid-flow, new reservations are refused and the drain keeps what fits and drops (counts) the rest — a budget clamp is reported distinctly from realloc OOM, which still re-links the segment. Exhaustion policy: offers that would break the budget are dropped and counted in `mmt_tcp_reasm_bytes_dropped()`; teardown frees every block, both images and the state. Metadata is reported separately and not charged: the per-flow `mmt_tcp_reasm_t` (120 B on LP64) and transient realloc copies. Alternatives rejected: a pure clamp of block + image bytes without the owed headroom (admitted pending bytes could then be unflattenable — a wedged drain or silent gaps — and would churn blocks); a new public knob or a global reserved-bytes gauge (public ABI growth for no extra guarantee — the existing `set_tcp_reassembly_limit()` is the budget); compacting partially-dead blocks (copies and pointer fix-ups on the packet path, unnecessary once duplicates never carve). Measured with the new `tcp-memory` fixture at the 4 MiB budget: AC1 workload 8 allocations / 114,912 B reserved; 8,129-B undrained segments peak 2,804,240 B (HEAD: 8,372,160 B); two-direction and image+pending streams peak at most 4,194,304 B (HEAD: 8,503,520 / 5,835,904 B); all admitted bytes flattened in order; the fairness cases (keep-alive, idle peer) refuse nothing until content is within 256 KiB of the budget, and a single in-order stream filled to the budget reallocates its image 17 times. `tools/phase0/tests/run_tcp_reassembly_perf_test.sh` still passes (0 in-order/reversed visits, duplicates dropped once, 64 KiB ceiling respected, teardown gauge 0).
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c` (`tcp_reasm_owed`, `tcp_reasm_image_append`, `tcp_reasm_drain`, `tcp_reasm_offer`, `clean_session_payload`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/tcp_segment.c` (`mmt_segblk_carve/release/free_all`, `tcp_seg_locate`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/tcp_segment.h`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`mmt_tcp_reasm_t`), `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` (`set_tcp_reassembly_limit`), `tests/resource_bounds/{run_tests.sh,test_tcp_memory.c,tcp_alloc_count.c,tcp_alloc_count.h}`.

- Q: Issue #381 (F-PERF-003, first part) — before the pending-segment insertion structure changes, what exactly does TCP reassembly emit for in-order, reversed, interleaved, duplicate, overlapping, 32-bit-wrapped, partially consumed and torn-down streams?
  A: Characterized as-is (post-#380) with byte-exact image assertions; no source change. Resolved policy readings, now pinned by `tests/tcp_pending_order`: (1) ordering is by wrap-aware sequence number (`tcp_seq_before`), so in-order, reversed and interleaved arrivals — including across 2^32 — flatten to the same stream; in-order appends and strictly reversed prepends walk 0 nodes. (2) Duplicates: the first segment with a given sequence number wins, whether it is still pending or already flattened, even when the later copy is longer or carries different bytes. (3) Overlaps are not trimmed: pending segments with overlapping ranges are concatenated in full in sequence order (the pre-#245 sorted-concat output), a segment straddling the consumed frontier is appended in full, and a segment whose whole range ends at or below the frontier is dropped — at offer time when the frontier already covers it, or at drain time when an earlier pending segment moves the frontier past it (a contained overlap). (4) Gaps are closed silently: the image carries no filler, and a late gap filler that arrives after later bytes were drained lies below the frontier and is dropped. (5) Drains are incremental and never rewrite the emitted prefix. (6) `clean_session_payload()` returns every allocation for pending-only, image-only and mixed sessions and is a no-op without reassembly state. Evidence, not desired behavior: evens-then-odds arrival walks `(n/2 - 1) * (n/2)` nodes — 261,632 visits at 1,024 segments and 67,100,672 at 16,384 — the quadratic cost the follow-up must remove; those two assertions are expected to change with it, deliberately, while every byte-output assertion must hold. Verified: new `tcp_pending_order` suite (330 checks) green default and ASan/UBSan; the #380 link stubs and gauges moved to `tests/resource_bounds/tcp_reasm_stubs.h` and are shared by both fixtures; `DEFAULT_SUITES` grew 26 → 27 with AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md and the CITATIONS registry reconciled; golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c` (`tcp_reasm_offer`, `tcp_reasm_drain`, `clean_session_payload`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/tcp_segment.c` (`tcp_seg_locate`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/tcp_segment.h` (`tcp_seq_before`), `tests/tcp_pending_order/{run_tests.sh,test_tcp_pending_order.c}`, `tests/resource_bounds/tcp_reasm_stubs.h`.

- Q: Issue #382 (F-PERF-003, second part) — how is out-of-order insertion into the TCP pending-segment store bounded?
  A: With an index, not a capacity limit: every characterized workload stays fully accepted. Interior inserts in `tcp_reasm_offer()` now locate their position through an insert-only AVL index over the pending list (`tcp_seg_idx_locate()` / `tcp_seg_idx_link()`, children and height carried in `tcp_seg_t`, root in `mmt_tcp_reasm_t.seg_root[dir]`) instead of the `tcp_seg_locate()` list walk. The list stays the source of truth for drain order; the tail-append and head-prepend fast paths still touch neither structure (0 visits), and `tcp_seg_idx_sync()` indexes those nodes lazily the next time an interior lookup needs the index — an empty index is built from the sorted list in O(n), otherwise the unindexed head prefix and tail suffix are inserted — so each node is indexed at most once. The locate path is recorded and reused to link the node after admission and carving (neither relinks pending segments), so a lookup is one O(log n) descent; duplicates are still refused before any carve. Ordering is `tcp_seq_before()`, consistent only while pending seqs span less than 2^31 (the same precondition as the list walk); beyond it the insert position is unspecified, but a sync always links every node — even one whose seq repeats an indexed node, which a head prepend can add in that regime — so no node stays unindexed and is re-walked by every later sync (QA review: skipping such nodes let 8,192 stuck prepends cost 813,294,061 visits; now 223,399). The drain drops the index with the list, and an image OOM that re-links a suffix resets it (heights cleared) for a lazy rebuild. Measured visits (one per node examined, including index builds): evens-then-odds 5,375 / 118,783 at 1,024 / 16,384 segments (issue #381 evidence 261,632 / 67,100,672), tail-anchored ascending 9,218 / 212,994, shuffled across 2^32 8,968 / 210,194 — at most 23.4-fold growth for 16x the segments; the wide-span case 21,894 / 223,399 at 1,024 / 8,192. Cost: the pending node grows from 80 to 96 B on LP64 (two index links; the height uses existing padding), charged inside the carve like the rest of the node, so the reserved-storage budget of #380 is unchanged — the AC1 workload now needs 8 blocks / 131,328 B instead of 7 / 114,912 B. Alternatives rejected: a per-offer walk cap (the 16,384-segment interleaved stream would be refused, changing characterized output); a search cursor (linear only for friendly orders, still quadratic for adversarial ones); indexing on every fast-path append (O(log n) on the in-order hot path). Verified: every byte-output assertion of `tests/tcp_pending_order` unchanged (only its visit pins moved, deliberately), new `tcp-order` fixture in `tests/resource_bounds` (default and ASan/UBSan), golden classification fingerprint unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c` (`tcp_reasm_offer`, `tcp_reasm_drain`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/tcp_segment.c` (`tcp_seg_idx_*`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/tcp_segment.h` (`tcp_seg_t`, `tcp_seg_idx_path_t`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`mmt_tcp_reasm_t.seg_root`), `tests/resource_bounds/{run_tests.sh,test_tcp_order.c,test_tcp_memory.c}`, `tests/tcp_pending_order/test_tcp_pending_order.c`.

- Q: Issue #383 (F-PERF-004) — how does the IPv4/IPv6 fragment map pick its eviction victim at the 1,024-datagram ceiling without walking the whole map on every arrival?
  A: With a maintained recency order. Every in-flight datagram embeds an intrusive `mmt_hlru_t` node (prev/next plus its map key) in the prefix shared by `ip_dgram_t` and `ipv6_dgram_t`; the circular list's sentinel lives on the handler (`ip_streams_lru`, `ip6_streams_lru`; the zeroed handler reads as an empty list). The arrival path stamps `last_activity` and calls `mmt_ip_frag_lru_touch()` (unlink + tail link, free when already the tail); `mmt_ip_frag_map_make_room()` — now shared by both paths — evicts from the list head through the stored key, and the dgram deallocators unlink, so completion, malformed removal, the age sweep, `mmt_ip_frag_map_drain()` and the `mmt_close_handler()` drain all keep the list exact without new hooks. The victim is therefore the least recently *updated* datagram: with non-decreasing packet timestamps that is exactly the old smallest-`last_activity` choice (ties, previously hash-walk order, are now the earliest updated), while a timestamp that runs backwards has no effect of its own: the update moves the datagram to the tail like any other, whereas the old scan would evict it next. The age sweep still walks the map once per expiry second; it is off the arrival path, and (unchanged by #383) `packet_session.c` only runs it on `ip_streams` — `ip6_streams` datagrams leave by completion, ceiling eviction or handler close. Measured: 10,000 replacement arrivals at the ceiling examine 10,000 victims (the old scan: 10,240,000 walker callbacks), make 0 walker callbacks and 20,000 index operations. Alternative rejected: a timestamp-ordered min-heap (keeps min-timestamp semantics under backwards clocks but costs O(log n) per fragment and a per-map array to allocate and free).
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ip_dgram.{c,h}` (`mmt_ip_frag_lru_*`, `mmt_ip_frag_map_make_room`, `mmt_ip_frag_map_evict_oldest`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ipv6_dgram.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/{proto_ip.c,proto_ipv6.c}` (fragment arrival paths), `src/mmt_core/private_include/hashmap.h` (`mmt_hlru_t`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`ip_streams_lru`, `ip6_streams_lru`), `tests/resource_bounds/{run_tests.sh,test_frag_eviction.c}`.

- Q: Issue #384 (plan task 2.5) — what is the resolved advisory status of the docs gems, the pinned GitHub Actions and the release-distro runtime packages, and how is it measured reproducibly?
  A: Measured by the new `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh` on 2026-09-23 (UTC), at the commit the report records; the full per-advisory output is committed as `tools/ci/dependency-advisories.md`. Scopes and sources: (1) all 40 gems resolved in `docs/Gemfile.lock` against OSV (RubyGems, database dated 2026-09-22 23:48 GMT) **and** the reviewed GitHub Advisory Database (one `affects=name@version` query per pin) — 0 affected; (2) the 15 SHA-pinned actions at the release named by their `# vX.Y.Z` comment against the reviewed GitHub Advisory Database (OSV cannot evaluate GitHub Actions versions) — 0 affected. The #385 targets were checked ad hoc, outside the committed report, with `gh api '/advisories?type=reviewed&ecosystem=actions&affects=<action>@<version>'` (and `type=unreviewed`): `ruby/setup-ruby` v1.325.0 (tag → `e8944e80fb94b20106697132f8c20c665fab29e9`) and `docker/setup-qemu-action` v4.4.0 (tag → `99012661954931238ded8c8b007157a8430204e1`, via `gh api repos/<action>/git/ref/tags/<tag>`) return no advisory; (3) the runtime packages the shipped `.deb`/`.rpm` declare (libc, libstdc++, libgcc, libxml2 — the `shlib-deps.sh` set for the `ENABLESEC=1` package build), resolved with `apt-get update`/`dnf repoquery` inside each pinned image of `tools/ci/base-images.txt` and checked against the distro's own OSV feed, keyed on the source package (`glibc`, `gcc-12`/`gcc-14`/`gcc`, `libxml2`) — the feeds hold no records under the binary names `libstdc++`/`libgcc`, so the report also prints each source's feed-record count. Distro advisories are judged against the distro revision, and every record is classified `fixed-by-backport` (fix landed without moving the upstream base — e.g. 40 for Ubuntu 22.04 libxml2 2.9.13), `backport-available`, `upstream-version-gap` or `unfixed`, so an old upstream base is never mistaken for an open advisory. Result: Rocky Linux 9 has 0 affected (21 feed records across glibc, gcc and libxml2, all fixed at or below the resolved revisions); Ubuntu 22.04, Ubuntu 24.04 and Debian 12 carry 31 affected High/Critical advisories (glibc and libxml2 only, all `unfixed` — the distro has not published a fixed revision), 3 Debian glibc advisories carry no rating in any source (Not Assessed), and CentOS Stream 9 is Not Assessed by construction (no advisory feed; its pinned digest also no longer resolves, tracked in #414). Severity is the highest available rating: several Ubuntu entries are distro-priority low/medium but NVD CVSS ≥ 7.0, so they count as High. Verdict: exit 1 — confirmed unresolved High/Critical advisories in distro runtime packages, so M1's advisory condition does **not** hold yet; the remediation has no in-repo patch (no fixed distro revision exists) and needs an explicit maintainer disposition (accept/track per advisory, or wait for distro fixes and re-run). No dependency was upgraded here — this task is measurement only. The script exits 2 (never 0) when any required scanner did not run, a pin comment is unreadable or an affected advisory has no rating; `--self-test` covers that fail-closed path offline. It is not wired into CI: it needs docker and live network, and would fail on the distro findings above.
  Source: `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh`, `tools/ci/dependency-advisories.md`, `docs/Gemfile.lock`, `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` (runtime soname→package map).

- Q: Issue #385 (plan task 2.6, F-DEP-101/F-DEP-102) — what changed between the pinned and target releases of the two reviewed action minors, and do the new pins resolve to those releases?
  A: Both are minor updates with no input, output or runtime change (neither `action.yml` is touched by the delta), so they are applied as plain pin bumps. `ruby/setup-ruby` v1.323.0 → v1.325.0 (`c-cpp.yml` docs-site job): two builder-bot commits (v1.324.0 adds truffleruby 40.0.0 / truffleruby+graalvm 40.0.0, v1.325.0 adds jruby 10.0.7.0 / 10.1.2.0) touching only `ruby-builder-versions.json`, `README.md` and the matching one-line `dist/index.js` version table; Ruby 3.3 resolution, `bundler-cache` and `BUNDLE_FROZEN` handling are unchanged. `docker/setup-qemu-action` v4.3.0 → v4.4.0 (`release-packages.yml` arm64 legs): `@docker/actions-toolkit` 0.96.0 → 0.100.0 (the only `package.json` change) plus lockfile-only transitive npm bumps, and `src/main.ts` now throws on any non-zero Docker exit (previously only when stderr was also non-empty) with a named error message — stricter failure reporting only, same binfmt image and `platforms` input. Pins were verified with `gh api repos/<action>/git/ref/tags/<tag>` (both are lightweight tags pointing straight at commits): v1.325.0 → `e8944e80fb94b20106697132f8c20c665fab29e9`, v4.4.0 → `99012661954931238ded8c8b007157a8430204e1`; each workflow's `# vX.Y.Z` comment names the new release. Re-running `bash tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh --only actions` with the new pins reports 0 affected advisories for both (verdict PASS for that surface); the committed `tools/ci/dependency-advisories.md` stays the #384 snapshot at its recorded commit.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml`, https://github.com/ruby/setup-ruby/compare/v1.323.0...v1.325.0, https://github.com/docker/setup-qemu-action/compare/v4.3.0...v4.4.0.

- Q: Issue #386 (plan task 3.1, F-DEP-001/F-DEP-002) — how does Pages publish the exact site CI verifies, and what does moving the publisher from github-pages 232 (Jekyll 3.10) to the locked Jekyll 4 graph change?
  A: One recipe, two callers. `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh` runs the source-tree link check, a `BUNDLE_FROZEN=true`/`JEKYLL_ENV=production` `bundle exec jekyll build` from `docs/Gemfile.lock`, the lock-unchanged diff (#372), the built-site link check, a renderer guard (fails unless Jekyll major 4) and writes `docs/_site/build-manifest.json` (Jekyll/kramdown/Ruby/Bundler versions, `Gemfile.lock` sha256, source commit, file count, `site_sha256` = sha256 over the sorted `<sha256>  <path>` lines of every other file). The PR gate (`c-cpp.yml` verify-helpers) and `pages.yml` both call it after the same pinned `ruby/setup-ruby` v1.325.0 step; `pages.yml` uploads `docs/_site` with `upload-pages-artifact` and `deploy-pages` publishes it unmodified — `actions/jekyll-build-pages` (and the unused `configure-pages`) are gone, so there is no deployment-time Jekyll 3 render. The deploy job alone holds `pages: write`/`id-token: write` and runs only on `refs/heads/main`. The repository Pages source was already `build_type: workflow` (read-only `gh api repos/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/pages`, 2026-09-23), so no settings change is needed. Renderer review against https://jekyllrb.com/docs/upgrading/3-to-4/: **Ruby** — 3.3 kept, above Jekyll 4.4's floor. **Permalinks** — no `permalink:` setting and no posts; every page keeps its `.html` URL (canonical URLs in a local ruby:3.3 build equal the live site's). **Includes** — only `docs/index.html` uses `{% include NAME.md %}` from `_includes/` with no parameters or `include_relative`, unaffected by Jekyll 4's include caching. **Plugins** — the graph is jekyll-remote-theme, jekyll-seo-tag, jekyll-relative-links; the github-pages auto-plugins are not carried over: `jekyll-optional-front-matter` (only this file lacked front matter — it now declares `layout`/`title` and `render_with_liquid: false`, a Jekyll 4 key, because entries quote `{% … %}` and `${{ }}` verbatim; `DECISIONS.html` stays published), `jekyll-default-layout`/`jekyll-titles-from-headings` (every other page already sets both), and `jekyll-github-metadata` (replaced by a static `github:` block in `_config.yml` holding the five fields the pinned cayman layout reads; `site.github.build_revision`, the stylesheet cache-buster, is supplied by `build-docs-site.sh` as the source commit through an overlay `--config`, so `style.css?v=<commit>` matches the live site). **baseurl** — formerly injected by the Pages builder, now declared as `url: https://montimage-projects.github.io` + `baseurl: /mmt-dpi` in `_config.yml`, so PR, local and published builds share it; `check-site-links.sh` already resolves the prefixed site-absolute links. **Markdown/Sass** — kramdown 2.5.2 + kramdown-parser-gfm (same major as github-pages 232's kramdown 2.4), Liquid 4.0.4 in both, and jekyll-sass-converter 3 (dart-sass) compiles cayman with deprecation warnings only.
  Source: `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (verify-helpers "Documentation checks"), `docs/_config.yml`, `docs/Gemfile`, `docs/Gemfile.lock`, https://jekyllrb.com/docs/upgrading/3-to-4/.

- Q: Issue #387 (plan task 4.1, F-TEST-004) — how does `--coverage` reach SDK code exercised by the integration suites, and how are those counters kept apart from the unit floor?
  A: With a build profile plus a runner-level harvest. (1) Profile: new `BUILD=coverage` (`rules/common.mk`, appended so no cited range moves) puts `-g -O0 --coverage` on `CFLAGS` and `CXXFLAGS` — the `.so` recipes link with `$(CXXFLAGS)`, so libgcov is linked into each library and `-Wl,-z,defs` still holds (kept) — and `rules/common-linux.mk` drops release hardening/LTO for it as for tsan (-O3 inlining and LTO would blur the function counts the controlled-call check relies on). `--coverage` exports `SDK_BUILD_PROFILE=coverage`, the variable the SDK-building suites already turn into `BUILD=`; `fault_injection` now skips its engine leg only for asan/tsan and passes the profile to its make lines. `SANITIZE` together with `--coverage` exits 2, since one run carries one SDK profile. (2) Harvest timing: objects build in-tree, so consumer counters land in `<repo>/src/**/*.gcda` (never in the throwaway prefix, which is why they survive its deletion), and the next SDK build deletes them (`-frandom-seed` recompiles drop the `.gcda` and rewrite the `.gcno`); `make -C sdk clean` does not. The runner therefore calls `tools/ci/sdk-coverage.sh harvest --consume` after every suite, pass or fail: it pairs each `.gcda` with the `.gcno` of the same build, runs `gcov -j` per source directory (asn1c reuses basenames), stores repo-relative line and function TSV in a `mktemp` directory outside `tests/coverage/` (CI uploads that directory) and deletes the harvested `.gcda` so a later suite cannot count them twice; stale `src/**/*.gcda` are deleted at run start. This assumes one SDK build per suite, which holds for all ten SDK-building suites today; a suite that rebuilt twice would lose its first build's counters. (3) Report: the top-level `summary.json` keys and `coverage.info` are left byte-for-byte unit-only, so `check-coverage-floor.sh`, `floor.json` and `report-uncovered-runs.sh` are unchanged. A new `cohorts` object carries `unit`, `sdk_integration` (with per-suite rows), `combined` (also written as `tests/coverage/coverage-combined.info`) and `generated_asn1c`, each with a `denominator` string. Paths are normalized to repo-relative form by stripping both the logical and the physical (`pwd -P`) repo root — the SDK compiles through `realpath` — and records with the same (path, line) are summed, so a source appears once. Cohort membership is decided by gcov's `.files[].file`, not by where the `.gcda` lives, because handwritten objects emit records for asn1c headers; everything under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/` goes to `generated_asn1c` and is excluded from the other three. First measurement: unit 81.9% (5,751/7,022, 32 files, same as before), sdk_integration 34.0% (14,568/42,887, 727 files), combined 41.1% (17,670/42,996), generated_asn1c 4.9% (1,053/21,492, 297 files); the mobile decoders `s1ap_common.c`, `ngap.c` and `nas_msg.c` are now measured (128/546, 86/393, 32/61 lines). (4) Proof: the new `sdk_coverage` delegate suite (`tools/ci/tests/test-sdk-coverage.sh`) builds and installs a `BUILD=coverage` SDK, runs an uninstrumented consumer that calls `mmt_version()` and `s1ap_entities_count()` or not, with `GCOV_PREFIX` so the hits never reach `src/`, deletes the prefix, harvests with the real helper (>= 1 hit with the call, 0 without) and checks the report on synthetic rows (logical/physical dedup, asn1c isolation, denominators, top-level keys unchanged, floor gate still green). It ignores `SANITIZE`/`--coverage` and runs `make -C sdk clean` on exit, so no coverage-profile objects are left for a later plain build to relink. Deferred to #388: sources whose objects wrote no `.gcda` (zero-hit files, so the denominators are not whole-SDK), per-family totals and a whole-SDK floor, the vendored llhttp files (`tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`), which still count as handwritten, and the counters `tcp_pending_order` and `resource_bounds` lose because they compile SDK sources into a `mktemp` directory their trap deletes (recovering them would change the unit denominator the floor pins).
  Source: `rules/common.mk` (`BUILD=coverage`), `rules/common-linux.mk` (`MMT_RELEASE_BUILD`), `tests/run_all_tests.sh` (`run_test_suite`, `write_coverage_report`), `tools/ci/sdk-coverage.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-sdk-coverage.sh`, `tests/sdk_coverage/run_tests.sh`, `tests/fault_injection/run_tests.sh`.

- Q: Issue #388 (plan task 4.2, F-TEST-004) — how is whole-SDK coverage measured, including zero-hit sources, and how is the audit's 82.9% 30-file cohort preserved?
  A: (1) Zero-hit sources: a consumer that loads a library makes gcov write a `.gcda` for every object in it, so the only sources missing after #387 were those in libraries no consumer loaded (e.g. the ENABLESEC engines when `installed_consumer` does not load them). `sdk-coverage.sh harvest --unexecuted` now also reads each `.gcno` with no `.gcda` — gcov reports it "not executed", every line at count 0 — and with `--consume` deletes those notes so the next suite does not re-read the same build. The runner passes it after every suite and deletes stale `src/**/*.gcno` at start, so only this run's builds count; a run that built a `BUILD=coverage` SDK (a subset run included) now ends with `make -C sdk clean`, closing the #387 gap that left coverage objects for a later plain build to relink. (2) Accounting: `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh` takes the build sources from the SDK's own object lists (`make ENABLESEC=1` prints `CORE_OBJECTS` … `FUZZ_OBJECTS`), classifies each record of `coverage-combined.info` by path prefix into families (`core`, `tcpip`, handwritten `mobile`, `business_app`, `dicom`, optional `security_fuzz`), generated (asn1c) or vendored (`tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`, so llhttp no longer counts as handwritten), and fails on a built handwritten source without records, a tracked one neither built nor justified, a stale justification, an empty family, an unclassified record, or either floor. Nine sources are justified in `floor.json` `sdk.excluded_sources`: the seven `src/examples/` programs and `mmt_tcpip_init.c` are in no library's object list, and `nas/util/decoder.c` is built but has no executable lines. Headers count in their family (their inline code is executable). Branch coverage stays Not Assessed. (3) First measurement (aarch64, GCC 13.3): core 83.9%, tcpip 39.91%, mobile 29.5%, business_app 89.22%, dicom 89.19%, security_fuzz 35.35% (1,437/4,064); whole SDK 46.03% (15,126/32,858 lines, 715 files); floor `whole_sdk_line_pct` 45.0 (one point for gcov attribution differences across gcc versions/architectures). (4) Original cohort: the audit report names no files, so the 30 were recovered by re-running `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage` on a `git archive` of `2ab7b73` — it reproduced 82.9% (5,660/6,831) exactly — and the list is pinned in `sdk.original_cohort.sources`. At HEAD the unit-only rate of those files is 82.87% (5,668/6,839), just under 82.9 because later code grew their denominator, so the gate measures the cohort on the combined records, where the integration suites also count: 83.29% (5,854/7,028). The unit-only figure is reported beside it. The #185 unit floor (`floor.json` top-level keys) is unchanged.
  Source: `tools/ci/sdk-coverage.sh` (`harvest --unexecuted`), `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh`, `tests/run_all_tests.sh` (coverage setup, `run_test_suite`, final clean), `tests/coverage/floor.json` (`sdk`), `tools/ci/tests/test-sdk-coverage.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (coverage job).

- Q: Issue #391 (plan task 4.5, milestone M3) — do the cleanup ratchets still hold after the coverage (#388) and accuracy (#390) tests, and does validation justify any new cleanup scope?
  A: Validation only — no rewrite. Measured on the branch base (`76f72075`): `count-init-wrappers.sh --strict` 0 definitions; `check-canonical-init-wrappers.sh` 0 canonical wrappers and no raw init-logic block repeated ≥3 times (2 distinct, each once — the DEAD repeated-block findings set is empty); `count-weak-types.sh --strict` 0/0/0 over `src/mmt_core/public_include/`; `lint-commented-code.sh` 0 blocks; `lint-markers.sh --strict` 80 markers, 0 without an issue reference. The one ratchet change: `lint-markers.sh` reported the count below its baseline (80 < 107) — #327 resolved 26 markers and #380 one more without tightening it — so `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt` moves 107 → 80, as the helper asks. The dependency PRs touched no `src/` files; their handwritten scripts are shellcheck-clean and carry no new markers. Two unused locals in `tools/phase0/gen_mobile_pcap.py` (`chunk_len`, `f1`) date from #144, not the new tests, so they stay out of scope under the task's "new evidence before expanding scope" rule.
  Source: `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`, `tools/ci/lint-markers.sh`, `tools/ci/count-init-wrappers.sh`, `tools/ci/check-canonical-init-wrappers.sh`, `tools/ci/count-weak-types.sh`, `tools/ci/lint-commented-code.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (verify-helpers job).

- Q: Issue #392 (plan task 5.1, F-DOCS-001) — what is the embedding lifecycle and who owns a handler?
  A: Two levels of state. Global: `init_extraction()` runs once, on one thread, before any handler exists, and its `false` return means nothing else may be called (not even `close_extraction()`, whose handler-map walk assumes a completed init). Per-handler: `mmt_init_handler()` (NULL + `errbuf` on failure) records the handler in the global map; `mmt_close_handler()` frees it and removes it from that map; `close_extraction()` runs last, force-closes any handler still registered, then unloads the plugins, so every handler pointer is dangling after it. Error paths release in reverse order. Worker ownership: one `mmt_handler_t` per worker, fed only by that worker; registration finishes before workers start; shutdown stops workers, closes each handler, then calls `close_extraction()` once on a single thread. `docs/THREADING.md` previously told embedders to call the internal `close_plugins()` / `free_registered_protocols()` instead of the public `close_extraction()`; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §3 omitted `init_extraction()` entirely and `docs/Global-Handler.md` cited a stale header path and `int` signatures. `src/examples/packet_handler.c` becomes the runnable reference (it ignored `init_extraction()`'s result and leaked the handler and global state on its error paths). A new `docs_lifecycle` suite pins the citations, the call order, and compiles/runs the installed example against a throwaway `MMT_BASE` prefix; it runs the example from a neutral CWD because `init_extraction()` prefers `./plugins` over `$MMT_BASE/plugins`. Alternative rejected: citing only function names without lines (the task requires file:line sources; the suite's registry keeps them from drifting).
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`mmt_init_handler`, `mmt_close_handler`, `init_extraction`, `close_extraction`), `src/mmt_core/src/plugins_engine.c` (`load_plugins`), `src/examples/packet_handler.c`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §3, `docs/THREADING.md`, `tests/docs_lifecycle/run_tests.sh`.

- Q: Issue #393 (plan task 5.2, F-UX-001) — where do the downloadable first-run source and capture live, and what makes the capture redistributable?
  A: Both are static files under `docs/first-run/` (`hello_packet.c`, `traffic.pcap`), which plain Jekyll copies verbatim into the site artifact, so they are served at `https://montimage-projects.github.io/mmt-dpi/first-run/` by the same build CI verifies; the landing page links them relatively so `check-site-links.sh` fails if either is missing from the source tree or `_site`. `docs/_includes/first-example.md` (shared by README and the site) now carries the exact `curl`/`gcc`/run steps, the expected output and the full source; the hero copy controls copy the same three commands. The capture is synthetic — ten Ethernet/IPv4 packets (a DNS lookup and one HTTP exchange) between RFC 5737 documentation addresses with locally administered MACs and fixed timestamps — generated by `tests/docs_onboarding/make_traffic_pcap.py`; `google-fr.pcap` was not reused because it is a capture of real third-party traffic whose redistribution terms are not recorded in the repository. The `docs_onboarding` suite regenerates the capture byte-for-byte, runs the displayed commands verbatim in an empty directory (site URL mapped to `file://` on `docs/` or `DOCS_SITE_DIR`, `/opt/mmt` mapped to a throwaway `MMT_BASE`), and diffs the output with the displayed one. `hello_packet.c` opens the capture before `init_extraction()` so a missing file is reported (with the fetch command) without touching MMT state. The USER_GUIDE §3 run line now fetches `traffic.pcap` instead of naming the repo-relative `src/examples/google-fr.pcap`, which only resolved from the checkout root (#392 review note).
  Source: `docs/first-run/hello_packet.c`, `docs/first-run/traffic.pcap`, `tests/docs_onboarding/make_traffic_pcap.py`, `tests/docs_onboarding/run_tests.sh`, `docs/_includes/first-example.md`, `docs/index.html` (hero), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §2–§3.

## 2026-09-24

- Q: Issue #394 (plan task 5.3, F-PERF-001..004) — how are the P1 adversarial resource checks preserved as reproducible budgets, and what does a run record?
  A: The four `tests/resource_bounds` fixtures stay the workloads (1,024/16,384 IPv6 inputs, 174,080 duplicate TCP offers, 1,024/16,384 out-of-order segments, 10,000 fragment replacements at the 1,024-datagram ceiling) and now report every deterministic count they already asserted as `@rb <fixture> metric|seed <key> <value>` lines (`rb_result.h`): workload scales, fixed seeds, accepted and refused work per workload, and counters (comparator calls, allocator calls, reserved bytes, list visits, victim visits, walker callbacks, index operations). `run_tests.sh` (default or `all`) strips those lines from the human log, writes `results.json` (schema `results.schema.json`; path overridable with `RESOURCE_BOUNDS_RESULTS`, git-ignored by default) and evaluates the committed `budgets.json`: `eq`/`le`/`lt` against a literal or a factor of another metric, and `sum` so offered work equals accepted plus refused. A missing, duplicated or malformed metric, a fixture without budgets, a failed budget or a failed fixture fails the run; the in-fixture CHECKs remain. Budgets hold the exact figures of the P1 issues: comparator calls below 751,360/200,273,920 with at most 24-fold growth, reserved storage at most 4,194,304 B against the 16,809,984 B baseline, pending-segment visits below 261,632/67,100,672 with at most 24-fold growth, and at most 10,000 victim callbacks against 10,240,000 with no map walk. Only counts are budgeted — no pps or timing guarantee (the epic defers timing benchmarks); the RSS-based phase0 harnesses (`run_memory_ceiling_test.sh`, `run_session_scale_test.sh`) stay separate because they need a built SDK and measure memory the counting fixtures cannot. CI enforcement of `results.json` is issue #395.
  Source: `tests/resource_bounds/{run_tests.sh,budgets.json,results.schema.json,rb_result.h,test_ipv6_hash_bounds.cpp,test_tcp_memory.c,test_tcp_order.c,test_frag_eviction.c}`.

- Q: Issue #396 (plan task 5.5, milestone M4) — does the documented first run work through the built site artifact, and what did the recorded browser checks find?
  A: Yes, now verified in CI. The new `site-onboarding` job in `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` builds the site with `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh` (the recipe Pages publishes, including the built-site link check) and runs `tests/docs_onboarding/run_tests.sh` with `DOCS_SITE_DIR=docs/_site`: the suite now reads the *rendered* `index.html` from that directory, requires both download targets to be byte-identical to `docs/first-run/`, runs the displayed fetch/compile/run steps verbatim in an empty directory (file URL percent-encoded, prefix shell-quoted, so paths with spaces work) and diffs the output; it also pins the copy-feedback live region and the mobile-menu toggle markup. Browser checks were recorded on 2026-09-24 against a locked Jekyll 4.4.1 build of this branch (Ruby 3.3.12 container; `site_sha256` 737a1fa7…, before this entry was added) served under `/mmt-dpi/`, with headless Chromium (Playwright) at 1280×800 (desktop) and 375×812 (mobile, touch): 26/26 checks pass across the two viewports — both download links HTTP 200 and byte-identical; all 26 landing-page links and fragment targets resolve; Tab reaches the four copy buttons in order and every one of the first 40 Tab stops is visible with a focus outline; Enter on a copy button copies exactly its `data-cmd`, shows "copied ✓", announces "… copied to clipboard" in `#copyStatus` and restores the label; the API tablist follows ArrowRight/End/Home; on mobile the toggle is shown, tap/Enter/Space open and close the menu (`aria-expanded` tracks it) and tapping a section link closes it; no console errors. The first pass found two defects, fixed here without redesign: the full `hello_packet.c` card widened the API grid past the viewport (content clipped by `body { overflow-x: hidden }` — 526 px on mobile, 71 px on desktop), fixed with `minmax(0, 1fr)` grid tracks and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the card's prose; and a `/favicon.ico` 404 at the origin root, fixed by declaring the site's own `favicon.ico`. The #393 review notes are closed too: the missing-capture error prints the `traffic.pcap` fetch hint only when that file was requested. Lifecycle (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §3) citations were already pinned by `docs_lifecycle`; the resource-limit page `docs/MMT-Handler.md` cited stale `sdk/include/mmt_core.h` lines and wrong return types, and now cites `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` — registered in the same suite so the citations cannot drift.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`site-onboarding`), `tests/docs_onboarding/run_tests.sh`, `tests/docs_lifecycle/run_tests.sh` (`CITING_DOCS`), `docs/index.html` (API grid, favicon), `docs/first-run/hello_packet.c`, `docs/_includes/first-example.md`, `docs/MMT-Handler.md`, `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh`.

- Q: Issue #421 — why did the ubuntu-22.04 arm64 package job intermittently fail with `qemu: uncaught target signal 11` from `ldconfig`, and why run the arm64 legs on a native runner rather than retry the step?
  A: The crash is in the emulation layer, not in the package: the failing log (run 35817872534, job 107043215139, attempt 1) prints the `qemu:` prefix — QEMU user-mode (`tonistiigi/binfmt@sha256:400a4873…`, qemu v10.2.3) delivering SIGSEGV while running the Ubuntu 22.04 (glibc 2.35) `ldconfig` — once in the apt libc-bin trigger (which apt tolerates, as in runs 35811132281 and 35802233452) and once at the explicit `ldconfig` in `tools/ci/build-package.sh`, which exits 139; attempt 2 of the same commit passed. Pinning the binfmt image cannot help — every failing and passing run already used the same digest — and a retry would mask a real `ldconfig` failure behind the same exit code. So the `build` matrix now selects `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (GitHub's native arm64 runner, free for public repositories, already used by the `resource-budgets` job in `c-cpp.yml`) for `arch: arm64` and drops `docker/setup-qemu-action`: no instruction is emulated, `docker run --platform linux/arm64` runs the same digest-pinned image natively, and `build-package.sh` keeps its strict `ldconfig`. Evidence on a native aarch64 host (the only kind the new legs use): `build-package.sh ubuntu-22.04 deb` in the pinned `ubuntu:22.04` arm64 image built, installed (libc-bin trigger and explicit `ldconfig` clean) and passed the installed-consumer check; 500 consecutive `ldconfig` runs in that image had 0 failures. One green CI run cannot prove flake-freedom; the argument is that the crashing component (the emulator) is no longer in the path. Native legs are also faster than emulated ones; the 60-minute cap stays.
  Source: `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`build` job `runs-on`), `tools/ci/build-package.sh` (smoke-test `ldconfig`), `tools/ci/base-images.txt` (header comment).

- Q: Issue #336 — the fuzz engine's built-in VoIP quality model used bare metric IDs (12, 15, 3) flagged "just a test", and `trapezoid_left()` carried a commented-out `kpi_value >= 0` bound. Define the IDs or delete the scaffolding? Keep the bound?
  A: Define, not delete: `init_voip_quality_estimation_struct()` is exported by `libmmt_fuzz` and exercised by the `rule_engine` suite, and its IDs are matched against XML-model `metric_id` attributes and the rule-registration helpers, so the values are a model contract. They became `VOIP_METRIC_ID_LOSS` (12), `VOIP_METRIC_ID_JITTER` (15) and `VOIP_METRIC_ID_QUALITY_INDEX` (3) in `mmt_quality_estimation_defs.h`, values unchanged so existing models keep matching. The bound stays out: the left-shoulder trapezoid is open by definition (membership 1 for every value up to parameter 0), and XML models may declare a negative `metric_range_low`, so a `>= 0` guard would leave such values in no grade. The dead line was replaced by a comment stating that, and `tests/rule_engine/test_fuzz_engine.c` now asserts both the named IDs and the open shoulder. Marker baseline 80 → 76.
  Source: `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/mmt_quality_estimation_defs.h`, `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/mmt_quality_estimation_engine.c`, `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/mmt_quality_estimation_utilities.c` (`trapezoid_left`), `tests/rule_engine/test_fuzz_engine.c`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

- Q: Issue #329 (F-DEAD-008, surfaced by #232) — should the protocol "Throughout" metrics accumulated in `update_proto_stats_on_packet()` / `update_proto_stats_on_new_session()` be replaced by periodic handlers?
  A: No — the current design stays. Each packet adds to cumulative per-protocol counters (`packets_count`, `data_volume`, `payload_volume`, `sessions_count`, first/last packet time, IP-fragment counters) only while `enable_protocol_statistics()` is on; the application owns the reporting cadence by reading them with `get_protocol_stats()` / `get_children_stats()` and clearing them with `reset_statistics()`, the same application-driven model as `register_session_timer_handler()` + `process_session_timer_handler()`. A periodic handler would not remove the per-packet work (a period's totals must still be summed packet by packet), would need a clock the core does not have in offline/pcap replay (packet timestamps, not wall time), and would add a new public registration API and callback semantics (snapshot vs. reset, per-thread handlers) — none of which a caller has asked for. Keeping it changes no public API, ABI or classification output. A future implementation would need: an opt-in `register_protocol_stats_handler(handler, interval, cb, user)` driven by packet timestamps from the packet path (like the session-timeout check, `process_timedout_sessions()`), a defined snapshot-then-reset contract that leaves the existing polling API unchanged, per-handler state only (no globals, per `docs/THREADING.md`), and a unit suite exercising interval boundaries on a pcap replay. The two `TODO(#329)` markers became plain comments pointing here, and `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt` dropped 72 -> 70.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_stats.c` (`update_proto_stats_on_packet`, `update_proto_stats_on_new_session`), `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h` (`get_protocol_stats`, `get_children_stats`, `reset_statistics`, `enable_protocol_statistics`, `register_session_timer_handler`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_session.c` (`process_session_timer_handler`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`process_timedout_sessions` calls), `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

- Q: Issue #334 — the commented-out HTTP stage machine deleted in #232 asked whether to enforce the exclude strategy "payload seen in both directions ⇒ not HTTP", and a second note asked whether `CONNECT` should classify as a distinct `PROTO_HTTP_CONNECT` instead of `PROTO_HTTP`. Enforce/add them, or keep the current detection?
  A: Keep the current behaviour for both; no classification or API change. Exclude strategy: `mmt_classify_http()` already excludes HTTP (`http_bitmask_exclude()`) on positive evidence — a first client payload that is not a known request line, a request that still has no line structure after 5 data packets, or a line that does not end in ` HTTP/1.x` — and `http_stage` (0 → 1 → 2) only tracks how far the request line was seen. A "both directions carried payload" rule adds no evidence the request-line check lacks: every HTTP/1.x exchange carries payload both ways, and a flow whose client request was not recognised is already excluded, so the rule could only drop flows the parser currently accepts, i.e. change classification with no identified false positive to fix. `CONNECT`: the method is recognised in the request-line table and exposed per message through the `http.method` attribute (`MMT_HTTP_CONNECT_CODE`, `http.h`), so consumers can already tell CONNECT tunnels apart; a new protocol ID would be a public API change (`src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip_protocols.h`, protocol names/attribute tables) and would reclassify existing flows. A future change to either would require: a new protocol ID and its registration (for `PROTO_HTTP_CONNECT`), a behaviour note in `CHANGELOG.md`, and — because both move session classifications — regenerating the phase0 golden-pcap baselines per `tools/phase0/README.md` (the `classification-gate` fingerprint check blocks the merge otherwise), with evidence of the false positive/negative it fixes. The two `http.c` comments that pointed at the open issue now state this decision.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http.c` (`mmt_classify_http`, `http_bitmask_exclude`, request-method table), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http.h` (`MMT_HTTP_CONNECT_CODE`, `HTTP_METHOD`), `tools/phase0/README.md`.

- Q: Issue #335 — the NAS decoder did not decrypt ciphered PDUs, decoded only the first TAI of a TAI list and stopped scanning the Attach Accept at the GUTI. What is implemented, and why is decryption deferred?
  A: The TAI list decoder (TS 24.301 §9.9.3.33) now decodes every partial list of all three types — one PLMN with non-consecutive TACs (00), one PLMN with consecutive TACs (01, expanded to one entry per TAC), several PLMNs (10) — into `tai[]` (at most 16 TAIs), and consumes the whole IE. Before, it always stopped after the first TAI, so with a multi-TAC list the Attach Accept read its ESM message container, and so the UE IP and the M-TMSI, from the wrong offset. The first TAI is still copied into the old flat fields, which are zeroed first. A number of elements above 01111 is read as 16, as the clause prescribes for a receiving UE. A partial list that overruns the IE length, the reserved type 11, or a 17th TAI stops decoding and sets `malformed`; the IE length is still consumed, so the rest of the message is still parsed. The Attach Accept decoder (TS 24.301 §8.2.1) walks every optional IE by its format (TS 24.007 §11.2.4: type-1 TV for IEI ≥ 0x80, fixed TV for LAI/EMM cause/T3402/T3423, TLV-E for 0x7X, TLV otherwise) instead of scanning byte by byte for 0x50. The byte scan could stop on any value byte equal to 0x50. The decoder records GUTI, LAI, MS identity, EMM cause, T3402, T3423, equivalent PLMNs, emergency number list, EPS network feature support, additional update result and T3412 extended value in a `present` bitmask; for a repeated IE only the first occurrence is kept (TS 24.301 §7.6.3), and other IEs are skipped and counted. An optional IE that overruns the message stops the walk and sets `NAS_EMM_ATTACH_ACCEPT_TRUNCATED` without failing the message; a GUTI with a wrong length is skipped and leaves no half-filled identity. Decryption is deferred to #452. EEA1/EEA2/EEA3 need the per-UE NAS security context (K_NASenc, NAS COUNT, algorithm, direction), which cannot be derived from the traffic, and the SDK has no API for a host to supply it — inventing one is a design decision outside this issue. Passing the payload through unchanged stays correct for EEA0 (null ciphering), which is what makes the protected Attach Accept readable in lab captures. Marker baseline 64 → 62.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/nas/ies/tracking_area_identity_list.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_mobile/nas/emm/nas_emm_attach_accept.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_mobile/nas/nas_msg.c` (`_nas_msg_decrypt`), `tests/nas_ies_tail/test_nas_ies_tail.c`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

## 2026-09-25

- Q: Issue #455 (follow-up to #331) — GRE sequence/loss tracking: implement `gre.seq_out`, `gre.seq_in`, `gre.seq_gap` and `gre.loss` (per-tunnel state keyed by endpoints + GRE key, an eviction policy and a reorder-vs-loss definition), or deprecate them?
  A: Deprecate. GRE has no session of its own — the session belongs to the inner flow — so implementing them needs a new handler-level table keyed by (outer src, outer dst, key) with its own eviction and a reordering window, i.e. new per-packet state and memory on a path every GRE packet takes, for four attributes no consumer has reported missing (they have always been `silent_extraction` and never produced a value). The raw inputs are already exported per packet (`gre.key`, `gre.seqnb`), so an application that needs loss/reorder accounting can keep that state with the policy it wants. The four ids stay reserved: the enum entries, aliases and `silent_extraction` registrations are kept, so name lookups and `register_extraction_attribute()` keep succeeding and the attribute count is unchanged; the header documents them as deprecated, never extracted. The new `parser_boundaries` `gre` fixture checks the ids still resolve and register, that none of them is ever extracted, and that `gre.key`/`gre.seqnb` extract within caplen (full and truncated frames, ASan-clean).
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip_attributes.h` (`gre_attributes`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_gre.c` (`gre_attributes_metadata`), `tests/parser_boundaries/test_gre_deprecated_attrs_api.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/run_tests.sh`.
