- Q: Issue #455 — GTP per-message-type detail: should `gtp_classify_next_proto()` handle echo, error indication, end marker and the other message types separately?
  A: No, dropped: the single non-G-PDU arm is already complete for classification. Per 3GPP TS 29.281 (§5.1, §7.1) only the G-PDU (type 255) carries a T-PDU; echo request/response, error indication, supported extension headers notification, end marker and every GTP-C message carry information elements only, so none of them encapsulates a further protocol and every one of them needs the same handling (truncate the inherited proto path at GTP). Separate arms would change no classification result while adding branches to the classification path, and the per-message distinction consumers need is already exported as `gtp.msg_type`; decoding GTP-C IEs would be new extraction work, not classification. The marker is replaced by a comment citing the spec; the phase0 golden fingerprint is unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_gtp.c` (`gtp_classify_next_proto`, `gtp_attributes_metadata`), `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh`.

- Q: Issue #455 — internal packet/flow struct realignment ("rebuild all memory areas to have a more aligned memory block") in `mmt_tcpip_internal_defs_macros.h`: do it now?
  A: Dropped. It is a layout refactor of the core with no measurement behind it: the note sat among macros, next to no struct, and named no field, target size or hot path. A reorder of `mmt_tcpip_internal_packet_struct` or the per-flow structs changes every protocol's field offsets and cache behaviour, so it is only worth doing against profiling data (pahole holes, per-session bytes, cache-miss counters from the phase0 benchmark) showing a gain; without that data it is risk with no demonstrated benefit. The marker is removed rather than retargeted; a future layout change starts from such a measurement.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_internal_defs_macros.h`.

- Q: Issue #455 — RTP quality-estimation setup (`rtp_initial_data_processing`, compiled only without `_MMT_BUILD_SDK`): who owns it, and should it become a generic helper?
  A: The fuzz engine (`libmmt_fuzz`) owns quality estimation; protocols only supply their metric values. Implemented: `init_application_quality_estimation_context(model, values, nb_values)` builds the estimation context of a model and binds `values[i]` to the model metric with `metric_index` i; the context owns the model, and `free_application_quality_estimation_context()` frees both (context first, as #447 requires). The helper validates everything `init_new_internal_application_quality_estimation_struct()` would `exit()` on and every slot `estimate_quality_index()` dereferences — NULL model or values, `nb_values` different from the model's metric count (the old RTP code bound two slots whatever the model held, leaving the rest uninitialised), a NULL slot, a metric index out of range, an invalid estimation-metric set — and frees the model on every refusal (the old code leaked it when the context could not be built). RTP now calls the pair in its init and cleanup paths. Testing it through `estimate_quality_index()` exposed an off-by-one in `calculate_index_array()`: it sampled `sample_nb + 1` points into a `SAMPLES_NB`-element stack array (stack smashing on the first estimate); it now samples indexes `0 .. sample_nb - 1`, the range every consumer reads. The `rule_engine` fuzz-engine test covers the refusals, the binding, an in-range estimate and heap balance, ASan-clean.
  Source: `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/mmt_quality_estimation_utilities.{c,h}`, `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/mmt_quality_estimation_calculation.c` (`calculate_index_array`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_rtp.c` (`rtp_initial_data_processing`, `rtp_session_data_cleanup`), `tests/rule_engine/test_fuzz_engine.c`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

- Q: Issue #452 (split from #335) — the NAS decoder passed the payload of a ciphered EPS NAS PDU (security header type 2 or 4) through unchanged, so for EEA1/EEA2/EEA3 the plain-message decoder parsed ciphertext. Should the SDK decrypt NAS (a key-supply API plus EEA1/EEA2/EEA3), or keep decryption out of scope?
  A: Keep it out of scope, and stop parsing ciphertext. Context: decryption needs the per-UE NAS security context — K_NASenc, the selected algorithm, the NAS COUNT (overflow and sequence number) and the direction — and only the algorithm is on the wire. Options: (1) a public key-supply API (per-UE keys keyed by an S1AP/UE identity, their lifetime and eviction, thread safety) plus SNOW 3G, AES-CTR and ZUC implementations checked against the TS 33.401 Annex C / TS 35.201-35.223 vectors, and NAS COUNT tracking across lost or reordered PDUs; (2) no decryption, but never parse a PDU known to be ciphertext. Chosen: (2). Option (1) is a large new public API and crypto surface with no user asking for it, and a passive probe rarely holds the subscriber keys; option (2) fixes the actual defect — ciphertext read as a UE IP or an M-TMSI. Implemented: a DownlinkNASTransport is now decoded only to read the Security Mode Command it carries (integrity protected, never ciphered, TS 24.301 §5.4.3.2); the type of ciphering algorithm of its Selected NAS security algorithms IE (§9.9.3.23) is kept per S1 connection, keyed by the (MME-UE-S1AP-ID, eNB-UE-S1AP-ID) pair, in a fixed 1024-slot table (`S1AP_NAS_CIPHERING_SLOTS`) direct-mapped by MME-UE-S1AP-ID: a newer connection overwrites its slot, a UEContextReleaseCommand frees it, and handler cleanup (`s1ap_entities_reset()`) clears the table, so the state never grows. `nas_decode_ciphered()` then decodes a ciphered PDU as plain text for EEA0, returns `DECODE_CIPHERED_PAYLOAD` without reading the payload for EEA1-EEA7 (4-7 are reserved and treated as non-null), and, when the algorithm is unknown (no Security Mode Command seen — capture started later, a handover or a new S1 connection re-using a NAS context, an evicted slot, or an initial UE message), decodes it only if its first octet is the one of a plain NAS message (0x07 for EMM, protocol discriminator 2 for ESM). Unknown stays best-effort rather than refused because the EEA0 lab and test captures this path exists for often do not contain the Security Mode Command, and refusing would lose their UE IP and M-TMSI; the first-octet check alone rejects about 15 of 16 random ciphertexts, and being read as an Attach Accept needs octet 1 = 0x07 and octet 2 = 0x42, a probability of about 1/65536 for a ciphertext. Integrity-only PDUs (types 1 and 3) are unaffected. A future key-supply API would plug in at the same place: the S1AP decoder already resolves a per-connection context before it calls `nas_decode_ciphered()`; that lookup would return the keys and COUNT alongside the algorithm, and `_nas_msg_decrypt()` would decrypt into a scratch buffer instead of returning NULL for EEA1-EEA3. Marker baseline 38 → 37.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/nas/nas_msg.{c,h}` (`nas_decode_ciphered`, `nas_get_security_mode_command_ciphering`, `_nas_msg_decrypt`), `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.h` (`DECODE_CIPHERED_PAYLOAD`), `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/s1ap_common.{c,h}` (`_decode_s1ap_downlinkNASTransport`, the ciphering table), `src/mmt_mobile/proto_s1ap.c` (`_free_entities_list`), `src/mmt_mobile/include/proto_s1ap.h`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `tests/nas_ies_tail/test_nas_ies_tail.c`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

- Q: Issue #330 — the weak-classifier marker backlog (46 `TODO/XXX(#330)` sites in `src/mmt_tcpip`): which flagged classifiers are wrong, and which flags were only unanswered questions?
  A: Seven sites were real gaps and are fixed; the other flags were verified or accepted and replaced by a plain comment recording the verdict. Fixed: MQTT on port 1883 now needs a well-formed fixed header (type nibble 1..15, a 1..4-byte Remaining Length varint) — the port alone matched any payload; port 8883 (MQTT over TLS) is unchanged. IPFIX (version 10) is read with its own header (message length at offset 2 must equal the datagram, export time at offset 4) — it was read with the NetFlow v5/v9 layout, so a real IPFIX message almost never matched; v5/v9 are unchanged. sFlow additionally needs agent address type 1 or 2 and the header length it implies — the `00 00 00 02/05` prefix alone is common in other UDP payloads. RADIUS accepts every RFC 2865/2866 and RFC 5176 code (1-5, 11-13, 40-45) instead of `code <= 5` (code 0 accepted, Access-Challenge refused), requires the 20-byte header including the authenticator (RFC 2865 §3; a 5-19-byte payload with a matching length field was accepted), and reads the length field only once the header is in the payload. A PASV `227` reply on an IPv6 FTP control connection dereferenced the NULL IPv4 header; it now records the control connection's server address, as the EPSV `229` path does; the IPv6 session tuples of the FTP analysis stored the address in 17-byte buffers that `ftp_copy_tupl6()`/`ftp_compare_tuple6()` handle as 32 bytes (heap overflow under ASan), now sized and zero-filled to 33. OSCAR's checker excludes a UDP flow at once (every signature needs TCP). Spotify's source-endpoint lookup is NULL-guarded. Found during the review: iMesh's BearShare-login match compared `Accept-Encoding: identity` with length `25 == 0` (a misplaced parenthesis), so it never fired; it now compares 25 bytes. Verified (no change): the UDP registrations of eDonkey, Jabber, POPO and the TCP ones of POPO, Icecast and SHOUTcast match on that transport; pcAnywhere-on-TCP and Gadu-Gadu-on-UDP exclude on the first packet; Gnutella/Pando already branch per transport; SIP's inline retransmission test equals the selection bitmask; ICMP deliberately classifies nothing after it; the offset-0 stacking in `proto_tcp.c`/`proto_udp.c` is right; Thunder already returns before the TCP search; the `http.c` header parser and the batman format attribute are unreachable (never registered). Accepted as deliberate strictness: exact-length STUN/RTP-over-TCP framing, STUN-gated RTP-over-TCP, TDS pre-login only, QQ 0x03, NetBIOS name query/response only, Zattoo fixed User-Agent, Meebo fixed offsets, the new-flow `memset` in `proto_tcp.c`. Marker baseline 37 → 0.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/inter_proto_classif_list.def`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_{mqtt,netflow,sflow,radius,ftp,oscar,spotify}.c` (fixes), `tests/parser_boundaries/test_classifier_audit_api.c`, `tools/ci/marker-baseline.txt`.

- Q: Issue #481 — which file is the cross-agent source of truth, and does the never-commit rule still cover all of `dist/`?
  A: `AGENTS.md`. It now carries the commands (build, the 30/30 test runner, subsets, sanitizer/coverage modes, the phase0 gates with a scratch `MMT_PREFIX`, the `--with-harnesses` pre-push tier, lint and doc validators), the layout map, the hard rules as Never / Ask-first constraints and the Done-when checks that `CLAUDE.md` used to hold; it no longer defers to `CLAUDE.md`. Facts with a single home (toolchain line, `MMT_BASE` contract, profile-switch clean rule) stay in `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` and are linked. The never-commit rule is narrowed from `dist/` to `dist/packages/` (the `tools/ci/build-package.sh` output, now in `.gitignore`): `dist/ZIP/` is hand-maintained installer source (F-DOCS-017), which supersedes the #369 entry's decision to keep the `dist/` wording. `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` checks each point.
  Source: `AGENTS.md`, `.gitignore` (`dist/packages/`), `tools/ci/build-package.sh` (output path), `git ls-files dist` (`dist/ZIP/*`), `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` (section 5).

- Q: Issue #482 — what stays in `CLAUDE.md` once `AGENTS.md` holds the shared rules, given the task names the token-efficiency notes as Claude-specific but also forbids repeating any `AGENTS.md` rule?
  A: Only the two imports and a comment: `@AGENTS.md` on line 1, then `@docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` (Claude-only syntax — `AGENTS.md` carries no `@imports`, so it links the environment notes, while the wrapper loads them in full). The token-efficiency block is identical for every agent, so it lives once, at the end of `AGENTS.md`, and reaches Claude through the import; a copy in `CLAUDE.md` would state each line twice, which the no-repeat criterion rules out. `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` (section 6) checks the first line, the absence of a commands/hard-rules block and that no substantive `CLAUDE.md` line appears in `AGENTS.md`.
  Source: `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` (*Token Efficiency*), `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` (section 6).

- Q: Issue #489 (F-CI-002) — how does the release workflow become the only publisher, given that a Release can still be created by hand and v1.9.0's verify-tag failure went unnoticed for 24 days?
  A: Three pieces, each statically re-checked by `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh`. (1) `tools/ci/check-release-tag.sh <tag>` adds an ancestry gate — the peeled tag commit must satisfy `git merge-base --is-ancestor` against `${RELEASE_BASE_REF:-origin/main}`, which `verify-tag` fetches explicitly; a missing tag or base ref or a shallow clone is exit 2, never a skip. The gate runs only for an explicit tag argument: the `GITHUB_REF_NAME` default stays version-only because c-cpp.yml's `version-agreement` job calls the script without an argument on tag pushes from a depth-1 checkout. (2) `softprops/action-gh-release` (which created or updated whatever Release existed) is replaced by `tools/ci/publish-release.sh`: it lists releases through the API (the by-tag endpoint hides drafts), refuses any existing Release for the tag except a draft authored by `github-actions[bot]` — the leftover of a failed attempt, deleted so a re-run can finish — creates the Release as a draft with every asset, then publishes it by id with `make_latest=legacy`, so a Release is never visible without its assets and an older-line patch never takes "Latest". (3) A `release-alert` job (`(failure() || cancelled())`, tag pushes only, job-scoped `issues: write` held by no other job, as `fuzz-alert` in c-cpp.yml) runs `tools/ci/release-alert.sh`, which opens or comments on `Release workflow failed for <tag>` with each job's result. Restricting who may create `v*` tags (a tag ruleset) and retiring the empty v1.9.0 Release are repository-setting and release-object changes left to a maintainer; the workflow can only refuse a Release that exists at publish time. `tools/ci/dependency-advisories.md` still lists softprops: it is a dated, commit-pinned measurement and is refreshed by its own script, not by hand.
  Source: `tools/ci/check-release-tag.sh`, `tools/ci/publish-release.sh`, `tools/ci/release-alert.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`verify-tag`, `release`, `release-alert`), `tools/ci/tests/test-release-gates.py`, `tools/ci/tests/test-release-publish.sh`.

- Q: Issue #491 (F-DEP-202, F-DEP-201, F-DEP-102) — `tools/ci/base-images.txt` claimed a dependency bot and a weekly run diff the pins, but neither existed; how are the CI image pins kept current when quay.io drops the previous `centos:stream9` manifest on every retag?
  A: Dependabot only reads images from Dockerfiles, so the pins are refreshed by `tools/ci/refresh-base-images.sh`: for each `name:tag@sha256:<digest>` line it resolves `name:tag` and `name@<digest>` with `docker buildx imagetools inspect --format '{{json .Manifest}}'` and reports `ok`, `drift` (tag moved) or `missing` (pinned digest gone — every pull fails); a repin target must be an index covering `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64`. `--check` exits 1 on any of these, 2 when a tag does not resolve at all (inconclusive); `--write` rewrites the pin in the baseline and every workflow quoting it. `.github/workflows/base-image-refresh.yml` runs it **daily** (weekly is too slow: two hand repins, #588 and #591, were needed within a day): a read-only `drift` job fails visibly on exit 1 or 2, and only on exit 1 a `repin` job runs `--write`. Because every pin is quoted in a workflow file and the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot be granted the `workflows` scope, GitHub refuses its push of such a commit; so the job force-pushes `ci/base-image-refresh` and opens or edits one PR only when the optional secret `BASE_IMAGE_REFRESH_TOKEN` (contents, pull-requests, workflows write) is set — such a PR also triggers CI, which a `GITHUB_TOKEN` PR would not — and otherwise opens or edits one issue, "Base image pins out of date", with the exact diff (`issues: write` is the job's only write scope). Creating that secret is a maintainer decision, not made here. A pinned-digest lookup counts as `missing` only when the registry answers "not found"; any other error is inconclusive (exit 2), so a rate limit never reads as a garbage-collected pin. The drifted Ubuntu/Debian pins found while building this were deliberately not repinned in the same change — the first scheduled run proposes them. `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh` now scans every workflow and composite action by default (`image:`/`container:` keys, `uses: docker://`, and the image operand of `docker run|create|pull`) and also fails on a baseline entry no workflow uses, so `buildpack-deps:bookworm` (asn1c gate) is pinned and recorded. Dependabot gains a `bundler` entry for `/docs`; `pipx install pre-commit==4.6.2` pins the CI hook runner (4.7.0 was under two weeks old).
  Source: `tools/ci/refresh-base-images.sh`, `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh`, `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `.github/workflows/base-image-refresh.yml`, `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`pre-commit` job), `tools/ci/tests/test-image-pins.sh`.

- Q: Issue #492 (F-DEP-204, plan task 1.4) — the committed #384 advisory snapshot (`tools/ci/dependency-advisories.md`, FAIL with 31 High/Critical distro advisories) had no disposition, the script ran in no workflow, and Debian could never pass because `unimportant` records counted as blocking. How does the measurement become a gate that can pass, and stay current?
  A: Four changes to `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh`. (1) Distro triage: a record whose distro marks it as having no security impact on that release — Debian `urgency: unimportant` (OSV `affected[].ecosystem_specific`, per release) or Ubuntu priority `negligible` (an OSV severity entry of type `Ubuntu`) — is listed with its severity but never blocks, because an upstream CVSS score merged in from an alias must not override the distro's decision (CVE-2019-1010022 is CVSS 9.8 upstream and `unimportant` in Debian). The 2026-10-21 run marks 9 Debian 12 and 4 Ubuntu records non-blocking this way. (2) Dispositions: `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt` holds one time-boxed entry per advisory (`id | target glob | expires | tracking | reason`, matched by id or alias); an unexpired entry makes that advisory non-blocking, an expired entry fails the run (exit 1) whether or not its advisory is still reported, and a malformed line makes the assessment incomplete (exit 2). It ships with 17 entries expiring 2026-12-31 for the advisories that still blocked after triage on 2026-10-21 — 15 High/Critical and 2 unrated, glibc/gcc-12/libxml2 in Debian 12 and gcc-12 in Ubuntu 22.04, all `unfixed` (no fixed distro revision exists, so there is no in-repo change). They are tracked, not accepted, and are a maintainer decision to confirm or replace. (3) Repository advisories: the direct docs gems (the lockfile's DEPENDENCIES; the repository comes from RubyGems metadata) and every pinned action are also checked against `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/security-advisories`, whose advisories the reviewed global database may not carry yet — GHSA-572m-7cg5-6j5r (jekyll-seo-tag 2.9.0) and GHSA-3343-386p-v26g (jekyll-remote-theme 0.5.2), both returning 404 from `/advisories/{id}`, are reported against `docs/Gemfile.lock` as Medium (non-blocking; #494 bumps them). Version ranges are evaluated with Gem::Version-style ordering, including the `a and b, or c` form repository advisories use; an unreadable range is a scanner failure, never a silent miss. (4) Image map: Debian 13 / `trixie` and the codename tags map to their OSV ecosystems, and `buildpack-deps` (the asn1c gate container recorded in `tools/ci/base-images.txt` by #491) is listed as an excluded CI tooling image instead of failing the run as unmapped. The weekly `dependency-advisories` workflow (Mondays, `contents: read` only, never on a pull request, so never a required check) runs the script over the images of `tools/ci/base-images.txt` and uploads the Markdown and JSON report as an artifact; the committed snapshot is deleted, and the measurement entries above that cite it remain as the historical record. The verdict logic is tested offline on every PR by `tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-advisories.py` (release_gates suite), which drives the real script on fixture trees against a local stub of OSV, the GitHub APIs and RubyGems.
  Source: `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh`, `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt`, `.github/workflows/dependency-advisories.yml`, `tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-advisories.py`, `tools/ci/tests/dependency-advisories-feeds.json`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`.

## 2026-10-21

- Q: Issue #490 (plan task 1.2, F-CI-004/F-CI-005/F-CI-014/F-SEC-011) — branch protection required the 16 contexts #184 set while later gates stayed advisory, the release-packages PR jobs were path-filtered (so no context of theirs could be required), approvals were 0 with code-owner review off while `.github/CODEOWNERS:3-6` claimed one code-owner approval, two required context names were fragile, and CODEOWNERS omitted the scripts and configs that decide what CI enforces. What does the repository change, and what is left to a maintainer?
  A: The repository records intent and checks it; protection itself is a maintainer action (the change cannot edit repository settings). (1) `.github/required-checks.txt` lists the 27 contexts to require, exactly as the check runs report: the 16 live ones verbatim plus `toolchain-floor`, `assertions-live`, `codeql`, `fuzz` (the fuzz-regression replay, #486), both `resource-budgets` legs, `site-onboarding`, `root-install`, `leak-gate`, `version-agreement` and `packages-gate`. (2) `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` derives every job's check-run name from the workflows (`name:` with matrix expressions substituted, else the job id, plus the ` (v1, v2)` matrix suffix with empty values dropped — the observed bare `unit-tests`; run-time `fromJSON` matrices as globs, an exact name winning over a glob) and fails when a context matches no job, or only jobs that never report on a pull request into `main` (no or path-filtered `pull_request` trigger, a tag/schedule `if:`); a name shared with such a job passes when one pull-request job reports it (`build` is also a `pages.yml` job). `--live` compares the list with live protection (read-only; needs admin read, so not in CI). It runs blocking in `verify-helpers`, which installs python3-yaml first; its self-test runs there with `REQUIRE_PYYAML=1` and from the `release_gates` suite, which skips it without PyYAML so the suites never depend on the runner image. (3) `release-packages.yml` lost its workflow-level `paths:` filter: a `changes` job matches the PR's merge-commit diff (`HEAD^1..HEAD`) against the same paths, `build`/`reproducibility` run only on a match (always on tags and dispatch), and the `if: always()` `packages-gate` passes when they were skipped for a non-packaging change and otherwise requires both to succeed. PR runs now cancel superseded runs; tag runs still never do. (4) Fragile names: the two non-required ones being added — `Leak regression over the golden corpus (Valgrind)` and `Version sites agree with rules/common.mk` — now report as `leak-gate` and `version-agreement`. The two fragile required ones stay, because renaming a required context in the same change leaves it unreported and blocks every PR: `Labelled-pcap precision/recall holds or improves (M9, issue` (the `#74` in the unquoted name is a YAML comment) and `unit-tests (-fsigned-char, hashmap memory hexdump mmt_utils mmt_inet_ntop)` (the suffix is the suite list); each rename is a follow-up change that swaps the context at its merge. (5) `.github/CODEOWNERS:3-6` states the intended review setting — 1 approving code-owner review — and CODEOWNERS now covers `tools/ci/`, `tools/phase0/ci/`, `scripts/`, `.gitleaks.toml`, `.gitleaksignore`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.shellcheckrc`, `install.sh`, `dist/ZIP/`, `rules/`, `sdk/Makefile`, `debian/` and `SECURITY.md`. The apply commands are in `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Required checks and review").
  Source: `.github/required-checks.txt`, `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`changes`, `packages-gate`), `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`, `version-agreement`), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`leak-gate`), `tools/phase0/README.md` (regenerated ci-gates block), `.github/CODEOWNERS`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `gh api repos/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/branches/main/protection` (16 contexts, `required_approving_review_count: 0`, read 2026-10-21).

- Q: Issue #509 (plan task 5.1, F-TEST-004) — `tips_eval.c` had 8 of 834 lines covered and no test fed packets through a loaded rule; which evaluator behaviour do the new characterisation tests pin, given that #510-#512 change the evaluator afterwards and some current verdicts contradict the rule's plain reading?
  A: The CURRENT verdicts, quirks included, so each later fix has to change an expectation on purpose. `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_eval.c` loads one single-property rule from `tests/rule_engine/eval/` through `init_sec_lib()` per forked child (the engine keeps its rules in process globals and has no unload; an `alarm` turns a hang into a failure), replays synthetic Ethernet/IPv4/UDP frames through `packet_process()` and asserts the ROOT rule's `nb_satisfied`/`nb_not_satisfied` counters plus the verdicts passed to the result callback. 43 scenarios cover THEN on the same packet, AFTER, BEFORE, the delay window, the packet-counter window, AND, OR, NOT, REPEAT, nested THEN, a `.1` event reference and `||`/arithmetic expressions, each with a satisfied and a not-satisfied case where the rule can produce both. Pinned quirks, each marked "current behaviour" in the test: an AND or NOT node without a delay of its own cannot span two frames (`check_for_timeout()` returns 0, which is `VALID`, and `check_time()` passes it on as an expiry), so the NOT rule's not-satisfied case comes from that expiry and the negated event on a fresh instance reports nothing; a verdict reached without any validated event is counted but not passed to the callback (no JSON history); an out-of-order timestamp is only logged (Error 23); a counter-only BEFORE rule never reports "A without a preceding B" (the `verify_left` probe runs only for a negative `delay_min`); a BEFORE sequence below the root never completes; a too-close B eliminates a BEFORE-window instance. Two shapes crash today and are deliberately not tested: NOT as the right son of a root THEN (NULL `curr_r` in `verify_not_backtrack()`, `src/mmt_security/tips_eval.c:1248`) and any rule with `keep_state` (the `copy_instance()` path) — #510 owns the `keep_state` test. Measured after `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage`: `tips_eval.c` 561/834 lines (67.3%) in `tests/coverage/coverage-combined.info`; `security_fuzz` 61.48%, whole SDK 51.05%.
  Source: `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_eval.c`, `tests/rule_engine/eval/*.xml`, `tests/rule_engine/run_tests.sh` (step 8), `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`analyse_incoming_packet`), `src/mmt_security/tips_eval.c`, `src/mmt_security/tips_report.c` (`rule_is_satisfied_or_not`), `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh`.

- Q: Issue #496 (plan task 2.3, F-DEP-305) — `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt` recorded only llhttp; BSD `queue.h` and the Apple/MIT-derived `mmt_inet_ntop.c` had their notices stripped in 1b02f7e0, the asn1c runtime had no recorded licence, and the release SBOM listed only the package. Where does the inventory live, and what happens to the two stripped files?
  A: A new machine-readable manifest, `tools/ci/vendored-components.json`, holds one entry per component: SPDX package `name` and `spdx_id`, version, upstream, pin, download location, purl, SPDX licence, copyright line, in-tree `paths` and `checks` (a file plus a regex whose groups, joined by `.`, must equal the recorded version or pin). It lists llhttp 9.4.3 (MIT, `release/v9.4.3`, checked against `LLHTTP_VERSION_*` in `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/llhttp.h`) and the asn1c runtime skeletons under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/common/` (BSD-2-Clause, mouse07410/asn1c `dbd0ed09…`, 0.9.29, checked against `ASN1C_REF`/`ASN1C_VERSION` in `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/ASN1C_VERSION`). `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt` keeps its one job — the files excluded from the clean-code metrics and the coverage denominators — unchanged in content: listing more there would move the cppcheck/marker ratchets and the coverage accounting. `tools/ci/vendored-components.py check` (blocking in `verify-helpers`, and in the release build job) fails when one of its entries belongs to no component. `vendored-components.py sbom` adds each component to the syft SPDX-JSON (which only holds the package file and `DOCUMENT DESCRIBES` it) as a package CONTAINed by the described root, in the build job before the artifact upload, so SHA256SUMS and the attestation cover the extended SBOM; `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh` checks that step stays wired. The two stripped files were replaced rather than re-noticed, since Linux/glibc is the only supported platform: `queue.h` is deleted and `hashmap.h`/`proto_ip_frag.h` include glibc `<sys/queue.h>` (they use only `LIST_HEAD/ENTRY/INIT/INSERT_AFTER/INSERT_HEAD/REMOVE`, the same BSD macros and field names); `mmt_inet_ntop.c` keeps its name, so the build lists, the suites, `tests/coverage/floor.json` and the required-check names are untouched, but now holds an original wrapper over glibc `inet_ntop` that keeps the exported symbol and its NULL returns. Its IPv6 text is now the canonical RFC 5952 form: a single zero group is no longer shortened to `::` (`2001:db8:0:1:1:1:1:1`, formerly `2001:db8::1:1:1:1:1`), and `::a.b.c.d` follows glibc's rules; no golden output, phase0 baseline or test expectation formats such an address. `hashmap.h` and `proto_ip_frag.h` are Montimage code that only included `queue.h`, so they are not components. Reviewed and not listed: every other tracked source outside `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/` was searched for third-party copyright/licence notices; `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_citrix.c` adopts nDPI's citrix detection logic (the ICA/CGP byte signatures and the `== 6`/`> 22` length thresholds, as its header says) re-expressed in MMT code, and `proto_syslog.c` is likewise "nDPI-inspired" — no nDPI source text is copied, so neither is a vendored component. The generated S1AP/NGAP codecs under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/{s1ap,ngap}` and the 3GPP ASN.1 modules under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/support/` are generator output and input, pinned by `ASN1C_VERSION` and `support/README`; the 3GPP specifications' terms are left to the licence-files task #497, which generates the notices from this manifest. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1 and `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh` ties it to `VERSION` (as for #494).
  Source: `tools/ci/vendored-components.json`, `tools/ci/vendored-components.py`, `tools/ci/tests/test-vendored-components.py` (run by the `release_gates` suite), `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (step "Add vendored components to the SBOM"), `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`), `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh`, `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_inet_ntop.c`, `tests/mmt_inet_ntop/test_mmt_inet_ntop.c`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/hashmap.h`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ip_frag.h`.

- Q: Issue #504 (plan task 4.1, F-BUG-009/F-BUG-202) — the public `data_defs.h` `#undef`'d `ntohl`/`ntohs` and redefined them as unconditional `swab32`/`swab16` macros that evaluated their argument up to four times, and `struct diameter_header` declared the command flags as four 1-bit bitfields in wire order, which GCC allocates LSB-first on little-endian hosts. What replaces the override, what happens to the `swab*`/`ntohll` helpers consumers may use, and how are the flags decoded?
  A: `data_defs.h:47-82` now includes `<arpa/inet.h>` and `<endian.h>` and no longer touches `ntohl`/`ntohs`, so every TU and every consumer gets the system definitions: single evaluation, and a no-op on big-endian hosts. On the little-endian hosts the SDK ships for, glibc's `ntohl`/`ntohs` compute the same values as the old swaps, and no in-tree call passes an argument with a side effect, so classification is unchanged. `swab16`/`swab32` stay as public unconditional byte swaps. `swab64` stays an unconditional 64-bit swap, now written with two `swab32` calls so it no longer depends on `ntohl`. `ntohll` becomes `swab64(x)` on little-endian hosts and `((uint64_t)(x))` when `__BYTE_ORDER == __BIG_ENDIAN`. It is still guarded by `#ifndef ntohll` and is now function-like (was the object-like `#define ntohll swab64`), and its two in-tree callers (`proto_dtls.c`, `proto_int.c`) already call it. The four flag bitfields become one `uint8_t flags` at byte offset 4 (`mmt_mobile_internal.h`), read through `DIAMETER_HDR_FLAG(hdr, DIAMETER_HDR_FLAG_{R,P,E,T}_MASK)` (0x80/0x40/0x20/0x10, RFC 6733 §3). `proto_diameter.c` checks `sizeof == 20` and `offsetof(flags) == 4` with `_Static_assert`. The attribute ids, aliases and types (`flag_r`…`flag_t`, `MMT_U8_DATA`) are unchanged; only the extracted values change: a CER (0x80) now reports R=1, where the old `flag_r` bitfield read the byte's least significant (reserved) bit. Out of scope and left as is: `copy_4bytes_order()` and the 24-bit `length`/`command_code` bitfields still assume a little-endian host. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, the same as for #494/#496.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h`, `src/mmt_mobile/mmt_mobile_internal.h`, `src/mmt_mobile/proto_diameter.c`, `tests/proto_classifiers/test_diameter_flags.c`, `tests/installed_consumer/test_byte_order.c` (run by `tests/installed_consumer/run_tests.sh` at -O0 and -O2 against the installed headers).

- Q: Issue #507 (plan task 4.4, F-BUG-201/F-BUG-220) — S1AP attribute extraction cached the parsed packet in `static __thread` variables keyed only by `packet_id`, a per-handler counter, and held raw pointers into the process-global entity store that any handler close frees; the NAS decoders wrote a process-global `errorCodeDecoder`. How is the cache keyed, and what does it hold?
  A: The key is (`packet->mmt_handler`, `packet_id`, store generation); `_free_entities_list()` bumps the generation under the entity `mutex`, so a handler that reuses a closed handler's address never hits the old entry. The cache holds copies of the packet's eNodeB/MME/UE entities taken under the mutex after the parent links are set, not entity ids re-resolved on each attribute (the issue's suggestion): a copy needs no per-attribute list walk, cannot resolve to a recycled id after a reset, and for a single handler yields the same values, since a packet's attributes are extracted with no store write in between. `errorCodeDecoder` was written on every error path and never read (its one reader, `CHECK_MESSAGE_TYPE`, has no caller), so the decoders now only return their code and `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.c`, which held nothing but the global, is deleted together with its `sdk.excluded_sources` entry in `tests/coverage/floor.json` (8 justified sources remain; the #388 entry above names 9). The `errorCodeDecoder` dynamic symbol leaves `libmmt_tmobile.so`; no installed header declared it. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #504.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/proto_s1ap.c` (`_free_entities_list`, `_extraction_att`), `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.h`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_packets.c` (`test_s1ap_two_handlers`), `tests/nas_ies_tail/test_nas_ies_tail.c` (`test_concurrent_decode_errors`), `tests/coverage/floor.json`, `docs/THREADING.md`.

- Q: Issue #508 (plan task 4.5, F-SEC-006/F-SEC-007/F-SEC-013) — the release-hardening block was gated on `$(filter gcc clang,$(CC))`, so `CC=/usr/bin/gcc`, `gcc-13` or a wrapper silently built without FORTIFY, stack protector, full RELRO or LTO; `check-hardening-flags.sh --built` only ever ran on unshipped host builds; the phase0 gates and two lint scripts used fixed `/tmp` paths. How is hardening keyed, what is checked on the shipped packages, and which paths moved to `mktemp`?
  A: `rules/common-linux.mk` probes `$(CC)` and `$(CXX)` once each with `-dM -E` on empty input (no compile) and derives `MMT_CC_ID`/`MMT_CXX_ID` (icc over clang over gcc); the block is gated on `MMT_CC_ID` being gcc or clang, and `rules/arch-linux(-gcc).mk` takes its GCC floor from the same probe instead of a second one. LTO additionally requires `$(CC)` and `$(CXX)` to report the same `__GNUC__`/`__GNUC_MINOR__`/`__GNUC_PATCHLEVEL__`, since objects are compiled by `$(CC)`, libraries linked by `$(CXX)`, and LTO bytecode does not cross GCC releases; otherwise LTO alone is dropped. Every skip prints a build `$(warning)` (unidentified compiler, LTO dropped, and the existing DEBUG/VALGRIND/sanitizer/coverage one); DEBUG, VALGRIND and `BUILD=asan|tsan|coverage` still disable the block. `check-hardening-flags.sh --built [DIR...]` checks the regular `*.so`/`*.so.*` files of the given directories (default `sdk/lib/`) and adds a `_FORTIFY_SOURCE` floor — at least one `__<name>_chk` import across the set, `__stack_chk_*` excluded. It is a floor, not proof: Ubuntu GCC fortifies by default, so an unhardened build passes it there; BIND_NOW is the built-object discriminator (an unhardened `CC=/usr/bin/gcc` build on main failed it on all five libraries), and the makefile side of FORTIFY is pinned by the new `tools/ci/tests/test-hardening-identity.sh` (run by the `release_gates` suite), which evaluates the real makefiles through a `make --eval` print goal. `release-packages.yml` runs the check in each build container right after `tools/ci/build-package.sh` installed the package, on `/opt/mmt/dpi/lib` and `/opt/mmt/plugins` — every distro and both architectures — and its packaging path filter now lists the checker so a checker-only change still exercises the matrix. `check_classification.sh`/`check_precision.sh` keep `MMT_PREFIX` as the override (the precision-gate job sets it to share the install with the accuracy oracle) and otherwise install into `mktemp -d /tmp/mmt-phase0.XXXXXX`, removed on exit; `capture_baseline.sh` does the same without `--prefix` (its valgrind SKIPPED note then names `<prefix>`); `check-gitleaks-allowlist.sh` and `lint-commented-code.sh` use `mktemp` instead of `/tmp/<name>.$$`. The `tools/phase0/gen_*.py` output defaults are explicit output paths and were left unchanged.
  Source: `rules/common-linux.mk` (identity probe, hardening gate, warnings), `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk` (`rules/arch-linux.mk` is a symlink to it), `tools/ci/check-hardening-flags.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-hardening-identity.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`changes` and `build` jobs), `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_precision.sh`, `tools/phase0/capture_baseline.sh`, `tools/ci/check-gitleaks-allowlist.sh`, `tools/ci/lint-commented-code.sh`.

- Q: Issues #500 and #499 (plan tasks 3.2 and 3.1, F-BUG-002/F-BUG-003/F-BUG-001) — a timeout-ring insert that could not fit the live milestone span (> 2^22 s) returned 0 like an allocation failure, so every caller ran the out-of-memory sweep; that sweep stopped mid-list at 65,000 sessions and then deleted the whole milestone, orphaning the rest of the list; and a tunnel child's raw `parent_session` pointer dangled once the outer session expired first. Clamp or park the outlier milestone, and reference-count the parent or have its expiry detach its children?
  A: Park, and reference-count. (1) A span the ring cannot reach is not OOM: `mmt_ring_grow()` reports it distinctly and `insert_session_timeout_milestone()` parks the milestone in an ordered side index (`std::map`, allocating only on that cold path), returning `MMT_TIMEOUT_PARKED` (2); `MMT_TIMEOUT_INSERTED` (1) and `MMT_TIMEOUT_NOMEM` (0) keep their values, so callers that test `== 0` or `== 1` are unchanged. Lookup, unlink, range expiry, iteration and teardown consult both stores, and a milestone lives in exactly one. Clamping was rejected: it changes when the session expires (early or never). A ring growth that fails on `calloc` is still `MMT_TIMEOUT_NOMEM`, so the existing double-OOM path keeps its trigger. (2) The forced sweep unlinks each session before freeing it (`force_session_timeout()`), so a milestone disappears with its last session and a budget stop (`MMT_OOM_SWEEP_BUDGET`, 65,000) leaves the remainder linked and expirable. It also skips a session that still has live tunnel children and the sessions of the packet being processed. (3) Detaching children needs a child list (two or three more pointers in every session); a count fits the existing padding after `next_tcp_seq_nr[2]`, so `sizeof(mmt_session_t)` is unchanged. `live_children` is incremented when an embedded session is linked (before its first ring insert, so the forced sweep that insert may trigger cannot free the parent) and decremented after the child is freed. A parent with live children is never freed: a sweep that reaches it re-arms it at or after the end of that sweep (`process_timedout_sessions()` now publishes `last_expiry_timeout` before sweeping), and `mmt_close_handler()` drains the ring in rounds (`expire_all_sessions()`, at most `PROTO_PATH_SIZE + 1`, then an unconditional drain), so children always expire first and an expiry handler can still read a child's parent. To keep the deferral rare, a child's milestone is carried up to every ancestor whose milestone is earlier, and a parent's own refresh never moves it below that. Tunnel parents whose inner flow has the longer timeout therefore now expire with their last child instead of on their own delay; the golden classification fingerprint and the precision baseline are unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.cpp` (parked index, `mmt_ring_grow`, `mmt_ring_drain`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/hash_utils.h` (`MMT_TIMEOUT_*`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_session.c` (`expire_session`, `defer_parent_expiry`, `expire_all_sessions`, `process_outofmemory_force_sessions_timeout`, `hold_ancestors_milestone`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`live_children`, `MMT_OOM_SWEEP_BUDGET`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`mmt_close_handler`), `tests/core_engine/test_core_engine.c` (`test_timeout_span_overflow`, `test_tunnel_parent_lifetime`), `tests/resource_bounds/test_timeout_sweep.c` (fixture `timeout-sweep`), `tools/phase0/tests/run_tunnel_parent_lifetime_test.sh`.

- Q: Issue #510 (plan task 5.2, F-BUG-209/F-BUG-210/F-BUG-211) — `store_history()` wrote packet strings and the cause into the verdict JSON raw, `get_data_from_pcap()` handed `strstr()`/`strcmp()` operand buffers with no terminator, and `copy_instance()` searched the JSON history for XML tags, so any rule with `keep_state` crashed on its first verdict (the #509 entry above). How are values escaped, how are operands terminated, and what does `keep_state` now keep?
  A: (1) One bounded escaper, `json_escape_into()` (the core of `convert_string_to_json_compatible()`, whose behaviour is unchanged), escapes the cause, header lines and inline string records into buffers sized `n*6+1`; pointer strings, the TCP payload and generic header lines, already escaped, are now appended piecewise (`json_append_string_attr()`) because their escaped form can exceed the 8192-byte formatting buffer, whose `snprintf()` used to cut off the closing `"},`. Two output-format corrections ride along, both for JSON validity: a 6-byte binary value is quoted (`"AA:BB:…"`, was a bare `AA:BB:…`) and a non-finite float prints `null` (was `nan`/`inf`). Bytes ≥ 0x80 still pass through unescaped. (2) Every operand buffer of `get_data_from_pcap()` is allocated with one trailing zero byte (`alloc_operand()`, `size + 1`) rather than switching XC/XD to `memmem()`: `size` and every comparison result are unchanged, and `strstr()`/`strcmp()` stop inside the allocation. (3) `copy_instance()` keeps the history members `"event_<id>":{…}` whose id `keep_state` lists, skipping each object by brace depth with string escapes honoured, and re-appends each with its comma; no kept member means no history, as on a fresh instance. Known limitation, pinned as "current behaviour" in `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_eval.c` and left as a follow-up: `analyse_incoming_packet()` (`src/mmt_security/tips.c`) copies every eliminated instance, including one that expired, timer and all, so after a timeout a `keep_state` rule reports not-satisfied on every later frame; restricting the copy to satisfied verdicts changes `tips.c`, which #511/#512 rework. The NOT-as-right-son crash in `verify_not_backtrack()` is outside #510 and still untested. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #504.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips_report.c` (`json_escape_into`, `json_append_string_attr`, `store_history`), `src/mmt_security/tips_extract.c` (`alloc_operand`, `get_data_from_pcap`), `src/mmt_security/tips_eval.c` (`filter_json_history`, `copy_instance`), `tests/rule_engine/test_overflow_family.c` (`test_store_history_escapes_strings`, `test_contains_unterminated_operands`), `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_eval.c`, `tests/rule_engine/eval/json_escape.xml`, `tests/rule_engine/eval/keep_state.xml`, `tests/rule_engine/run_tests.sh` (jq checks in steps 6 and 8).

- Q: Issues #511 and #512 (plan tasks 5.3/5.4, F-BUG-212/213/214, F-BUG-221/216 and part 1 of F-BUG-215), resolved together: the evaluator retried a NOT_VALID instance forever, read 4-byte U32/IP values through an LP64 `unsigned long`, called embedded `#function(...)`s through an unchecked `dlsym()` with an unbounded argument count, and the loader trusted any well-formed XML, ended the process on the rest, hard-coded the reaction folder and allowed a second engine. What is rejected at load, what changed at run time, and which pinned expectations moved?
  A: (1) Load errors are returns: `init_sec_lib()` now returns `int` (0, or -1 with the reason on stderr; the symbol is not in the installed headers, as for #236), `read_rules()` likewise, and `tips_xml.c` has no `exit(` left. Rejected at load: an element other than `<beginning>`/`<property>`/`<operator>`/`<event>` (Error 12a — its attributes were written through a NULL rule), an empty `delay_min`/`delay_max` (12b — index -1), an `<operator>`/`<event>` with no parent on the father stack (12c — the stack underflowed), a THEN without exactly two branches, an AND with fewer than two, an OR/REPEAT with none or a NOT without exactly one (12d — `verify()` dereferences them unchecked), an expression that climbs above its own node through a leading operator or a surplus `)` followed by more expression (37b — NULL father; a surplus `)` that ends the expression is still tolerated, as the old parser stopped on the empty rest), an unknown attribute (9a — it used to register protocol 0 silently), a constant the operand type cannot hold (3g — `get_xdata()` returned NULL and `create_instance()` copied from it; this rejects `META.utime > 0`, a timeval constant), an empty embedded-function parameter, trailing comma included (3f — `#f(a,)` passed one parameter fewer than written), an embedded function with more than four parameters (3e) or that the library does not export (3h), for an expression leaf and for a `#function` reaction alike. The failed load frees what it built (`free_rules()`), so LSan sees no leak, and releases the engine. `rules_minimal.xml` gave its properties both THEN branches and dropped the `META.utime > 0` comparison for this; META.UTIME is now registered by every load, on the loading handler, instead of once per process from the first expression. (2) One engine per process: an atomic claim in `init_sec_lib()` makes a second call, or the loser of a race, return -1 (Error 105) before allocating anything; `docs/THREADING.md` states the rule. Moving the state per handler is plan task 13.2 (the rest of F-BUG-215). (3) `analyse_incoming_packet()` logs a NOT_VALID existing instance (Error 41) and eliminates it like any final result; `keep_state` re-creates only a satisfied instance (`COUNT_SATISFIED_ELIMINATE`) — the #510 follow-up — so the `keep_state` scenario that expires at 4 s now pins 1 satisfied + 1 not satisfied (was 1 + 2, "current behaviour"); that is the only expectation changed in `test_rule_eval.c`. (4) Embedded functions: the library is opened once and each symbol resolved when the rule loads (`embedded_funct_resolve()`, `rule.funct`); the call goes through the exact `void *f(void *, ...)` signature of its 0..4 parameters with unused slots NULL; `#f()` is now a zero-argument call (it used to pass one uninitialised argument); a NULL result fails the condition instead of ending the process (Error 123). (5) LP64: U32 and IP values are read as `uint32_t`/bytes in `get_my_data()`, `compare_in_table()`, `compare_values()`, `compute()` (whose U32 result is computed and stored in 64 bits, the 8 bytes `verify_segment()` copies as `MMT_U64_DATA`), `get_xdata()` and `store_history()`. (6) Reactions: the folder is `MMT_SEC_REACTION_DIR` when it holds an absolute path, else `$(MMT_BASE)/probe/conf` baked in by `rules/common.mk` (make variable `MMT_SEC_REACTION_DIR_DEFAULT`, named apart from the environment variable so an exported run-time value is not baked in; a stamp recompiles `tips_report.o` when it changes, like `plugins_engine.o`); the folder is quoted as one shell word in the command; `detection_<n>.data` is created with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` and mode 0600, and a number whose file already exists — a planted symlink included — is skipped, so the script's first argument stays the file's number. Carried over from #509/#510: NOT as the right son of a root THEN no longer crashes — `verify_not_backtrack()` stops at the rule instance instead of climbing to its `next` instance or a NULL father — and is pinned as "current behaviour" (the NOT node's no-delay timer reads as expired on the next frame, so even B satisfies it). Left as found: a root AND/OR that validates on a fresh instance still hits the "Should not be here2" exit in `analyse_incoming_packet()`, and a rule instance does not copy `type_rule`, so `detection_<n>.data` always starts with "attack". No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #510.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`init_sec_lib`, `engine_state`, `analyse_incoming_packet`), `src/mmt_security/tips_xml.c` (`processNode`, `apply_attribute`, `attach_to_father`, `create_boolean_expression`, `climb_with_operator`, `funct_check_call`, `validate_rule_tree`, `read_rules`, `free_rules`), `src/mmt_security/tips_extract.c` (`embedded_funct_resolve`, `funct_get_params_and_execute`, the LP64 reads), `src/mmt_security/tips_report.c` (`reaction_dir`, `open_detection_file`, `generate_command`), `src/mmt_security/tips_eval.c` (`verify_not_backtrack`), `rules/common.mk` and `sdk/Makefile` (`MMT_SEC_REACTION_DIR`), `tests/rule_engine/run_tests.sh` (steps 4 and 8), `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_engine.c` (`load-error`, `reinit`, `race`), `tests/rule_engine/test_rule_eval.c`, `tests/rule_engine/malformed/*.xml`, `tests/rule_engine/eval/{and_root,then_not,embedded_funct,reaction}.xml`, `tests/rule_engine/embedded_functions.c`, `docs/THREADING.md`.

- Q: Issue #501 (plan task 3.3, F-BUG-004/F-BUG-008/F-BUG-017) — handlers may unregister attribute handlers from inside their callbacks, but the dispatch loops kept using the attribute that unregistration freed and skipped the handler that shifted into the closed slot; `fire_attribute_event()` left `attr->data` pointing at the caller's stack local after the dispatch; and `unregister_attribute_handler()` decremented `registration_count` even for a handler that joined an attribute already registered with `register_extraction_attribute()`, releasing that registration. Defer the free, clear the payload, record the owner?
  A: All three. (1) Both dispatch loops (`fire_attribute_event()`, `proto_process_attribute_handlers()`) run inside a per-handler bracket, `mmt_handler_t.attr_dispatch_depth`; an attribute released while it is open is unlinked from the arrays at once but chained on `attr_free_pending` (through the otherwise unused `attribute_internal_t.next`) and freed when the outermost dispatch returns, and `mmt_close_handler()` flushes the chain too. After each callback the loops re-derive where to continue instead of stepping an index. The handler loops record the successor (the handler function at the next slot; functions are unique per attribute) before the callback and continue from wherever it sits afterwards; a handler that was last ends the walk, so handlers its callback registers (inserted at the head) wait for the next dispatch. So while the successor stays registered nothing after it is skipped: a callback that unregisters itself or handlers other than its successor, or unregisters and re-registers itself (the only way to change its `user_args`), runs once and the remaining handlers each run once. When the successor itself left, the loop falls back to the slot after the handler just run, or to that same slot when that handler left too; and a successor re-registered to the head is resumed there. Those cases can replay or skip handlers when the same call also re-registered handlers or removed ones that already ran; edge cases left as found. Each handler loop is therefore bounded by an iteration budget — the handler count when it started, raised whenever the count grows past its high-water value during the dispatch (`dispatch_budget_update`) — so it always terminates: before the successor rule a lone handler re-registering itself, or two handlers swapping each other in, looped forever. The element loop continues at the first element whose field_id is greater than the one just run (the elements stay sorted by field_id, one per field of the protocol). Every element therefore runs at most once per dispatch, a slot released and re-created for the same field is not replayed, an element present when the walk reaches its field runs whatever was added or removed before it, and an element added below the current field waits for the next packet. field_id strictly increases every iteration, so that walk needs no budget: an element budget skipped an untouched later element when a callback moved a handler from an element already run to a new field ahead. (2) `fire_attribute_event()` sets `attr->data` to the payload before each handler and to NULL after the dispatch; both extraction paths re-arm a NULL `data` to the attribute's own scratch area (right after the struct), so an event fired on an extractable attribute cannot leave its next extraction writing through a dead pointer. Every event site, including the HTTP parser callbacks and the IPv4/IPv6 RTT events, goes through that function. (3) `attribute_internal_t.extraction_owner` records the handler whose registration created the slot (the only handler registration that counts in `registration_count`); unregistering any other handler leaves the count alone, and the slot is released once both counts are zero. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #510.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`rearm_attribute_scratch`, `attr_dispatch_enter`/`attr_dispatch_leave`, `next_attribute_handler`, `next_attribute_element`, `dispatch_budget_update`, `fire_attribute_event`, `proto_process_attribute_handlers`, `generic_data_extraction`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`release_registered_attribute`, `flush_deferred_attribute_frees`, `release_extraction_registration`, `unregister_attribute_handler`, `register_attribute_handler`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h`, `tests/core_engine/test_core_engine.c` (`test_attr_dispatch_safety`), `docs/MMT-Attributes.md`.

- Q: Issue #505 (plan task 4.2, F-BUG-007/F-BUG-013/F-BUG-014/F-BUG-015/F-BUG-019/F-BUG-107, F-DOCS-005) — six helpers lacked a guard: `mmt_header_line_pointer_snprintf()` wrote `buff[-1]` for `len <= 0`, `str_replace()` called `strcat(…, NULL)` on adjacent matches, `_is_registered_protocol()` read through NULL registry slots, `init_http_parser()` used its allocation unchecked, `clean_packet()` freed a reassembled datagram only on `DLT_EN10MB`, and one `static int seen` backed the U32 and U8 TCP connection events. `docs/Memory-Management.md` also told contributors not to check allocations, contradicting `src/mmt_core/src/memory.c:8-17`. What does each guard do, and where are the regression tests?
  A: (1) The header-line formatter returns -1 for a NULL buffer or attribute or `len <= 0`, like the other `*_snprintf` formatters, and prints an empty string for a line with no data. (2) `str_replace()` skips the empty gap between adjacent matches and returns NULL when its prefix copy cannot be allocated. (3) A NULL slot (before `init_extraction()`, after `close_extraction()`, or past the slot where an out-of-memory `init_extraction()` stopped) is unregistered, and registering into one returns NULL instead of crashing in `init_protocol_struct()`. The edits keep `packet_registry.c` and `packet_pipeline.c` at their line counts, so their `path:line` citations do not move. (4) `init_http_parser()` returns NULL and frees whatever it made when any of its three allocations fails; the session keeps `session_data` NULL, which the analysis and cleanup functions already skip. (5) `clean_packet()` frees `ipacket->data` whenever it differs from `original_data`. Only IPv4/IPv6 fragment reassembly replaces it, always with an `mmt_malloc()` buffer, on any link stack. (6) Each fire site passes a block-scope literal of the declared width: `(uint32_t){1}` for `tcp.syn_received` and `(uint8_t){1}` for `tcp.established` and `tcp.closed`. A handler that writes through the payload no longer changes later events, and the U8 events no longer read 0 on a big-endian host. The memory page now states the NULL-return contract and the caller's duties; the rest of the page is unchanged (allocation-failure scope only). Test placement: `str_replace` cases are in `test_mmt_utils.c`. The formatter needs `packet_processing.c`, which links on its own with two stubs, so the `mmt_utils` suite builds a second binary, `test_attr_snprintf`. `packet_registry.c` needs most of the core, so the NULL-slot lookups run in `core_engine` after its final `close_extraction()`. The HTTP-parser sweep is a unit-level fault-injection binary that runs in every profile (the engine leg is skipped under sanitizers). The leak harness replays the fragment captures as Linux cooked frames on the `PROTO_SLL` stack. The event payload test is the `parser_boundaries` fixture `tcp-events`. Each new test failed on the unfixed sources: SEGV, ASan negative-size-param, SEGV, SEGV, LSan 36,792 bytes in 18 blocks, and 2 bad events per U8 event respectively. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #510.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c` (`mmt_header_line_pointer_snprintf`), `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_utils.c` (`str_replace`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`_is_registered_protocol`, `get_protocol_struct_for_registration_if_free`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/http_parser_integration.h` (`init_http_parser`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`clean_packet`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h`, `docs/Memory-Management.md`, `tests/mmt_utils/test_attr_snprintf.c`, `tests/mmt_utils/test_mmt_utils.c` (`test_str_replace_adjacent_edges`), `tests/core_engine/test_core_engine.c` (`test_null_slot_lookups`), `tests/fault_injection/test_fi_http.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/leak_regression_test.c` (`non_en10mb_reassembly`), `tests/parser_boundaries/test_tcp_event_payload_api.c`.

- Q: Issue #506 (plan task 4.3, F-PERF-103/F-PERF-109) — the DNS queries/answers/auth-records, FTP request/parameter/response-value and NDN string extractors returned heap objects through `attribute_t.data` that nothing freed, one leak per read, and `mmt_check_ndn_payload()` leaked its TLV root on every match. Who owns an extracted heap value, and when is it freed?
  A: The handler that processed the packet. A new public call, `mmt_packet_scratch_adopt(ipacket, value, release)` (`plugin_defs.h`; `mmt_opaque_t`/`bool` per the weak-type ratchet), chains the value on a per-handler list whose records are bump-allocated from an `mmt_arena_t` created on first use; `packet_process()` releases the list and rewinds the arena before it hands the next packet over, and `mmt_close_handler()` releases it and frees the arena (through `free_registered_extraction_attributes()`). So a value is valid while its packet is processed and its handlers run, consumers must not free it, and a handler that never reads such an attribute pays one pointer test per packet. The reset sits in `packet_process()`, the only caller of `mmt->process_packet`, rather than at a per-attribute "previous value" slot: an attribute can be read several times per packet (each read of a packet-scope attribute re-extracts it), and an unregistered attribute is freed between packets, so a per-attribute owner would either leak the earlier reads or write through a freed attribute. A per-packet arena for the parsed objects themselves (the issue's other suggestion) would mean rewriting the DNS/NDN parsers' allocations; the parsers keep `malloc()` and the extractor hands over the result with its matching release (`dns_free_query_list`/`dns_free_answer_list`, `free`). An extractor sets `data` only when the adoption succeeds; on failure the value is already released and the attribute reads as not extracted. `ftp_get_command_str()` returned the literal `"UNKNOWN_CMD"` on two paths and now returns a heap copy so its result can always be freed. NDN error exits that leaked parsed TLV nodes are fixed alongside (`ndn_TLV_parser_name_comp()` on a non-name-component TLV, and the content, key-locator, signature-value and exclude extractors when the node is absent). Pinned by the leak-gate case `extractor_heap_attrs` (all thirteen attributes read twice per packet over `dns_query.pcap`, the three FTP captures and crafted NDN and DNS frames; on the unfixed sources Valgrind reports 26 definite-loss records) and `tests/ndn_dissector` (`test_issue506_error_exits`, adoption checks). `phase0_throughput` gained an opt-in `PHASE0_ATTRS` that reads the named attributes and prints the live RSS per iteration: over a 22,320-packet FTP/DNS mix and 30 iterations the library RSS went 5,344 → 12,640 KiB on the unfixed build and 5,092 → 5,104 KiB with the fix. Behaviour change for embedders: a caller that freed one of these values must stop (it now double-frees), and one that kept a value past the packet must copy it. The security engine's `store_tuples()` kept a `MMT_STRING_DATA_POINTER` reference's pointer in the rule instance (#326), so a rule referencing one of these FTP/NDN strings from an earlier event would read a released value (a use-after-free where it used to be a leak; session strings such as `ftp.user_name` already dangled once the session expired): it now copies the string, terminator included, into the rule instance, so a reference outlives the packet (and the session) it came from; the comparison and substitution readers (`get_data_from_pcap()`, `get_value()`) and embedded-function parameters use that copy. Pinned by `test_reference_string_outlives_packet` in `tests/rule_engine/test_overflow_family.c` (the unfixed engine reports a heap-use-after-free there under `SANITIZE=asan`). No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #505.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`packet_process`, `mmt_packet_scratch_adopt`, `mmt_packet_scratch_release`, `mmt_packet_scratch_destroy`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`free_registered_extraction_attributes`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h`, `src/mmt_core/public_include/plugin_defs.h`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_dns.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_ftp.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ndn.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/leak_regression_test.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_leak_regression_test.sh`, `tools/phase0/phase0_throughput.c`, `tests/ndn_dissector/test_ndn_dissector.c`, `docs/MMT-Attributes.md`, `docs/Memory-Management.md`.

- Q: Issue #513 (plan task 5.5, F-DEP-302 part 1 of 3) — the S1AP/NGAP APER runtime in `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/common/` is the 2020 skeleton of the pinned generator (`mouse07410/asn1c @ dbd0ed09`) and carries GHSA-x882-jvjm-xxh9 (`INTEGER_decode_aper()` never bounded a constrained INTEGER's length determinant: a signed shift by up to 56 places and over-long encodings accepted) and GHSA-rcj5-gcvg-x796 (`OPEN_TYPE_*_get()` formed `NULL + offset` for an unallocated member). The generated tree must never be hand-edited and the `asn1c-regen.yml` gate must stay byte-identical; how is the fix carried without the full generator upgrade (#521)?
  A: As patches the regeneration recipe applies. `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/patches/*.patch` are unified diffs against the generated `common/` runtime (each with a header naming the advisory and the upstream fix it backports); `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile` applies them, sorted, at the end of both `gen-s1ap` and `gen-ngap` — both targets rewrite `common/`, and the pinned asn1c overwrites the skeletons, so the second target re-applies them; a patch that is already applied is skipped and one that does not apply exactly (`--fuzz=0`) fails the target, so a pin bump that makes a patch obsolete is loud. The committed `common/INTEGER.c` and `common/OPEN_TYPE.c` are the recipe's output, regenerated with the pinned asn1c in the gate's `buildpack-deps:bookworm` image; every other generated file is byte-identical, and `asn1c-regen.yml` now also runs when `patches/**` changes. The backport is targeted, not upstream's rewritten `INTEGER_aper.c`: reject `range_bits` wider than the `unsigned long` accumulator, reject a length above `max_range_bytes` before aligning or reading payload, accumulate unsigned and add the lower bound in unsigned arithmetic, so every conformant length decodes to the same value as before. The OPEN_TYPE patch fails the decode when `*memb_ptr2` is NULL, the selector returns no type descriptor, or its index exceeds the open type's `elements_count`, in the BER, XER, UPER and APER `_get` functions; every open type in the committed tree is `ATF_NOFLAGS` (S1AP r10.5.0 carries IE values as ANY, NGAP members are embedded), so the NULL-member branch is defence in depth and the selector checks are what the regression reaches. S1AP reaches the INTEGER flaw through BitRate (34-bit range, up to 8 octets declared for 5; signed overshift) and eNB-UE-S1AP-ID (24-bit range, 4 declared for 3; over-long acceptance), the latter in a field `s1ap_decode()` reads: the unfixed decoder returned 0 with `enb_ue_id` 1 for a 4-octet encoding. Regression: the `s1ap-integer` fixture of `parser_boundaries` (`tests/parser_boundaries/test_s1ap_integer_length.c`, linked like `s1ap_ngap_decode` against `libmmt_tmobile.a` whole-archive) — red on the unfixed runtime (UBSan abort under `SANITIZE=asan`, failed checks and a SEGV without it), green after. The `generated_asn1c` coverage cohort excludes the patched files as before. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/patches/0001-INTEGER-aper-length-determinant.patch`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/patches/0002-OPEN_TYPE-unallocated-member.patch`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile` (`apply-patches`), `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/common/INTEGER.c` (`INTEGER_decode_aper`), `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/common/OPEN_TYPE.c`, `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, `tests/parser_boundaries/run_tests.sh`, `tests/parser_boundaries/test_s1ap_integer_length.c`, mouse07410/asn1c GHSA-x882-jvjm-xxh9 (fixed in v1.5.2, ce45145ad4) and GHSA-rcj5-gcvg-x796 (e9600cf7f2).

- Q: Issue #495 (plan task 2.2, F-DEP-003/F-DEP-101/F-DEP-304) — the build-dependency lists contradicted the code (an HTTP/2 library probed, linked and installed though nothing calls it; libxml2 installed by `install.sh` for a profile that never builds the engines; libpcap declared as if a library needed it) and five files carried them with no single source. What does each list hold now, how is it kept honest, and what did the W2 patch/minor batch move?
  A: (1) nghttp2 is gone: no source calls an `nghttp2_*` function (the only mention is a commented include in `src/examples/extract_all.c`) and HTTP/2 is the in-tree dissector `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/http2.c`, so the pkg-config/linker probe and the `NGHTTP2_CFLAGS`/`NGHTTP2_LIBS` uses leave `rules/common.mk` and `rules/common-linux.mk`, and every `install.sh` branch drops its nghttp2 package. `objdump -p` showed no NEEDED entry before (`--as-needed` dropped the flag) and shows none after. The removed make lines are replaced by explanatory comments of the same length, so the many `rules/*.mk:N-M` citations `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` checks by boundary stay valid; `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` keeps its soname map (a mapper, not a declaration). (2) libpcap is examples-only: no SDK library includes `<pcap.h>` or links `-lpcap` (`src/mmt_security/public_defs.h` no longer includes it, so the old install-build-deps rationale was stale). It stays where a capture-reading program is compiled — `install.sh` (the documented first run compiles `hello_packet.c -lpcap`, exercised by `install-smoke.yml`), `tools/ci/install-build-deps.sh` (the installed-consumer check in `build-package.sh`), the CI bootstrap action and the toolchain line — and leaves the rpm `BuildRequires:`, which only rpmbuild reads. The EL8/EL9 CRB enablement therefore stays in both installers. (3) libxml2 is ENABLESEC-only: `install.sh` builds the default profile and drops it; the package build (`ENABLESEC=1`), `BuildRequires:`, the bootstrap action and the toolchain line keep it. (4) One source: `tools/ci/build-deps.txt` holds `role <role> <manager> <pkg>...` rows (build, enablesec, examples, package, verify, devtools) and `list <id> <manager> <role>...` rows; `tools/ci/check-dependency-lists.sh` extracts eleven lists from the five files (six `install.sh` branches, the deb/rpm functions of `install-build-deps.sh` minus the best-effort `|| true` dnf-plugins-core helper, `BuildRequires:` with version floors stripped, the bootstrap action without its `${{ inputs.extra-packages }}` expression, and the `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` toolchain line) and fails unless each equals the union of its roles; a list found zero or two times, an unknown list id or an undefined role also fails. The canonical apt build set is `build-essential gcc g++ make git`, which added `gcc` to the deb container list and `g++` to the toolchain line. `BuildRequires:` is a literal list checked by that lint; only the deb `Depends:` and the rpm `Requires:` are derived by `shlib-deps.sh` (the #219 wording is narrowed accordingly). `tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-lists.sh` runs the real lint on fixture trees where one list or manifest row diverges (run by the `release_gates` suite, so the suite count stays 30; also a blocking `verify-helpers` step). (5) Action pins: the five SHA-pinned actions a patch/minor behind their latest same-major release on 2026-10-22 move to the peeled commit of that release, each with its `# vX.Y.Z` comment — `actions/download-artifact` v8.0.1 → v8.0.2, `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.1 → v7.0.2, `anchore/sbom-action` v0.24.2 → v0.24.3, `github/codeql-action` (init/analyze) v4.38.0 → v4.39.1, `ruby/setup-ruby` v1.325.0 → v1.332.0; the others were current. (6) Gems: `bundle lock --update --minor --strict` (Bundler 2.6.9, `ruby:3.3`, Gemfile unchanged) — `--strict` keeps `public_suffix` on 6.x, its major belongs to the P2 modernize wave; jekyll-relative-links 0.8.0 → 0.9.1, listen 3.10.0 → 3.10.1, rubyzip 3.6.0 → 3.7.0, sass-embedded 1.102.0 → 1.106.1, google-protobuf 4.35.1 → 4.36.2 (plus its declared rake/bigdecimal dependencies), and the `aarch64-linux-gnu` variants the lock's existing `aarch64-linux` platform was missing. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1.
  Source: `tools/ci/build-deps.txt`, `tools/ci/check-dependency-lists.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-lists.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`), `rules/common.mk`, `rules/common-linux.mk`, `install.sh` (`install_deps_linux`), `tools/ci/install-build-deps.sh`, `sdk/Makefile` (rpm target), `.github/actions/bootstrap-deps/action.yml`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1, `docs/Gemfile.lock`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`.
- Q: Issue #497 (plan task 2.4, F-CI-109) — the packages shipped no LICENSE or third-party notices, the RPM spec said `License: proprietary` (sdk/Makefile rpm target), and the one package bundled the GPL engine `libmmt_security`. What licence does each package declare, from which evidence, and how are the engines split out? The answers of `debian/license-audit.md` §3 are not on `main` (it lives in the #475 working tree), so they were not used.
  A: The expressions come only from evidence in this tree, and `python3 tools/ci/vendored-components.py notices --check` (verify-helpers job and the release build job) re-derives them, failing on drift. Montimage's own code is Apache-2.0: `LICENSE` and the README's licence section. Every `src/mmt_security/*.c` (5 of 5) carries the "GPL version 3 … or (at your option) any later version" notice, so `libmmt_security` is GPL-3.0-or-later; the check fails if one of them loses it. `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/*` carries no notice at all, so the repository `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0) is the only evidence for `libmmt_fuzz`. The vendored components of `tools/ci/vendored-components.json` (#496) are llhttp (MIT, in `libmmt_tcpip`) and the asn1c runtime (BSD-2-Clause, in `libmmt_tmobile`), and none of them lies in the engine sources (the check enforces this). That gives `mmt-dpi` = `Apache-2.0 AND BSD-2-Clause AND MIT` (its own licence AND every manifest licence) and `mmt-dpi-security` = `GPL-3.0-or-later AND Apache-2.0`, recorded as `PKG_LICENSE`/`SEC_PKG_LICENSE` in `sdk/Makefile`. `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` is generated from the manifest (`notices`) and committed, so packaging needs no Python. It holds the package licence table and, per component, the upstream, the pin, the paths, the copyright line and the full licence text from `LICENSES/<id>.txt`: MIT is llhttp's upstream wording, BSD-2-Clause the SPDX text, and GPL-3.0-or-later the FSF GPL-3 text (sha256 `3972dc97…`). The engines move to their own package, `mmt-dpi-security`: the staged `libmmt_security*`/`libmmt_fuzz*` (libraries, links, archives), `dpi/include/fuzz` and the GPL text as `COPYING`. It requires exactly the same `mmt-dpi` version, because `libmmt_security` resolves 12 `libmmt_core` symbols at load time (`nm -D`; rules/common-linux.mk -z,defs note) — NEEDED does not show that dependency, and the first plan, which assumed a standalone package, was dropped for this reason. `check-package-deps.sh --verify-package` requires that one sibling entry and exempts only it from the NEEDED-backing checks. The deb carries an unversioned `Replaces: mmt-dpi`, so it takes over the files a pre-split `mmt-dpi` owns: the revision is a git hash, so a version bound cannot order. Both install orders were tested in ubuntu:24.04. Each package installs `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` under `<prefix>/dpi/share/doc/<package>/` (one directory per package, so no path has two owners). `make install`, the ZIP installer and `install.sh` (for a pinned tag whose Makefile predates this) lay down the same files, and the install manifest records them, so `make uninstall`/`install.sh --uninstall` remove them. The RPM main `%files` names its libraries instead of globbing `/opt/mmt/*`, because the glob plus `%exclude` still generated build-id links for the engine libraries in the main package. `_rpmfilename` uses `%%{NAME}` because a plain `%{NAME}` expands to the main name and the security RPM overwrote it. `tools/ci/check-package-split.sh` (run by `build-package.sh` on every matrix row, and by the installer suite on the `.deb`) checks the licence files, the split and each `.rpm`'s `%{LICENSE}`. The release workflow expects the two packages per row and writes an SBOM for each; the vendored components are added to the `mmt-dpi` SBOM only. **Open question, not resolved here:** the S1AP/NGAP codecs under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/{s1ap,ngap}` are asn1c output generated from 3GPP ASN.1 modules (TS 36.413, TS 38.413; `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/support/README`), and nothing in this repository records the terms of those specifications. The expressions above therefore make no claim about them, and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` says so. Adding a `LicenseRef-` term needs the maintainer's licence review (the #475 audit) and goes through `tools/ci/vendored-components.json` and `notices`. The issue's AC names an "Apache-2.0-only assertion at tests/installer/run_tests.sh:154-155"; no such assertion exists on `main`, so the suite gains licence-field assertions and none is removed. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1.
  Source: `LICENSE`, `README.md` (License), `src/mmt_security/*.c` (headers), `src/mmt_fuzz_engine/*`, `tools/ci/vendored-components.json`, `tools/ci/vendored-components.py` (`notices`), `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES`, `LICENSES/`, `sdk/Makefile` (`PKG_LICENSE`, install, `--private-prepare-build-dir`, deb, rpm, zip, clean), `rules/common-linux.mk` (-z,defs note), `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/check-package-split.sh`, `tools/ci/build-package.sh`, `tools/ci/check-reproducible-build.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml`, `install.sh`, `dist/ZIP/install.sh`, `tests/installer/run_tests.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-vendored-components.py`.
- Q: Issue #498 (plan task 2.5, F-CI-112/F-UX-005) — how does `curl -fsSL https://github.com/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/releases/latest/download/install.sh | sh` install the native package verifiably, without cutting a release to test it?
  A: (1) The release job runs `tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh "$GITHUB_REF_NAME" release` before `SHA256SUMS` and `attest-build-provenance`: every versioned package gets a byte copy under the stable alias `<package>_<distro>_<amd64|arm64>.<ext>`, `manifest.json` lists distro, arch, package, format, alias, versioned source and sha256, and `install.sh` is `tools/ci/bootstrap-install.sh` rendered for the tag with the same rows embedded (one source for both). The generator fails closed on an unparseable name, a version other than the tag's, a distribution the bootstrap does not map, a wrong format, a duplicate alias, a security package without its SDK package, or existing outputs. The attestation step's `bundle-path` is published as `mmt-dpi.intoto.jsonl`; it is the one asset `SHA256SUMS` does not list, because it is signed itself. `publish-release.sh` is unchanged (it uploads `release/*`; idempotency stays with #595). (2) The bootstrap is POSIX `sh` (dash has no `pipefail`, so digests are compared as values).
