It maps `/etc/os-release` to a matrix distro (Ubuntu 22.04/24.04, Debian 12, Rocky/Alma/RHEL 9 → `rocky-9`, CentOS Stream 9), downloads, checks every sha256 before anything else, and then verifies provenance offline: `gh attestation verify <pkg> --bundle mmt-dpi.intoto.jsonl --repo montimage-projects/mmt-dpi --signer-workflow …/release-packages.yml --source-ref refs/tags/<tag> --deny-self-hosted-runners`, in a scrubbed environment (no token, `GH_HOST`, `GH_REPO`; `HOME`/XDG dirs in the temp dir). Only then does it run `apt-get update` + `apt-get install`, or `dnf install`, so dependencies and the loader drop-in come from the package. There is no skip option. `MMT_BOOTSTRAP_BASE_URL` may point at a mirror or a `file://` copy: the digests are embedded and the provenance is checked against Sigstore, not the mirror. A custom `MMT_BASE` is refused with a pointer to the source installer. Trust anchor: an unverified `curl | sh` trusts the release's `install.sh` over TLS; the verify-first path (`gh attestation verify install.sh`) is documented in README. (3) The GitHub CLI is the verifier, not cosign: the `--bundle` check needs no login (probed on v1.9.1: rc 0 for the released `.deb`, rc 1 for a modified file and for `--source-ref refs/tags/v1.9.0`), and its tarball is ~15 MB against ~135 MB for cosign. The pin is `cli/cli` v2.101.0 (2026-09-15), `gh_2.101.0_linux_amd64.tar.gz` 9bca2d1c…072b8 and `_arm64` b57e8063…5640f, taken from `gh_2.101.0_checksums.txt` and checked against the API asset digests and `gh attestation verify -R cli/cli`. The maintainer bumps it by editing `GH_VERSION`/`GH_SHA256_*` in `tools/ci/bootstrap-install.sh` after the same three checks. The pin is recorded here rather than in a manifest lint (no existing lint tracks downloaded release binaries). (4) Testing without a release: `tools/ci/tests/test-release-bootstrap.sh` (release_gates suite, offline) covers the generator and the bootstrap's detection, lookup, digest check, refusals and identity flags. `bootstrap-dry-run` in `release-packages.yml` (behind `packages-gate`) renders the set from the PR's full package matrix. `.github/workflows/bootstrap-smoke.yml` builds a fixture from the latest published release's real packages and real Sigstore bundle (`tools/ci/make-bootstrap-fixture.sh`) and runs `tools/ci/smoke-bootstrap.sh` in ubuntu:24.04, debian:12 and rockylinux:9: three tampered legs must exit with the sha256 or attestation refusal and install nothing (package changed after rendering; changed before rendering; bootstrap pinned to another tag), then the one-liner installs, `ldconfig -p` lists `libmmt_core`, and the first run matches with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` unset, starting from cold apt metadata. Scheduled and post-release runs use the published one-liner once the latest release is newer than v1.9.1. For such a release a missing bootstrap asset fails the run, and a failure opens the "Bootstrap smoke failing" issue. (5) First-run prerequisite (F-UX-005): `docs/_includes/first-example.md` names them in prose — `tests/docs_onboarding` requires exactly three displayed commands. The commands are `sudo apt-get install gcc libpcap-dev` and `sudo dnf --enablerepo=crb install gcc libpcap-devel` (Rocky/Alma/CentOS Stream 9; RHEL 9 enables `codeready-builder-for-rhel-9-$(arch)-rpms` via `subscription-manager`). The hero step-3 comment names gcc and libpcap-dev / libpcap-devel. The prerequisite list is a user-facing first-run line, not a build-dependency list, so it stays out of `tools/ci/build-deps.txt`. The issue's v1.9.2 AC (`gh release view v1.9.2` lists the assets) can only be checked at the next release; the release checklist step 4 now names the bootstrap assets. The exact asset count stays with #603.
  Source: `tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh`, `tools/ci/bootstrap-install.sh`, `tools/ci/make-bootstrap-fixture.sh`, `tools/ci/smoke-bootstrap.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-release-bootstrap.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (release, bootstrap-dry-run, packages-gate), `.github/workflows/bootstrap-smoke.yml`, `tools/ci/check-release-verification.sh`, `docs/_includes/first-example.md`, `docs/index.html` (hero step 3), `README.md`, `docs/Prepare-for-a-new-released-version.md`.

## 2026-10-22

- Q: Issue #517 (plan task 6.4, F-DEP-007/F-DEP-006) — Ruby 3.3, which builds the documentation site in the PR gate and on Pages, is security-only until 2027-03-31; ten gems of the Jekyll 4 graph have had no release in three years or more. Which Ruby runs the docs toolchain now, and are the unmaintained gems replaced or accepted?
  A: (1) F-DEP-007: Ruby 3.4 (4.0 is out of scope). The three `ruby/setup-ruby` steps — `c-cpp.yml` verify-helpers and site-onboarding, `pages.yml` build — say `ruby-version: "3.4"`, and `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` now fails when any workflow's `ruby-version:` differs from the release `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1 states. `bundler-cache: true` keys its cache on the Ruby version, so the first 3.4 run misses the 3.3 cache and `bundle install` compiles eventmachine 1.2.7's native extension against the 3.4 ABI in the same job, as every later lock change does. Migration review against the Ruby 3.4 release notes: the gems 3.4 moves from default to bundled (`base64`, `bigdecimal`, `csv` among them) are already resolved in `docs/Gemfile.lock` (Jekyll 4.4 declares `base64`/`csv`, google-protobuf `bigdecimal`), so Bundler loads them without a warning; chilled string literals only warn under `-W:deprecated`; the Prism default parser, the `Hash#inspect` format and the quoting change in error messages do not reach the site output. Evidence, `ruby:3.4` (3.4.11) and `ruby:3.3` (3.3.12) containers on this commit's tree: `bundle lock` under 3.4 with the locked Bundler 2.6.9 rewrites `docs/Gemfile.lock` byte-identically (the graph has no Ruby-version-dependent resolution, so the regenerated lock is the committed one); `bash tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh` under `BUNDLE_FROZEN=true` passes on both (Jekyll 4.4.1, 57 pages, 78 files). The two `site_sha256` values differ only through `assets/css/style.css.map`, whose `sources` name the random temporary directory jekyll-remote-theme unpacks cayman into; every other file is byte-identical, so the digest moves on any two builds and a change to it is read file by file, never as a renderer change. (2) F-DEP-006 accepted, not replaced: forwardable-extended 2.6.0 (2016), colorator 1.1.0 (2016), eventmachine 1.2.7 (2018), pathutil 0.16.2 (2018), safe_yaml 1.0.5 (2019), jekyll-watch 2.2.1 (2019), kramdown-parser-gfm 1.1.0 (2019), mercenary 0.4.0 (2020), em-websocket 0.5.3 (2021) and rb-fsevent 0.11.2 (2022) — last releases per the RubyGems API on 2026-10-22, each the newest published version — are all required by Jekyll 4.4.1 itself (directly, or through em-websocket, listen and pathutil), which has no successor (4.4.1 is the latest Jekyll). The risk is bounded: the gems run only in the docs build, on trusted input (this repository's Markdown and the pinned cayman theme), never in the SDK or its packages; eventmachine/em-websocket serve only `jekyll serve --livereload`, which CI never runs, and rb-fsevent is macOS-only. Trigger to revisit: a Jekyll 5 release (or a Jekyll 4.x release that drops one of these dependencies) — then move the Gemfile to it and re-run this review; a published advisory against any of the ten is caught earlier by `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh` (gems surface).
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (verify-helpers, site-onboarding), `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1 (Documentation-site toolchain), `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` (build/test contract), `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh`, `docs/Gemfile.lock`, https://www.ruby-lang.org/en/news/2024/12/25/ruby-3-4-0-released/, https://www.ruby-lang.org/en/downloads/branches/.

- Q: Issue #519 (plan task 7.1, F-DEP-004) — the docs graph locked `public_suffix` 6.0.2 although `addressable (2.9.0)` already allows `< 8.0`. Does 7.x need a migration, and what does it change in the built site?
  A: No migration; nothing in the site changes. Spike, from the upstream CHANGELOG (weppos/publicsuffix-ruby) 6.0.2 → 7.0.5: 7.0.0 (2025-11-17) raises the minimum Ruby to 3.2 and updates the list definitions — its only breaking change, met by the Ruby 3.4 of #517; 7.0.1 and 7.0.3 update the definitions; 7.0.2 drops symlinks and unneeded files from the gem package; 7.0.4/7.0.5 fix the release workflow and Rakefile (7.0.3 and 7.0.4 were never published to RubyGems, which lists 7.0.0, 7.0.1, 7.0.2, 7.0.5). No public API is removed or renamed. `bundle lock --update public_suffix --conservative` (Bundler 2.6.9, `ruby:3.4`) changes one lock line, `public_suffix (6.0.2)` → `(7.0.5)`; no other gem moves. The only consumer is addressable (URI handling in Jekyll and jekyll-remote-theme). Two frozen `bash tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh` runs on the same commit, one per lock, produce byte-identical sites except `build-manifest.json` (it records the lock digest) and `assets/css/style.css.map`, whose only difference is the random jekyll-remote-theme unpack directory (#517 entry). So the `site_sha256` change this PR shows comes from that map and, across commits, from `site.github.build_revision` — the source commit stamped into every page's `style.css?v=` — not from public_suffix.
  Source: `docs/Gemfile.lock`, `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh`, https://github.com/weppos/publicsuffix-ruby/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md, https://rubygems.org/gems/public_suffix/versions.

- Q: Issue #520 (plan task 7.2, F-DEP-005) — `BUNDLED WITH 2.6.9` pins the Bundler that `ruby/setup-ruby` installs for the docs jobs while 4.0.x is current. What does Bundler 4 remove or change for this toolchain, and does the frozen install still hold?
  A: Bundler 4.0.23 (2026-10-14, the latest 4.0.x), set with `bundle update --bundler=4.0.23` under `ruby:3.4`: the only lock change is the `BUNDLED WITH` line; no gem moves. Spike, from the RubyGems/Bundler 4.0.0 CHANGELOG (ruby/rubygems, Bundler "Breaking changes"; the repository's `doc/UPGRADING.md` holds only the upgrade recipe): every remembered CLI flag is removed (`bundle install --path/--deployment/--frozen/--without/...` — this repository passes none: `build-docs-site.sh` sets `BUNDLE_FROZEN=true` in the environment and runs plain `bundle check`/`bundle install`/`bundle exec`, and `ruby/setup-ruby` v1.332.0 uses `bundle config set --local path|deployment`, which Bundler 4 keeps); `bundle viz`/`bundle inject`, the `allow_offline_install` setting, multiple global sources and the Capistrano/Vlad `deployment` entrypoints are removed (none used); `cache_all` and `lockfile_checksums` default to true and the patchlevel is hidden from `RUBY VERSION`; frozen mode now fails when a `CHECKSUMS` section has an empty registry checksum; the default install path and global cache changes were deferred to Bundler 5. Lockfile: `lockfile_checksums` writes a `CHECKSUMS` section only into a newly generated lock — updating an existing one, as here, adds none, so the lock keeps its form and `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh`'s parser (which skips that section anyway) is unaffected; adding checksums (`bundle lock --add-checksums`) is a separate integrity decision, not taken here. Bundler 4 requires Ruby ≥ 3.2 (`ruby/setup-ruby` falls back to Bundler 2 below that), met by #517's Ruby 3.4. Verified in `ruby:3.4` with `BUNDLE_FROZEN=true`: the `ruby/setup-ruby` sequence (`gem install bundler -v 4.0.23`, `bundle config set --local path`/`deployment true`, `bundle install --jobs 8`, eventmachine compiled natively) followed by `bash tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh` passes with the manifest reporting Bundler 4.0.23; with the image's default Bundler 2.6.9, `bundle install` installs 4.0.23 and restarts with it. `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1 names the Bundler major, and `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh` fails when it differs from the lock's `BUNDLED WITH`.
  Source: `docs/Gemfile.lock`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1, `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh`, `tools/ci/build-docs-site.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, https://github.com/ruby/rubygems/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md (4.0.0), `ruby/setup-ruby` `bundler.js` at `b1da4809` (v1.332.0).

- Q: Issue #514 (plan task 6.1, F-DEP-301/F-DEP-307) — GCC 15 (ubuntu-26.04) defaults to C23 while GCC 11–14 default to gnu17, and no CI job built the SDK on a compiler newer than GCC 13. How is the C dialect pinned, how is C23 still exercised, and what did GCC 15 break?
  A: (1) The pin is `CFLAGS += -std=gnu17` in `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk` (`rules/arch-linux.mk` is a symlink to it), placed after `include $(RULESDIR)/common-linux.mk` because `rules/common.mk:30` resets `CFLAGS` with `:=`, and followed by `CFLAGS += $(MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS)` so the caller's last `-std` wins (`make -C sdk ENABLESEC=1 MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS=-std=gnu2x` builds as C23). It goes into `CFLAGS` rather than `CC` so it survives a command-line `CC=` override and leaves the #508 compiler-identity probe (which runs `$(CC)` on empty input) untouched. It is C-only (C++ keeps `CXX := g++ -std=c++11`) and GCC-only (`arch-linux-clang.mk`/`arch-linux-icc.mk` are unchanged). `tools/ci/tests/test-hardening-identity.sh` asserts the last C `-std` is gnu17 (also for an absolute `CC`), that `CXXFLAGS` carries no gnu17, and that `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS=-std=gnu2x` comes after the pin; it evaluates make without a job-level `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS`. (2) The ceiling is the `toolchain-ceiling` job in `c-cpp.yml`: a native `ubuntu-26.04` runner (GCC 15) with `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS=-std=gnu23` builds `ENABLESEC=1` and runs the full suite; make imports the variable from the environment, so every SDK-building suite also builds the SDK as C23, while the suites' own test programs keep the `-std` their `run_tests.sh` pins (GCC 15's C23 default where none is pinned) and get the GCC 15 diagnostics. A native runner rather than a digest-pinned `ubuntu:26.04` container: no root-in-container artefacts in the checkout and no overlap with the `ubuntu:26.04` pins of #515. `ubuntu-26.04` is a GitHub preview runner label — that risk is accepted and disclosed in the job comment, `.github/required-checks.txt` and `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1; the floor job is unchanged. (3) GCC 15 fallout: `tests/mmt_utils/test_attr_snprintf.c` called `mmt_attr_snprintf` with no prototype (no public header declares it) and GCC ≥ 14 rejects implicit declarations, so it declares it locally like `tests/core_engine/test_core_engine.c`. The `rule_engine` libxml2 heap-balance check (`tests/rule_engine/test_fuzz_engine.c`) failed because libxml2 2.15 grows its own heap over its first ~35 parse cycles (measured 15280 → 17648 B, then flat through 1024 cycles; a libxml2-only `xmlParseFile`/`xmlFreeDoc` loop reproduces the whole growth, and the SDK loop after it is flat) — parser settling, not an SDK leak. The baseline now follows 64 warm-up cycles instead of 1; the 64 measured cycles and the `64 * 16` B budget are unchanged, and a rules set leaked per cycle still fails it. (4) `toolchain-ceiling` is listed as a required context, but live branch protection is not changed here: an admin applies the list after merge (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, "Required checks and review").
  Source: `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk`, `rules/common.mk:30`, `tools/ci/tests/test-hardening-identity.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`toolchain-ceiling`, `toolchain-floor`), `.github/required-checks.txt`, `tests/mmt_utils/test_attr_snprintf.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c` (`mmt_attr_snprintf`), `tests/rule_engine/test_fuzz_engine.c`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1–§2.

- Q: Issue #515 (plan task 6.2, F-DEP-203) — the release matrix built only the previous distribution generation. Which current-generation legs join it, from which images, and what did they need?
  A: (1) Four legs join the `build` matrix of `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` on both architectures: `ubuntu-26.04` and `debian-13` (.deb), `rocky-10` and `centos-stream10` (.rpm). Each image is pinned by its multi-arch index digest (amd64 and arm64 checked with `docker buildx imagetools inspect`) and recorded in `tools/ci/base-images.txt`. Rocky Linux 10 comes from `rockylinux/rockylinux:10`, because the Docker Hub official `rockylinux` repository stops at 9 (`docker.io/library/rockylinux:10: not found`); its index digest is the one `quay.io/rockylinux/rockylinux:10` serves. The `reproducibility` job gains an `ubuntu-26.04` deb leg (GCC 15, libxml2 2.15) beside the existing `ubuntu-24.04` and `rocky-9` legs. (2) Bootstrap manifest: the `FORMAT` table of `tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh` and `detect_distro` in `tools/ci/bootstrap-install.sh` map the four distributions (Rocky, AlmaLinux and RHEL 10 → `rocky-10`, CentOS Stream 10 → `centos-stream10`). `tools/ci/tests/test-release-bootstrap.sh` now requires the `FORMAT` table to equal the build matrix's legs and formats, so a leg cannot be added to one and not the other. (3) Ubuntu 26.04 ships libxml2 2.15, whose soname is `libxml2.so.16`, in the package `libxml2-16`; there is no `libxml2` package. The derived `Depends: libxml2` made `mmt-dpi-security` uninstallable there (`apt-get` "Depends: libxml2 but it is not installable"). `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` now maps `libxml2.so.2` to `libxml2` and `libxml2.so.16` to `libxml2-16`, and leaves every other libxml2 soname unmapped (exit 2) instead of guessing; `tools/ci/tests/test-shlib-deps.sh` (release_gates suite) pins the mapping. The rpm side keeps `libxml2`, because EL10 ships `libxml2.so.2`. EL10 keeps `libpcap-devel` in CRB, which `tools/ci/install-build-deps.sh` already enables. (4) The weekly advisory gate maps `ubuntu:26.04` to the OSV ecosystem `Ubuntu:26.04:LTS` and `rockylinux/rockylinux:10` to `Rocky Linux:10` (both answer OSV queries), and lists `centos:stream10` as Not Assessed like Stream 9 (no feed). An unmapped image would fail that gate. Its probe reads the runtime package names of each image, so `ubuntu:26.04` probes `libxml2-16` (`PKG_RENAMES`). A local native scan on 2026-10-22 found twelve blocking records on `debian:13`, all unfixed in trixie (gcc-14, glibc, libxml2): eleven of the advisories #492 dispositioned for `debian:12`, plus CVE-2026-86139. They get the same time-boxed `unfixed` dispositions in `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt`, expiring 2026-12-31. After that, the native surface reports no blocking record. (5) Not extended yet: `bootstrap-smoke.yml` builds its fixture from the latest published release, which has no current-generation packages before the next tag; `install-smoke.yml`'s published path builds the pinned `v1.9.1` tag, which predates the gnu17 pin of #514 and was never built with GCC 15. Both gain the new distributions after the first release that ships them.
  Source: `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`build`, `reproducibility`), `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh`, `tools/ci/bootstrap-install.sh`, `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-shlib-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-release-bootstrap.sh`, `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh` (`DISTROS`), `README.md` ("Pre-built packages").

- Q: Issue #516 (plan task 6.3, F-DEP-103/F-DEP-205/F-DEP-206/F-DEP-207) — the `toolchain-floor` job ran on the `ubuntu-22.04` runner image (deprecated 2026-09-17, removed 2027-04-17), CentOS Stream 9 (EOL 2027-05-31) and Ubuntu 22.04 (standard support ends 2027-06-01) are release legs, and the asn1c gate ran on `buildpack-deps:bookworm`. Where does the floor job run now, when do the old legs go, and does the toolchain floor move?
  A: (1) `toolchain-floor` keeps its name — the required context in `.github/required-checks.txt` is unchanged — and now runs on `ubuntu-24.04` in the `ubuntu:22.04` container, pinned to the digest the release leg already uses (GCC 11.4, glibc 2.35). It installs git before `actions/checkout`, so the checkout is a real clone and not a tarball. It then installs the release containers' dependency set (`tools/ci/install-build-deps.sh ubuntu-22.04 deb`) plus `jq`, `pkg-config` and `curl`, which the hosted image used to provide (`docs_onboarding` and the release-bootstrap self-test need curl). The build and the suites run as an unprivileged `floor` user through `runuser`, as on a hosted runner: run as root, the installer suites skip their non-root checks. A local run of these steps (arm64) printed GCC 11.4.0 / glibc 2.35, built the SDK and passed 30/30 suites. (2) The asn1c gate runs on `buildpack-deps:trixie`, pinned by index digest (host GCC 14.2). The regeneration recipe in that container reproduces the committed `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/` byte for byte (`git diff --cached --stat` empty), and a control run on the old Debian 12 image (GCC 12.2) gives the same tree. Both runs were on arm64; CI runs amd64. No workflow names a bookworm image or runs on `ubuntu-22.04` any more, and `tools/ci/tests/test-image-pins.sh` asserts both. (3) Removal dates: the `ubuntu:22.04` image (release leg, install-smoke leg, floor container) and the `quay.io/centos/centos:stream9` image (release and install-smoke legs) retire on **2027-04-30**, a month before their distributions' end dates above. Each line in `tools/ci/base-images.txt` carries `# retire-by: 2027-04-30 (#516)`. `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh`, a blocking step of the required `verify-helpers` job (push, pull request and its weekly schedule; `base-image-refresh.yml` runs it only in its `repin` job, after a drift), warns from 60 days ahead (2027-03-01) and fails from that date until the image's workflow legs and its line are gone. The release jobs' "Verify pinned base image is recorded" steps strip trailing comments before their whole-line match, so the annotation never fails a leg. The fix is removal; moving a date needs a new entry here. `refresh-base-images.sh --write` keeps the comment when it repins (tested). (4) Debian 12 stays: it is in Debian LTS until 2028-06-30, and its open glibc/libxml2 advisories are dispositioned until 2026-12-31 in `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt` (#492), to be decided again then. Only the asn1c tooling image, which ships nothing, moved to Debian 13. (5) The floor does not move. `MMT_GCC_MIN := 11`, `MMT_GLIBC_MIN := 2.34` and `MMT_LIBSTDCXX_MIN := $(MMT_GCC_MIN)` (`rules/common.mk`) stay, and so do the shlib floors `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` derives from them (`libc6 (>= 2.34)` / `glibc >= 2.34`, `libstdc++6 (>= 11)` / `libstdc++ >= 11`). Rocky 9 (GCC 11.5, glibc 2.34, EOL 2032-05-31) stays in the matrix and still carries that exact combination, so retiring Stream 9 and Ubuntu 22.04 removes no leg the floor depends on. When `ubuntu:22.04` retires, the floor job moves to the pinned `rockylinux:9` image (GCC 11.5 on glibc 2.34, exactly the declared floor) with the same GCC-major assertion. The floor next moves when Rocky 9 itself retires.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`toolchain-floor`), `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `tools/ci/check-image-digests.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-image-pins.sh`, `rules/common.mk` (`MMT_GCC_MIN`, `MMT_GLIBC_MIN`, `MMT_LIBSTDCXX_MIN`), `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1.

- Q: Issue #518 (plan task 6.5, F-DEP-306/F-CI-011) — cppcheck was pinned to 2.13 (ubuntu-24.04's package, Dec 2023) and the findings ratchet stood at 24 while the scan measured 13. Where does the newer release come from, what is the ratchet now, and what happened to the findings it adds?
  A: (1) The release is the one the issue names, 2.22 (2.22.0, published 2026-09-19). No distribution package carries it, and an apt/pacman package would move under the ratchet at the next archive refresh, so `tools/ci/install-cppcheck.sh` builds it from the upstream tag tarball (`github.com/cppcheck-opensource/cppcheck`, the project's current home — `danmar/cppcheck` redirects there) pinned by sha256 `d74945de…ece57b`, with upstream's release options (`MATCHCOMPILER=yes`, no `_GLIBCXX_DEBUG`), and refuses a binary whose `--version` is not `Cppcheck 2.22.0`. The `lint` job (name, and so the required check, unchanged) now runs in the digest-pinned `ubuntu:24.04` container already recorded in `tools/ci/base-images.txt` and builds it there; shellcheck comes from that container's apt (0.9, as on the runner). `CPPCHECK_PINNED_VERSION` is `2.22`, and `tools/ci/tests/test-cppcheck-version.sh` (release_gates suite) fails when the installer's major.minor differs from the pin, the tarball is not sha256-pinned, the lint job's container is not digest-pinned, or the job installs a distribution cppcheck. (2) The gate passes `--library=gnu` in both passes: the SDK is GNU C built by GCC only, and gnu.cfg defines `__builtin_expect` and kernel-style `likely()`/`unlikely()`. cppcheck receives no `-I`, so the project's definitions (`mmt_utils.h`, `branch_optimization.h`) are invisible to it. Without the library every `if (unlikely(p == NULL)) return` guard is opaque, and 2.22 reported the dereference behind it (all 8 `memory.c` arena findings, one of them error-severity). (3) On the unchanged tree 2.22 reported 38 findings (2 error-severity, which would have failed the error gate); 2.13 reported 13, 12 of them among the 38 (its 13th, `tips_xml.c:602`, 2.22 no longer reports). Fixed in code (no behavior change): the `hash_utils.cpp` timeout-ring "leak" (return the pointer instead of a `reinterpret_cast` cppcheck cannot follow), `str_combine()`'s branch conditions (`mmt_utils.c`), the unreachable inner `offset >= len` test in `mmt_path_snprintf()`'s proto-path loop (`packet_processing.c`), three dead `ptr = NULL` stores after `free` (`ndn.c`, `proto_ip_frag.c`, `tcp_segment.c`), three duplicated bound tests that repeat the test on the line above in `proto_ssl.c`, `%u` for the `uint32_t` protocol id in two examples, and an `unsigned long` cast for `tv_usec` under `%06lu` in the `tips_*` engines. Suppressed inline, each with its reason beside it: `uninitMemberVarNoCtor` on `mmt_oa_table`/`mmt_int_map` (malloc'd C-layout structs, never constructed), the `tcp_segment.c` carve path (cppcheck loses the grow branch's `b = nb`), `nas_msg.c` (it does not follow the decoder macro's `while (buffer == NULL) return` guard) and the conversation-proto OR in `mmt_tcpip_plugin_internal.h` (the OR is the value returned, not a truth value). (4) The ratchet is the measured count, 0: any finding 2.22 reports is now a CI failure until fixed or suppressed with a reason. The phase0 classification fingerprint is unchanged.
  Source: `tools/ci/install-cppcheck.sh`, `tools/ci/run-cppcheck.sh` (`CPPCHECK_PINNED_VERSION`, `LIBRARIES`), `tools/ci/cppcheck-ratchet.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/test-cppcheck-version.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`lint`), `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1, the source files named above, https://github.com/cppcheck-opensource/cppcheck/releases/tag/2.22.0.

- Q: Issue #521 (plan task 7.3, F-DEP-302 part 2 of 3, spike) — the asn1c pin was held at `mouse07410/asn1c @ dbd0ed09` (0.9.29) because the recipe's `-gen-PER` flag was removed upstream. What does moving to v1.5.3 take: which flags replace `-gen-PER`, what changes in the generated skeletons, and is the output reproducible?
  A: (1) Pin: tag `v1.5.3` is commit `e6c2799ac9e64702dd425b47d838aa27b2495e7b` (2026-10-07); `ASN1C_VERSION` records it with the GitHub archive tarball's sha256 (`51da7f83…`, identical over two downloads). (2) Flags: v1.5.x split PER at b5ed2c1f into `-gen-UPER`/`-gen-APER` and generates every codec (BER, XER, OER, UPER, APER, JER, CBOR, print, random-fill) unless told otherwise; the `-gen-*` options only add to that default. The dissectors decode aligned PER only (`aper_decode`, `aper_encode_to_buffer`; XER only behind `XER_FPRINT` under `DEBUG`), so `-gen-PER` maps to `-gen-APER` plus `-no-gen-UPER -no-gen-JER -no-gen-CBOR` (`ASN1C_CODECS` in the recipe): UPER is what 0.9.29's `-gen-PER` emitted besides APER and nothing calls it; JER and CBOR are new. BER, XER, OER, print and random-fill stay. The generator writes those choices to `common/asn_config.h` (`ASN_DISABLE_UPER_SUPPORT`, …), which the runtime headers include, so the SDK build needs no extra `-D`. `ASN1C_PREFIX` (the ngap/ naming) and the s1ap/ngap file sets are unchanged (713 and 1,130 files, same names). (3) Version string: v1.5.x computes it at autoreconf time from `git describe` and the commit date and falls back to `NOGIT-<build date>` without git metadata — a tarball build on 2026-10-22 reported `NOGIT-2026-10-22`, and every generated header embeds it, so the gate could never be byte-stable. The recipe stamps `ASN1C_VERSION_STRING=v1.5.3-0-ge6c2799a-2026-10-07` (what `git describe --tags --long` plus `%ad` of the tag reports in a clone) and `ASN1C_BUGURL` (upstream's tarball fallback) into `configure.ac` before `AC_INIT`, and `asn1c-regen.yml` requires `asn1c -v` to print exactly that pair. A tarball keeps the sha256 integrity check; a git clone would make the string depend on the clone's abbreviation length. (4) Skeletons: the runtime is split per codec (`INTEGER_aper.c`, `OPEN_TYPE_aper.c`, `aper_*`, `*_ber.c`, `*_xer.c`, …), and both #513 advisories are fixed upstream — `INTEGER_decode_aper()` rejects `range_bits` wider than its `uintmax_t` accumulator and a determinant above `max_range_bytes` before aligning (ce45145ad4), `OPEN_TYPE_aper_get()` fails a selector result without a descriptor or past `elements_count` and allocates or fails an unallocated member (e9600cf7f2); both commits are ancestors of the tag. The #513 patch no longer applies (`2 out of 2 hunks FAILED` on `common/INTEGER.c`), as designed. `ANY_decode_aper()` now reads the general length determinant (`aper_get_length(pd, -1, -1, -1, &repeat)`, a five-argument signature), which is the body the #427 load-time override carried. Unknown-extension handling changed upstream from fail to skip (`ASN_REJECT_UNKNOWN_EXTENSIONS` restores it); the only path S1AP/NGAP reach is an APER ENUMERATED extension index past the map, which 0.9.29 already accepted (it substituted the first enumerator), so the strict macro is not defined — defining it would make the decoders stricter than before. (5) Layout: v1.5.3 holds many members by pointer that 0.9.29 embedded — its recursion breaker adds a deeper cycle search and makes `-findirect-choice` cover every CHOICE alternative, including the NGAP IE value unions' primitives. Examples: the `S1AP-PDU` alternatives, `E-RABLevelQoSParameters` and its ARP, `UE-S1AP-IDs.uE-S1AP-ID-pair`, every NGAP `protocolIEs` and IE value. `-fcomplex-threshold=500` (the maximum) and `-fprefer-import-source` do not change it, and dropping `-findirect-choice` only mixes embedded and pointer members, so the flags stay and the dissector glue adapts (#522). NGAP `protocolIEs` are declared as `struct NGAP_ProtocolIE_Container *` — a forward-declared name no header defines — while the descriptor (`ATF_POINTER`, `asn_DEF_NGAP_ProtocolIE_Container_124Pn`) allocates the parameterized instance; the glue reads it through asn1c's `A_SEQUENCE_OF` view. (6) Measured: run in the pinned `buildpack-deps:trixie` image with the workflow's own steps, the regeneration differs from the 0.9.29 tree in 1,988 generated files, +104,024/−33,264 lines (common/ 145 files: 72 added, 2 removed (`BOOLEAN.c/.h`), 71 modified; s1ap/ 713 and ngap/ 1,130 modified). A second regeneration in a fresh tree was byte-identical.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/ASN1C_VERSION`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile` (`ASN1C_CODECS`), `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, mouse07410/asn1c v1.5.3 (`configure.ac` version m4, `asn1c/asn1c.c` option parsing and default flags, `libasn1compiler/asn1c_C.c` `expr_break_recursion`, `skeletons/INTEGER_aper.c`, `skeletons/OPEN_TYPE_aper.c`, `skeletons/ANY_aper.c`, `ChangeLog` v1.5.3 and the v1.5.2 upgrade notice).

- Q: Issue #522 (plan task 7.4, F-DEP-302 part 3 of 3) — with the generator pinned at v1.5.3 (#521), what does committing the regenerated tree take: are the #513 backport patches still needed, what must the handwritten dissector glue change, and does any verdict move?
  A: (1) The tree under `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/{common,s1ap,ngap}` is the regeneration recipe's output, copied unmodified from a run of the `asn1c-regen.yml` steps in its pinned `buildpack-deps:trixie` image (measurements in the #521 entry). (2) The patches are dropped with the recipe's `apply-patches` step and the `patches/` directory: v1.5.3 carries both upstream fixes (#521 entry), and the `s1ap-integer` fixture of `parser_boundaries` (`test_s1ap_integer_length.c`, unchanged checks) passes against the unpatched v1.5.3 runtime, under `SANITIZE=asan` too. (3) Glue, outside the generated tree. `s1ap_common.c`: the `S1AP-PDU` alternatives, `E-RABLevelQoSParameters` with its ARP, and `uE-S1AP-ID-pair` are read through their pointers, each NULL-checked (a NULL PDU alternative fails the decode); the #427 constructor that patched `asn_OP_ANY.aper_decoder` is removed, because v1.5.3's `ANY_decode_aper()` has the same body and the old one no longer compiles (`aper_get_length()` gained an argument); `<arpa/inet.h>` is included for `ntohl()`, which the old `asn_system.h` pulled in through `<netinet/in.h>`. `ngap.c`: the IE container is read through `_NGAP_IE_COUNT`/`_NGAP_IE_AT`, which view the allocated `NGAP_ProtocolIE_Container_124Pn` as its leading `A_SEQUENCE_OF` (the header's `struct NGAP_ProtocolIE_Container *` names no defined type); every IE value and message alternative is a pointer, so `RAN-UE-NGAP-ID` goes through a NULL-safe `_get_set_ran_ue_ngap_id()` and the AMF-ID and NAS-PDU helpers return on NULL. GCC 13 only warns on these mismatches (GCC 14+ errors), so the glue was rebuilt with `-Werror=incompatible-pointer-types -Werror=int-conversion -Werror=implicit-function-declaration` until clean. The `rules/common.mk` #443 comment and a `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh` comment that described the removed constructor are reworded on the same lines. (4) Gate: `asn1c-regen.yml` now also runs on pull requests that touch `common/`, `s1ap/` or `ngap/`, so a hand edit to the generated tree fails the PR instead of the next weekly run. `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh` gains a step 0 that fails when a generated s1ap/ngap source does not name the generator `ASN1C_VERSION` pins, when `common/` holds another generator's output, or when the recipe applies a patch again. (5) Tests: `s1ap_ngap_decode` builds its S1AP item and NGAP InitialUEMessage through the new pointers and adds three checks — ARP priority level extracted through the two pointer levels, RAN-UE-NGAP-ID read (GET) and rewritten by `encode_ngap()` (SET) through the pointer IE value. (6) Verdicts: the phase0 classification fingerprint and precision/recall metrics match the committed baselines unchanged. No CHANGELOG line: the topmost section is the released 1.9.1, as for #518.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/common/` (`INTEGER_aper.c`, `OPEN_TYPE_aper.c`, `ANY_aper.c`, `asn_config.h`), `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/s1ap_common.c`, `src/mmt_mobile/ngap/ngap.c`, `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/test_s1ap_integer_length.c`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_precision.sh`.

## 2026-10-26

- Q: Issue #523 (plan task 7.5, F-DEP-303 part 1 of 2) — the decoders were generated from S1AP r10.5.0 and NGAP r15.2.0 while the r16.1.0 modules sat unused in `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/support/`. Can the recipe generate from r16.1.0 without rewriting the dissectors, and what does r16.1.0 add?
  A: Yes, fingerprint-neutral. (1) NGAP: `gen-ngap` takes `support/ngap-r16.1.0/38413-g10.asn` with the same flags. Every type, member and `value_PR` name the glue uses is unchanged; r16.1.0 only adds IEs and alternatives. asn1c names the parameterized IE-container instances after the source line of `ProtocolIE-Container`, so `NGAP_ProtocolIE_Container_124Pn` became `…_6976Pn`: the `ngap.c` comment and the one test that names an instance follow, and `test_s1ap_integer_length.c` reads the IE member array through `asn_DEF_NGAP_UEContextReleaseCommand_IEs.elements` instead of the numbered `asn_MBR_…_169`. (2) S1AP: `s1ap_common.c` decodes the OAI-style schema of the r10.5.0 inputs (moved from `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/asn1_R10.5` in 7896937a) — `S1ap-` types, each message a `SEQUENCE OF S1ap-IE` whose `value` is an `ANY` decoded per IE id — while 36413-g10.asn is the class-based 3GPP module, whose typed open types would need a dissector rewrite (what d0274d95 generated in 2020 and 9e5b76da reverted). `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/s1ap-simplify.py` applies that simplification mechanically instead of by hand: it drops the classes, object sets and parameterized containers; turns each message's `ProtocolIE-Container` into `s1ap-<Msg>-ies SEQUENCE (SIZE (0..s1ap-maxProtocolIEs)) OF S1ap-IE`, each `ProtocolIE-SingleContainer` into `S1ap-IE`, each container list into `SEQUENCE (SIZE (lb..ub)) OF S1ap-IE` and each `ProtocolExtensionContainer` into `S1ap-IE-Extensions`; prefixes types `S1ap-` and values `s1ap-`; and lists the `id-*` constants as named numbers of `S1ap-ProcedureCode`/`S1ap-ProtocolIE-ID`. The encodings agree: a ProtocolIE-Field is an id, a criticality and an open type, which APER reads back as `ANY`. `gen-s1ap` writes the four derived modules to `support/s1ap-r16.1.0/simplified/` (committed, so the regen gate also proves the derivation) and generates from them; python3 is now an explicit dependency of the gate's container (buildpack-deps already carried it through mercurial). Ten `s1ap_common.h` includes follow r16.1.0 renames (e.g. `tunneling` → `tunnelling`, `Synchronization` → `Synchronisation`, the MDT types → `M1…`); no `S1ap_*` identifier the glue uses changed. In `common/` only the S1AP output the recipe leaves there changes (`S1AP-PDU.[ch]`, `asn_constant.h`); the runtime skeletons are byte-identical. (3) Inventory: `support/DELTA-r16.1.0.md`, written by `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/delta-inventory.py` from the constants and object sets: S1AP +15 procedures, +131 protocol IEs, 2 procedure and 1 IE constant renamed (codes unchanged), +245 type definitions; NGAP +3 procedures, +41 protocol IEs, 1 IE constant renamed (`id-CoreNetworkAssistanceInformation` → `…ForInactive`, id 18), +68 types. Each new IE lists the messages or IE types that carry it, the families task 17.5 (#574) maps. It also lists what changed in place: IEs retyped in an object set both releases define (NGAP `id-AdditionalUL-NGU-UP-TNLInformation` → `UPTransportLayerInformationList`, `id-QosFlowToReleaseList` → `QosFlowListWithCause`, both inside OCTET STRING transfer containers the dissector does not decode), types whose root before the extension marker differs (S1AP: `CauseRadioNetwork`, whose r10.5.0 enumerator `invals1ap-id-qos-combination` was a prefixing accident of the OAI schema, `TraceDepth`'s `s1ap-maximum`, `ImmediateMDT`, `ENB-StatusTransfer-TransparentContainer`; NGAP: 13 types), and types that only gained extension additions. No renamed enumerator is used by the glue. The r10.5.0 and r15.2.0 inputs stay for that inventory. (4) Tests: `s1ap_ngap_decode` step 0 fails when a generated s1ap/ngap source was found in another input, when the derived schema or the inventory is stale; `test_rel16_generation` checks r16.1.0 constants, round-trips NRUESecurityCapabilities (IE 269) through `S1ap-IE`'s `ANY`, and has `decode_ngap()` read a SecondaryRATDataUsageReport (procedure 52) carrying HandoverFlag (IE 143). `test_s1ap_rel16_independent_vector` checks the derivation against an encoder that does not share it: a 129-octet InitialContextSetupRequest encoded by asn1c v1.5.3 run on the unmodified, class-based 36413-g10.asn (`ASN1C_PREFIX=S1AP_ -findirect-choice`, the recipe's codec flags), with Rel-16 content in each rewritten container — the E-RAB item's iE-Extensions (BearerType, id 233), its QoS parameters' iE-Extensions (Packet-LossRate, id 273) and the message-level NRUESecurityCapabilities (id 269). The derived codec consumes all of it, every IE and extension value decodes, and `s1ap_decode()` extracts QCI, ARP, TEID and gateway. (5) Verdicts: the phase0 classification fingerprint and precision/recall metrics match the committed baselines unchanged; the S1AP/NGAP suites pass under `SANITIZE=asan` and the mobile pcap harness replays its 28 captures cleanly.
  Source: `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/Makefile`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/s1ap-simplify.py`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/delta-inventory.py`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/support/README`, `src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/support/DELTA-r16.1.0.md`, `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/s1ap_common.h`, `src/mmt_mobile/ngap/ngap.c`, `.github/workflows/asn1c-regen.yml`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/test_s1ap_integer_length.c`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_precision.sh`.

## 2026-10-27

- Q: Issue #641 — quay.io garbage-collected the pinned `quay.io/centos/centos:stream10` digest (`6f8d6188…`) when the `stream10` tag moved to `99b8fe27…`, so the `centos-stream10 (amd64/arm64)` release legs and `packages-gate` failed with `manifest unknown` on PR #639, a day after #638/#640 fixed the same failure for `stream9`. How is a garbage-collected quay.io CentOS Stream pin caught before it breaks PR CI?
  A: (1) Repin: `bash tools/ci/refresh-base-images.sh --write --registry quay.io` moved `stream10` to `sha256:99b8fe27dd4958b5dc2630ce35f5f6a7ff7bf9b2b1a72078102fde50d3277550` (an index covering linux/amd64 and linux/arm64) in `tools/ci/base-images.txt` and `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml`, the only two files quoting it; `stream9` was `ok`. The same day's full `--check` also reported `drift` (not `missing`: the old digests still pull) for `ubuntu:22.04`, `ubuntu:24.04` and `ubuntu:26.04`; as in the #491 entry above they are not repinned here — the daily run proposes them. (2) Measured, not assumed: quay.io keeps no dated tag for CentOS Stream (only `stream10`, `stream10-minimal` and the 2024 `-development` tags), and the superseded manifest 404s as soon as the tag moves (`6f8d6188…` already 404s less than two hours after the 01:05 UTC retag; the earlier `b6456f91…` 404s as well). The `stream10` tag moved on 10-07, 10-14, 10-20, 10-22 and 10-27, at 01:05–17:10 UTC, so a GC break can start at any hour. No polling cadence can come before a GC that happens at retag; what a cadence controls is how long CI stays broken afterwards. The daily `'41 5 * * *'` run has in fact started between 09:46 and 11:31 UTC (10-21 to 10-26), so the old bound was up to about a day plus the repin time. (3) Cadence: `.github/workflows/base-image-refresh.yml` keeps the daily full check and adds an hourly `'17 * * * *'` run that checks only the quay.io pins (`--registry quay.io`, a new option of `refresh-base-images.sh` that limits `--check` and `--write` to entries whose name starts with `<host>/`; a host no entry uses is exit 2). It is selected by `github.event.schedule` equal to the workflow's `QUAY_HOURLY`. The hourly run makes no Docker Hub request (rate limits) and does not fail every hour on a Docker Hub tag that moved while its old digest still pulls. Any violation it finds starts the same full `--write` repin job. The bound is best-effort — about an hour after the retag plus the repin time — because GitHub delays scheduled runs under load. (4) The hourly cadence exposed two defects in the repin job, fixed in the same change. First, its issue lookup `gh issue list --author app/github-actions` matched nothing, so every failing run opened a new "Base image pins out of date" issue (#611, #631, #634, #635, #636 are duplicates, none ever edited). It now searches by title and keeps the oldest open issue whose author is the `app/github-actions` bot. Second, with `BASE_IMAGE_REFRESH_TOKEN` set it would have force-pushed a new commit every hour while a pin stays missing, restarting the repin PR's CI; it now skips the push when `ci/base-image-refresh` already carries the same tree. Concurrency is one group per scope (an hourly run can never replace a pending daily or dispatched full check) plus one shared group for the repin job. (5) Rejected: mirroring the CentOS Stream images to GHCR so a pin is never collected — it needs `packages: write`, a maintainer decision on package visibility and cannot be verified from a PR; pulling the floating tag when the pinned digest is gone — it gives up digest pinning (F-SEC-007). The mirror is still the only way to close the retag-to-repin window entirely. Closing the five duplicate issues is a maintainer step.
  Source: `tools/ci/refresh-base-images.sh` (`--registry`), `.github/workflows/base-image-refresh.yml` (`schedule`, `QUAY_HOURLY`, `concurrency`, repin job), `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`centos-stream10` matrix row), `tools/ci/tests/test-image-pins.sh`.

- Q: Issue #644 — the weekly dependency-advisories gate (`.github/workflows/dependency-advisories.yml`, run 40459748819) ended NOT ASSESSED, exit 2, with no scanner failure. What made it inconclusive, and how are the two causes it could be — the debian:13 glibc records and the CentOS Stream legs — handled?
  A: (1) The cause was two affected records on `debian:13@sha256:4c25275…` for glibc `2.41-12+deb13u4`: `DEBIAN-CVE-2026-6368` and `DEBIAN-CVE-2026-6791` (aliases `CVE-2026-6368`/`CVE-2026-6791`), with no rating in any source (`ratings` empty, severity null) and `unfixed` in trixie, so `apply_policy` marks each `NOT ASSESSED (no rating)` (`tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh:1090`) and the verdict turns incomplete (`:1064`). They are the same upstream glibc CVEs dispositioned for `debian:12` (`tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt:43-44`) on the same evidence, so the debian:13 entries take that pair's expiry (2026-12-31) and #492 tracking (`tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt:68-73`) rather than #515's. The #515 measurement of 2026-10-22 (entry above) counted the twelve blocking debian:13 records and missed these two unrated ones; that entry stays as written. (2) CentOS Stream 9 and 10 were not the cause: they map to no OSV ecosystem (`tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh:154-157`) and are only appended to Not Assessed (`:990`), never to the failures, so a no-feed target never sets the exit code — a PASS carries a "(N target(s) Not Assessed)" suffix (`:1069`); only blocking/expired advisories (exit 1) or failures/unrated advisories (exit 2) change it. That policy is now stated in the script header (`:52-57`, which named only Stream 9). The Stream 9 leg retires by 2027-04-30 (`tools/ci/base-images.txt:41`, #516); Stream 10 has no retire-by date. (3) Verified locally: `--only native` against a tree holding the `debian:13` and both CentOS Stream pins (`ROOT_DIR`) exits 2 with the two records `NOT ASSESSED (no rating)` before the entries and exits 0 after, `PASS … (2 target(s) Not Assessed …)`. A full local native run also fails `ubuntu:22.04`, `ubuntu:24.04` and `debian:12` with `exec format error` on an aarch64 host whose cache holds their amd64 variants; that is the host, not the gate. The fixture self-test adds an unrated record without and with a disposition and the two Stream images (`tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-advisories.py:233-258`).
  Source: `tools/ci/check-dependency-advisories.sh` (header, `DISTROS`, `NO_FEED_REASON`, native loop, `measure` verdict, `apply_policy`), `tools/ci/advisory-dispositions.txt`, `tools/ci/base-images.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/test-dependency-advisories.py`, `tools/ci/tests/dependency-advisories-feeds.json`, `.github/workflows/dependency-advisories.yml`.

- Q: Issues #528 (plan task 8.5, F-TEST-010) and #552 (plan task 13.3, F-CI-007/F-CI-009/F-CI-015) — the `-fsigned-char` leg is a no-op on x86-64, so ARM's unsigned plain `char` was never tested; the lint job scanned the tree twice; docs-only PRs ran every C job; `enablesec` re-ran the `rule_engine` suite `unit-tests` already runs. How are both done without a PR that waits on a context it removed?
  A: (1) Two new single-context jobs, `unit-tests-arm` (full suite on the native `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runner) and `unit-tests-unsigned-char` (full suite on x86 with `EXTRA_CFLAGS` and `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS` set to `-funsigned-char`), each asserting `__CHAR_UNSIGNED__` first. Adding `runner`/flag keys to the `unit-tests` matrix was rejected: it renames the two required `unit-tests` contexts. The `-fsigned-char` leg stays — removing a required context deadlocks the PR that removes it — and is a candidate for removal in a follow-up that also edits protection. The bootstrap apt cache key now includes `runner.arch`. (2) `tools/ci/run-cppcheck.sh` runs one `--enable=warning` scan and gates error severity on its XML report: error-severity checks are always enabled, so the dropped error-only rescan (16.5 min of the 33.8 min lint run 40448488680) could only repeat what the report holds. The lint job also restores a `--cppcheck-build-dir` with `actions/cache` (keyed on the installer and the gate script), so cppcheck reanalyses only changed files; a cold cache costs one full scan (~16 min there). (3) A `changes` job (`tools/ci/classify-changes.sh`, allowlist: `docs/**` and root-level `*.md`; empty diff = code) gates the C jobs of `c-cpp.yml` and `phase0-baseline.yml`. A job skipped by `if:` reports success, so single-context jobs skip at job level, with `!cancelled()` so a failed `changes` runs them (fail-closed). A skipped matrix job reports only its bare name and would leave each leg pending, so `unit-tests`, `sanitizer-tests` and `resource-budgets` always start and skip their steps; `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` now rejects a skippable job-level `if:` on a matrix job a required context names, and `harness-gate` accepts a skipped harness matrix only when `changes` succeeded with `code=false`. `build` stays unfiltered (its protocol-count check reads `README.md`, `docs/index.html`, `docs/_config.yml`) and `doc-validators` runs `docs_lifecycle` when `unit-tests` skips. (4) `enablesec` now only builds `ENABLESEC=1`. No required context is removed or renamed; `unit-tests-arm` and `unit-tests-unsigned-char` are added to `.github/required-checks.txt` and need the maintainer protection update after merge.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`changes`, `unit-tests-arm`, `unit-tests-unsigned-char`, `lint`, `enablesec`, `doc-validators`), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`changes`, `harness-gate`), `.github/actions/bootstrap-deps/action.yml`, `.github/required-checks.txt`, `tools/ci/classify-changes.sh`, `tools/ci/run-cppcheck.sh`, `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-cppcheck-version.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh`, GitHub Actions docs ("Using conditions to control job execution": a skipped job reports success).
- Q: Issue #529 (Task 8.6) — which vestigial build and doc files go, how is the vendored-source list parsed once, and what threshold does the commented-out code lint use?
  A: (1) Deleted: `rules/project.mk`, which nothing includes (`sdk/Makefile:17` includes `rules/arch-$(ARCH).mk`, which includes `rules/common-linux.mk`, which includes `rules/common.mk`), and `docs/docs/mmt_list_protocols.csv`, which no page links; `docs/_config.yml:34` no longer names `*.csv` among the nested assets it excludes. Kept: `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk` — `rules/arch-linux.mk` is a symlink to it (mode 120000), so the pair is one file under two names, not a duplicate. (2) `tools/ci/vendor-paths-lib.sh` is the single shell parser of `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`: `vendor_paths_load [FILE]` (`:18-29`) fills `VENDOR_PATHS` and returns 2 when the list is missing. `report-function-shape.sh`, `lint-markers.sh`, `lint-commented-code.sh`, `lint-packet-path-io.sh`, `count-init-wrappers.sh`, `check-canonical-init-wrappers.sh` (which hands the parsed list to its Python through `VENDOR_PATHS_LINES`), `run-cppcheck.sh`, `check-sdk-coverage.sh` and `scripts/validate-doc-drift.sh` source it; each script's output on 340947c5 is unchanged. `scripts/validate-doc-drift.sh:221-227` now also requires the six metric scripts to source the helper. `.shellcheckrc` sets `external-sources=true` so the pre-commit hook, which passes only changed scripts, follows the `# shellcheck source=` directives instead of reporting SC1091; it disables no rule. `tools/ci/vendored-components.py` keeps its own Python reader. (3) `tools/ci/lint-commented-code.sh:36` lowers `MIN_RUN` from 5 to 2 and gates on a count ratchet rather than on zero (`:37-49`, `:146-157`): 121 runs (293 lines) measured on 340947c5, recorded in `tools/ci/commented-code-ratchet.txt`; a rise is exit 1, a drop asks for the baseline to be tightened. Rejected: a per-path allowlist of the 121 runs, and deleting them now — they sit in 49 C files under `src/`, the sweep would churn the files #540–#548 are editing, and a C change needs the phase0 gates. The `Commented-out code lint` step comment in `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`) now describes the ≥2-line ratchet instead of the old zero-blocks ≥5 gate; it was updated once the PR #649 that also edits that workflow had landed.
  Source: `rules/arch-linux.mk` (symlink), `docs/_config.yml`, `tools/ci/vendor-paths-lib.sh`, `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`, `tools/ci/lint-commented-code.sh`, `tools/ci/commented-code-ratchet.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/test-commented-code-ratchet.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `scripts/validate-doc-drift.sh`, `.shellcheckrc`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`).
- Q: Issue #524 (plan task 8.1, F-TEST-005/006/007/015) — how does coverage come to measure what runs, per family, with ratcheting floors?
  A: (1) Harvest fix: `tests/tcp_pending_order` and `tests/resource_bounds` compiled their objects into a `mktemp` directory their exit trap deleted, so under `--coverage` the `.gcda` vanished before the runner aggregated them (the gap the #387 entry deferred). Their objects now go to the suite directory, as in `tests/core_engine` (`.gitignore` covers `*.o` and `*.gcda`); the work dir keeps logs and results. `proto_tcp.c`, `tcp_segment.c`, `proto_ip_frag.c` and the other sources the two suites compile now appear in `instrumented_sources`. Three uncovered runs longer than 10 lines appeared with them (`tcp_seg_new()`, `tcp_seg_new_in_arena()`, the TCP option extractor's not-found reset); case I of `tests/tcp_pending_order` drives each with assertions, through a configurable `get_packet_offset_at_index()` stub in `tests/resource_bounds/tcp_reasm_stubs.h` (default -1, unchanged for resource_bounds). (2) Harness cohort: `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh --profile coverage` builds one `BUILD=coverage` SDK and replays every harness against it, whatever its profile, except `COVERAGE_SKIP`: `run_classified_flow_fastpath_test.sh`, `run_position_unknown_guard_test.sh` and `run_session_lookup_perf_test.sh` assert debug counters armed only by `#if !defined(NDEBUG) || defined(MMT_BUILD_ASAN)`, which the release `-DNDEBUG` of `BUILD=coverage` compiles out, and `run_mt_tsan_test.sh` refuses a non-TSan SDK. A skip entry naming no harness exits 2; the option is explicit (never read from `SDK_BUILD_PROFILE`), so the `--with-harnesses` arm and the default invocation are unchanged. A full `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage` runs it after the suites as one extra result entry, harvests the build once, and `sdk-coverage.sh report --harness-dir` adds `cohorts.harness` and joins it into `combined`; per-suite harvests moved to a `suites/` sibling so the report does not read the harness harvest as a suite. Measured (aarch64, GCC 13.3): 46 harnesses run, 4 skipped; harness 48.4% (19,236/39,749 lines, 718 files); combined 60.1% (26,588/44,238). (3) Family floors: `floor.json` `sdk.family_floors` gates each family on its own denominator; `check-sdk-coverage.sh` exits 2 when a family has no numeric floor and 1 when a measured family (security_fuzz included) is below it. Measured: core 86.38%, tcpip 61.37%, mobile 64.75%, business_app 89.82%, dicom 89.19%, security_fuzz 71.42%, whole SDK 65.53% (22,145/33,793 lines; 46.03% at #388). Each floor is the measurement minus 1, rounded down to 0.1 — for dicom and security_fuzz, whose value matched the pre-change x86-64 CI run (89.19, 71.42) within 0.1, the minimum of the two is the base: core 85.3, tcpip 60.3, mobile 63.7, business_app 88.8, dicom 88.1, security_fuzz 70.4, `whole_sdk_line_pct` 64.5 (was 45.0). (4) Unit re-baseline: the two suites grow the unit denominator, so `library_line_pct` falls to 74.7% (6,717/8,989 lines; 82.1% when the 80.0 floor was set at #243) while instrumentation widens to 42 files — no source lost coverage. As at #177 and #241, the floor is re-baselined, not lowered to silence a failure: `library_line_pct` 73.7 (measured minus 1), `instrumented_files` 42 (ratcheted up from 29), `required_instrumented_files` unchanged. (5) The `coverage` CI job runs `tools/ci/report-uncovered-runs.sh` without `--report-only`: the unit tracefile has 0 contiguous uncovered runs longer than 10 lines (1,200 runs in all), and a new one fails the job. Deferred to #554: replaying the golden fingerprint and the accuracy corpus under coverage. The pre-push tier (`bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --with-harnesses`) was already documented in AGENTS.md and `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §8, so no change was needed there.
  Source: `tests/tcp_pending_order/run_tests.sh`, `tests/tcp_pending_order/test_tcp_pending_order.c` (case I), `tests/resource_bounds/run_tests.sh`, `tests/resource_bounds/tcp_reasm_stubs.h`, `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh` (`COVERAGE_SKIP`), `tests/run_all_tests.sh:242-272`, `tools/ci/sdk-coverage.sh` (`report --harness-dir`), `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh` (`sdk.family_floors`), `tools/ci/tests/test-sdk-coverage.sh`, `tests/coverage/floor.json`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`coverage` job), measured `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage` run on 2026-10-27 (31/31 entries passed).

- Q: Issue #525 (plan task 8.2, F-TEST-011) — what counts as a "contract test" for an `MMTAPI` function, where do the tests live given the 30/30 suite count, and what is "the v1.9.x release libraries" baseline for `abidiff` when the shipped packages carry no DWARF?
  A: (1) A function is tested only when `tests/core_engine/test_api_contracts.c` has exactly one compiled entry for it: `CONTRACT(fn, behaviour, error)` or `NO_ERROR_PATH(fn, behaviour, "reason")`. Both take `&fn`, so a misspelled or undeclared name fails to compile; the runner fails an entry whose behaviour or error function makes no assertion and a waiver with an empty reason. A mention in a test file does not count (the issue's "54 of 234" was a mention count; at HEAD 235 names are declared and 45 were never mentioned). `tools/ci/check-api-contracts.sh` compares the entries with `tools/ci/check-public-decls.sh --list` as sets — a missing, stale or duplicate entry fails — and requires the waived names to equal `tests/core_engine/api-contract-waivers.txt` exactly, so a new waiver is a reviewed diff. The first measurement is 141 `CONTRACT` and 94 `NO_ERROR_PATH` entries: the waived functions dereference a required argument and define no failure value (session and attribute accessors, parameterless calls, total string helpers, unconditional setters), so an error path would have to be invented. (2) The contracts are a second binary of the existing `core_engine` suite (same objects plus `mmt_attr_sprintf_compat.c`, no `--wrap`), so `DEFAULT_SUITES` stays at 30 and no validated `tests/run_all_tests.sh` citation moves. (3) The type-level baseline is the `v1.9.1` tag rebuilt by `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh` with `CC='gcc -g' CXX='g++ -std=c++11 -g'` — the release profile (LTO, hardening, `-O3`) plus DWARF; `DEBUG=1` is not used because it clears `MMT_RELEASE_BUILD` and builds at `-O0`. A second, symbol-level leg compares the shipped `v1.9.1` ubuntu-24.04 `.deb` (checksum-verified against the release `SHA256SUMS`) with stripped copies of the `HEAD` libraries. A local control run found the shipped `.deb` and the rebuilt tag symbol-identical. (4) The `abi-diff` job is report-only and not in `.github/required-checks.txt` until #536. The first report (aarch64): `libmmt_core` CHANGED (6 added functions, among them `mmt_packet_scratch_adopt`), `libmmt_tmobile` INCOMPATIBLE (removed exported variable `errorCodeDecoder`), the other three unchanged. Every non-static symbol is still exported until #533 hides them, so `--headers-dir` limits the type report to the public headers.
  Source: `tests/core_engine/api_contract.h`, `tests/core_engine/test_api_contracts.c`, `tests/core_engine/api-contract-waivers.txt`, `tests/core_engine/run_tests.sh`, `tools/ci/check-api-contracts.sh`, `tools/ci/check-public-decls.sh` (`--list`), `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`abi-diff` job), `rules/common-linux.mk` (`MMT_RELEASE_BUILD` under `DEBUG`), `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk:2-3`, `tools/ci/tests/test-api-contracts.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-abi-diff.sh`

- Q: Issue #526 (plan task 8.3, F-TEST-008/009/014/016) — the RADIUS suite tested a replica of `proto_radius.c` plus nine source greps, nothing executed the #99 TCP give-up, the memory-ceiling gate skipped unmeasured pcaps, and three phase0/installer gates asserted source text. Which gates does F-TEST-016 cover, what replaces each check, and which guards cannot be observed?
  A: (1) F-TEST-016 names three source-text gates, each now an executed assertion: the `count_muls` multiply count over `ipv4_session_hash`/`ipv6_session_hash` (a renamed function counted 0 and passed) became pinned golden outputs for fixed IPv4/IPv6 keys plus a "differs from the replaced FNV-1a loop" check in `session_lookup_perf_test.c` (little-endian values; a big-endian host fails closed); the `signal_handler` sed in `tests/installer/run_tests.sh` became a signal-delivery run — `extract_all` reads a FIFO the shell holds open, SIGINT/SIGTERM must end it with status 0 and one `clean()` pass within 10 s; the `init_extraction` awk/`configured_protocols.c` grep in `run_oom_no_abort_test.sh` became the `oom_init_exit_sweep` run below. The neighbouring `stop_flag` grep went with the sed. The `pcap_breakloop` grep stays: the live `pcap_loop` path needs capture privileges, and offline the flag and `pcap_breakloop` each stop the loop alone (libpcap's offline read checks `break_loop` before each record) — mutation runs: removing only `stop_flag = 1` keeps the signal test green, removing only `pcap_breakloop` is caught by the kept grep, a no-op handler times out, a handler calling `clean()` crashes, the default SIGINT disposition exits 130. A `printf`/`fflush` inside the handler is an async-signal-safety hazard, not observable behaviour, and is no longer asserted. The static exit() scan stays as a second layer, fail-closed: its `^bool init_extraction(` anchor must match once. (2) RADIUS: the suite now builds the SDK (an SDK-building suite, §3 of AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md) and feeds crafted frames through `packet_process`. Seven of the nine deleted grep patterns map to cases that fail under their own mutation: `current_tlv->len < 2` (a len-1 attribute misparses the next bytes as User-Name; len 0 hangs the walk, bounded by `timeout`), the caplen truncation guard (three truncated attributes), `vlen > BINARY_64DATA_LEN` (User-Name len 67/255), `BINARY_64DATA_LEN - 1` (Called-Station-Id len 255), `->len < 6` (NAS-IP-Address len 2-5), `v_remaining < 6` (a 4-byte VSA whose vendor-id read crosses into a crafted next attribute) and `tlv->len > sub_remaining` (over-long sub-attribute). No coverage is claimed for two: the sub-attribute `tlv->len < 2` (`proto_radius.c:1992`) is masked — every 3GPP extractor re-checks the length (e.g. `:1722`, `:1737`) and its mutation leaves the suite green — and the type bounds `:1994`/`:1996` duplicate each other; removing both is caught only under `SANITIZE=asan` (heap-buffer-overflow at `:1997` on the sub-type 28 case). The suite also exposed `read_be32` (`:49`) shifting an int-promoted byte left by 24, undefined for a first octet >= 0x80 (UBSan aborts on 192.168.1.10); it now casts each byte to `uint32_t` before shifting (the values GCC produced are unchanged) and the suite reads both 100.64.1.10 and 192.168.1.10 — that cast is the only `proto_radius.c` change (the other src/ edits are comments in `proto_ip.c`/`proto_ipv6.c`). (3) The OOM sweep runs under `LD_PRELOAD` of `tests/fault_injection/fi_alloc.c` (new `fi_set_observer` hook) and exports `exit`/`_exit`/`_Exit` (`-rdynamic`); `dladdr` on the return address names the caller. A call from `libmmt_core` or `libmmt_tcpip` — the old guard's scope — or an unresolvable caller fails the gate; calls from other DSOs are reported per DSO. The first runs found none gated and 12-13 runs ending in a plugin `exit(0)` (`src/mmt_mobile/mmt_mobile_plugin.c:20-32`, `src/mmt_business_app/mmt_business_app.c:16`): out of scope here, a maintainer follow-up — return 0 there, then widen the gate to every DSO. A clean `init_extraction()` makes 21,852 allocations from 3,523 distinct call sites (8-frame backtrace); failing the first allocation of each site takes ~11 s on 16 cores, failing every index (`--all`) 63 s, too slow for a 4-core runner. `exit()` inserted on the names-map failure in `init_extraction`, in `register_attribute_with_protocol` (beyond the static scan) and in `init_tcpip_plugin` each fail the sweep. (4) The memory-ceiling gate fails (exit 1, naming the pcap) on a missing listed pcap, a failing driver or a non-numeric `library_rss_kib`; the #99 give-up is pinned at packet 41 by `tests/tcp_pending_order/test_tcp_classify_giveup.c` (shipped `proto_tcp.c`) and on the SDK path by `classified_flow_fastpath_test.c` (asan profile, checker-walk delta per packet).
  Source: `tests/radius_hardening/run_tests.sh`, `tests/radius_hardening/test_radius_hardening.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_radius.c:49,1983-1997,2037-2038`, `tests/tcp_pending_order/test_tcp_classify_giveup.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c` (issue #99 give-up in both pre-classification functions), `tools/phase0/tests/classified_flow_fastpath_test.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_memory_ceiling_test.sh`, `tools/phase0/tests/session_lookup_perf_test.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_session_lookup_perf_test.sh`, `tests/installer/run_tests.sh`, `src/examples/extract_all.c:238-300`, `tools/phase0/tests/oom_init_exit_sweep.c`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_oom_no_abort_test.sh`, `tests/fault_injection/fi_alloc.c`, `src/mmt_mobile/mmt_mobile_plugin.c:20-32`, `src/mmt_business_app/mmt_business_app.c:16`.

- Q: Issue #532 (plan task 9.3, part 2 of the M3 coverage push) — mobile was 65.08% and security_fuzz 71.42% against targets of 70% and 60%. What raises mobile, and which floors move?
  A: (1) `src/mmt_mobile/ngap/ngap.c` missed 297 of its 400 lines: only InitialUEMessage was decoded by any test, so every other per-message decoder (`ngap.c:119-485`) and the `SET_ACTION` path of `encode_ngap()` never ran. `test_ngap_message_decoders()` in `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c:1379` drives 17 vectors (`:1314`) through `decode_ngap()` and `encode_ngap()`: nine initiatingMessage, six successfulOutcome and one unsuccessfulOutcome type, plus both UE-NGAP-IDs CHOICE arms. Each vector checks the procedure code, the PDU choice and the UE ids; the DL/UL NAS transport vectors also check NAS-PDU extraction. Each vector then rewrites the ids, decodes the result and checks them again. The IE values come from the library's own APER encoder. The tests stay in the existing SDK-building suite, so the count holds at 30/30 and the lines land in the `sdk_integration` cohort that `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh` measures. (2) Measured with `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage && bash tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh` on aarch64 with GCC 13.3: mobile 75.33% (1,808/2,400 lines, 65.08% before). `sdk.family_floors.mobile` moves from 63.7 to 74.3, measured minus 1 rounded down to 0.1 as in the #524 entry. security_fuzz re-measured unchanged at 71.42% (3,122/4,371): it already met the 60% target, and its 70.4 floor was already measured minus 1, so it does not move. business_app (89.82%) and dicom (89.19%), named in the title, had no threshold and are unchanged. (3) Not touched: `whole_sdk_line_pct` (66.98% measured, ratchet owned by #554) and the tcpip floor (#531).
  Source: `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c:1314,1379`, `src/mmt_mobile/ngap/ngap.c:119-612`, `tests/coverage/floor.json:18` (`sdk.family_floors.mobile`), `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh` (`sdk.family_floors`), measured `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage` run on 2026-10-27 (31/31 entries passed).

- Q: Issue #533 (plan task 9.4, part 1) — libmmt_core exported every non-static symbol (487 dynamic: 483 `T`, 4 `R`). The acceptance criterion caps `nm -D --defined-only sdk/lib/libmmt_core.so.*` at "the count of MMTAPI declarations + 5". What counts as an MMTAPI declaration, which symbols stay exported, and how do tests that call core internals still link?
  A: (1) MMTAPI is the export marker. The core objects are compiled with `-fvisibility=hidden`, appended at the end of `rules/common.mk` like the #443 asn1c block so no cited range moves. Inside the SDK build MMTAPI is `extern __attribute__((visibility("default")))`, with a plain `extern` fallback for non-GNU compilers. For consumers it stays `extern`, because visibility only matters where a symbol is defined. MMTCALL is unchanged. (2) An MMTAPI declaration is a distinct name declared with the MMTAPI token, function or data, in `src/mmt_core/public_include/*.h` or `src/mmt_core/private_include/*.h`, outside comments and preprocessor lines. `scripts/validate-exported-symbols.sh` fails when the export count exceeds that count + 5, when any export is outside the set (`_init`/`_fini` aside), or when a name of the set is not exported — a definition compiled without seeing its MMTAPI declaration is hidden, and the check prints the missing names. It prints both numbers: 279 exports for 279 declarations, a budget of 284. A deliberately leaked definition fails it with exit 1, and so does `mmt_print_info` hidden with an `.hidden` assembler directive. (3) The export set, measured with `comm -12` of every plugin library's `nm -D --undefined-only` against the baseline exports, is: 235 MMTAPI functions. 20 utilities declared in installed headers without MMTAPI (19 in `mmt_utils.h`, plus `mmt_print_info` in `mmt_core.h`); the research counted 21, but `nm` lists 20. The four `MMT_DPI_PROFILE_*` objects, now `MMTAPI const` (an `extern` declaration in both modes, so no tentative definition). And the 20 private internals libmmt_tcpip binds to after `dlopen()`: 11 in `hash_utils.h`, 4 `hashmap_*`, `mmt_strnstr` and the 4 `mmt_tcp_reasm_stat_*` in `packet_processing.h`. Their MMTAPI marking says "exported for libmmt_tcpip until #534". libmmt_tmobile, business_app, tdicom, security and fuzz need no non-MMTAPI core symbol. The 20 utilities are installed API, so they keep MMTAPI with MMTCALL and get contract entries (16 CONTRACT; 4 NO_ERROR_PATH waivers: `hex2int`, `str_compare`, `mmt_print_info`, `str_print_array`, each with its reason). The checker matches the waiver list exactly, so it can grow in review. The test-only getters become hidden: `mmt_tcp_reasm_insert_visits`/`bytes_*`/`resident_bytes`, `mmt_reassembly_*`, `mmt_classify_*`, `mmt_caplen_guard_*`, `mmt_oa_*`. 208 symbols leave the table. (4) Tests that call hidden internals link `libmmt_core.a` through `tools/phase0/tests/static_core_link.sh`, never alongside `-lmmt_core`. The helper adds `-Wl,--whole-archive`, which keeps the weak-referenced counters resolvable, plus `-rdynamic`, which lets plugins and `-lmmt_tcpip` bind to the executable's MMTAPI symbols. It adds `-lstdc++ -ldl -lpthread -lm` and the profile flags read from the archive's undefined runtime symbols (`__asan_`, `__ubsan_`, `__tsan_`, `__gcov_`). That detection is used because under `run_all_harnesses.sh --profile coverage` a harness's own build line does not name the prefix's profile. Moved, by building and linking against the hidden shared core: the phase0 harnesses classified_flow_fastpath, dicom, extraction_caplen_prologue, position_unknown_guard, session_lookup_perf, session_scale and tcp_reassembly_perf (undefined references, or weak counters resolving to NULL), the `tests/fault_injection` engine leg, and parser_boundaries' `dtls_wire_extent_unit`. ip_frag_bounds still links against the shared core: `hashmap_walk` stays MMTAPI. (5) `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh` reports libmmt_core as INCOMPATIBLE because symbols were removed. That is expected; the check is report-only until #536. No classification logic changed, and the phase0 golden fingerprint is unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_exports.h:5-22`, `rules/common.mk:589-600`, `scripts/validate-exported-symbols.sh:151-210`, `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_core.h:834-851,1351`, `src/mmt_core/public_include/mmt_utils.h:36-276`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/hash_utils.h:26-66`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/hashmap.h:117-122`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h:791-899`, `tests/core_engine/test_api_contracts.c:2293-2494,3091-3114`, `tests/core_engine/api-contract-waivers.txt`, `tools/phase0/tests/static_core_link.sh`, `docs/Exported-Symbols.md`.

- Q: Issue #534 (plan task 9.5, visibility part 2) — the protocol plugins exported every non-static symbol: libmmt_tcpip 1,750 dynamic symbols (123 of them vendored llhttp), libmmt_tmobile 35, libmmt_business_app 3, libmmt_tdicom 8. The acceptance criteria cap `nm -D --defined-only sdk/lib/libmmt_tcpip.so.*` at 10 and ask for a CI check that fails when an exported set changes without an update to `docs/Exported-Symbols.md`. Which symbols stay exported, how is the set gated, and how do the tests that call plugin internals still link?
  A: (1) As for the core (#533), MMTAPI is the export marker: the TCPIP, LIBMOBILE, LIBBAPP and LIBDICOM objects are compiled with `-fvisibility=hidden` (C and C++), in a block appended at the end of `rules/common.mk` so no cited range moves; the asn1c objects were already hidden (#443). MMTAPI only carries `visibility("default")` under `_MMT_BUILD_SDK`, which business_app and dicom did not get, so the same block adds it to them; in their sources it changes nothing else. No new macro and no version script. (2) Kept, all MMTAPI: libmmt_tcpip 5 — `init_proto`, `cleanup_proto`, `init_tcpip_plugin`, `cleanup_tcpip_plugin` (`mmt_tcpip_plugin.h`) and the #237 alias `mmt_search_irc_ssl_detect_ninty_percent_but_very_fast`, which the #237 note promises to keep until 2.0.0 (`proto_irc.c`). libmmt_tmobile 6 — `init_proto`/`cleanup_proto` (declared in `mmt_mobile_internal.h`), `s1ap_entities_update`/`_count`/`_reset` (`proto_s1ap.h`) and `update_ngap_data`, now declared in the installed `proto_ngap.h` because 5Greplay calls it (`src/forward/forward_packet.c:240,260`, linked `-l:libmmt_tmobile.so`). libmmt_business_app 2 — `init_proto`/`cleanup_proto` (`mmt_business_app_internal.h`; the plugin has no header declaring a function). libmmt_tdicom 8 — unchanged, the eight `dicom.h` declarations. Hidden, among others: `s1ap_decode`, `init_mobile_plugin`, `init_proto_ips_data`, the llhttp parser and the tcpip classifiers and extractors (still linkable from the static archives). The two installed mobile headers now include `mmt_exports.h`, because `mmt_mobile_internal.h` includes them before `mmt_core.h`. Every symbol the plugins import from the core is still exported by it. (3) Not changed: the 20 core internals libmmt_tcpip binds to (`hash_utils.h`, `hashmap.h`, `mmt_strnstr`, `mmt_tcp_reasm_stat_*`) stay MMTAPI; dropping them means moving that code into the plugin, which no criterion asks for — deferred. (4) Gate: `scripts/validate-exported-symbols.sh` keeps the #533 core checks unchanged in behaviour (they now sit under `if "core" in libs`, and the core table ends at its last row instead of the end of the file) and adds a `## libmmt_<name>` section per plugin with a `| symbol |` table. The check fails, naming each `+`/`-` symbol, unless the table equals `nm -D --defined-only` of the library (`_init`/`_fini`/`_edata`/`_end`/`__bss_start` aside); `--write` regenerates the tables and keeps the prose. A missing plugin library in `sdk/lib` is built like the core one (the doc-validators job builds nothing first). `MMT_EXPORTS_LIB_DIR`, `MMT_EXPORTS_DOC` and `MMT_EXPORTS_LIBS` point it at a fixture; a missing library there is exit 2. The required doc-validators job already runs every `scripts/validate-*.sh`, so no workflow changed. The fixture self-test `tools/ci/tests/test-exported-symbols.sh` (run by the release_gates suite, builds no SDK) covers match, an unrecorded export, a removed one, a missing section, `--write` and the exit-2 cases; it goes red when the validator's added- or removed-symbol detection is disabled, and the real check fails with `+ mmt_search_irc_ssl_detect` when that function is given default visibility. (5) Tests that call plugin internals link the archive through `mmt_static_plugin_link LIBDIR NAME...` in `tools/phase0/tests/static_core_link.sh`: `--whole-archive libmmt_NAME.a`, `-Wl,--exclude-libs,libmmt_NAME.a`, the archive's sanitizer/coverage flags (the detection is now shared with `mmt_static_core_link`) and `-ldl -lpthread -lm`, before the core. `--exclude-libs` keeps the static copy out of the executable's dynamic symbol table even under `-rdynamic`, so the plugin `init_extraction()` loads always binds to its own copy, in every profile (`-fno-semantic-interposition` is release-only). Moved: the 31 phase0 harnesses whose sources call tcpip internals (found by comparing each harness object's undefined symbols with the libmmt_tcpip archive), the parser_boundaries API tests dtls_wire_extent_unit, dtls_wire_extent_api, nfs_rpc_header_bounds and tcp_options_bounds, and nas_ies_tail (tmobile). Before, the harness's linked libmmt_tcpip.so interposed on the dlopen()ed copy, so one copy held all state; now there are two. sip_methods failed on it: its `init_proto_sip_struct()` returned without filling the static copy's file-scope bitmasks, because the plugin had registered PROTO_SIP first. The five harnesses that call an `init_proto_*_struct()` (dtls_classify_guard, quic_min_len, rtp_bounds, sip_methods, skype_removal) now `unregister_protocol_by_id()` the protocol and register it again through the static copy, and check that this returns `PROTO_REGISTERED`. (6) Test change: `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh` asserted that libmmt_tmobile.so still exports `init_proto`, `cleanup_proto` and `s1ap_decode` after #443 hid asn1c. It now asserts `init_proto`, `cleanup_proto`, `s1ap_entities_count` and `update_ngap_data`, and that `s1ap_decode` is not exported, which is the intended #534 change. (7) Rejected: per-plugin version scripts (a second list that can drift from the headers, and archive objects would stay default-visible, so `-rdynamic` static links would export them, as #533 found for the core); an unhidden test-only build (the tests would stop exercising the shipped libraries, and `run_all_harnesses.sh` would need a fourth build); hiding tcpip alone.
