(8) Downstream: mmt-probe uses `init_tcpip_plugin` from the static archive only; mmt-security, mmt-reader and mmt-plugin-generator call no plugin internal (plugin-generator's http2/ndn/quic plugins define names tcpip used to export, so hiding them removes those collisions); 5Greplay keeps `update_ngap_data`. `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh` will report the four plugins INCOMPATIBLE (symbols removed); it is report-only until #536. No classification code changed; the phase0 golden fingerprint and precision baseline are unchanged. The #533 entry's citation `scripts/validate-exported-symbols.sh:151-210` now reads `:211-269` (entries are append-only).
  Source: `rules/common.mk:602-615`, `src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip_plugin.h:17-24`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_irc.c:372-377`, `src/mmt_mobile/include/proto_s1ap.h:231-234`, `src/mmt_mobile/include/proto_ngap.h:26-32`, `src/mmt_mobile/mmt_mobile_internal.h:25-29`, `src/mmt_business_app/mmt_business_app_internal.h:17-21`, `src/mmt_dicom/include/dicom.h:111-123`, `scripts/validate-exported-symbols.sh:272-347`, `tools/phase0/tests/static_core_link.sh:29-111`, `tools/ci/tests/test-exported-symbols.sh`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh:171-181`, `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` (Protocol plugins).

- Q: Issue #657 (CI audit 2026-10, §5.5 R5) — branch protection on `main` requires branches to be up to date (`strict: true`), so every merge makes each open PR update and re-run its full CI. Adopt a merge queue and drop strict up-to-date, or keep the current setting? What must the repository provide for a queue, and which required-check set goes with it?
  A: (1) Decision: adopt, pending the admin settings change (AGENTS.md lists branch-protection work as "Ask first"; the PR that prepared the repository changed no setting). The queue tests each merge result once, so merging one PR no longer makes the other open PRs re-run. (2) Repository side: `c-cpp.yml`, `phase0-baseline.yml` and `release-packages.yml` — the only workflows reporting a context of `.github/required-checks.txt` — trigger on `merge_group`. Their `changes` jobs send every non-`pull_request` event down the run-everything path, so a merge-group run skips neither the docs-only filter's C jobs nor the package legs: the queue runs the full check set. Each trigger replaced a comment line, so no line cited elsewhere (`c-cpp.yml:389-394,407` in `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md`, checked by `scripts/validate-agent-environment.sh`) moved, and the `concurrency:` blocks are unchanged: a merge-group run's `github.ref` is its own `gh-readonly-queue/main/pr-…` branch, so queue runs never cancel one another. The secret-scan job's gitleaks-action does not handle `merge_group` and fails there; it is `continue-on-error`, and the pinned-binary fallback it already relies on (no `GITLEAKS_LICENSE`) runs the scan. (3) Enforcement: `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` (verify-helpers, blocking) now fails a required context whose workflow has no `merge_group` trigger (or one without `checks_requested` or excluding main), or whose job-level `if:` keeps it off merge-group runs: an `event_name` pin that does not name `merge_group`, `!= 'merge_group'`, or a value only pull requests carry (`github.event.pull_request`, `github.head_ref`, `github.base_ref`). A job skipped that way would report success without running in the queue. `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh` pins each case. (4) Settings (admin): `strict: false` on the classic required status checks, and a repository ruleset with a `merge_queue` rule on `refs/heads/main` — the classic protection API has no merge-queue setting. Values: `SQUASH` (the only merge method the repository allows), `ALLGREEN`, build 5, merge 1–5, wait 5 min, and a 120-minute check response timeout: the longest required job chain is `changes` (5) + package `build` (60) + `bootstrap-dry-run` (10) + `packages-gate` (5) = 80 minutes of job timeouts, plus runner queueing. The commands are in `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Required checks and review"). (5) Required-check set (overlaps #601): live protection requires 18 contexts, the committed list 30. Applying the committed list, as `CONTRIBUTING.md` documents, makes them equal (issue AC 4) but is a separate maintainer call: the 12 added contexts include `toolchain-ceiling`, on the `ubuntu-26.04` preview runner label, and `packages-gate`, whose legs flaked in the audit window (`docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md`). In a queue a flaky required context evicts its entry and, with batching, the entries built with it. Either apply the full list with the queue, or apply the queue with the live 18 and trim `.github/required-checks.txt` to match in a follow-up; `bash tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh --live montimage-projects/mmt-dpi` reports the drift either way.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`on:`, `changes`, `secret-scan`), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`on:`, `changes`), `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`on:`, `changes`, `build`, `bootstrap-dry-run`, `packages-gate`), `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` (`group_trigger`, `off_group_if`), `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh`, `.github/required-checks.txt`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Required checks and review"), `tools/ci/assert-gitleaks-ran.sh`, `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §5.5 and §8, live `gh api repos/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/branches/main/protection` (read-only, 2026-10-27: `strict: true`, 18 contexts).
- Q: Issue #653 (CI audit 2026-10, §4 and §5.1 R1) — the `lint` job's cppcheck scan checks every `#ifdef` configuration of every file (up to 12, cppcheck's default `--max-configs`), 28–30 min on a cold cache. Which configuration does a pull request gate on, where does the full scan still run, and how is the ratchet kept per mode?
  A: (1) `tools/ci/run-cppcheck.sh` takes `CPPCHECK_MODE`: `full` (the default, so a local run or any caller that sets nothing keeps today's scan) or `pr`, which adds `--max-configs=1 -DNDEBUG -D_MMT_BUILD_SDK` — the defines the default SDK build passes (`rules/common.mk:41`, `rules/common.mk:276`). With `-D`, cppcheck checks only that configuration: a probe with out-of-bounds writes under `#ifndef NDEBUG` and `#ifdef OTHER` reported neither in `pr` mode and both in `full`; `--max-configs=1` without the defines would check the `NDEBUG`-undefined branch the build never compiles. Macros a reachable header defines still apply. Any other mode value exits 2. (2) `tools/ci/cppcheck-ratchet.txt` holds one `<mode> <count>` line per mode, read for the mode that ran; a missing line, or the old bare integer, exits 2. `full 0` is unchanged; `pr 0` was measured with cppcheck 2.22.0 built by `tools/ci/install-cppcheck.sh` in the lint job's digest-pinned `ubuntu:24.04` image (722 files, one configuration each, 0 findings, 50 s cold at `-j4` on a 20-core host) and is confirmed by the PR's own `lint` run. (3) The `lint` job sets `CPPCHECK_MODE` from the event: `pull_request` → `pr`; push to `main`, tags, `merge_group` and the weekly schedule → `full`. The job id, its `needs`/`if:` and the required context stay as they were, and both gates (error severity and ratchet) run in both modes. The results cache is keyed on the mode, so the two modes' per-file results never mix; `main` saves only `full` caches, so a PR's first run scans cold in `pr` mode. (4) Until the merge queue is enabled the full scan runs after merge (every push to `main`) and weekly, not before merge. A newer `main` push can still cancel an in-flight one under the workflow's current concurrency group (issue #660 changes that); the full scan checks the whole tree against its ratchet, so the next completed `main` or weekly run reports a finding a cancelled run missed. (5) The `c-cpp.yml` edit is line-neutral: the lint job's header comment was condensed, so the `c-cpp.yml` line citations in `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` do not move.
  Source: `tools/ci/run-cppcheck.sh` (`MODE`, `CONFIG_ARGS`, baseline read), `tools/ci/cppcheck-ratchet.txt`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`lint`: `env`, cache key), `tools/ci/tests/test-cppcheck-version.sh` (scan-mode cases), `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §1, `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §4.1–§4.2.
- Q: Issue #655 (CI audit 2026-10, §3.3 and §5.3 R3) — `phase0-baseline.yml` ran each `tools/phase0/tests/run_*.sh` as its own matrix job (50 jobs, 50 identical SDK builds, queued behind the 20-concurrent-job cap). How many jobs replace them, how is every harness still guaranteed to run, and how is a failure still attributable to one harness?
  A: (1) Five legs of one `harness` matrix — the issue's cap — each running a slice of `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh`, which already built one SDK per profile group: `asan-1of3` to `asan-3of3` (`--group asan --shard K/3`, 14 harnesses each), `default` (`--group none,default`, 7) and `tsan` (`--group tsan`, 1). Main's per-script legs put an asan harness's run step at about 70 s, about 42 s of it the asan SDK build, so one asan half would carry roughly 6–7 min of harness time; thirds keep the longest leg near 5 min inside the 10-min wall-clock budget. `list-harnesses` is gone. `profile_of` now ignores comment lines: `run_tcp_reassembly_perf_test.sh` named `BUILD=asan` only in its header, so the runner had grouped it with asan although it builds a non-sanitized SDK for meaningful timings (the per-script matrix ran it that way); it now groups default, as its header states. (2) Coverage: the runner enumerates `run_*.sh` at run time, `--group` selects by the profile `profile_of` reads from each script (exactly one of four), and the K/N shards of a selection are disjoint and cover it, so a new harness lands in exactly one leg with no workflow edit. `tools/ci/tests/test-harness-shards.sh` (release_gates suite; verify-helpers with PyYAML required) runs every leg's `--list` and fails when a harness is missing or in two legs, and proves on a fixture that an added harness is picked up. (3) Attribution: `--log-dir` keeps one log per harness and a `verdicts.tsv`; the runner prints a verdict table, wraps each harness in a `::group::`, emits an `::error` annotation naming a failing harness and appends the table to the job summary; each leg uploads its logs as `phase0-harness-logs-<leg>`. (4) `harness-gate` keeps its name, its `needs: [changes, harness]` and its verdict logic, so the required contexts in `.github/required-checks.txt` are unchanged; the old `harness-run_*.sh` contexts were never required.
  Source: `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh` (`--group`, `--shard`, `--list`, `--log-dir`), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`harness`, `harness-gate`), `tools/ci/tests/test-harness-shards.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`), `tools/phase0/README.md` (*CI gate*), `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §3.3, §5.3.
- Q: Issue #656 (CI audit 2026-10, §5.4 R4 and §6) — the #552 filter only told docs from code, so every other pull request ran the whole suite and harness set. Which tests does a pull request run, how is a test a change needs never left out, and how does every required context still report?
  A: (1) Five tiers, the highest any changed path reaches: `docs` (docs/, root Markdown: the C jobs skip, as since #552); `tooling` (tools/ci, scripts, non-workflow .github files, root dotfiles, when no workflow, action, runner, tools/phase0, sdk, rules or other non-suite file references them, transitively through tools/ci and scripts: the tooling-tagged delegate suites plus the suites that reference the file, in `unit-tests`, and `lint`; the gated legs skip); `tests` (one suite's or harness's files that nothing outside them references: those, the smoke suites and harnesses, every suite leg, the harness legs and the phase0 gates); `family` (a tcpip dissector, src/mmt_mobile, src/mmt_dicom, src/mmt_business_app: the family-tagged suites and harnesses plus every suite or harness that names the changed file, and every suite a tools/ci or scripts file naming it reaches — a self-test a suite delegates to, such as the one sdk_coverage runs, followed as a tooling change is, so a consumer outside tools/ci, scripts and the suites makes it full —, the smoke set, the gates, `codeql` and `fuzz`); `full` (everything). `tools/ci/classify-changes.sh` answers `code`, `tier`, `full`, `gates`, `suites` and `harnesses`; the map is `tools/ci/test-map.txt` (path globs, first match wins; tagged suite and harness entries). (2) Full is the default for every doubt: src/mmt_core, any `.h`/`.inc`/`.def` under src/, the L2–L4 and tunnel dissectors every family rides on (proto_sctp.c, proto_tcp.c, proto_ip.c, ...), the shared tcpip helpers (tcp_segment.c, http.c, http2.c, ndn.c, rfc2822utils.c, ip_session_id_management.c), the rest of src/mmt_tcpip and src/, rules/, sdk/, workflows, actions, both runners, the classifier, map and checker, tools/phase0 outside tests/, tools/ci/fuzz/ (read by directory), headers and helpers shared by the harnesses, an unmapped path, an empty list, an unreadable or malformed map, a suite or harness the map lacks. Pushes, tags, the schedule and merge-queue runs feed the classifier no path, so they are always full. (3) Quoted paths: the diff keeps git's default core.quotePath, so a non-ASCII or control-byte name arrives C-quoted; a path starting with `"`, holding a backslash, a byte outside printable ASCII or a `.`/`..`/empty component is full (the #601 packaging filter failed open on such a path). (4) Fail-closed wiring: every workflow condition on these outputs reads `!= 'false'` (`tier != 'tests'` for codeql and fuzz), never `== 'true'`, so a failed `changes` job (empty outputs, under `!cancelled()`) runs every job on every suite and harness; the classifier writes its six lines only after classifying the whole list, and the step appends them to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` only when it succeeded. (5) Which jobs run where: gates=false (docs, tooling) skips unit-tests-arm, unit-tests-unsigned-char, toolchain-floor, toolchain-ceiling, assertions-live, the sanitizer-tests steps, codeql, fuzz and the phase0 jobs; tests skips codeql and fuzz; full=false skips coverage, root-install, enablesec and abi-diff — coverage because its floors measure every suite and harness, so a subset would fail them; resource-budgets runs its steps on full or when resource_bounds is selected. Job ids, `name:`s and matrix values are unchanged, matrix jobs keep `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}` and skip steps, and harness-gate accepts skipped legs on `gates=false` as on `code=false`, so all 30 required contexts report on every tier. (6) Map completeness: `tools/ci/check-test-map.sh` (verify-helpers, and the release_gates suite through `test-classify-changes.sh`) fails on a DEFAULT_SUITES suite or run_*.sh without an entry, a stale entry, an unknown tag, an entry whose files load a family's library or sources without that family's tag or `smoke` (so dicom_test and s1ap_ngap_decode carry tcpip) — an entry's files include the tools/ci and tools/phase0 files its suite files or run_<stem>.sh name, and those each tools/ci file names, transitively, so a suite carries what its delegate loads (sdk_coverage and installed_consumer carry mobile, dicom and business_app; the tooling delegates and business_app carry the families their delegates name), and a family-only entry naming no source of one of its tcpip, mobile, dicom or business_app tags (each needs one; core and security sources are full) — the harnesses that name no dissector source (dns_parser_test, http_session_test, ...) are smoke, and so are the whole-SDK tests that carry a family only through its runtime dependency (dicom_test, s1ap_ngap_decode, nas_ies_tail, rule_engine), so a change to another family still runs them. (7) References: a reference is the file's basename (with its parent directory when two tracked files share it) in any tracked file outside docs/, src/, Markdown, the map and the classifier's self-test; a C file counts as using a C source or header only through an `#include` line. (8) Residual risk: a test that reads a file by directory listing or a computed name, without naming it, is not found by reference; the merge queue's `merge_group` run and the push to main run the full set, so such a miss is caught before or right after merge, never silently.
  Source: `tools/ci/classify-changes.sh`, `tools/ci/test-map.txt`, `tools/ci/check-test-map.sh`, `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh` (`--only`), `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`changes` and every job reading its outputs), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml` (`changes`, `harness`, `harness-gate`), `tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-harness-shards.sh`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` (*Required checks and review*), `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §5.4, §6.

- Q: Issue #654 (CI audit 2026-10) — the SDK-consuming suites each ran `make -C sdk clean` and rebuilt the same SDK, a dozen builds per run. How does one run build the SDK at most once per profile while the suites stay runnable on their own, and what happens to coverage?
  A: (1) Groups: the runner maps each suite to a shared build (`sdk_group_of`). `default`: hashmap, fault_injection (only when `SDK_BUILD_PROFILE` is not asan/tsan, since its engine leg, the only SDK consumer in it, is skipped there), citrix_ica_detection, http_header_case, s1ap_ngap_decode, radius_hardening, nas_ies_tail, parser_boundaries, docs_lifecycle, docs_onboarding. `enablesec` (`ENABLESEC=1`): rule_engine, installed_consumer. Two groups, not one ENABLESEC=1 superset: `make install` refuses a build whose baked plugin directory differs (`sdk/Makefile:70-80`), so one build serves one prefix, and the default-group suites keep testing the package configuration without the engines, as they did. The `enablesec` build is incremental on top of `default` when both are needed: `ENABLESEC=1` adds only the engine objects and their per-object flags (`rules/common-linux.mk:6-12`, `rules/common.mk:584-587`) and recompiles `plugins_engine.o` through the plugin-path stamp, so the result equals a clean ENABLESEC=1 build; the installer suite already relied on this. A full run makes 2 builds (1 clean). (2) Eager, before the first suite, not on first use: a failed build fails the run at once with the build log's tail, so no consumer falls back to building itself, and the build count is visible up front (`sdk_build <n>: group=<g> profile=<p> [incremental]`, `sdk_builds=<n>`, `Shared SDK builds:` in the result table). (3) Consumers: run with `MMT_SDK_PREBUILT=1` and `MMT_PREFIX` (also `MMT_FI_PREFIX` for fault_injection), the convention of `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh`; they skip clean/build/install, refuse a prefix with no installed SDK, and never remove it. Without `MMT_SDK_PREBUILT` each suite builds as before, so `bash tests/<suite>/run_tests.sh` stays standalone; hashmap, docs_lifecycle and docs_onboarding read `MMT_PREFIX` only in prebuilt mode. Other suites run with `MMT_SDK_PREBUILT` unset. (4) Own builds kept: installer and sdk_coverage test the build and install mechanics themselves, so they are not consumers; a run therefore still discards an existing `sdk/` build. (5) Coverage: the shared builds are `BUILD=coverage` under `--coverage`. Right after each one the runner copies the `.gcno` notes under `src/` into a per-group snapshot and, after all group builds, deletes them (and any `.gcda`) from `src/`, so suites that build nothing still harvest nothing and own-build suites see only their own notes. A consumer is harvested with `--gcda-root` the checkout and `--gcno-root` a fresh copy of its group's snapshot, so its per-suite record is its own counters plus that build's zero-hit objects — what its own build gave before; `tools/ci/sdk-coverage.sh` is unchanged. (6) Two suites depended on state a shared prefix outlives: s1ap_ngap_decode read the asn1c symbol set from the in-tree objects, which an own-build suite may have cleaned or replaced by then; it now reads the asn1c members of the installed `libmmt_tmobile.a` (member names are source basenames, unique across `src/mmt_mobile`). On a fresh build the set equals the old one restricted to built objects (6327 symbols); the old glob also picked up stale untracked `.o` files with no source. installed_consumer moved a plugin and the security engine out of the prefix and back without a trap; an EXIT trap now restores them. (7) Test: `tools/ci/tests/test-shared-sdk-build.sh` (release_gates suite, no SDK build) runs a copy of the runner over fake suites with a stub `make`. Measured: full default run 103 s, 30/30, against 201 s on the same machine before.
  Source: `tests/run_all_tests.sh:174-186` (`sdk_group_of`), `tests/run_all_tests.sh:204-232` (consumer environment and harvest), `tests/run_all_tests.sh:285-335` (shared builds), `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/run_tests.sh:165-175`, `tests/installed_consumer/run_tests.sh:37-53`, `tools/phase0/run_all_harnesses.sh` (`run_group`), `tools/ci/tests/test-shared-sdk-build.sh`, `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §3.

## 2026-10-28

- Q: Issues #659 and #658 (CI audit R7/R6, `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §5.6–§5.7) — which redundant PR jobs can be folded, and which variant legs can leave pull requests for the merge queue, when this change may not edit branch protection or rulesets and every context protection requires today must still report on `pull_request` and `merge_group`?
  A: (1) Protection requires 18 contexts (`gh api …/branches/main/protection`, read 2026-10-28); `toolchain-floor`, `toolchain-ceiling`, `assertions-live`, `packages-gate`, `leak-gate`, `site-onboarding` and both `resource-budgets` legs are not among them. The merge-queue ruleset carries only the `merge_queue` rule, so the queue waits only for those 18: a leg moved off pull requests whose context is not required would run in the queue but gate nothing. So only `unit-tests-unsigned-char` and the `-fsigned-char` leg of `unit-tests` (both required) leave pull requests now: the job skips at job level, the matrix leg skips its steps (a new matrix key would rename its required context). `toolchain-floor`, `toolchain-ceiling`, `assertions-live`, `coverage` and the full package matrix stay on pull requests until a maintainer applies `required-checks.txt` to protection and decides the coverage cadence (audit §8 items 2–4). `check-required-checks.sh` now enforces this: in an always-on workflow, a job- or step-level `if:` testing `github.event_name != 'pull_request'` needs a `merge_group` trigger and a required context of its own or of an aggregate that `needs` it, and `--live` fails when that context is not live-required. Path-filtered workflows (`install-smoke`, `bootstrap-smoke`) are exempt. (2) Folds: `enablesec` (the shared ENABLESEC=1 SDK build `tests/run_all_tests.sh` makes for `rule_engine`/`installed_consumer`, and `codeql`); `ASan/UBSan profile compiles` (now a `sanitizer-tests (BUILD=asan, asan)` step: clean, `BUILD=asan` build, `nm -D … __asan_init`); `site-onboarding` (now the last step of `verify-helpers`, which already builds the site); `resource-budgets (ubuntu-24.04)` (`resource_bounds` is in `DEFAULT_SUITES`, and its `run_tests.sh` runs the budget checker and its `--self-test`). Four runner jobs fewer per code PR. (3) `enablesec` and `ASan/UBSan profile compiles` are still required by protection, so each keeps an `if: false` stub under its name in `c-cpp.yml`: a skipped job reports success and takes no runner. The contexts move from `required-checks.txt` to the new `.github/retired-checks.txt`, which the checker reads (each must name a stub; none may also be required). The asan stub moved out of `phase0-baseline.yml`, so the generated gate list in `tools/phase0/README.md` no longer shows it. `check-release-gates.sh` no longer requires `ASan/UBSan profile compiles` on a tag commit: the stub reports `skipped`, and the profile check is inside the required `sanitizer-tests` asan leg. (4) Not folded: the `run_leak_regression_test.sh` harness leg. It feeds the required `harness-gate` with the ASan/LSan oracle against a `BUILD=asan` SDK, while `leak-gate` (Valgrind, default SDK) is not required. Folding it would stop a leak regression from blocking a merge and drop the ASan/UBSan oracle, and the #216 entry above keeps both oracles deliberately.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`changes` comment, `unit-tests`, `unit-tests-unsigned-char`, `sanitizer-tests`, `verify-helpers`, `resource-budgets`, the retired `enablesec`/`asan-build` stubs), `.github/workflows/phase0-baseline.yml`, `.github/required-checks.txt`, `.github/retired-checks.txt`, `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh`, `tools/ci/check-release-gates.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-release-gates.py`, `tests/run_all_tests.sh:174-186` (`sdk_group_of`), `tests/resource_bounds/run_tests.sh:390-395`, `tools/phase0/tests/run_leak_regression_test.sh:42-47`, `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §5.6–§5.7, §8.

- Q: Issue #530 (F-TEST-013) — how does the SDK get a coverage-guided, in-process fuzz target with a corpus that persists between scheduled runs, when clang cannot build the whole SDK and the target found SDK bugs within seconds?
  A: (1) Target: `tools/ci/fuzz/packet_fuzzer.c` reads a classic pcap from memory (either byte order, µs or ns magic; records clamped to the bytes left; no magic = one Ethernet frame), so seeds, regressions and golden captures replay unchanged. Each frame is copied into a heap buffer of exactly caplen bytes, so ASan sees any read past it (`phase0_classify` hands libpcap's larger buffer to `packet_process`, which hides such reads), and each input gets a fresh handler so a finding reproduces from its own file. (2) Build: a full `ARCH=linux-clang` build stops at `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/common.h:16` (`#error` because clang predefines `__LITTLE_ENDIAN__`), so `run-fuzz.sh --libfuzzer` builds only the `libmmt_core.a`, `libmmt_tcpip.a` and `includes` targets with `ARCH=linux-clang BUILD=asan CC="clang -fsanitize=fuzzer-no-link -fsanitize-ignorelist=…"` and links them statically. No `rules/*.mk` change. Every plugin archive defines `init_proto()`, so only core and TCP/IP link together; the target calls `init_tcpip_plugin()` after `init_extraction()` and runs from a directory with no `plugins/`. The TCP/IP archive holds every F-TEST-013 dissector. The script cleans `sdk/` on exit. (3) `tools/ci/fuzz/ubsan-ignorelist.txt` turns off clang's `function` check (every `int init_proto_X_struct()` is called through `int (*)(void)`, `configured_protocols.c`) and its `alignment` check (it flags the issue #57 `memcpy` from a `uint32_t *` in `ip_session_id_management.c`); both fire on well-formed traffic before any real finding. The gcc gate keeps `-fsanitize=undefined` in full. (4) Seeds: no tracked capture carried SMB, NFS, IRC, IMAP, POP, SMTP or NetBIOS, so `tools/ci/fuzz/gen_seeds.py` writes one deterministic capture per family into `tools/ci/fuzz/seeds/`. They stay out of `tools/phase0/ci/pcaps` so the golden fingerprint cannot move. The run fails with exit 2 when the committed seeds differ from the generator or a seed is no longer classified as its family (`MMT_FUZZ_PRINT_PATHS`). (5) Verdicts: a new `libfuzzer-{crash,leak,timeout,oom,slow-unit}-*` artifact, exit 77/78 (`-error_exitcode`/`-timeout_exitcode`) or a sanitizer banner is a finding (exit 1). A real ASan abort exits 1, not 77, and leaves an artifact, and seeds and regressions passed as file arguments leave no artifact, so the exit code alone cannot decide. Any other non-zero exit is harness breakage (2). Each finding gets `<artifact>.log`, which `tools/ci/fuzz-alert.sh` lists, and `<artifact>.pcap` (`tools/ci/fuzz/to_pcap.py`), the form to commit to `tools/ci/fuzz/regressions`. (6) CI (`fuzz` job, same required context): every event replays the seeds and the `*.pcap` regressions as individual files, so `-max_len` does not truncate them; this part blocks. Other events also replay the cached corpus, but `continue-on-error`, because its content follows the cache, not the commit. The schedule fuzzes 1200 s (`-max_len=65536`, `-rss_limit_mb=2560`; a 300 s single-process probe plateaued near 500 MB), minimises with `-merge=1`, saves under `fuzz-corpus-<run_id>` (cache keys are immutable) and uploads a 30-day `fuzz-corpus` artifact. Every event restores the cache, which keeps the weekly entry from reaching the 7-day eviction. The job timeout went from 60 to 80 minutes. (7) Bugs found: within minutes, before the fixes, the target found ten SDK defects that the gcc gate never reported: caplen over-reads in `ethernet_classify_next_proto`, both TCP pre-classification functions, `llc_classify_next_proto` (header and SNAP id), `ip_classify_next_proto`, `ip6_classify_next_proto` and `mmt_check_tpkt`; a pointer write one past `packet_tlvs[0xFF]` for RADIUS TLV type 255 (now `RADIUS_TLV_SLOTS` = 0x100, which also bounds the reset loop); soulseek's `usrlen` bound (it omitted the 14 bytes that follow the name); and `NULL + 0` in `llhttp_execute` (NULL payload) and `ip_dgram_update_holes` (empty fragment). Each fix is a minimal bounds check. Each reproducer is a committed regression pcap that crashed the unfixed tree under the target and runs clean after the fix. After the fixes, 5 minutes on 16 workers found nothing.
  Source: `tools/ci/fuzz/packet_fuzzer.c`, `tools/ci/fuzz/gen_seeds.py`, `tools/ci/fuzz/to_pcap.py`, `tools/ci/fuzz/ubsan-ignorelist.txt`, `tools/ci/run-fuzz.sh` (libFuzzer mode), `tools/ci/tests/test-fuzz-verdicts.sh`, `tools/ci/fuzz/regressions/manifest.txt`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`fuzz`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_{ethernet,tcp,llc,ip,ipv6,tpkt,radius,soulseek,http,ip_dgram}.c`.

- Q: Issue #537 (plan task 10.3, F-BUG-010/F-CLEAN-011) — how do the installed headers stop defining `debug`/`check`/`log_err`/`sentinel`/`likely`/`unlikely`/`true`/`false` for every consumer and stop compiling the class tables into it, when ~60 library sources use those macros through `mmt_core.h`, and doc citations pin line numbers in the touched files?
  A: (1) `dbg.h` moved to `src/mmt_core/private_include/` (no longer copied to `$(MMT_INC)`) and also holds `likely`/`unlikely`, removed from `mmt_utils.h`. Library code reaches it through its internal headers — `packet_processing.h`, `mmt_common_internal_include.h`, `mmt_mobile_internal.h`, `mmt_business_app_internal.h` — plus `memory.c`, which includes none of them; a source that missed it would compile `unlikely(...)` as an implicit function and fail the `-Wl,-z,defs` link, so a clean build proves coverage. `types_defs.h` includes `<stdbool.h>` instead of defining `false 0`/`true 1` (C keeps the same values; C++ consumers no longer get a macro over their keywords). (2) The six `get_content_class_*`/`get_application_class_*` bodies moved unchanged to `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_classes.c`; `mmt_tcpip.h` keeps `MMTAPI` declarations, so libmmt_tcpip exports them (recorded in `docs/Exported-Symbols.md`) and a consumer calling them links `-lmmt_tcpip` — source-compatible, but a link line with only `-lmmt_core` must add it. The bodies did not move into libmmt_core: core is protocol-agnostic and its export budget counts only core-header declarations. Classification is untouched (the functions are reporting helpers no dissector calls), so the phase0 fingerprint cannot move. (3) The acceptance path `src/mmt_tcpip/public_include/mmt_tcpip.h` does not exist; the installed header is `src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip.h`, now with no `static inline`. (4) "Every installed header" includes `dicom/dicom.h`, whose source-tree relative includes (`../../mmt_core/public_include/…`) did not resolve once installed; it now names `plugin_defs.h`/`mmt_core.h` through the include path, and the dicom objects (and the `dicom_dissector` suite) get `-I src/mmt_core/public_include`. (5) Edits to `mmt_core.h`, `types_defs.h`, `packet_processing.h`, `memory.c` and `rules/common.mk` keep their line count, so existing `path:line` citations (`docs_lifecycle`, `validate-agent-environment.sh`, `docs/Memory-Management.md`) stay valid. (6) Test: `tests/installed_consumer/test_header_macros.c`, compiled `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` against a generated list of every installed header, once with its own `debug`/`check`/`likely`/`unlikely` defined first and once with none (no internal name may be defined), then calling the six lookups through `-lmmt_tcpip`. Against the previous headers it fails: `"debug" redefined`, `"check" redefined`, and `dicom.h` does not resolve.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/private_include/dbg.h`, `src/mmt_core/public_include/{mmt_core,mmt_utils,types_defs}.h`, `src/mmt_tcpip/include/mmt_tcpip.h`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_classes.c`, `src/mmt_dicom/include/dicom.h`, `rules/common.mk:249`, `tests/installed_consumer/run_tests.sh`, `tests/installed_consumer/test_header_macros.c`, `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` (`## libmmt_tcpip`).
- Q: Issue #539 (10.5, F-DEAD-013 / F-CLEAN-017) — how are the protocol callbacks and TCP reassembly links typed, what does "count `mmt_opaque_t`" mean for a `--strict` ratchet over 51 opaque parameters, and what replaces the SDK's lone C++ TU and its `$(CXX)` link?
  A: (1) `protocol_t`'s six callback fields carry the existing `plugin_defs.h` typedefs (`generic_sessionizer_function`, `generic_session_data_initialization_function`, `generic_session_data_cleanup_function`, `generic_session_context_cleanup_function`, `generic_proto_context_init_function`, `generic_proto_context_cleanup_function`) and every cast at the call and store sites is gone; `mmt_tcp_reasm_t`'s `seg_head`/`seg_tail`/`seg_root` are `struct tcp_seg_struct *` (forward-declared; `tcp_seg_t` is that struct). Same pointer size, so the private layout is unchanged; `test_api_contracts.c` now compares the fields with the typed functions, which only compiles while the types agree. (2) The ratchet gains an `opaque` metric: an `mmt_opaque_t`/`mmt_const_opaque_t` parameter counts unless its name is an allowlisted opaque role, and an unnamed one always counts; `--strict` and the `opaque 0` baseline require 0. Allowlisted roles: `user`, `user_args`, `user_data`, `args`, `data`, `handler_condition`, `proto_data` (caller-owned context the SDK passes back untouched); `key`, `key_1`, `key_2`, `value` (generic map and scratch-slot payloads); `x`, `y` (`mmt_realloc`/`mmt_free`/`mmt_memcmp` raw memory); `src` (`mmt_inet_ntop`, the `inet_ntop` contract); `stack_internal_context` (plugin-owned per-stack state); `protocol_context` — inside the core always a `protocol_instance_t *`, but that type is private and every plugin defines its sessionizer/context callbacks with a `void *` first parameter, so retyping the public typedefs would expose the private struct and turn each plugin definition into an incompatible-pointer-type error under GCC 14+; it stays an opaque handle the plugin passes back to the core's session helpers. The allowlist equals today's parameter names, so `tools/ci/tests/test-weak-types.sh` (run by `release_gates`) proves the metric can fail: fixture headers with a non-allowlisted name and an unnamed parameter must fail both modes. (3) `hash_utils.cpp` became `hash_utils.c`: the open-addressing tables and the timeout ring are unchanged; the iteration/expiry snapshots are malloc arrays sized by a counting scan before any slot is detached (an allocation failure dispatches nothing and detaches nothing, as `reserve()` throwing did); `std::sort` over `pair<milestone, head>` is `qsort` by milestone then head address (milestones in a due set are distinct, so the order is identical); the `std::map` parked-milestone index is a sorted array with binary search, grown by malloc+copy so a failure leaves it intact and returns `MMT_TIMEOUT_NOMEM` (0, the value the old `catch(...)` returned). The seed supply, probe bounds and dispatch order are unchanged, so the phase0 fingerprint cannot move. The `try/catch(...)` wrappers had nothing left to catch, and the three insert-failure `printf` diagnostics go through `debug()` (`dbg.h`), compiled out of release builds — `tools/ci/lint-packet-path-io.sh` scans `src/mmt_core/src/*.c`, which the `.cpp` escaped (issue #246; no test or gate reads those lines); likewise a prose comment ending in `;` was reworded for `lint-commented-code.sh`; `test_core_engine_new.cpp` (an operator-new interposer whose `ce_set_new_budget` no test called) is deleted, and the four suites that compiled the TU with `g++` compile it with `$CC` (`test_session_table` and `test_ipv6_hash_bounds` are C now). `tests/coverage/floor.json` names `hash_utils.c` in both `required_instrumented_files` and `sdk.original_cohort.sources` — the same file under its new name, not a cohort change. (4) The seven `.so` recipes link with `$(CC) $(CXXFLAGS)`: CXXFLAGS stays the link-flag carrier (hardening, sanitizer, coverage, LTO), only the driver changes, so libstdc++ (and the implicit `-lm` of `g++`, already explicit for libmmt_core and needed by no plugin) leaves NEEDED. LTO is keyed on `$(CC)` being GCC alone; the #508 CC-vs-CXX same-release check and its "LTO SKIPPED" warning are gone with the `$(CXX)` link, and `test-hardening-identity.sh` asserts a mismatched `CXX` no longer drops LTO. `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` derives Depends from NEEDED, so `libstdc++6`/`libstdc++` drop out of the packages by themselves; `check-package-deps.sh` now rejects a literal libstdc++ entry instead of exempting it. `MMT_LIBSTDCXX_MIN` stays (shlib-deps maps a libstdc++ NEEDED, should one return). `g++` stays in the toolchain line and `tools/ci/build-deps.txt` (cppcheck build, hardening self-test). Edits to `rules/common-linux.mk` and `rules/common.mk` keep their line count, so the `validate-agent-environment.sh` citations hold; only the `:271` row changed its pattern (`$(CC)`).
  Source: `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h`, `src/mmt_core/src/{hash_utils.c,packet_registry.c,packet_session.c}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_tcp.c`, `rules/common-linux.mk:77-80,147-175,271`, `rules/common.mk:487-494`, `tools/ci/count-weak-types.sh`, `tools/ci/weak-types-baseline.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/test-weak-types.sh`, `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-hardening-identity.sh`, `tests/hashmap/test_session_table.c`, `tests/coverage/floor.json`.
- Q: Issue #543 (plan task 11.4, F-CLEAN-006/F-CLEAN-004) — how do the 183 per-attribute RADIUS extractors become table-driven without changing any attribute's output, what does the acceptance grep measure, and what happens to the drift between the twin stats updaters `update_proto_stats_on_packet`/`_on_new_session`?
  A: (1) The 184 extraction functions of `proto_radius.c` came in 13 body shapes: the header authenticator; a 64-byte-clamped binary copy (12 packet attributes); a 63-byte copy plus NUL (3 packet, 5 3GPP); a big-endian 32-bit read behind `len < 6` (20 packet, 2 3GPP); a raw copy of 16 or 8 bytes behind `len < 2 + size` (IPv6 addresses, Framed-Interface-Id); a first-byte read behind `len < 3` (3GPP RAT type); a pointer to the raw TLV (the 119 `radius_avpN` attributes); and 16 stubs returning 0. They are now four `static` functions: `radius_authenticator_extraction` (unchanged), `radius_tlv_extraction`, which reads `radius_tlv_desc[extracted_data->field_id]` (TLV array, type, copy shape, size — 48 entries), `radius_avp_extraction`, which derives the TLV type from the contiguous `RADIUS_AVP1..102`/`RADIUS_AVP224..240` ids (a `_Static_assert` pins the contiguity), and `radius_unsupported_extraction` for the stubs. Dispatching on `field_id` needs no core change: `register_extraction_attribute` sets it on the struct the core hands every extractor (`packet_registry.c`), as the generic `general_*` extractors already rely on `position_in_packet`. Since #534 the plugin is built with hidden visibility, so the removed globals were not exported and `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` is unchanged. (2) The acceptance grep `^static.*radius_.*_extraction` returned 0 before the change, because none of the 183 functions was `static`; it now counts the four extractors, and `grep -cE '_extraction\(const ipacket_t' proto_radius.c` — every extractor definition, static or not — went from 184 to 4. (3) Byte identity is tested, not argued: `tests/radius_hardening/test_radius_extraction_golden.c` registers all 187 RADIUS attributes, sends 24 crafted frames twice (every packet type 0..255 but 26 and every 3GPP sub-type 0..27 at value lengths around each guard — 0-8, 14-18, 61-66, 200 — an empty frame, repeated types, mixed lengths) and folds, per attribute, presence and the whole attribute buffer (68 bytes for binary attributes, so clamp, NUL and stale tail all count; for pointer attributes the TLV's offset and bytes) into an FNV-1a digest. `radius_extraction.golden` was generated by that program against the unchanged parser; the table-driven build reproduces it exactly, and two seeded mutations (3GPP IMSI copied as BIN64, AVP224..240 shifted by one type) changed exactly the IMSI and the 17 AVP lines. (4) The twins had drifted since 2018: the first packet of a session added `p_hdr->original_len - proto_offset` to `payload_volume`, the others `p_hdr->len - proto_offset`, and only the others counted IPv6 (182) fragments. The single `update_proto_stats(…, new_session, …)` keeps both differences, keyed on `new_session`, rather than reconcile them here: the counters are public statistics and the task is a behaviour-neutral clean-up. Which side is right (most likely `original_len` and IPv6 on both paths) is a follow-up for the maintainer. `packet_pipeline.c` makes one call and fires the session event afterwards, as before.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_radius.c` (`radius_tlv_desc`, `radius_tlv_extraction`, `radius_avp_extraction`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_stats.c` (`update_proto_stats`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`proto_packet_process`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c` (`register_extraction_attribute`), `tests/radius_hardening/test_radius_extraction_golden.c`, `tests/radius_hardening/radius_extraction.golden`, `tests/radius_hardening/run_tests.sh`.
- Q: Issue #546 (plan task 12.2, F-CLEAN-003/016/020/021) — how does the classifier callback get a typed verdict without breaking the ~300 in-tree `int` classifiers or out-of-tree C and C++ plugins, what replaces the `-1` returned by unsigned public functions, are the never-written `proto_statistics_t` direction counters written or removed, and how does the misspelled export get a correct name?
  A: (1) `generic_classification_function` returns `mmt_classify_verdict_t` in C. The enum gains a never-returned negative enumerator, `MMT_CLASSIFY_VERDICT_INT_ANCHOR_` (INT_MIN), so GCC and Clang make `int` its compatible type: a classifier declared `int fn(ipacket_t *, unsigned)` still converts without a cast or `-Wincompatible-pointer-types` (an error by default from GCC 14). Without the anchor GCC picks `unsigned int` and warns. The in-tree classifier definitions therefore stay unchanged. C++ has no conversion between those function-pointer types, so a C++ translation unit keeps the `int` typedef under `#ifdef __cplusplus`. The ABI is the same: the enum is int-sized and the functions are `extern "C"`. A plan challenger raised that C++ break, and the C++ branch closed it. `tests/core_engine/verdict_typedef_probe.c`, compiled with `-Werror` as C and as C++ by the core_engine suite, holds both views. `check-typed-accessors.sh` asserts the C typedef line, because `_Generic` cannot tell an int-compatible enum from `int`. The `protocol_t` classify fields (`classify_me`, `pre_classify`, `post_classify`) use the typedef. The core walk keeps the `MMT_CLASSIFY_MATCHED_MASK` test, so binary plugins that return 2 or 3 behave as before, and the golden fingerprint is unchanged. (2) Six unsigned public functions returned `-1`: `get_protocol_id_at_index`, `get_protocol_index_by_id`, `get_protocol_index_by_name`, `get_session_id_from_packet`, `get_active_session_count` and `hex2dec`. Triage counted three; the two index lookups returned an `int retval = -1`. They now return named sentinels with the value `-1` converted to their return type: `MMT_NO_PROTO_ID` (UINT32_MAX), `MMT_NO_PROTO_INDEX` (UINT_MAX), `MMT_NO_SESSION_ID` and `MMT_INVALID_SESSION_COUNT` (UINT64_MAX), and `MMT_HEX2DEC_ERROR` (ULONG_MAX). Callers that compare with `(type) -1`, or that store an index in an `int` and test it against `-1`, as the in-tree dissectors do, see no change. `check-public-decls.sh` now fails when an unsigned-returning `MMTAPI` definition returns `-1`, either a literal or a local initialised to `-1`, and `tools/ci/tests/test-api-contracts.sh` covers both forms. (3) The direction counters are written, not removed. `proto_statistics_t` is public, and the internal struct is cast to it, so removing the fields would shift `sessions_count` and every later field: an ABI break. `update_proto_stats` splits each packet by the session `proto_session_management()` has just resolved: `[0]` is the session's setup direction (uplink, as `get_session_ul_packet_count`), `[1]` the other. A layer crossed with no session counts in the totals only. `get_children_stats` and `reset_proto_stats` carry the split too. No phase0 baseline reads these fields. (4) `is_free_protocol_id_for_registration` is the new `MMTAPI` function, and internal callers use it. The misspelled name stays an exported `MMTAPI` wrapper with `__attribute__((deprecated))`, scheduled for removal in 2.0.0 (CHANGELOG). Both names have API-contract entries.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/public_include/plugin_defs.h` (`mmt_classify_verdict_t`, `generic_classification_function`, `is_free_protocol_id_for_registration`), `src/mmt_core/public_include/{data_defs.h,mmt_core.h,mmt_utils.h}`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`mmt_classify_proto_struct`, `mmt_classify_next_struct`), `src/mmt_core/src/{mmt_data.c,mmt_utils.c,packet_session.c,packet_registry.c,packet_stats.c}`, `tools/ci/check-public-decls.sh`, `tools/ci/check-typed-accessors.sh`, `tests/core_engine/{verdict_typedef_probe.c,run_tests.sh,test_api_contracts.c}`.
- Q: Issue #549 (plan task 12.5, F-BUG-012/F-CLEAN-010) — where do the per-protocol analysis/classification switches live once `enable_/disable_protocol_*` stop writing the shared `protocol_t`, and how is the 2,000-line `packet_registry.c` split?
  A: (1) The switches are `analysis_enabled` / `classify_enabled` in `protocol_instance_t`, the per-handler `configured_protocols[]` snapshot: `mmt_init_handler()` sets both to 1 for every protocol, the four APIs write only the handler they are given, and `proto_packet_analyze()` / `proto_packet_classify_next()` read the instance they are handed. The `status` fields of `mmt_classify_next_struct` and `mmt_proto_data_analysis_struct` are removed — private structs, not installed — so no code can read a stale global flag. Accesses stay relaxed atomics (`proto_status_load`/`proto_status_store`, issue #69): a handler is single-threaded, but a caller may toggle a switch from another thread, and a relaxed load is a plain load. Two behaviours change, both only off the documented path: disabling a protocol on one handler no longer disables it on the others, and a later `mmt_init_handler()` or `init_protocol_struct()` no longer re-enables or resets it for existing handlers. Every handler still starts with everything enabled, so the golden classification and precision fingerprints are unchanged. (2) The split follows the file's seams, moving functions verbatim: `packet_registry.c` keeps the protocol table and its mutex, the name map, protocol stacks, attribute-metadata and name/id lookups and `init_extraction()`/`close_extraction()`, so the OOM static guard (`tools/phase0/tests/run_oom_no_abort_test.sh`) still anchors there; `packet_registry_classify.c` holds the classification/analysis/session callback registration; `packet_registry_handler.c` the handler lifecycle, the handler bookkeeping map and its mutex (#67), the per-handler switches and `setDataLinkType`; `packet_registry_attr.c` per-handler attribute, attribute-handler and packet-handler registration. The two statics that crossed a seam stay private to their file behind three internal entry points: `get_protocol_slot()` (the raw descriptor `mmt_init_handler()` snapshots) and `init_handlers_map()`/`close_handlers_map()` (the handler-map halves of extraction init/teardown). The core objects build with `-fvisibility=hidden`, so none of them is exported. `tests/coverage/floor.json` lists all four files in `sdk.original_cohort`, so that cohort still measures the 30 audited sources' lines.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`protocol_instance_struct`, `proto_status_load`, the `packet_registry*.c` prototypes), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry_classify.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry_handler.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry_attr.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`proto_packet_classify_next`, `proto_packet_analyze`), `tests/core_engine/test_core_engine.c` (`test_per_handler_protocol_switches`), `tests/core_engine/test_api_contracts.c` (`AC_STATUS`), `docs/THREADING.md`.
- Q: Issue #553 (plan task 13.4, F-CI-008/F-CI-010) — how are SDK builds cached without changing a default build, and how does one local command mirror the pull-request gates without copying them?
  A: (1) Cache: `MMT_CCACHE` is an opt-in launcher appended last to `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk`: set, it prefixes `CC`/`CXX` (an `override`, so a command-line `CC=` is prefixed too), defaults `CCACHE_BASEDIR` to the checkout, and stops the build when the launcher is missing; unset, `CC`/`CXX` are exactly `gcc`/`g++ -std=c++11`. The compiler-identity, FORTIFY, CET and `-frandom-seed` probes run before the block, on the bare compiler, so the hardening set is the same either way. `BUILD=coverage` is never cached: measured, a cached `--coverage` object carries the gcov counter path of the build that made it, and the `sdk_coverage` suite's harvest failed 8 checks. The suites inherit the variable from the environment (`MMT_CCACHE=ccache bash tests/run_all_tests.sh`), so `tests/run_all_tests.sh` is unchanged and no cited range of it moves. (2) Stable compile lines: every object's command line carried the install prefix (the plugin-directory define) and the commit hash (`GIT_VERSION`), so the suites' throwaway prefixes defeated ccache's direct mode. On 4 cores a warm SDK build took 26.5 s with preprocessor-mode hits against 13.8 s with direct-mode hits (31 s uncached). Both defines now reach only the object that reads them — `plugins_engine.o`, which already depends on the plugin-directory stamp, and `packet_processing.o` (`mmt_version()`) — through target-specific variables appended to `rules/common.mk`. `GIT_VERSION` is captured on the old blank line 29, before the `nogit` package-revision fallback, so a build without git still bakes an empty hash as before. Every `sdk/lib` file of an `ENABLESEC=1` build is byte-identical to main's, with an explicit `GIT_VERSION` and without git. The `ENABLESEC=1` security objects keep their per-prefix reaction-directory define, so that group still misses in direct mode. (3) CI: `unit-tests` and `sanitizer-tests` restore a ccache per leg and per profile, and push and schedule runs save one entry per commit. Pull requests and merge-queue runs only read it, which keeps the cache budget of #660. The save condition is written positively (`push || schedule`) so neither job becomes queue-only (`tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh`). (4) Local gates: `tools/ci/local-gates.sh` reads the `run:` steps of the `lint`, `verify-helpers` and `doc-validators` jobs from `c-cpp.yml` and runs them with their env. It skips `uses:` steps and three named set-up steps; a set-up name that disappears from the workflow exits 2, and so does any `${{ }}` expression or step `if:` it has no pull-request answer for. The steps run in an image built from the `lint` job's digest-pinned `ubuntu:24.04` with the pinned cppcheck, PyYAML, gawk, a UTF-8 locale and the locked gems. It runs as the caller, with a linked worktree's git directory mounted and a fresh `docs/.jekyll-cache`. Its Ruby is Ubuntu's 3.2, not CI's 3.4. (5) Acceptance criterion 1 (a warm second run ≤ 60 % of a cold one) is not met, and no build-system change here can meet it. Since #654 a run builds the SDK twice, not ~150 times, and most of its wall time is script-bound suites and uncached LTO links. Measured with `MMT_CCACHE=ccache`: 153 s cold → 152 s warm on 20 cores, and 267 s → 234 s (88 %) with 4 CPUs (`taskset -c 0-3`, `JOBS=4`). 75 % of compiler calls hit across the two runs, 99.9 % of them in direct mode. The cold run already hits within itself, because the installer suite rebuilds the same sources. (6) `.pre-commit-config.yaml` pins gitleaks to the v8.30.1 tag's commit (`83d9cd68…`, `# frozen:` comment).
  Source: `rules/arch-linux-gcc.mk` (issue #553 block), `rules/common.mk:29-31` and its appended target-specific variables, `sdk/Makefile:19-29`, `src/mmt_core/src/plugins_engine.c:50-60`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_processing.c:742`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`unit-tests`, `sanitizer-tests`, `verify-helpers`), `tools/ci/local-gates.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-ccache-launcher.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-local-gates.sh`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, measured runs on 2026-10-28.
- Q: Issue #527 (plan task 8.4, F-BUG-313) — what does "every classifier registered in the default TCP/UDP chains" count, how is a positive and a negative case per classifier checked when the chains register 143 distinct functions, and what does the "seeded random/length abstention set" record?
  A: (1) A classifier is a distinct `mmt_check_*` function of an uncommented `MMT_INTER_PROTO(PROTO_TCP|PROTO_UDP, fn, weight)` row of `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/inter_proto_classif_list.def` (160 rows, 143 functions; a function registered on both transports counts once). The oracle parses that file, so a registration change moves the requirement with it. (2) Each corpus case may list the functions it exercises in `classifiers`; a function is covered when a positive and a negative/ambiguous case name it. Covering all 143 in one change was out of scope, so uncovered functions must sit in `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/coverage-pending.txt` with a reason, and the list only shrinks: an entry that is covered or no longer registered fails, as does an uncovered function missing from it; CI passes the base commit (`--base HEAD^1`), and an entry that commit's list lacks for a function it already registers fails, so a covered function cannot move back to pending. The first tranche covers the 48 functions of weight 9–40 plus the common weight-50 ones (telnet, RDP, LDAP, BGP, PPTP, MSSQL, IRC, RTSP, SIP, STUN, RTP, SNMP, TFTP, RADIUS, GTP, MGCP, IPP); 95 remain pending. (3) A genuine flow the SDK misses is recorded as an `ambiguous` case that expects abstention (BitTorrent DHT, MySQL 8 greeting), as the QUIC draft-29 case already was, and a port-only acceptance as an `unknown`-family case that expects the false accept (`acc_unknown_mqtt_port`), as the malformed S1AP case already was: a classifier fix then has to update the reviewed expectation. No classifier source changed. (4) The abstention set is regenerated at gate time from a fixed seed (SHA-256 counter stream: payload bytes, ports, server addresses) — 12 lengths × TCP/UDP × 8 flows — and rendered after the labelled metrics of `precision.txt` with its own unknown rate; it never enters the labelled confusion matrix, so the FTP/HTTP rows are unchanged.
  Source: `tools/ci/tests/check-accuracy-corpus.py` (`load_chain`, `load_pending`, `check_coverage`, `check_base_ratchet`), `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/corpus.json`, `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/coverage-pending.txt`, `tools/phase0/gen_tcpip_pcap.py` (`ACC_CLASSIFIER_FLOWS`, `ABSTENTION_SEED`, `_gen_abstention_pcap`), `tools/phase0/ci/check_precision.sh` (step 4/4), `tools/phase0/ci/render_precision.py` (`abstention_metrics`, `render_abstention`), `tools/phase0/ci/baseline/precision.txt`.

- Q: Issue #535 (plan task 10.1, F-CLEAN-001 part 1, F-CLEAN-002) — which `ipacket_t` fields are internal, how is the private part attached without breaking the engine's hand-out of `ipacket_t *`, and why does `internal_packet` stay public?
  A: (1) Eleven of the twelve fields the header marked internal move to the engine-private `ipacket_internal_t` (`src/mmt_core/private_include/ipacket_internal.h`): the three embedded `proto_hierarchy_t` stores, `internal_p_hdr`, `original_data`, the three offset-cache fields, `proto_headers_offset_owned`, `mmt_current_classifier` and `mmt_classifier_claim`. Every packet the engine processes is an `ipacket_internal_t` (the handler's `current_ipacket`, the reassembly slot's `ipacket`, the NDN dummy packet), and what it hands out is its `pub` member — the first member — so `ipacket_internal()` converts back. (2) `ipacket_internal_t` overlays `pub` with an anonymous struct naming the same 19 fields, each pinned by `MMT_PIN_MIRROR` (same offset, `__builtin_types_compatible_p` type), so code that owns the storage (the engine's packet set-up, test fixtures) keeps writing `pkt.data`; accesses through a union member take the union's alias set in GCC and Clang, so the overlay is alias-safe under `-O3 -flto`. `attribute_internal_t` gets the same overlay over `attribute_t`, and the engine passes `&attr->pub` where it cast the record (its `data_type` mirror is now the `enum data_types` of `attribute_t`, not `int`). (3) `internal_packet` stays in the public prefix: it is a pointer to an incomplete type, so its presence leaks no layout, and the TCP/IP plugin dereferences it about 590 times in 139 files — moving it is a mass rename the minimal-diff rule forbids. (4) The public offsets are pinned with absolute LP64 values (amd64 and arm64 are the only shipped targets, `PROTO_PATH_SIZE` 16); C++, pre-C11 and other targets skip the asserts. (5) `invalidate_packet_offset_cache()` wrote a now-private field from a header inline, so it became an exported function (API-contract `NO_ERROR_PATH`, waiver added). (6) Test and phase0 fixtures that drive real engine code declare `ipacket_internal_t` and pass `&pkt.pub`; fixtures whose suite stubs the offset helpers keep `ipacket_t`.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h` (`struct ipacket_struct`, the `_Static_assert` block, `invalidate_packet_offset_cache`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/ipacket_internal.h`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`attribute_internal_struct`, `mmt_ipacket_slot_t`, `mmt_handler_struct.current_ipacket`), `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_data.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c`, `tests/core_engine/test_api_contracts.c`.
- Q: Issue #536 (plan task 10.2, F-CLEAN-001 part 2) — what SONAME does each library carry, where do the runtime links live, what counts as a "recorded" incompatible change for the blocking `abi-diff` gate, and what did v1.9.0 change that its notes missed?
  A: (1) `SOVERSION := 1`, appended at the end of `rules/common.mk` (so no cited line range moves) and independent of `VERSION`; all seven links in `rules/common-linux.mk` pass `--soname=$(LIB…).so.$(SOVERSION)`, so `readelf -d sdk/lib/libmmt_core.so.*` shows `libmmt_core.so.1` (v1.9.1 and earlier: the unversioned `libmmt_core.so`). No library NEEDs another `libmmt_*` (the plugins resolve core symbols from the process), so only consumers record the SONAME. (2) `sdk/lib/` keeps one regular `lib<name>.so.$(VERSION)` per library: many scripts glob `sdk/lib/*.so.*`, and `install -m 0755 $(SDKLIB)/*.so.*` would dereference a link into a duplicate file. The runtime link `lib<name>.so.1 -> lib<name>.so.$(VERSION)` (relative) is laid down by every install route instead — `make install` (for the libraries installed; recorded in the manifest, so `uninstall` removes it), the `.deb`/`.rpm` staging (owned by the package rather than left to the postinst `ldconfig`; the security split moves the engines' links into `mmt-dpi-security`), the offline ZIP (`SOVERSION` stamped into `mmt-install-common.sh` by `make zip` like `VERSION`), and `install.sh`'s manifest records whatever SONAME links the pinned tag's Makefile laid down. The plugin loader only loads `*.so` names (`src/mmt_core/src/plugins_engine.c:14`), so a `.so.1` link never loads twice. The unversioned `lib<name>.so` stays as the development link; moving it to a `-dev` package is the Debian track (#538). `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh` maps `libmmt_*.so*` to no package, so the derived Depends/Requires are unchanged. (3) "Recorded": `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh` turns an INCOMPATIBLE verdict into RECORDED when both SONAMEs are readable (`READELF -d`) and differ — the bump is what makes the loader refuse a consumer linked against an older SOVERSION, which is the safety the policy buys from SOVERSION 1 on (a consumer linked against v1.9.1 names the unversioned `lib<name>.so`, which every route still installs as the development link, and plugins are dlopen'ed with no NEEDED on `libmmt_core`, so neither is refused: both must be rebuilt, as the CHANGELOG says); an unreadable SONAME or a library missing on one side stays INCOMPATIBLE (fail-closed). `--fail-on incompatible` counts only unrecorded breaks, `--fail-on change` counts RECORDED too. A libabigail suppression file was not added: no false positive needs one yet, and a waiver that is not a SONAME bump would leave the old consumer loading the new library. Both legs of the `abi-diff` job (the rebuilt v1.9.1 tag with types, the shipped v1.9.1 `.deb` symbols) run with `--fail-on incompatible`; HEAD passes because every SONAME moved. `abi-diff` joins `.github/required-checks.txt` (`check-required-checks.sh` accepts it: a non-matrix job whose skipped run, outside the full tier, reports success, and which the merge queue always runs); making it live-required is a branch-protection change for a maintainer. `ABI_BASELINE` and the script's default `baseline_ref` move to each new tag in the release follow-up PR (`docs/Prepare-for-a-new-released-version.md`), and `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh` (the required `version-agreement` job) fails unless both equal `install.sh`'s `RELEASE_TAG`, so the move cannot be skipped. Until a release carries SONAME `.so.1`, every library is RECORDED against v1.9.1 and the live gate cannot fail: that is the policy, not a hole — no released library has SOVERSION 1 yet, so any further break before that release is covered by the same unreleased bump; from the first `.so.1` release on, an incompatible change needs SOVERSION 2. The failure path is exercised by the `test-abi-diff.sh` fixtures until then. (4) v1.9.0 widened `ipacket_t.ipv6_ext_headers_offset` from `uint16_t` to `uint32_t` (#201) and appended `proto_headers_offset_owned` (#199), `mmt_current_classifier` and `mmt_classifier_claim` (#252): compiled against the v1.8.0 and v1.9.0 headers on LP64, `sizeof(ipacket_t)` is 648 and 704, and `ipv6_ext_headers_len`, `nb_reassembled_packets`, `proto_hierarchy`, `session` and `data` sit at 112/184/456/480/560 and 144/216/488/512/592 — recorded in the Unreleased CHANGELOG section with the policy, since the v1.9.0 section is released.
  Source: `rules/common.mk` (`SOVERSION`), `rules/common-linux.mk` (`--soname`), `sdk/Makefile` (`install`, `MMT_SONAME_LIBS`, `--private-prepare-build-dir`, `zip`), `dist/ZIP/install.sh`, `dist/ZIP/mmt-install-common.sh`, `install.sh` (`write_manifest`), `tools/ci/abi-diff.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-abi-diff.sh`, `tests/installer/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`abi-diff`), `.github/required-checks.txt`, `git show v1.8.0:src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h`, `git show v1.9.0:src/mmt_core/public_include/data_defs.h`.
- Q: Issue #545 (plan task 12.1, F-CLEAN-013) — what replaces the compiled-out `MMT_LOG` and the stdout-writing S1AP macros, which diagnostics move to it, and how is "one logger, off by default, never stdout, with failure counters" met without a public-API change?
  A: (1) One internal logger, `src/mmt_core/private_include/mmt_log.h` + `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_log.c`: levels error/warn/info/debug, threshold `off` unless `MMT_DPI_LOG_LEVEL` (off, error, warn/warning, info, debug or 0-4, read once on first use) or `mmt_log_set_level()` raises it; a `SHOWLOG=1` (`-DDEBUG`) build defaults to `debug`. It writes only to stderr, one `fwrite` per formatted line (512-byte message, truncated lines stay newline-terminated). Each call site owns a static `mmt_log_site_t`: at most `MMT_LOG_SITE_BURST` (10) lines per monotonic second, the rest counted and reported as "(N earlier line(s) suppressed)" on that site's next line. `mmt_log_event()` counts every call per level before the threshold check, so failures stay countable with logging off; `mmt_log_get_stats()` returns those counts plus lines written, rate-limited and failed. All shared state uses `__atomic` builtins (the core_engine suite runs the concurrent-writer test under TSan in `SANITIZE=tsan`). A disabled call evaluates no argument. `mmt_log_debug()` compiles to nothing without `DEBUG`, so a release build cannot be switched to print the decoded, subscriber-identifying fields the S1AP trace levels carry (F-SEC-016); the SHOWLOG exposure is unchanged except that it now goes to stderr. (2) The five functions are `MMTAPI` in the private header — `libmmt_tmobile` calls them across the library boundary — so `libmmt_core` exports 5 more symbols (`docs/Exported-Symbols.md` regenerated; an addition, not an ABI break) and `check-api-contracts.sh`, which reads only `public_include`, needs no entry. No public statistics struct grows: `ipacket_t` and the handler statistics are pinned by #535/#536, so exposing the counters through the public API is left to a follow-up. (3) Moved to the logger: `S1AP_ERROR`/`S1AP_WARN` (were `fprintf(stdout)` per malformed PDU), the four raw `fprintf(stderr)` of `s1ap_decode()`, the NAS decoder's `LOG`, and `S1AP_TRAF/INFO/DEBUG` and `dbg.h`'s `mmt_debug_log()` as its debug level. `XER_FPRINT` (DEBUG only) writes to stderr. One-shot plugin init failures (S1AP's four `S1AP_ERROR` in `init_proto_s1ap()`, the mobile, business-app and DICOM init `fprintf(stderr)`) use `mmt_stderr_log`, the operator channel `dbg.h` reserves for them: routing them through an off-by-default logger would hide a broken install. Kept as they are: `dbg.h`'s `debug()`/`log_err()`/`log_warn()`/`log_info()`/`check()` — the unit suites compile those sources standalone without linking the core, `debug()` is compiled out of release builds and the others sit on allocation/init failure paths — and `mmt_stream_printf` (caller-supplied `FILE *`). (4) `MMT_LOG`, `MMT_LOG_BITTORRENT`, `MMT_LOG_GNUTELLA`, `MMT_LOG_EDONKEY` expanded to `{}`, so their 1,180 call sites (120 files, all under `src/mmt_tcpip`) and the unused `mmt_log_level_t`/`mmt_debug_function_ptr` typedefs of `mmt_tcpip_internal_defs_macros.h` are deleted rather than revived: reviving them would compile 1,180 never-built format strings into hot dissectors. Four sites were the only statement of an unbraced `if` (`proto_irc.c` twice, `proto_soulseek.c` twice); the `if` went with them (side-effect-free conditions), since deleting only the call would have captured the next statement. Evidence: the default-profile `libmmt_tcpip.so` `.text` disassembly (addresses normalised) before and after differs only in two `__LINE__` immediates of `init_proto_gtp_struct`, and `.rodata` is byte-identical, so the fingerprint cannot move. (5) `tools/ci/lint-packet-path-io.sh` also scans `src/mmt_mobile` (minus the generated `asn1c/`), `src/mmt_business_app` and `src/mmt_dicom`, and adds a rule the old macro-definition exemption hid: no macro definition in those trees, `src/mmt_core/src`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib` or `src/mmt_core/private_include` may write to stdout (`printf(`, `puts(`, `fprintf(stdout`, `xer_fprint(stdout`); it flags the pre-#545 `s1ap_common.h`.
  Source: `src/mmt_core/private_include/mmt_log.h`, `src/mmt_core/src/mmt_log.c`, `src/mmt_core/private_include/dbg.h`, `src/mmt_mobile/s1ap/s1ap_common.{h,c}`, `src/mmt_mobile/proto_s1ap.c`, `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.h`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_internal_defs_macros.h`, `tools/ci/lint-packet-path-io.sh`, `tests/core_engine/test_mmt_log.c`, `tests/s1ap_ngap_decode/test_s1ap_ngap_decode.c`, `docs/Exported-Symbols.md`.

- Q: Issue #551 (plan task 13.2, F-BUG-215 part 2 of 2 and F-BUG-011) — how does the security engine's process-global state move per handler without threading an engine argument through ~6,000 lines of legacy loader/evaluator/reporter code, and how are hash-table seeds made unpredictable without making the golden fingerprint, the precision baseline or the fixed-seed resource budgets nondeterministic?
  A: (1) Engine: every global and static that held one engine's state — `op`, `top_rule`, `packet_count`, `p_meta`/`a_utime`, `token2`/`token3` (tips.c), the loader's `bot_rule`/`root_rule`/`top_father`/`bot_father`/`expr_root`/`expr_text` (tips_xml.c) and `corr_mess` (tips_report.c) — is a field of `mmt_sec_engine_t` (tips_internal.h), allocated by each `init_sec_lib()` and passed as the packet handler's argument. The units reach it through the thread-local `sec_cur`, bound by each entry point (load, `analyse_incoming_packet()`, `close_sec_lib()`, the summaries) for its own duration and restored on return; outside them it points to a zeroed, never-registered engine, so the internal functions the suites call directly (`tokenize()`, `xml_summary()`, `get_data_from_pcap()`) behave as on the zeroed globals. An explicit engine parameter was rejected for this change: the loader alone would change ~15 signatures and the evaluator already threads `verify_ctx_t` through every call; the binding gives the same isolation — two engines on two threads, or interleaved on one, never share state — with a mechanical diff. (2) #512's process-wide `engine_state` CAS became a mutex-guarded registry of live engines keyed by handler: one engine per handler (a second `init_sec_lib()` on it is still Error 105, and of two racing calls exactly one loads — `register_packet_handler()` returns success for an id already registered, so the claim cannot rely on it), any number per process. New internal entry points `mmt_sec_engine_of()` (the suites read an engine's rule counters through it) and `close_sec_lib()` (unregisters packet handler 1, eliminates live instances, frees rules, options and tokens). The plan's independent challenge (sensitive-change gate) added four guards: an engine is published (`loaded`, under the registry lock) only once its load succeeded, so `mmt_sec_engine_of()`, `close_sec_lib()` and the summary fallback never see a half-built or failing engine; a registry entry is keyed by the handler's address, so an entry whose handler was closed without `close_sec_lib()` is recognised — the live handler's packet handler 1 is not `analyse_incoming_packet` with that engine as argument — and freed when a later handler reuses the address, instead of answering Error 105 for it; `init_sec_lib()` refuses (Error 42) a handler whose packet handler 1 is already taken, since `register_packet_handler()` would report success without registering and leave the engine unfed; and `close_sec_lib()` unregisters id 1 only once that check proved it is the engine's. `print_summary()`/`xml_summary()` keep their argument-less signatures and report the engine bound on the calling thread, else the most recently loaded live engine — the one engine #512 allowed. Kept process-wide on purpose: `counter_detection` names `detection_<n>.data` files in the one reaction folder, so it stays shared but is claimed with an atomic add whose value `generate_command()` now uses (it re-read the global); the embedded-function `dlopen` handle is shared, its first open serialised by a mutex; `xallocated_memory_size` is an atomic statistic. Removed as write-only: `temptemp` (tips_eval.c) and `OutputFile` (tips.c). libmmt_security is default-visibility and outside the ABI diff and the export doc, so dropping the exported `op`/`top_rule`/`packet_count`/`p_meta`/`a_utime`/`token2`/`token3` data symbols touches no gate. (3) Seeds: the per-table counter of #379 now starts from a base drawn once per process (`pthread_once`, first table creation) with `getrandom(GRND_NONBLOCK)`, falling back to a time/pid/address/clock mix; one draw per process rather than per handler because every table's seed is already a distinct step of the counter — a per-handler draw would cost a syscall per handler and change nothing an attacker can observe. `MMT_DETERMINISTIC_SEED` set to a non-empty value other than `0` keeps the legacy base `0x243F6A8885A308D3`, so the sequence is exactly the pre-#551 one; a set-uid/set-gid process (`getauxval(AT_SECURE)`) ignores it. `check_classification.sh`, `check_precision.sh` and `tests/resource_bounds/run_tests.sh` export it, so their committed baselines stay reproducible by construction rather than by the observation that no output depends on table order (the only seeded-table iterations are teardown frees and `iterate_through_mmt_handlers`). `mmt_oa_table_seed()` exposes a table's seed to the hashmap suite without an export (core is `-fvisibility=hidden` and it is not MMTAPI); `test_session_table seed` prints the first two seeds, `run_tests.sh` requires two runs to differ and two `MMT_DETERMINISTIC_SEED=1` runs to agree, and the binary asserts the legacy values under the variable (its exit status fails the suite).
  Source: `src/mmt_security/tips_internal.h` (`mmt_sec_engine_t`, `sec_cur`, `sec_bind`), `src/mmt_security/tips.c` (`analyse_incoming_packet`, `init_options`, `init_sec_lib`, `close_sec_lib`, `mmt_sec_engine_of`, `find_engine_locked`, `engine_is_registered`, `free_engine`, `with_summary_engine`), `src/mmt_security/tips_xml.c`, `src/mmt_security/tips_report.c` (`open_detection_file`, `generate_command`, `print_engine_summary`, `xml_engine_summary`), `src/mmt_security/tips_extract.c` (`embedded_funct_resolve`), `src/mmt_security/tips_eval.c`, `src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry_attr.c` (`register_packet_handler`), `src/mmt_core/src/hash_utils.c` (`mmt_oa_seed_init`, `mmt_oa_next_seed`, `mmt_oa_table_seed`), `tools/phase0/ci/check_classification.sh`, `tools/phase0/ci/check_precision.sh`, `tests/resource_bounds/run_tests.sh`, `tests/hashmap/{run_tests.sh,test_session_table.c}`, `tests/rule_engine/{run_tests.sh,test_rule_engine.c,test_rule_eval.c}` (`two-engines`, `engine-lifecycle`), `docs/THREADING.md`.

- Q: Issue #658 (ci audit R6) — which variant legs leave the pull-request path for the merge queue while branch protection does not yet require `toolchain-floor`, `toolchain-ceiling`, `assertions-live` or `packages-gate`, and is checking the coverage floors once per merge acceptable (ci audit §8 item 4)?
  A: The merge queue waits only for live-required contexts, so a leg leaves the pull-request path only when a live-required context still fails with it. (1) `coverage` is live-required: it skips on pull requests and gates the queue itself. Its floors are whole-repo metrics (`tests/coverage/floor.json`) and it already ran only on the `full` tier, so one check per merge, before the change reaches `main`, is the cadence chosen; a contributor reads the numbers from the queue run's `unit-suite-coverage` artifact. (2) `toolchain-floor` and `assertions-live` skip on pull requests and are needed by `build`, which is live-required: `build` runs under `!cancelled()` (a job skipped by a failed need reports success) and its first step fails unless each leg succeeded, a skip being accepted on a pull request only. `build` is the natural aggregate — both are variant builds on the standard `ubuntu-24.04` runner — and it costs the queue its own run after the slower leg (1.2 min median in `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §3). This makes the two legs merge-blocking in the queue before their contexts are required live, which is what the issue asks ("nothing merges without them"); a flake in either now evicts its queue entry. `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` refuses an aggregate whose job-level `if:` is not exactly `always()` or `!cancelled()` or that never reads the need's result, and `tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh` runs `build`'s gate step over every event and result. (3) `toolchain-ceiling` stays on pull requests: it runs on the `ubuntu-26.04` preview label, whose required-context status the #657 entry above leaves to a maintainer; through `build`, a stalled or retired preview runner would hold every queue entry until the 120-minute response timeout. (4) The package matrix stays on pull requests: `packages-gate` is in another workflow, so no live-required job can need it, and until a maintainer requires it a failed package leg in the queue would merge. Requiring `toolchain-ceiling` and `packages-gate` (the `required-checks.txt` apply command in `CONTRIBUTING.md`) is the prerequisite for moving them, the latter keeping one deb, one rpm and one reproducibility leg on pull requests.
  Source: `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`build`, `coverage`, `toolchain-floor`, `toolchain-ceiling`, `assertions-live`), `.github/required-checks.txt`, `tools/ci/check-required-checks.sh` (`aggregate_flaw`), `tools/ci/tests/test-required-checks.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-classify-changes.sh`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` (*Required checks and review*), `docs/ci-audit-2026-10.md` §5.6, §8, `gh api repos/montimage-projects/mmt-dpi/branches/main/protection` (live contexts, 2026-10-28).

- Q: Issue #531 (plan task 9.2) — the tcpip family already measured 61.37% when #524 set its floor at 60.3, so the first acceptance criterion (≥ 60%) held before this issue; what does "raise tcpip coverage on the dissectors that actually classify" add, and where do the tests live?
  A: (1) The criterion is met by #524, but the issue's subject is the classifier bodies, so the issue ships tests rather than a bare re-statement of #524's number. Ranking `tests/coverage/coverage-combined.info` by uncovered lines per function put the nDPI-derived classifiers first: `search_ddl_domains()` (444 lines, a generated Host-suffix matcher), then MSN, IMAP, PPLive, Gnutella, Yahoo, BitTorrent, Gadu-Gadu, RTP, NetBIOS, Soulseek, QQ, Jabber, DirectConnect, Oscar, iMesh, AIMini, Zattoo, STUN and SopCast. (2) The tests are one packet/API-path fixture, `classify`, in the existing `tests/parser_boundaries` suite (which already builds the SDK and holds the `classifier-audit` sweep), not a new suite, so `DEFAULT_SUITES` stays at 30. `test_tcpip_classify_api.c` replays 33 opening exchanges (TCP handshake first) plus 71 direct-download Hosts through `mmt_init_handler` + `packet_process`, one host pair per case because classifiers keep per-IP state, and registers and reads back every attribute of each protocol. Port-based classification is off by default (`src/mmt_core/src/packet_registry.c:1000`), so each case only passes through its payload checks; payloads steer clear of the earlier classifiers that claim them first (FTP for `USER`, DICOM for a leading 0x01, DNS for query-shaped headers). (3) Under `SANITIZE=asan` the NetBIOS cases exposed `netbios_classify_next_proto()` overlaying a struct ending in a pointer (8-byte aligned) on the packet — a UBSan misaligned access — and reading `source_port` (bytes 8-9) without a caplen check. It now reads the two fields byte-wise after an `offset + 10 <= caplen` guard; the result depended only on those two fields, so classification is unchanged (golden fingerprint and precision gates pass). (4) Measured (aarch64, GCC 13.3, full `bash tests/run_all_tests.sh --coverage` then `bash tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh`): tcpip 66.21% (17,332/26,174 lines; 61.44% re-measured at the parent commit, 61.37% at #524), whole SDK 70.66% (23,883/33,796).
