The tcpip floor moves to measured minus 1, rounded down to 0.1: 60.3 → 65.2. `whole_sdk_line_pct` stays 64.5 because this issue ratchets the family it targets; M3's whole-SDK push owns that floor. (5) The fix deleted two commented-out lines, so `tools/ci/commented-code-ratchet.txt` banks 121 → 120 blocks.
  Source: `tests/parser_boundaries/test_tcpip_classify_api.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/run_tests.sh` (`classify`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/proto_netbios.c` (`netbios_classify_next_proto`), `tests/coverage/floor.json` (`sdk.family_floors.tcpip`, `sdk.note`), `docs/AGENT_ENVIRONMENT.md` §3, `tools/ci/commented-code-ratchet.txt`.

## 2026-10-29

- Q: Issue #475 — how does the Debian source package (`debian/`) lay out the SDK, and how does it coexist with the upstream `make deb` package that `tools/ci/build-package.sh` ships to GitHub Releases?
  A: (1) Two packagings, two jobs. `make -C sdk deb` (unchanged) builds the upstream release asset: one `mmt-dpi` package (plus `mmt-dpi-security` with `ENABLESEC=1`, #497) installing under `/opt/mmt` with `make install`'s layout and loader drop-in. `debian/` is what a Debian archive builds itself: `dpkg-buildpackage` runs `make -C sdk` and `make -C sdk install-fhs` (`debian/rules`) and splits the result into `libmmt-dpi1` (`/usr/lib/<triplet>/libmmt_*.so.1` and `.so.1.9.1`, plus `/usr/lib/<triplet>/mmt-dpi/plugins/*.so`, relative links to those libraries) and `libmmt-dpi-dev` (`/usr/include/mmt-dpi`, the `libmmt_*.so` development links, `pkgconfig/mmt-dpi.pc`, the examples under `/usr/share/doc/libmmt-dpi-dev/examples`). The security and fuzz engines are not built for Debian. The two never share a file name or a path, so neither changes the other. (2) `install-fhs` (end of `sdk/Makefile`) is a separate target, not a mode of `install`: it writes under `DESTDIR` at the `MMT_LIB`/`MMT_INC`/`MMT_PLUGINS`/`MMT_EXAMS` the caller names (it refuses the `/opt/mmt` defaults), never compiles (`--check-built` refuses a build whose baked-in plugin directory differs, as for `install`), and writes no loader drop-in, no install manifest and no licence files — the package manager owns those. `install`, `uninstall` and the `MMT_BASE` contract are untouched. (3) The 2026-09 version of this work carried an opt-in `SOVERSION ?=` and its own `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS`; on rebasing over main both were dropped in favour of main's: every build links `lib<name>.so.$(SOVERSION)` since #536 (`SOVERSION := 1`, which `debian/rules` no longer overrides, so the package follows upstream's ABI number), and #514 already appends `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS` to `CFLAGS`, which `debian/rules` fills from `dpkg-buildflags`. The plugin libraries keep main's link (no `DT_NEEDED` on `libmmt_core`, #533/#534 export maps unchanged); `debian/rules` points `dh_shlibdeps` at the core with `-l`. (4) The tag gate's `debian/changelog` check is not added to `tools/ci/check-release-tag.sh`: `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh` already requires the first `debian/changelog` entry's upstream version (epoch, revision and `+ds`/`+dfsg` removed) to equal `VERSION`, on every pull request (`version-agreement`) and in `verify-tag`. (5) CI: the `debian-package` job of `release-packages.yml` (amd64 and arm64, `debian:13` pinned in `tools/ci/base-images.txt`) runs `tools/ci/build-debian-package.sh` — `dpkg-buildpackage -b`, `lintian --fail-on error`, install, then `debian/tests/installed-consumer` — and sits behind `packages-gate`; its amd64 leg also builds the byte-reproducible source tarball (`tools/ci/build-source-tarball.sh --verify`), which the `release` job publishes beside the packages so `SHA256SUMS` and the attestation cover it. The Debian `.deb`s themselves are never published. Not resolved here: the licence-provenance blockers of `debian/license-audit.md`, the ITP bug number and the `UNRELEASED` distribution in `debian/changelog` — #475 stays open for the official-channel submission; #538 and #550 build on this layout.
  Source: `debian/rules`, `sdk/Makefile` (`install-fhs`), `rules/common.mk` (#536 block), `rules/arch-linux.mk:20-21`, `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh:75-81`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`debian-package`), `tests/installer/run_tests.sh` (sections 5b and 7b).

- Q: Issue #527, second tranche — which pending classifiers get cases now, how is a classifier recorded when no genuine flow can reach it, and what happens to the pending checkers issue #540 may move out of the default chains?
  A: (1) This tranche covers 20 classifiers of services still in use, chosen ahead of the defunct-service checkers: `mmt_check_usenet`, `mmt_check_http_application_activesync`, `mmt_check_icecast`, `mmt_check_shoutcast`, `mmt_check_ndn_http`, `mmt_check_dcerpc`, `mmt_check_afp`, `mmt_check_citrix`, `mmt_check_flash`, `mmt_check_stun_tcp`, `mmt_check_iax`, `mmt_check_eclipse_tcf`, `mmt_check_mqtt`, `mmt_check_xdmcp_tcp`, `mmt_check_xdmcp_udp`, `mmt_check_teamviewer_tcp`, `mmt_check_teamviewer_udp`, `mmt_check_steam`, `mmt_check_worldofwarcraft` and `mmt_check_xbox` (registered on both transports, detected over UDP only); Steam, Xbox and World of Warcraft use legacy signatures, and if #540 moves a covered classifier out of the default chains its cases must name it no longer (the oracle fails on a case naming an unregistered classifier); 68 of 143 are covered and 75 remain pending. Each case is built from the protocol's specification (or, for proprietary protocols, the published reverse-engineered layout, said so in its review notes) and also meets what the classifier's code requires; where the two differ only the conformant variant is used, and the difference is written into the case: the SHOUTcast source password must start with `123456` (`proto_shoutcast.c:27`), RTMP's C0+C1 is sent as one 1537-byte PSH segment, because the generator always sets PSH and `proto_flash.c:44-63` otherwise needs non-PSH partial segments, NDN is detected over UDP only because `proto_ndn_http.c:218` excludes the protocol on the payload-less TCP SYN, and STUN over TCP only with RFC 4571 framing (`proto_stun.c:125-126`). (2) The MQTT negative is a genuine SOCKS5 greeting on 1883, whose first byte carries the reserved MQTT packet type 0 (`proto_mqtt.c:22`), so it exercises only that rejection branch; plain text on 1883 stays the known false accept `acc_unknown_mqtt_port`. (3) A classifier no genuine flow can reach stays pending, with the cause as its reason, rather than getting a case shaped to the bug: `mmt_check_vnc` (`proto_vnc.c:52`) and `mmt_check_tpkt` (`proto_tpkt.c:67`) exclude themselves on the TCP handshake, `mmt_check_tds` is shadowed by `mmt_check_mssql` earlier in the chain, which claims every conformant TDS header (Window 0) off port 102 (`proto_mssql.c:41-51`), framed `mmt_check_rtp_tcp` runs only after STUN is detected (`proto_rtp.c:822-831`) and the pipeline then dispatches the flow to `mmt_check_stun_tcp` alone (`src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c:740-758`), its only other route being a defunct Yahoo framing (`proto_rtp.c:811-817`), `mmt_check_spotify` needs Spotify's own 78.31.8.0/22 range and `mmt_check_direct_download_link` real hoster names, which the corpus may not carry. (4) F-BUG-311's list of the 49 defunct checkers is not in the repository, so the remaining "no reviewed case yet" entries are deferred until #540 settles which checkers leave the default chains; an entry #540 removes from the chains must then leave the pending list too (the oracle fails on a pending entry the chains no longer register).
  Source: `tools/phase0/gen_tcpip_pcap.py` (`ACC_CLASSIFIER_FLOWS`), `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/corpus.json`, `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/coverage-pending.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/check-accuracy-corpus.py` (`check_coverage`), `tools/ci/tests/test-accuracy-corpus.py`.

- Q: Issue #538 — should the Debian/Ubuntu release legs now publish the `debian/` FHS packages (`libmmt-dpi1`, `libmmt-dpi-dev`) in place of the `make deb` `/opt/mmt` packages, how is the install layout de-duplicated, and how is the source tarball signed when no release key exists yet?
  A: (1) Not switched, conservatively. Every Debian/Ubuntu distribution of the release matrix (Ubuntu 22.04, 24.04, 26.04, Debian 12, 13; amd64 and arm64, the build matrix's pinned digests) now runs `tools/ci/build-debian-package.sh` — `dpkg-buildpackage -b`, `lintian --fail-on error`, install, `debian/tests/installed-consumer` — in the `debian-package` job, which keeps its id (so `packages-gate`, `release` and `release-alert` are unchanged) and gains a `distro` matrix; this supersedes item (5) of the #475 entry above, whose job built on `debian:13` only. It stays a separate job, not a step of the `build` legs, so a slow apt or lintian run cannot push a `make deb` leg past its 60-minute cap. Its `.deb`s are still not published: the release assets, `make-release-bootstrap.sh` (whose name pattern accepts only `mmt-dpi*_…` packages) and the bootstrap installer stay on the upstream `make deb` packages, so the bootstrap keeps installing on ubuntu:24.04 and debian:12 exactly as before. Publishing the FHS packages and moving the bootstrap to them changes the installed layout of every bootstrap user (`/opt/mmt` → `/usr/lib/<triplet>`) and has no `mmt-dpi-security` counterpart (`debian/` builds no ENABLESEC engines), so it is left to the maintainer as release work; #538 stays open for it. (2) `sdk/Makefile` defines the install layout once — `MMT_LAYOUT_PLUGINS`, `MMT_LAYOUT_LIBS` (core + plugins) and `MMT_LAYOUT_SEC_LIBS` — and `install` (dev and SONAME links, plugins), the manifest and `uninstall` lists (`MMT_SONAME_LIBS`, `MMT_LIB_LINKS`, `MMT_PLUGIN_FILES`), `SEC_ENGINES_BUILT`, the deb/rpm staging, the rpm `%files` and `install-fhs` (`FHS_LIBS`/`FHS_PLUGINS`) iterate it. The engines are named literally there because `rules/common.mk` defines `LIBSECURITY`/`LIBFUZZ` only under `ENABLESEC`, while `install` and the package split test for the engine files at run time: an `ENABLESEC=1` build installed or packaged by a plain `make install`/`make deb` must keep its engine links. An `ENABLESEC=1` install, an uninstall and `make deb` (with and without `ENABLESEC`) were compared file by file with the previous Makefile and are identical; the generated rpm spec differs only in trailing whitespace. The installer suite fails on a second hard-coded plugin/engine list (with a negative fixture). (3) `debian/watch` now finds the release asset `mmt-dpi-<ver>.tar.gz` through the GitHub releases API (`searchmode=plain`, `repacksuffix=+ds`, `dversionmangle=auto`) instead of GitHub's unsigned tag archives. No signing key was created: `debian/upstream/signing-key.asc` and the `MMT_RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY` secret must come from the maintainer. `tools/ci/sign-source-tarball.sh` is wired in so it activates once they exist — `--check` (run by `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh`) requires the committed key and `pgpsigurlmangle` in `debian/watch` together; the tag-only `release` job signs every tarball before `SHA256SUMS` and the attestation, verifies the `.asc` with `gpgv` against the committed key alone, warns and ships unsigned while neither exists, and fails when only one half (key or secret) is present.
  Source: `sdk/Makefile` (`MMT_LAYOUT_*`, `install`, `--private-prepare-build-dir`, `rpm`, `install-fhs`), `rules/common.mk:225-228`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`debian-package`, `release`), `tools/ci/sign-source-tarball.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/test-source-signing.sh`, `debian/watch`, `debian/README.source`, `tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh:59-62`, `tests/installer/run_tests.sh` (section 4c).

- Q: Issue #550 — how does the RPM recipe build from the source tarball, how does every install route ship `mmt-dpi.pc`, and are `make deb`/`make rpm`, `tools/ci/shlib-deps.sh`, `tools/ci/check-package-deps.sh` and the offline ZIP retired?
  A: (1) `mmt-dpi.spec` (repository root) is the distribution recipe, the RPM counterpart of `debian/`: `Source0` is the reproducible release tarball of `tools/ci/build-source-tarball.sh`, `%build`/`%install` pass one variable set (`MMT_LIB=%{_libdir}`, `MMT_INC=%{_includedir}/mmt-dpi`, `MMT_PLUGINS=%{_libdir}/mmt-dpi/plugins`, `MMT_EXAMS`, `GIT_VERSION`) to `make -C sdk` and `make -C sdk install-fhs`, the distribution flags go through `MMT_EXTRA_CFLAGS` and an environment `LDFLAGS` (a command-line `LDFLAGS` would override the `-Wl,-z,defs` `rules/common-linux.mk` adds to libmmt_core), `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` comes from the `%changelog`, `%check` runs the compiler-only suites `debian/rules` runs, and the packages are `mmt-dpi` (libraries, plugins) and `mmt-dpi-devel` (headers, links, `mmt-dpi.pc`, examples); no ENABLESEC engine is built (their licence differs, as in `debian/`). Requires/Provides come from rpm's own dependency generators. The new `rpm-package` job of `release-packages.yml` runs `tools/ci/build-rpm-package.sh` on the four EL images of the build matrix (Rocky 9/10, CentOS Stream 9/10), amd64 and arm64: `rpmbuild -ba`, `rpmlint` on the spec, SRPM and RPMs (an error fails; measured 0 errors), install, `pkg-config` with no `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`, then the installed consumer. rpmlint is in EL9's AppStream but only in EPEL on EL10, so the script installs the distribution's `epel-release` there. No Fedora leg: the release matrix pins no Fedora image. (2) Not switched, conservatively, as #538 left the Debian side: the spec keeps the Name `mmt-dpi` because it is the successor of the `make rpm` package, so the two are alternatives (incompatible layouts, unordered Release fields) and must not be published side by side; the job uploads nothing, since the `release` and `bootstrap-dry-run` jobs collect every `*.rpm` among the artifacts. Retiring `make deb`/`make rpm`, `shlib-deps.sh` and most of `check-package-deps.sh` means switching the published assets and the verified bootstrap (`tools/ci/make-release-bootstrap.sh`) to the `debian/` and spec packages, which is the maintainer's call; it stays open on #550. (3) `mmt-dpi.pc` comes from one template in `sdk/Makefile` (`mmt_write_pc`) written by `make install` (`$(MMT_LIB)/pkgconfig`, recorded in the install manifest, removed by `uninstall`), by `install-fhs` and by the `make deb`/`make rpm` staging, which also ships a `/usr/share/pkgconfig/mmt-dpi.pc` link so a root-installed package is found without `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`. For the /opt-style routes its `rpath` variable puts `-Wl,-rpath,${libdir}` in `Libs`, so a consumer runs without `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`; install-fhs leaves it empty. `tools/ci/tests/run-installed-consumer.sh` now compiles through `pkg-config --cflags --libs mmt-dpi` (the install's own `.pc` first on `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`, its libdir/includedir/plugindir checked against the install under test) and drops the rpath on the `--root-install` leg so that leg still proves the loader cache; `pkg-config` joined the release containers' build dependencies (`tools/ci/build-deps.txt` role `pkgconfig`). The spec's own `BuildRequires:` (gcc, make) is not a `build-deps.txt` list: the `rpm-package` job builds the spec in each EL container, which also carries the job's own tools, so it shows the list suffices there (a minimal-buildroot check such as mock is not run). (4) The offline ZIP is retired: no workflow built or published it (no release carries one), so `dist/ZIP/` and the `zip` target are deleted and `install.sh` no longer sources `dist/ZIP/mmt-install-common.sh` — its own `validate_mmt_base`/`prefix_writable`, which the installer suite had kept byte-identical to the shared copies, are now the only definitions; the version-agreement check reads `mmt-dpi.spec`'s `Version:` in place of the ZIP fallback. (5) `MMT_EXAMPLES_SRC` named `html_integration.c`, `html_integration.h` and `reconstruct_body.c`, which do not exist, and `google-fr.pcap`, which no rule copies (only `.c` files have one) and whose redistribution terms are unrecorded (`debian/license-audit.md`); it now names exactly the `src/examples/*.c` programs, `mmt_export_info.c` included (`tests/coverage/floor.json` already called it installed).
  Source: `mmt-dpi.spec`, `tools/ci/build-rpm-package.sh`, `.github/workflows/release-packages.yml` (`changes`, `rpm-package`, `packages-gate`, `release`), `sdk/Makefile` (`mmt_write_pc`, `install`, `MMT_LIST_INSTALLED`, `--private-prepare-build-dir`, `rpm`, `install-fhs`), `rules/common-linux.mk:247`, `rules/common.mk` (`MMT_EXAMPLES_SRC`), `install.sh`, `tools/ci/tests/run-installed-consumer.sh`, `tools/ci/build-deps.txt`, `tools/ci/install-build-deps.sh`, `scripts/validate-release-prep.sh`, `tests/installer/run_tests.sh`, `git ls-files dist`.
- Q: Issue #542 (F-CLEAN-012) — which `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/*.c` files are registration-only stubs, what row form replaces them in `proto_init_list.def`, and how is the change shown to be fingerprint-neutral?
  A: A stub is a file whose whole content is the four includes, the empty "PROTOCOL INTERNAL CODE" markers and `int init_proto_<x>_struct()` calling `init_protocol_struct_for_registration(PROTO_<X>, PROTO_<X>_ALIAS)` then `register_protocol(…, PROTO_<X>)`. 502 tracked files match that template byte for byte with the names substituted; four more (`proto_afreeca.c`, `proto_free.c`, `proto_ip_in_ip.c`, `proto_ctp.c`) differ only in trailing whitespace, a missing final newline or one commented-out line, so 506 files went (657 → 151). Every one of them was named by exactly one `MMT_PROTO_INIT` row and called from nowhere else, and every alias is the id's `_ALIAS` macro (even where the file name differs from the protocol, e.g. `proto_lockerz.c` registered `PROTO_LEMONDE`); the ids and aliases were read from the files, not derived from the row's display name. Each such row became `MMT_PROTO_SIMPLE(PROTO_<X>, PROTO_<X>_ALIAS, "<display name>")` in place, so the table order — and with it the registration and classifier-chain order — is unchanged. The display name is kept as a third argument (the issue's sketch was `(id, alias)`) so the init-failure message and `tools/gen-protocol-list.sh`, which now reads both row forms, stay byte-identical: `docs/PROTOCOLS.md` regenerates unchanged at 650 initializers. `configured_protocols.c`'s `proto_init_table[]` gained `id`/`alias` fields and a row with no init function goes to the file-static `mmt_register_simple_protocol()`, the stubs' body verbatim; `mmt_common_internal_include.h` expands the new rows to nothing. The init symbols were already hidden by #534's `-fvisibility=hidden`, so `nm -D` of `libmmt_tcpip.so` and `docs/Exported-Symbols.md` are unchanged; the library shrinks from 1,212,328 to 1,097,296 bytes (default profile). `mmt_export_info` output (every protocol and attribute with its id) is identical before and after (670 protocols, 7,450 attributes), and the phase0 classification and precision gates pass against the committed baselines. `docs/Add-New-Protocol.md`'s lemonde walk-through — itself a stub — now adds a `MMT_PROTO_SIMPLE` row instead of a file, and `tools/ci/test-map.txt` drops its eight entries for deleted tunnel stubs (`configured_protocols.c` and the list are already `full`). The `registration` fixture of `tests/parser_boundaries` (`test_simple_registration_api.c`) expands the list's `MMT_PROTO_SIMPLE` rows and checks each against the registry after `init_extraction()`. Coverage: every stub was 5 executable lines with 4 hit (only the never-taken `else return 0` missed), so deleting them removed 2,530 lines, 2,024 of them hit, from the tcpip family denominator of `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh`. A `--coverage` run on this branch measured tcpip 64.79% (14,690/22,670), against 66.32% (16,708/25,191) on main on the same aarch64 GCC 13.3 machine, below the 65.2 floor; x86-64 CI main measured 66.32% (16,710/25,196) and projects 64.79% (14,686/22,666) with the stub records removed, so the floor below holds there too. Uncovered tcpip lines fell from 8,483 to 7,980, so nothing that was exercised lost its tests; `sdk.family_floors.tcpip` is re-baselined to 63.7 (measured minus 1, the rule the floor note states), recorded in the note. Whole SDK (70.36%), the original 30-file cohort (90.35%) and the unit floor (79.9%) still hold.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/proto_init_list.def`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/configured_protocols.c` (`mmt_register_simple_protocol`, `init_tcpip_plugin`), `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_common_internal_include.h`, `tools/gen-protocol-list.sh`, `docs/Add-New-Protocol.md`, `tools/ci/test-map.txt`, `tests/parser_boundaries/{run_tests.sh,test_simple_registration_api.c}`, `src/examples/mmt_export_info.c`, `tests/coverage/floor.json` (`sdk.family_floors.tcpip`), `tools/ci/check-sdk-coverage.sh`.
- Q: Issue #548 (plan task 12.4, F-CLEAN-008 part 2) — how are the remaining over-200-line functions of the security engine, DICOM and NDN split without a behaviour change, what replaces the NAS `CHECK_*DECODER` macros that hid `return`, and what holds the result while `tools/ci/report-function-shape.sh` still needs explicit files?
  A: (1) Each function became a dispatcher over `static` helpers; no exported symbol or prototype changed. `store_history` (`src/mmt_security/tips_report.c:619`, was 413 lines at depth 7) writes through the `history_json_t`/`history_attr_t` contexts; `create_boolean_expression` (`tips_xml.c:925`) keeps the operators and hands constants, attributes and function calls to parsers; `compare_values` (`tips_extract.c:879`) dispatches per type family, `get_data_from_pcap` (`tips_extract.c:1296`) per operand kind; `_extraction_att` (`src/mmt_dicom/dicom.c:410`) per PDU type over a `dicom_att_ctx_t`; `ndn_session_data_analysis` (`src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ndn.c:2119`) per step, and `ndn_name_components_at_index` (`ndn.c:639`, depth 7) lost a level to `ndn_name_component_string`. Existing quirks are kept (the date GT/GTE year test, the r2 SKIP marking v1, NDN's leak paths). `compare_values` was 60/168 lines covered, so 49 characterisation checks in `tests/rule_engine/test_overflow_family.c` pin it per type family before the split. (2) The NAS checks are `static inline` functions that only check — `nas_check_pdu` and `nas_check_length` (`src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.h:121-137`) — and each call site returns their negative result itself; `CHECK_RESULT_DECODER` became an explicit `if (ret < 0) return ret; decoded += ret;`. The log lines stay in two do/while(0) macros expanded at the call site (`NAS_LOG_PDU_CHECK`, `NAS_LOG_IEI_MISMATCH`, `decoder.h:139-155`), because `mmt_log_warn` records `__FILE__`/`__LINE__` and one rate-limit state per expansion (`src/mmt_core/private_include/mmt_log.h:87-93`): moving them into a header function would have named `decoder.h` in every NAS diagnostic and merged the per-site rate limits (a plan-challenge finding). The operands are `int64_t`: every argument is evaluated in its own type and converted losslessly, and `-Wsign-compare` showed the only mixed comparisons have a non-negative signed side, so every comparison result is unchanged. `tests/nas_ies_tail` pins the return codes, the unsigned-wrap and negative-remainder cases and the caller file in the log line. `RETURN()` and `CHECK_MESSAGE_TYPE` (205 uses) are out of scope. With pinned cppcheck 2.22 the `nullPointerRedundantCheck` suppression in `nas_msg.c` is no longer needed, so it went with its comment; the ratchet stays 0 in both modes. (3) The `verify-helpers` job runs the shape report in gate mode (≤200 lines, depth ≤6) over `src/mmt_security/*.c`, `src/mmt_dicom/*.c`, `ndn.c` and the NAS sources (`.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml:600`), a blocking step that holds the acceptance criterion until issue #547 re-aims the report at all of `src/`. `tools/ci/function-shape-tips.txt` and `param-count-tips.txt` were regenerated. The phase0 classification and precision baselines are unchanged.
  Source: `src/mmt_security/{tips_report,tips_xml,tips_extract}.c`, `src/mmt_dicom/dicom.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/ndn.c`, `src/mmt_mobile/nas/util/decoder.h`, `src/mmt_mobile/nas/{nas_msg.c,util/octet_string.c,ies/*.c,emm/*.c,esm/*.c}`, `tests/rule_engine/test_overflow_family.c`, `tests/nas_ies_tail/test_nas_ies_tail.c`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`), `tools/ci/{function-shape-tips,param-count-tips}.txt`.
- Q: Issue #540 (F-BUG-311, F-DEAD-003/004/005/012) — which classifiers count as defunct-service checkers, how are they taken out of the default chains while staying reachable, and which never-reached paths go?
  A: (1) F-BUG-311's list of 49 registrations is not in the repository (it was an audit artifact, as the #527 entry above notes), so it was reconstructed with one conservative rule: a checker leaves the default chains only when the service or client protocol it matches is unequivocally retired. That gives 38 rows of `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/inter_proto_classif_list.def`, 35 functions: TCP — `fasttrack`, `msn_tcp`, `yahoo_tcp`, `oscar`, `filetopia`, `manolito_tcp`, `imesh_tcp`, `mms`, `pando`, `tvants_tcp`, `tvuplayer_tcp`, `ppstream_tcp`, `zattoo_tcp`, `feidian_tcp`, `i23v5`, `socrates_tcp`, `pcanywhere`, `veohtv_tcp`, `openft`, `stealthnet`, `meebo`, `aimini_tcp`, `world_of_kung_fu` (23); UDP — `msn_udp`, `yahoo_udp`, `oscar`, `manolito_udp`, `imesh_udp`, `pando`, `tvants_udp`, `tvuplayer_udp`, `ppstream_udp`, `zattoo_udp`, `feidian_udp`, `socrates_udp`, `pcanywhere`, `veohtv_udp`, `aimini_udp` (15) (each `mmt_check_` prefixed); `zattoo_*` match Zattoo's retired P2P client protocol, not its current streaming service. A first cut also took 11 rows (9 functions) of borderline services; review moved them back to the default chains, in place, because disabling still-live protocols by default would be a recall loss on real traffic: Gnutella (`gnutella`, TCP and UDP — the decentralized network still runs and gtk-gnutella is maintained), NetEase POPO (`popo`, TCP and UDP — still offered as an enterprise IM), SopCast (`sopcast_tcp`, `sopcast_udp` — its P2P network still carries live streams), Kontiki (`kontiki` — the technology continues as Kollective's enterprise ECDN), PPLive/PPTV (`pplive_tcp`, `pplive_udp` — PPTV still operates), WinMX (`winmx` — a community-run peer network is still online) and AppleJuice (`applejuice` — community clients and servers are still distributed). Checkers of services still operated stay in the default chains even where their signature is old: eDonkey, DirectConnect, Soulseek, Gadu-Gadu, QQ, Thunder, Second Life and the games (Warcraft III, Florensia, MapleStory, Dofus, Fiesta, CrossFire, Guild Wars, Half-Life 2, Quake, Battlefield, Armagetron), as do Steam, Xbox and World of Warcraft, which have accuracy-corpus cases. (2) Mechanism: the rows become `MMT_INTER_PROTO_LEGACY(parent, fn, weight)` in place, so registration order and weights — and with them the chain order under the legacy profile — are unchanged. `init_tcpip_plugin()` registers them as before and then marks their chain node (`mmt_classify_proto_t.legacy`) through `mmt_classifier_mark_legacy()`, a static inline of the private `packet_processing.h`, so libmmt_core gains no export (the exported-symbol list, the API contracts and the ABI diff are unchanged). `proto_packet_classify_next()` skips a legacy node unless the handler's new `legacy_classify` lever is set; a skipped node is not counted as a checker call. (3) The lever has no field in the public `mmt_dpi_profile_t` (adding one would change the struct a caller builds by value): it is set only by named profiles — the predefined `legacy` profile (the `default` levers plus the lever; `MMT_DPI_PROFILE=legacy` works too) and a custom profile with an optional sixth column of 1 — while `mmt_apply_dpi_profile()` with any struct, and every other named profile, clears it, so each application fully determines the handler's levers. (4) MSN no longer re-inspects flows already classified SSL (the `PROTO_SSL` branches of `mmt_search_msn_tcp()`, the entry condition of `mmt_check_msn_tcp()` and the SSL bit of its detection mask go, and with them the unused `msn_ssl_ft` flow bit-field). (5) Never-reached paths deleted: `init_http_proto_struct()` (nothing registered it; `proto_http.c` owns HTTP) and the undefined `init_http_proto_struct_new()` declaration, the three `#ifdef PROTO_WINDOWS_UPDATE` blocks of `http.c` (the macro is defined nowhere) and `tcp_seg_new()` (no caller; reassembly carves segments from the segment store or the flow arena). The legacy HTTP session parser `init_http_proto_struct()` used to register (`http_session_data_analysis()` and its helpers) is still pinned by three phase0 harnesses (`http_session`, `http_method_classification`, `http_scanner`) and is left for a follow-up, as are the always-true `#ifdef PROTO_*` guards (192 at this commit; `PROTO_WINDOWS_UPDATE` was the only guard macro left undefined) and the test-only hostname matchers the issue mentions, which no acceptance criterion names. (6) Classification impact: the CI golden fingerprint (`tools/phase0/ci/baseline/classification.txt`) and the precision/recall baseline (`precision.txt`) are unchanged — none of their flows was won by a legacy checker — so neither baseline is regenerated. Over the full mmt-test golden set (`tools/phase0/golden_pcaps.txt`) the fingerprint is also unchanged; on the throughput captures, `smallFlows.pcap` moves 296 packets of genuine 2010-era MSN Messenger sessions (port 1863) from `tcp.msn` to `tcp.unknown`, and `bigFlows.pcap` moves 20 packets of Yahoo Messenger YMSG keep-alives (port 5050) from `tcp.yahoo` to `tcp.unknown` (its other 841 `tcp.yahoo` packets are Yahoo web traffic attributed by hostname, which stays); under `MMT_DPI_PROFILE=legacy` every capture classifies exactly as on main. (7) Tests: the `dpi_profiles` suite pins the profile (by name, environment and file column, and that `full` leaves the lever off), the walk gate and the marker on a fake chain; the `classify` fixture of `parser_boundaries` replays the 8 cases of defunct-service protocols twice — not classified by a default handler, classified by a `legacy` one — against the real plugin; the accuracy oracle drops the 35 functions from `coverage-pending.txt` (its chain parser never matched `MMT_INTER_PROTO_LEGACY`, now said so and self-tested), and the 33 remaining entries deferred to #540 (the 9 functions moved back among them) now say the checker stays in the default chains.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/inter_proto_classif_list.def`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/configured_protocols.c` (`init_tcpip_plugin`), `src/mmt_core/private_include/packet_processing.h` (`mmt_classify_proto_struct`, `mmt_classifier_mark_legacy`, `legacy_classify`), `src/mmt_core/src/packet_pipeline.c` (`proto_packet_classify_next`), `src/mmt_core/src/dpi_profiles.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/{proto_msn.c,http.c,http.h,tcp_segment.c}`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/tcp_segment.h`, `tests/dpi_profiles/test_dpi_profiles.c`, `tests/parser_boundaries/test_tcpip_classify_api.c`, `tools/phase0/ci/accuracy/coverage-pending.txt`, `tools/ci/tests/{check,test}-accuracy-corpus.py`, `docs/DPI-Profiles.md`.

## 2026-10-30

- Q: Issue #547 (plan task 12.3, F-CLEAN-008 part 1, F-CLEAN-018) — how are the over-200-line functions of `src/mmt_tcpip` split without a classification change, why is the direct-download host list not moved into `doted_host_names`, and what holds the result over all of `src/`?
  A: (1) At 8fa3a816, 14 `src/mmt_tcpip` functions were over 200 lines (`ndn_session_data_analysis` was already split by #548). Three were data, not logic: `get_application_class_by_protocol_id` (656-line switch) reads the designated-initializer table `application_class_by_protocol_id[PROTO_MAX_IDENTIFIER]` in `mmt_tcpip_classes.c` (an id outside the table, or unlisted, is `PROTO_CLASS_UNKOWN` as the switch's `default` was; old and new agree for every id from -50 to 4,999); `mmt_http_init_detection_bitmask` lost its 380-line `http_detected_protocols` list to file scope in `http.c`; `search_ddl_domains` (`proto_direct_download_link.c`) keeps its GET/POST, length, request-line and `:port` preconditions and replaces the generated suffix trie with `ddl_host_suffixes` (76 rows, one per accepting branch, in the trie's order) and the matcher `ddl_host_matches()`: a row matches when the host ends with it and the byte before it is ' ' or '.', which is the test every accepting branch made. Parsing the trie gave the rows; a differential harness ran the old trie and the new matcher on 3.2M hosts (every suffix of every row, one-byte mutations and deletions, cross-row concatenations under 10 prefixes, and random strings over the trie alphabet) under ASan/UBSan with no difference. The issue's sketch was to move the hosts into `doted_host_names`; that is a different mechanism (the hostname trie of `mmt_tcpip_classif_utils.c`, run at another point of the chain, with its own exact/suffix rules), so it would change which flows become DDL and when — rejected as not fingerprint-neutral. The other 11 functions (`mmt_search_msn_tcp`, `mmt_int_search_bittorrent_tcp_zero`, `mmt_classify_gnutella`, `mmt_check_pplive_tcp`, `mmt_classify_imap`, `_extraction_int_report_att`, `_mmt_parse_packet_line_info`, `mmt_search_yahoo_tcp`, `mmt_search_gadugadu_tcp`, `mmt_classify_jabber_tcp`, `mmt_check_netbios_udp`) became dispatchers over `static` stage helpers whose moved text is unchanged apart from indentation; each file states its convention for the former `return;`/`goto` exits (a file-local enum or the original return value with 0 as fall-through), and quirks are kept (MSN's duplicated "asym (1)" block runs twice on a first packet as before). No exported symbol or prototype changed. (2) `tools/ci/report-function-shape.sh` and `tools/ci/report-param-count.sh` take an optional FILE list: without one they cover every tracked `src/` C source except generated asn1c (`src/mmt_mobile/asn1c/`) and `tools/ci/vendor-paths.txt`, and gate (≤200 lines, depth ≤6; ≤6 parameters) against `tools/ci/function-shape-ratchet.txt` (6 depth-only rows) and `tools/ci/param-count-ratchet.txt` (3 rows). A recorded function passes at no more lines/depth/parameters than its row; an unrecorded or grown offender fails, and so does a row whose function is within the limits or gone, or that records more than today's measure, so a fix must delete or tighten its row in the same change (stricter than the count ratchet of `lint-commented-code.sh`, which only notes a drop: a per-function row left loose would re-allow the regression). A row of a file outside an explicit FILE list stays dormant; same-named functions of one file share a row. `--emit-ratchet` prints the rows. The `verify-helpers` step of `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` that #548 scoped to the engine, DICOM, NDN and NAS sources now runs both scripts with no argument, and the advisory `proto_irc.c` shape entry went (the src/-wide gate covers it). `tools/ci/classify-changes.sh` does not read the two ratchet files as references — they name `src/` functions as data, like the test map, and verify-helpers runs the gate on every tier — so a source they name keeps its family tier instead of turning a pull request `full`. Scope is `.c` sources: the `static inline` bodies of `src/` headers are not measured. `tools/ci/tests/test-shape-ratchet.sh`, run by the `release_gates` suite, pins the modes on a fixture repository. (3) Verified: `report-function-shape.sh --report-only` shows no `src/mmt_tcpip` function over 200 lines; the default build is green with the same warnings; every tracked pcap (195) classifies identically to main under the default and the `legacy` profile; the phase0 classification and precision gates pass against the unchanged baselines; the split IMAP/Jabber/NetBIOS/INT functions were also run side by side with the HEAD copies on generated packet sequences with identical state and output.
  Source: `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_classes.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/mmt_tcpip_utils.c`, `src/mmt_tcpip/lib/protocols/{http.c,proto_direct_download_link.c,proto_msn.c,proto_yahoo.c,proto_gadugadu.c,proto_bittorrent.c,proto_gnutella.c,proto_pplive.c,proto_imap.c,proto_jabber.c,proto_netbios.c,proto_int.c}`, `tools/ci/{report-function-shape.sh,report-param-count.sh,function-shape-ratchet.txt,param-count-ratchet.txt}`, `tools/ci/tests/test-shape-ratchet.sh`, `tests/release_gates/run_tests.sh`, `.github/workflows/c-cpp.yml` (`verify-helpers`), `docs/Add-New-Protocol.md`.
